# The Japan Year Book 1917 — Who's Who & Directories

2,094 entries, LLM-transcribed. These entries were transcribed by the vision model of Opus 5.5. Before using any of these details, you must verify specific details with the original source which is linked to whenever possible.


## The Japan Year Book

**Princess Toshiko** consort of the Prince, daughter of the late Prince Taruhito Arisugawa, was b. May 21st, 1858; married to the Prince Oct. 6th, 1876. Issue :—two sons and a daughter (Marchioness Yamanouchi). — p. 40 ([scan leaf 89](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n89/mode/1up))

**Prince Hiroyasu** Rear-Adm., 1st son, born Oct. 16th, 1875; succeeded to the House of Prince Kwachō Apr. 23rd, 1883, but returned to the present house on Jan. 16th, 1904; studied in the Naval Academy and in Germany, was wounded on board the Mikasa in the Battle of Yellow Sea, Aug., 1904; studied in England 1909-10; Captain of the Takachiho 1910; Rear-Adm. '13. Res. as above. — p. 40 ([scan leaf 89](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n89/mode/1up))

**Princess Tsuneko** consort of Prince Hiroyasu, daughter of the last Shogun, was b. Sept. 23rd, '82; married Jan. 9th, '96. Issue :—four sons and three daughters. — p. 40 ([scan leaf 89](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n89/mode/1up))

**Prince Kunika** 2nd son of Prince Sadanaru, was b. in March, 1880. — p. 40 ([scan leaf 89](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n89/mode/1up))

**Princess Bunshu** sister to Prince Sadanaru, was b. in '44; abbess at Nara. — p. 40 ([scan leaf 89](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n89/mode/1up))

**Arisugawa** The House was founded by Prince Yoshikoto (1603-38 A.D.) son of the 106th Emperor. The late Prince Takehito, 10th of the line, died in July 1913 heirless and the reigning Emperor has ordered his third son Nobuhito to become a future head of the House. Res. Sannen-cho, Koj., Tokyo. — p. 40 ([scan leaf 89](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n89/mode/1up))

**Dowager Princess Yasuko** 4th daughter of the late Marquis Mayeda, born Feb. 8th, 1864; married to the late Prince Takehito, Dec. 11th, 1880. Issue:—a daughter (Princess Isako) who married in '08 to Prince Yoshihisa Tokugawa. — p. 40 ([scan leaf 89](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n89/mode/1up))

**Dowager Princess Tadako** 7th daughter of the late Count Mizoguchi, born May 12th, 1855; married to the late Prince Taru-hito, widow in 1895. — p. 40 ([scan leaf 89](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n89/mode/1up))

**Kan-in** The House was founded by Prince Nao-hito (1703-'52 A.D.) eldest son of the 113th Emperor. Prince Kotohito, head, son of Prince Kuni-iye Fushimi and younger brother of Prince Sadanaru Fushimi, was born Sept. 22nd, 1865; studied at the Mil. Boys School '77-81 and at French mil. school, '82-91; Lieut.-Col. (Cav.) '95; Maj-Gen. '01; Lieut.-General '05; Commander of the 1st Army Division Feb. 1906; full General, and Supreme Councillor of War, Nov. '12; is Hon. President of the Red Cross Society. Res. Nagata-cho, Koj., Tokyo. — p. 40 ([scan leaf 89](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n89/mode/1up))

**Princess Chieko** consort, 2nd daughter of the late Prince Sanemi Sanjō, was born on May 25th, 1872; married to the Prince Dec. 19th, 1891. Issue : a son and five daughters. — p. 40 ([scan leaf 89](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n89/mode/1up))

**Higashi-Fushimi** (formerly Komatsu) Prince Yorihito, younger brother of Princess Fushimi and Kan-in, 17th son of the late Prince Kuni-iye, was b. Sept. 19th, 1867; studied in England and France '81-90; was second in command of cruiser Caitose which sank the Russian cruiser Novic at Korsakof, Saghalien, Aug. 20th, 1904; Captain of Kasuga, 1905; attached to the Naval Staff Board '06; 3rd class Golden Kite; Rear Adm. '09; Vice-Adm. '13; attended the coronation ceremony of King George V. with his consort. Res. Tame-ike, Akasaka, Tokyo. — p. 40 ([scan leaf 89](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n89/mode/1up))


## Imperial Court

**Princess Kaneko** consort, 1st daughter of the late Prince Tomosada Iwakura ; was b. Aug. 26th, 1876, married on Feb. 10th, 1898. Issue :—none. — p. 41 ([scan leaf 90](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n90/mode/1up))

**Kwacho.—Prince Hirotada** 4th of the line, head, 2nd son of Prince Hiroyasu Fushimi ; was b. Jan. 26th, 1902 ; made to succeed to the House in 1904, in exchange for his father who had represented it. Res. Mita Dai-machi, Tokyo. — p. 41 ([scan leaf 90](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n90/mode/1up))

**Yamashina.—Prince Takehiko** 3rd of the line, head, 1st son of the late Prince Kikumaro ; was born Feb. 13th, 1898. He has four brothers and one sister. Res. Fujimi-cho, Koj., Tokyo. — p. 41 ([scan leaf 90](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n90/mode/1up))

**Dowager Princess Tsuneko** mother, 5th daughter of the late Prince Tadayoshi Shimazu ; was b. Feb. 7th, 1874 ; married to the late Prince Nov. 26th, 1902 ; widow '08. — p. 41 ([scan leaf 90](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n90/mode/1up))

**Kaya.—Prince Tsunenori** 2nd of the line, head, 1st son of the late Prince Kuninori ; was b. Jan. 27th, 1900. The Prince has two sisters. Res. Kyoto. — p. 41 ([scan leaf 90](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n90/mode/1up))

**Dowager Princess Yoshiko** eldest daughter of the late Marquis Daigo ; was b. Oct. 20th, 1865 ; married to the late Prince 1892 ; widow 1910. — p. 41 ([scan leaf 90](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n90/mode/1up))

**Kuni.—Prince Kuniyoshi** 2nd of the line, head, 3rd son of the late Prince Asahiko ; Maj.-Gen.; was b. July 23rd, 1873 ; Sub-Lieut. 1897 ; was attached to Gen. Kuroki's Staff during the Russo-Japan War as Major (Inf.) and was granted 4th class Golden Kite ; Colonel in '08, studied in Germany '07-10 ; Maj.-Gen. '13. Res. Ichibancho, Tokyo. — p. 41 ([scan leaf 90](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n90/mode/1up))

**Princess Chikako** consort, 7th daughter of the late Prince Tadayoshi Shimazu ; was b. Oct. 19th, 1879 ; married on Dec. 13th, 1899 ; travelled through Europe '09-10. Issue :—three sons and three daughters. — p. 41 ([scan leaf 90](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n90/mode/1up))

**Prince Taka** 5th son of the late Prince Asahiko ; was b. 1875 in Kyoto ; Acting Grand Custodian of the Great Shrine of Ise. Married in 1905 Lady Shizuko, daughter of Viscount Minase. Res. Kyoto. Issue :—a son and two daughters. — p. 41 ([scan leaf 90](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n90/mode/1up))

**Nashimoto.—Prince Morimasa** 2nd of the line, head, Maj.-Gen.; 4th son of the late Prince Asahiko ; was b. March 9th, 1874 ; Sub-Lieut. '97 ; on the outbreak of the recent War hurried back from France where he was studying at a military academy ; was attached to General Oku's Staff as Capt. of Infantry ; Major 1906 ; Lieut.-Col. '08 ; granted 4th class Golden Kite ; Colonel '00. He started for Europe April, 1907 to resume his study, returning home July, '09. Res. Aoyama Kitamachi, Tokyo. — p. 41 ([scan leaf 90](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n90/mode/1up))

**Princess Itsuko** consort, and daughter of Marquis Nabeshima ; was b. Feb. 2nd, 1882 ; married Nov. 28th, 1900, and made tour in Europe '80-89. Issue :—2 daughters. — p. 41 ([scan leaf 90](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n90/mode/1up))

**Kitashirakawa.—Prince Narihisa** 3rd of the line, head, 2nd son of the late Prince Yoshihisa ; was b. April 18th, 1887 ; and married with Princess Fusako, 7th daughter of the late Emperor Meiji, b. Jan. 28th, 1890. Is Captain of Artillery. Res. Takanawa Minami-cho, Tokyo. Issue :—a son and two daughters. — p. 41 ([scan leaf 90](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n90/mode/1up))

**Dowager Princess Tomiko** mother, adopted daughter of the late Prince Hisamitsu Shimazu ; was b. Aug. 8th, 1862 ; widow in 1895. Prince Narihisa has two more brothers and four sisters, of whom the eldest was married to the son and heir of Count Kanroji in 1914, and the second Princess Takeko to Count Hoshina in 1911. — p. 41 ([scan leaf 90](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n90/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Komatsu** Dowager Princess Yoriko, consort of the late Prince Akihito Komatsu; 1st daughter of the late Yorishige Arima; was b. June 18th, 1852; and married Nov. 6th, 1869: widow Feb. 18th 1903. Res. Hashiba, Asakusa, Tokyo. — p. 42 ([scan leaf 91](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n91/mode/1up))

**Takeda** Prince Tsunehisa, Major, Cavalry; was b. '82; eldest son of the late Prince Kita-shirakawa. The house was newly created on March 30th, '06. He married Princess Masako, 6th daughter of the late Emperor Meiji, b. Sept. 30th, 1888; was decorated with 5th class Golden Kite. Res. Takanawa Minami-cho, Tokyo. Issue:—a son. — p. 42 ([scan leaf 91](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n91/mode/1up))

**Asaka** Prince Yasuhiko, Capt., Infantry; was b. '87; 8th son of the late Prince Kuni; the House having been newly created on March 30th, '06; married with Princess Nobuko, 8th daughter of the late Emperor Meiji, b. Aug. 7th, 1891. Res. Takanawa Nishi-daimachi, Tokyo. Issue:—two sons and a daughter. — p. 42 ([scan leaf 91](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n91/mode/1up))

**Higashi-Kuni** Prince Naruhiko, Capt., Inf., 9th son of the late Prince Kuni; was b. '87 in Kyoto. Was ordered by the late Emperor on Nov. 3rd, '06 to set up the House; married in May, 1915 Princess Toshiko, youngest sister to the Emperor, b. May 11th, 1896. Issue:—a son. — p. 42 ([scan leaf 91](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n91/mode/1up))


## Who's Who in Japan

**Abe, Isoh** Prof. in the Waseda Univ., b. '65 in Fukuoka-ken; studied in the Doshisha, Kyoto; then a pastor and afterward studied at Hartford College and at Glasgow; Principal of the Doshisha Middle School, which he resigned and was for some years connected with several schools in Tokyo, finally with Waseda where he has charge of Athletic Association and Preparatory Dep't. Authority on social politics and local administration; wrote several books. Add. Zoshigaya, near Tokyo. — p. 47 ([scan leaf 96](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n96/mode/1up))

**Abe, Ko** Mem. House of Peers and Lord-in-Waiting of the Kinkei Hall, b. '48 in Morioka, and entered civil service early in Meiji era either at the head office or in the provinces; M.P. for his native place in '92; Director of Shrines and Temples Bureau '96-99, Governor of Chiba '99-01, Governor of Niigata '01-03, nominated Member of the House of Peers '03; was again Governor of Niigata '05 to be transferred to Gov. of Tokyo Feb. '08 – Dec. '12. Add. Oicho, near Tokyo. — p. 47 ([scan leaf 96](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n96/mode/1up))

**Abe, Taizo** ex-Pres., now Dir., of Meiji Life and Fire Ins. Cos; b. in '49 at Toyohashi, Aichi-ken; entered Keiogijiku in '68; was its teacher for some years, then at the Imp. Univ., Tokyo, when he was 22 years old; visited U.S.A. in '77 under Viscount (then Mr.) Tanaka, Privy Councillor; resigned Gov. post and started the Meiji Life Insurance Company the following year '81, then founded ten years later the Meiji Fire Insurance Co., pioneer concerns in Japan. He was president of the Life Ins. Co. for more than 30 years. Add. Mita, Shiba, Tokyo. — p. 47 ([scan leaf 96](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n96/mode/1up))

**Abe, Teijiro** Lieut.-Gen. (ret. '17), b. 1861 in Niigata; Sub-Lieut. of Engineering Corps '72, studied at Military Staff Col. '87-89, then attached to the Staff Office; was Chief of staff of 2nd Depot Division during the Russo-Japanese War; Com. of Japanese Garrison at Tientsin '07, then Commander of Tsushima Garrison; Chief of Mil. Fortification Bureau '14-16. Add. Tsunohazu, Yodobashi, Tokyo. — p. 47 ([scan leaf 96](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n96/mode/1up))

**Adachi, Aizo** Lt.-Gen. (ret. '17), b. in '63 in Tokushima; Artillery Sub-Lieut '83, student of the Staff Col. '88-91, was Com. 11th Reg. during Russo-Japanese War, Chief Staff of 12th Division, then Commander of Garrison of Pescadores, Formosa; Inspector of Commissariat '14-16. Add. Sakamachi, Yotsuya, Tokyo. — p. 47 ([scan leaf 96](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n96/mode/1up))

**Adachi, Kenzo** M.P., b. '64 in Kumamoto; went to Korea and established several newspapers to promote the Japanese interest in the peninsula; made great efforts in regard to the organization of Kokke: - to (political party); Parliamentary Senior Secretary, of Foreign Department in the Okuma Cabinet. Add. Tsukudo-Hachiman, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 47 ([scan leaf 96](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n96/mode/1up))

**Adachi, Mine-ichiro** D.C.L. (Japan). Min. to Switzerland; b. '69 in Yamagata-ken, graduated law of the Imp. Tokyo Univ. in '92, attaché in '92, sent to Rome in '93, removed to Paris in '97, relieved of the post in '03 and attached to the head office, was in suite Peace Envoy to Portsmouth in '05, decorated with 3rd class Rising Sun with annuity in '06 for service in connection with the War. He was senior Councillor at head office before the transfer to Councillor of Paris Embassy 'important', then the Minister to Mexico '13-16. Add. Bern, Switzerland. — p. 47 ([scan leaf 96](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n96/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Adachi, Tsunayuki** Mem. of House of Peers, ex-Inspector-General of Metropolitan Police Board; b. in Satsuma, '59; filled post of chief Police Commissioner at several provinces; was appointed Director of Police Bureau, Home Office in '01; Inspector-General in '03, which post resigned in Oct. '05 in consequence of the memorable anti-peace agitation Sept. 5th, '05. Add. Nishi-kata-machi, Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 48 ([scan leaf 97](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n97/mode/1up))

**Aiba, Yosaburo** novelist, nom de plume Koson; b '55; is a prominent figure among the contemporary novelists, reputed for his light and delicate style; was connected for a while with the Yomiuri Shimbun, and now a regular contributor to the Tokyo Asahi. His chief works are Murotake, Kusano-Yuki, and some others. Add. Asahi Office, Tokyo. — p. 48 ([scan leaf 97](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n97/mode/1up))

**Akaboshi, Tenta** Gov. of Yamaguchi; b. '68 in Kumamoto; grad. Law-Imp. Tokyo Univ. '96; on duty at District Taxation Offices '97-8 Councillor of Justice Dep't '00, Secretary of Dep't of Agriculture and Commerce '03; Judge, Adm. Lit. Court '07; Gov. of Kumamoto, '13-14. Add. Yamaguchi. — p. 48 ([scan leaf 97](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n97/mode/1up))

**Akashi, Motojiro** Lieut.-Gen., Com. of 6th Division; b. at Fukuoka '65, Sub-Lieut. of Art. '83, studied at the Staff College '87 and then in Germany '94-95, staff officer of the Guards '95, was military attache to the French and the Russian Legations, held similar past at Berlin, '04-05, Maj.-Gen. '07, Chief Staff to Korean Garrison '08, then transferred to Com. of Gendarme, Korean Residency-Gen.; Vice-Chief of the General Staff Board '13-15; the present post since '15. Add. Hiroshima. — p. 48 ([scan leaf 97](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n97/mode/1up))

**Akashi, Yoichiro** Dir. of General Edu. Bur., Dept. of Education, and Dir. of Bur. for Compiling the Restoration History; b. in Tokyo '76; grad. Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ. '97, studied in France and Germany '02-04, Personal Secretary to the Minister in addition to the Councillor '05, promoted to present post '16. Add. Harukicho, Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 48 ([scan leaf 97](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n97/mode/1up))

**Akatsuka, Shosuke** Consul-General at Canton since '12; b. in Kagoshima '72; grad. the Law Coll., Imp. Tokyo Univ. '98; passed diplomatic service examination '98; Eleve-Consul at Fusan, Sec. at Berlin, Washington; Consul at Manila; Sec. Foreign Dep't, '09. Add. Japanese Consulate-General, Canton, China. — p. 48 ([scan leaf 97](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n97/mode/1up))

**Akimoto, Okitomo** Viscount (cr.' 84), formerly Daimyo of Tatebayashi; b. '57 in Gumma-ken; Minister Resident and Minister Plenip. from '90 to 92; once mem. of House of Peers and a leader of the Viscounts' coterie in the House, he being identified with the Seiyu-kai; a great connoisseur of pictorial art and a member of the Academy of Arts (London). Add. Surugadai, Tokyo. — p. 48 ([scan leaf 97](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n97/mode/1up))

**Akita, Kiyoshi** Prop. of the Sekai (daily paper), ex-M.P., barrister; b. '81 in Tokushima; studied law in some private Univ. and appointed judge; gave up the profession and entered the Niroku which was renamed the Sekai in '14. Add. Fujimaye, Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 48 ([scan leaf 97](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n97/mode/1up))


## Who's Who

**Akitsuki, Satsuo** ex-Ambassador at Vienna; b. '58 in Miyazaki-ken; graduated the former Law Col., of Justice Dep't in '84, studied at official expense in Belgium and Germany from '85 to '91, Councillor in '92, transferred to Dep't Councillor; Consul at Fusan in '96; Legation Sec. at Seoul in the same year, transferred to Paris in '99, to Petrograd in '02, Minister Resident to Sweden-Norway in '04; Minister at Brussels in May '07; Ambassador at Vienna Dec., '09-June, '14. Add. Bentencho, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 49 ([scan leaf 98](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n98/mode/1up))

**Akiyama, Genzo** barrister in Tokyo, b. '59 in Shizuoka-ken; graduated Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ., '79; appointed a clerk at the Justice Dep't; next Judge at various local courts; Chief of the Nagasaki District Court '87 and next of Yokohama District Court '92; Judge of the Supreme Court '98 which post he soon resigned to open law offices in Tokyo and Yokohama. Add. Kogai-cho, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 49 ([scan leaf 98](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n98/mode/1up))

**Akiyama, Masanosuke** D.C.L. (Japan), Councillor of Chosen Gov't-Gen.; b. '66 in Hiroshima-ken; graduated Law, Imp. Univ. of Tokyo in '86, to be connected afterward with civil affairs section of the War and Navy; removed to Seoul in '13. Add. Yakoji-machi, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 49 ([scan leaf 98](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n98/mode/1up))

**Akiyama, Shinshi** Rear-Adm., Com. of 2nd Torpedo Flotilla; b. in '69 Ehime, being brother to Gen. Akiyama. Graduated from Naval Academy, is said to have been the most trusted adviser of Adm. Tōgo in Russo-Japanese war, when he was Commander on board the Admiral's flagship Mikasa. Chief of Naval Affairs Bureau till February '16 then despatched to Europe and America; present post in December '16. Add. Shimoshibuya, Tokyo. — p. 49 ([scan leaf 98](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n98/mode/1up))

**Akiyama, Teisuke** M.P., b. at Kurashiki, Okayama-ken, '68, grad. Law Imp. Univ. Tokyo '90, earning his expenses by hard working; was appointed about '92 junior auditor of the Board of Audit; started the Niroku (now Sekai). Add. Nishiki-cho, Kanda, Tokyo. — p. 49 ([scan leaf 98](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n98/mode/1up))

**Akiyama, Yoshifuru** Gen., Com. of Chosen Garrison; b. '56 in Ehime-ken; Sub-Lieut., '79; Major-Gen., '99; Lieut-Gen., '09; fought in Japan-China War, Boxers' Trouble and Russo-Japanese War, Superintendent of Cavalry, Military Education Bd.; then Commander 13th Div., '13; Com. of Guards Division '15, present post '16; Gen. Nov. '16. Add. Seoul, Chosen. — p. 49 ([scan leaf 98](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n98/mode/1up))

**Amano, Tameyuki** D.C.L. (Japan), economist. President of the Waseda University; b. in Saga-ken, '59; grad. Tokyo University, '82; has devoted himself from that time to educational work at Waseda, of which he is one of the "three main pillars"; sat at 1st and 2nd sessions in the House of Rep'tatives, but soon withdrew from the uncongenial atmosphere of practical politics. Wrote "Principles of Economics" and others; is edt. and prop. of the "Oriental Economist," a thrice-a-month journal of high authority and extensive circulation. Add. Iidamachi, Tokyo. — p. 49 ([scan leaf 98](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n98/mode/1up))

**Ando, Izumi** Lieut.-Gen., Com. of 13th Div.; b. '65 in Kochi-ken; Sub-Lieut. '83, Capt. '93, Maj.-Gen. '10; for several years attache to Legations abroad; Com. of 1st Inf. Brigade '10; promoted Lieut.-Gen. and transferred to present post, Feb., '15. Add. Takata, Niigata-ken. — p. 49 ([scan leaf 98](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n98/mode/1up))

**Ando, Kenkichi** Mg. Dir. of the Nippon Elec. Light Co., mem. of the Tokyo City Assembly; b. '62 in Gifu-ken; studied in the Keio Univ.; passed examinations for Higher Civil service and the Bar; in '12 giving up the practice, entered business. Add. Nakackachimachi, Shitaya, Tokyo. — p. 49 ([scan leaf 98](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n98/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Ando, Kensuke** Mayor of Yokohama ; b. '54 in Kochi-ken ; entered the diplomatic service ; studied law in a Russian Univ., and was appointed Judge ; in '96 appointed Gov. of Niigata, next transferred to Chiba and Nagasaki ; once elected M.P. for Aichi-ken. Add. Shimo-Osaki, near Tokyo. — p. 50 ([scan leaf 99](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n99/mode/1up))

**Ando, Taro** Pres. of Temperance Society ; b. '46 in Tokyo ; joined Enomoto's pro-Tokugawa movement at Hakodate ; was given office at the Foreign Office when released from imprisonment and was in the suite of the late Prince Iwakura's memorable envoy early in Meiji era. Consul at Hongkong, '74-85, then at Shanghai '86, and Consul-General at Honolulu '85-89, where he was converted, returning home as fervent Christian and total abstainer. Subsequently held post of Director of Com. Bureau, Foreign Office, and of Commerce and Ind., Agri. and Com. Dep't. Add. Hommura-cho, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 50 ([scan leaf 99](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n99/mode/1up))

**Ando, Teibi** Baron (cr. '05), Gen., Gov.-Gen. of Formosa ; b. at Iida, Nagano-ken, in '53 ; was educated at the now defunct Osaka Military School and was appointed Sub-Lieut. '73 ; Maj. '83 ; Colonel '94; Maj.-Gen. '98; Lieut.-Gen. '05; Gen. in '14; present post in '15. In the Russo-Japanese war he at first commanded the 19th Brigade of Infantry and was soon made to lead the 10th Division. He assaulted the enemy's position at Wan-paoshan in the memorable battle of Mukden ; Commander of Korean Garrison 1913-15 ; present post in '15. Add. Kagacho, Ichigaya, Tokyo. — p. 50 ([scan leaf 99](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n99/mode/1up))

**Ando, Yasutaro** ex-Chief of Electric Lighting Bureau, Tokyo City Office ; was b. in 1868 in Yamanashi-ken. He was Dir. of the municipalized Tokyo Street Railway which he left in '14, and is now Auditor of the Kinugawa Hydro-electric Co. Add. Shiba Park, Tokyo. — p. 50 ([scan leaf 99](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n99/mode/1up))

**Anesaki, Masaharu** D. Litt. (Japan), Prof. in Coll. of Literature, Imp. Tokyo Univ.; b. '73 in Kyoto ; after grad. Philosophy at the Univ. in '96, studied in Germany and England for a few years ; Prof. at Harvard '13-15. Add. Hakusan Goten, Koishikawa, Tokyo. — p. 50 ([scan leaf 99](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n99/mode/1up))

**Anraku, Kanemichi** ex-Chief Police Commissioner Tokyo, Mem. House of Peers ; b. '50 in Kagoshima ; was connected with the Police service from about 1875, next held Governorship of Yamaguchi, Fukushima, Gifu, etc.; resigned the Commissioner-ship in '08 when the Seiyu-kai Ministery fell, and entered commercial circle. Pres. of Chiyoda Gas Co. till '11, and reappointed to the Chief Police Commissioner '11-13 ; retired the post at the fall of Saionji Cabinet but appointed for 3rd time '13-14. Add. Nishiokubo, Tokyo. — p. 50 ([scan leaf 99](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n99/mode/1up))

**Aoki, Nobuzumi** Lieut.-Gen., military advisor to China and ex-Com. of Port Arthur ; b. in '59 in Miyazaki-ken ; Sub-Lieut. of Cavalry in '79 ; studied in Belgium 1890-93 ; Col. '02 ; Maj.-Gen. '07 ; has repeatedly been attached to the Japanese Legation at Peking. Add. Peking. — p. 50 ([scan leaf 99](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n99/mode/1up))

**Aoki, Tetsuji** D. C. L., lawyer and patent attorney ; b. in '75 in Gifu-ken ; grad. from the Keio-gijuku '97 ; passed the examination for the Bench in '98 and was a probationary Judge at Kobe for a few months. Despatched by the Alma mater to Germany to study Commercial Law at Berlin and Gettingen '99-02 ; Prof. at the Keio, left it in '08. and opened law office. Add. Shirokane, Imazato, Tokyo. — p. 50 ([scan leaf 99](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n99/mode/1up))


## Who's Who

**Aoki, Tetsutaro** Dir. of the Industrial Bank of Japan ; b. '67 in Okayama ; had long been connected with Yokohama Specie Bank and was its London Agent for many years till about 1904 ; Gov. Commercial Agent at Hongkong '10-12. Add. Osaki, near Tokyo. — p. 51 ([scan leaf 100](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n100/mode/1up))

**Aoyama, Tanemichi, M.D** Dean and Prof. of Med. Coll., Imp. Tokyo Univ.; b. '59 in Gifu-ken ; studied medicine in Germany; was almost killed by pest at Hongkong in '94 where he with Dr. Kitazato was despatched by the Government to investigate the disease, and by which he was affected. He is one of most distinguished experts in internal diseases, and is physician-in-ordinary to the Court. Add. Yumi-cho, Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 51 ([scan leaf 100](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n100/mode/1up))

**Arai, Kentaro** Dir. of Finance Dept. of Korean Gov.-Gen. ; b. '63 in Niigata and after grad. Law, Imp. Univ. Tokyo in '90 he was connected with the Treasury in which he rose to the position of the Vice-Minister in '06; removed to the present position in '07. Add. Seoul, Korea. — p. 51 ([scan leaf 100](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n100/mode/1up))

**Arai, Taiji** Mem. House of Peers, director of various commercial establishments in Formosa; b. '51 in Sendai; studied under Nakae Chomin the Rousseau of Japan; assisted late Mr. Tomita in the establishment of the Bank of Japan; then presidents of Kanegafuchi Cotton Spinning Co., etc.; elected representative of the Highest Tax Payers for Miyagi '99. Add. Yedogawa, Koishikawa, Tokyo. — p. 51 ([scan leaf 100](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n100/mode/1up))

**Arakawa, Gitaro** Mem. House of Peers; b. '62 in Tokyo; graduated Col. of Law, Imp. Univ. Tokyo in 84, to enter civil service in the Home Office. Was Councillor at the head office and next Secretary at Toyama, Gumma, Kanagawa; Governor of Tottori '97, Miye '99, Kagawa 1900. and Nagasaki '03-10; Mayor of Yokohama '10-13. Add. Nakanegishi, Shitaya, Tokyo. — p. 51 ([scan leaf 100](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n100/mode/1up))

**Arakawa, Goro** Chief Edit. of Hiroshima Chugoku, M.P. and Par. Jr. Secy. of Communication Dept.; b. '65 in Hiroshima; grad. Kokugakuin and Nippon Univ.; became Edit. of present daily; established a girls' school, in Hiroshima. Returned to the House since '04; Par. Secy. in '15. Add. "Chugoku Shimbun," Hiroshima. — p. 51 ([scan leaf 100](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n100/mode/1up))

**Arakawa, Miyoji** Minister to Madrid (app. 1909); b. '57 in Kagoshima; grad. Eng'ing Col., Tokyo, in '80; clerk at the Legation, London, in '86; attaché in '87, Eléve Consul at Tientsin in '90, at Ninsen '94, attached to the 2nd Army during the Japan-China War, Consul at Tientsin in '95, at Suchow in '96, Consul at London in '96, where he was promoted to Consul-General, decorated with 3rd Class Rising Sun with grant of money for the war. Add. Japanese Legation, Madrid. — p. 51 ([scan leaf 100](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n100/mode/1up))

**Arichi, Shinanosuke** Baron (cr. '00). Vice-Admiral (retired). Mem. House of Peers; was b. in Choshu, '43; Lieut.-Commander, '72; Commander of Yokosuka Admiralty and Kure; then successively Chief of Naval Staff; Commander-in-Chief of Standing Fleet, &c., is at present devoting himself to promoting the cause of the Japan Maritime Society which he presides. Add. Awaji-cho, Kanda, Tokyo. — p. 51 ([scan leaf 100](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n100/mode/1up))

**Ariga, Nagabumi** Manager of the Mitsui Firm ; b. '65 in Osaka-fu, and younger brother to Dr. Nagao Ariga ; grad. Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ., in '89 ; was in Government service for a long while, ultimately rising to be a Bureau Director in Dep't of Agriculture and Commerce about '02. Add Iigura, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 51 ([scan leaf 100](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n100/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Ariga, Nagao** D.C.L. & D. Lit. (Japan), b. in Settsu, '60; grad. Imp. Tokyo Univ., '81; Private Secretary to late Prince Ito (then Pres. of Privy Council), '87; afterwards Councillor at the Department of Agriculture and Commerce; took charge of affairs relating to international law during the Japan-China War of '94-95; and held a similar post in the later campaign. He was Professor at Waseda University, a recipient of Imp. Japanese Academy prize in '12 and adviser to China where he became an ardent supporter of Yuan Shih-kai. Add. Myogadani, Koishikawa, Tokyo. — p. 52 ([scan leaf 101](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n101/mode/1up))

**Arima, Ryokitsu** Vice-Adm., Chief of Naval Education Board since '16 and hero of the Port Arthur blockading operation in Russo-Japanese war; b. at Wakayama '62; midshipman '88, successively held posts of Captain of cr. Kasagi, chief staff of Takeshiki Naval Port, Captain of cr. Iwate, Dir. Naval Artillery School, etc.; promoted to Rear-Adm. '09; Com. of 1st Squadron '12; appointed Vice-Admiral '13; President of the Naval Academy '14. Add. Naval Department, Tokyo. — p. 52 ([scan leaf 101](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n101/mode/1up))

**Arima, Sumibumi** Vis., Sub. Lieut of Cavalry (ret), Lord steward to of Prince Higashi-Fushimi; b. '68 of a former daimyo's family of Maruoka, Echizen; graduated from College of Agriculture, Imp. Tokyo Univ. '97; Chamberlain to the Crown Prince '00-10. Add. Ukyo-machi, Yotsuya, Tokyo. — p. 52 ([scan leaf 101](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n101/mode/1up))

**Arima, Yorikazu** Count, formerly Daimyo of the Kurume clan, 5th son and heir to his predecessor; b. '64, served in the Imperial Court for years as Chamberlain. Add. Hashiba, Asakusa, Tokyo. — p. 52 ([scan leaf 101](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n101/mode/1up))

**Arimatsu, Hideyoshi** Dir. of Legislative and Pension Bureaux, b. at Okayama, '63; grad. '88 from the German Language School, Tokyo and was in the Justice Dep't till '93; Councillor of the Dep't of Agr. and Commerce, then of Home Office and of the Legislative Bureau '94 to '08; attended Inter'nal Sen'ry Conference held at Madrid and similar confence for exconvicts at Antwerp in '98; transferred to the Police Bureau same year; again Councillor of the Legislative Bureau in '02; Dir. of Police Bureau in '03; Governor of Miyeken '04, transferred to the Police Bureau in '08, Dir. of Imp. Foresty Bureau, '11-13; Chief Sec. to the Privy Council '14-16. Add. Hommuracho, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 52 ([scan leaf 101](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n101/mode/1up))

**Ario, Keiju** Mng. of the Hypothec Bank of Japan, b. '49. in Gifu-ken; was once an official of the Treasury, then entered banking circle and was appointed to present post on its foundation. Add. Kami-fujimayo, Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 52 ([scan leaf 101](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n101/mode/1up))

**Arisaka, Shozo** D. Eng., Inspector of Naval Ordance and Prof. at the Imperial University of Tokyo; b. in '67 in Tokyo; graduated his special course at the University in '90; sent by the Navy to France where he studied at Hotchkiss and was steadily promoted, meanwhile devising a new plug for 12th quickfirers; has been sent twice to England on official business. Add. Tamagawa, Ebara-gun, Tokyo-fu. — p. 52 ([scan leaf 101](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n101/mode/1up))

**Arita, Jo** Major-General, Com. of 2nd Brigade of Field Artillery. b. '63 in Hiroshima; graduated at the Military College '86; appointed Sub-Lieut. '86; Capt. '96; Major '98; Colonel '03; held the position of the Chief Staff to the 4th Depot Division as well as of the Commissary Staff during the Russo-Japanese war; was awarded the 3rd Order of the Golden Kite for his services. Com. of 1st Heavy Art. Brig. '13. Add. Konodai, near Tokyo. — p. 52 ([scan leaf 101](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n101/mode/1up))


## Who's Who

**Ariyoshi, Akira** Consul-General at Shanghai, b. '76 in Kyoto; graduated from Tokyo Higher Commercial School '98; passed the examination for the diplomatic services '98; appointed Consul at Fusan, Newchang, and Secretary to Japanese Embassy at Paris, then present post in '09. Add. Japanese Consulate-General, Shanghai. — p. 53 ([scan leaf 102](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n102/mode/1up))

**Ariyoshi, Chuichi** Gov. of Kanagawa, b. '70 in Tango; grad. Law, at Imp. Tokyo University in '96, Sec. Home Office till '08, Governor of Chiba-ken '08–10, transferred to Dir. of Gen. Affairs, Korean Res.-Gen. in '10, Gov. of Miyazaki '11–15. Add. Yokohama. — p. 53 ([scan leaf 102](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n102/mode/1up))

**Asabuki, Eiji** ex-Director of the Mitsui Company; b. '49 in Oita-ken was in youth a hot-headed anti-foreign fanatic and came up to Tokyo to make attempt on the late Fukuzawa, a pioneer advocate of foreign learning, to be soon converted by him into one of his eager disciples. After grad. the Keiogijuku, he entered the Mitsubishi; started direct export business of raw silk in which he failed. Next he entered the Mitsui and pushed its spinning business (Kanegafuchi Sp. Mill) to the present state of prosperity. Add. Kobikicho, Kyobashi, Tokyo. — p. 53 ([scan leaf 102](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n102/mode/1up))

**Asabuki, Tsunekichi** Mng.-Dir. Mitsukoshi Dry Goods Store, Ltd.; b. '77 in Tokyo, son of the above, studied at Keio-Gijiku and in England; entered Bank of Japan; then Mitsui Bussan and transferred to New York Branch; later established the Chiyoda-gumi. Add. Akashicho, Kyobashi, Tokyo. — p. 53 ([scan leaf 102](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n102/mode/1up))

**Asada, Nobuoki** Baron (cr. '07), Gen. (ret. '16), 2nd Class Golden Kite and Grand Cordon of Rising Sun (war honour), b. at Kawagoye (near Tokyo), '48; grad. Mil. Academy at Osaka, '73 and commissioned ensign; fought in the Civil War of '77 as Capt. (local rank), Maj. '84, Lieut.-Col. '87, Col. '94, Major-Gen. '97 and Chief Staff of Eastern Mil. Section; Com. of a Brigade of various Divisions; took active part as Brig. Com. at Fenshuiling, and succeeded Gen. Hasegawa after Liaoyang as Com. of the Guards Division in '04-5 war; promoted to full Gen. '12. Superintendent of Education Board '12-13; then mem. of Military Council of War. Add. Ichigaya, Tokyo. — p. 53 ([scan leaf 102](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n102/mode/1up))

**Asada, Tokunori** Mem. House of Peers, b. '48 in Kyoto; First Class Councillor to Japanese Legation at Washington, U.S.A. '74; Councillor of the Department for Foreign Affairs, '79; Director of the Bureau of Commercial Affairs, '86; Governor of Nagano-ken and next of Kanagawa-ken, '99; Vice-Minister for Foreign Affairs, '00; then that of Communications, which he resigned, '02. Add. Shimo Rokubancho, Tokyo. — p. 53 ([scan leaf 102](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n102/mode/1up))

**Asahina, Chisen** journalist, b. '62 in Mito; left '83 the Imperial Tokyo University, law, unfinished on account of ill health; shortly afterward joined the staff of the Nichi Nichi, when Viscount Ito (Miyoji) was proprietor, and was its editor for many years. Went twice to Europe; left the Nichi Nichi about 1905 when the proprietor changed, and is at present a journalistic free lance. Add. Omori, near Tokyo. — p. 53 ([scan leaf 102](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n102/mode/1up))

**Asakawa, Toshinobu** Lieut.-Gen., Superintendent of the Horse Adm. Bureau; b. '60 in Yamanashi; grad. Mil. College '86, Capt. '98; Col. '04; Lieut-Gen. '09; Maj-Gen. '14, was awarded 3rd Class Golden Kite in connection with the Russo-Japan war. Add. Okubo, nr. Tokyo. — p. 53 ([scan leaf 102](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n102/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Asano, Nagakoto** Marquis, Lord-in-Waiting of the Jako Hall; b. 42; is of the former Daimyo of Hiroshima with 426,000 koku which was founded by Nagamasa 1546-1610 A.D. one of the great generals of Hideyoshi; was accredited to Rome, 1882 '84. Add. Yayoicho, Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 54 ([scan leaf 103](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n103/mode/1up))

**Asano, Osuké** Dr. E., chief electrician of the Department of Communications, b. in '59 in Okayama; grad. electricity at the Gov. Eng. College in '81, since then he has chiefly been connected with that Department, meanwhile despatched to Europe and America two or three times. Add. Kogaicho, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 54 ([scan leaf 103](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n103/mode/1up))

**Asano, Soichiro** President of the Toyo Steamship Co., and Dir. of many other concerns; was b '48 in Toyama-ken. He arrived in Tokyo '71 with empty purse; proceeded to Yokohama supporting himself by hawking cheap viands; bought from the Yokohama Gas Works coaltar and other refuses then left unutilized, and laid the foundation of the present fortune. In '75 he brought the Gov. Cement Works at Fukagawa and began to produce "Asano Cement" now widely known; started tank oil business, '93 and promoted with others the Toyo S.S. Co., '95. Add. Shiba Tamachi, Tokyo. — p. 54 ([scan leaf 103](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n103/mode/1up))

**Atomi, Takino** b. in '40 at Osaka, one of the distinguished lady painters and educators; founder and proprietor of the Atomi Girls' School. Visited U.S.A. on the occasion of St. Louis Fair. Add. Yanagicho, Koi., Tokyo. — p. 54 ([scan leaf 103](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n103/mode/1up))

**Awazu, Seiryo** D. C. L. (Japan), Prof. of Imp. Tokyo Univ., Waseda Univ., etc., insurance expert; was b. at Kyoto in '71; grad. Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ., '94, and has since been engaged in insurance business at the Department of Agriculture and Commerce, as organizer of a number of life insurance cos., besides undertaking teaching. Attended the World's Insurance Congress held at New York '03. Add. Misakicho, Kanda, Tokyo. — p. 54 ([scan leaf 103](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n103/mode/1up))

**Ayai, Tadahiko** Aud. of Murai Sav. Bank, Shibaura Iron Works, and Chief of Acct. Sec. of Tokyo Electric Co.; b. '71 in Oita-ken; grad. Post and Telegram School in Tokyo; served Communication Dept. for years; went to U.S.A. to accomplish his study in '90; after returning home he joined the Murai Bros. & Co. Add. Takada, near Tokyo. — p. 54 ([scan leaf 103](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n103/mode/1up))

**Baba, Genji** Judge of Court of Cassation; b. '60; grad. Law, Imp. Univ. of Tokyo, class '85; to be appointed a junior judge in Tokyo Court and Judge of Supreme Court, and ultimately rising to the present post in Apr. '13. Add. Mita Tsunamachi, Tokyo. — p. 54 ([scan leaf 103](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n103/mode/1up))

**Baba, Michihisa** b. '47 at Iwase, Toyama-ken, of a family of shipowner, is Pres. Iwase Bank and Dir. Tokyo Marine Ins. Co., Imp. Marine Ins. Co., Innoshima Dockyard, and others. Is a millionaire in Toyama and one of the wealthiest individual ship-owners in Japan. Add. Tori-Nichome, Nih., Tokyo. — p. 54 ([scan leaf 103](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n103/mode/1up))

**Chiwaki, Morinosuke** Principal of Tokyo Dental College, b. '70 at Abiko, Hitachi; grad. from Keio Univ. and entered The Tokyo Shimpo; learned dentistry under Kisai Takayama, famous dentist; established present Coll. 1900. Add. Misakicho, Kanda, Tokyo. — p. 54 ([scan leaf 103](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n103/mode/1up))


## Who's Who in Japan

**Chinda** Sutemi, Viscount (Bar. '07, Vis. '11), Ambassador to St. James' Court; b. at Hirosaki, '56; grad. from an American University, '81; appointed a clerk of the Foreign Office, '85 and had charge of the Telegraph Section in the same office; appointed Consul at San Francisco, '90; removed to Chemulpo; Consul-General at Shanghai, '95 Minister Resident at Brazil, next at the Hague, '99-00; Minister Ple. Envoy Ext. at Petrograd, 1900; recalled in '01 by the then Foreign Min. Baron Komura to take charge of the Office while the Minister was away to Portsmouth and Peking. In recognition of this special service he was raised to Peerage. Amb. to Court of Berlin '08; Washington Nov. 1911; London '16. Add. Japanese Embassy, Great Britain. — p. 55 ([scan leaf 104](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n104/mode/1up))

**Chujo** Seiichiro, Architect, Hon. Mem. of Society of Architects (London), and of Royal Society of Arts (London); b. '68 at Yonezawa; grad. Eng. Coll. (Architects', Imp. Tokyo Univ.; further studied at Cambridge (Eng.); on returning home entered Edu. Dept.; again studied in England '03-07; opened Sone-Chujo Office '08, besides his own. Add. Hayashi-cho, Komagome, Tokyo. — p. 55 ([scan leaf 104](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n104/mode/1up))

**Dan** Takuma, D. Eng., Director of Mitsui Bank, Mitsui Mining Co., &c.; b. '78 at Fukuoka; was sent for study to America early in Meiji era by the Lord of Fukuoka and completed the course of mining at the Boston Tech. Institute, U.S.A., and was for some years teacher of English at Gov. school; technical official at the Gov. Meteorological Observatory and next at the Miike Coalmine, with the purchase of which by the Mitsui he entered the service of the Firm. Add. Harajuku, Sendagaya, Tokyo. — p. 55 ([scan leaf 104](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n104/mode/1up))

**Date** Munenobu, Marquis, b. '50, son of Munenori who took a distinguished part in upholding the cause of the Restoration; this scion had held fief of 100,000 koku at Uwajima. Add. Shirokane, Shiba, Tokyo. — p. 55 ([scan leaf 104](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n104/mode/1up))

**de Becker** J. E. LL.B.; D.C.L. International Lawyer who specializes in cases requiring knowledge of European, American and Japanese Laws. B. in London, England '63, came to Japan in '87, is the Legal Adviser to the Yokohama and Tokyo Foreign Board of Trade and standing Counsel of the leading foreign Banks in Japan. Local correspondent of the Society of Comparative Legislation, London. Has been practising law since '94, and is widely known as one of the foremost lawyers of this country under the name of "Kobayashi Beika." Has contributed several works to Japanese legal literature such as "Annotated Civil Code of Japan," "Commentary on the Commercial Code of Japan," "Elements of Japanese Jurisprudence," etc.; is also the author of several books in a lighter vein dealing with various phases of Japanese social life, history and phenomena; is an ardent advocate of drastic reform in Criminal Procedure. Add. 75 Yamashitacho, Yokohama and Yuigahama, Kamakura. — p. 55 ([scan leaf 104](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n104/mode/1up))

**Den** Keniiro, Baron (cr. '07). Mem. of House of Peers and Minister of Communications since '16; b. '55 in Hyogo-ken; held at several places post of local Chief Police Inspector; Vice-Min. of Communications, Jan.-Jun. '98; President of the Kansai Rly Co., at the same time M.P. for Hyogo-ken; was again Vice-Min. of Communications '00-06. He is now a prominent figure in business circles. Add. Hommuracho, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 55 ([scan leaf 104](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n104/mode/1up))


## Who's Who

**Dewa, Shigeto** Admiral, Baron (cr. '07), holder of 2nd Class Golden Kite and Grand Cordon of the Rising Sun; b. '55 at Aizu; grad. Naval Academy in '82; was Capt. of cruiser Takachiho in the Japan-China war 1894-95; chief of committee to bring home Tokiwa from England '99; Rear-Adm. 1900, and commanded the Standing Squadron at the time of the Boxer's trouble; afterward transferred to Chief Construction Board at Yokosuka, Dir. of Naval Affairs Bureau, etc.; despatched to U.S.A. to represent Japan at Panama Exposition in '15. Add. Mita Tsunacho, Tokyo. — p. 56 ([scan leaf 105](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n105/mode/1up))

**Dohi, Keizo** M.D., dermatologist, Prof. Med. Col., Imp. Tokyo Univ. and Chairman of the Japanese Derm. Society; grad. the Med. Col. '91 and Univ. Hall; studied his speciality in Austria, Germany, and France, '94-98; appointed to the present situation on his returning home; was on suite of the late Prince Komatsu when His Highness attended the Coronation Ceremony of King Edward, '02. Corresponding member of the Vienese ('02), Berliner ('03), and Parisian Derm. Society ('11), recommended to the honorable member of the Italian Derm. Society '05; Editor of the Japanese Journal of Derm. & Urinology. Add. Shimo-Nibancho, Koj., Tokyo. — p. 56 ([scan leaf 105](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n105/mode/1up))

**Doi, Michio** Chairman of the Osaka Chamber of the Commerce, Pres. Osaka Elec. Light Co., and Keihin Elec. R'ly Co., etc.; b. in Kanagawa, '41; was a Judge till '34 when he entered the service of the Konoike family, Osaka, of which he is now an adviser. Visited U.S.A. in '09 as one of the Business men's party invited by American Chamber of Commerce. Add. Jeancho, Kitaku, Osaka. — p. 56 ([scan leaf 105](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n105/mode/1up))

**Ebara, Soroku** educationalist, a leader of the Seiyu-kai, Member of House of Peers since '12; b. in Numazu, '42; was first a military officer; and next for somewhile Secretary of the Prefecture of Shizuoka 70. He is chiefly known in connection with subsequent non-official career; has sat several times in the House where he lately represented Tokyo. Is Director of Azabu Middle School, Mem. of the Higher Education Council and is a devout Christian, and founder and Pres. of Y.M.C.A. Tokyo. Add. Hommuracho, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 56 ([scan leaf 105](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n105/mode/1up))

**Ebina, Danjo** one of veteran pastors; b. '56 in Yanagawa; studied at the Foreign Language School in Kumamoto; then theology at the Doshisha; has subsequently led an active life in evangelistic and educational world, having established a number of churches and schools, being at present pastor of the Hongo Church, Tokyo. Add. Hayashicho, Koishikawa, Tokyo. — p. 56 ([scan leaf 105](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n105/mode/1up))

**Egi, Chu** Barrister, b. '58, brother of Senshi Egi; grad. Law, Tokyo Imp. Univ., class '84; he practised his profession, giving lectures on Criminal Law in the Chuo Univ. all the while; an official of the Judicial Office and of the Foreign Office; again resumed his former profession. Has written "Principles of Criminal Law," and is a noted satirist. Add. Yochomachi, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 56 ([scan leaf 105](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n105/mode/1up))

**Egi, Senshi** Mem. House of Peers, Lord-in-Waiting of the Kinkei Hall, brother of above, b. '53, studied Law at Tokyo Gov. Univ. and was in service of Education Dept. '72-77, transferred to Home Office as Dir. of Local Adm. Bureau '90-97, was next Governor of Ibaraki, Aichi, Hiroshima and Kumamoto till '06 when he resigned on account of illness. Add. Yochomachi, Okubo, Tokyo. — p. 56 ([scan leaf 105](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n105/mode/1up))


## Who's Who in Japan

**Egi, Yoku** Mem. of House of Peers (nom. '16), b. '73 in Yamaguchi-ken, adopted son and heir of the above; grad. Law Coll., Imp. Tokyo Univ. '97, and studied at Univ. Hall; Sec. Chief of Colonial Bureau (abolished later) '10-13; appointed Chief Secy. of 2nd Okuma Cabinet, 1914-16. Add. Mita Shikokumachi, Tokyo. — p. 57 ([scan leaf 106](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n106/mode/1up))

**Eguchi, Teijō** Mng. of Business Dep't, Mitsubishi Firm; was b. '65 in Kochi; grad. from Tokyo Higher Com. School '87 and after having been a school teacher for same years, entered the Firm '91, first as member on the staff of Banking Dep't; sub-Mng. of Mining Dep't at Head Office in '97; to the present post '00. Add. Akagishita, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 57 ([scan leaf 106](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n106/mode/1up))

**Eitaki, Hisakichi** ex-Con.-Gen. at Honolulu '12-14; b. '66 in Echigo; studied law and became Judge in '93 and transferred to Foreign Office the same year, as Sec. at Consulate at Fusan in '93, was Consul at Ninsen, Shanghai, Shashi, Sydney and Hankow between '94 and '05, Consul-General at Shanghai '05 to '09, then at Chientao till '12. Add. Tozuka, Tokyo. — p. 57 ([scan leaf 106](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n106/mode/1up))

**Ema, Shun-ichi** Barrister, M.P., Mem. of the Tokyo Municipality; b. '61, in Totomi, and adopted by the Ema. Early came up to Tokyo to study politics; was admitted to the Bar in '93 and afterwards elected Pres. of the Tokyo Barrister's Association; was elected Vice-Pres. of Tokyo Prefectural Assembly '89. Add. Kuromoncho, Shitaya, Tokyo. — p. 57 ([scan leaf 106](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n106/mode/1up))

**Endo, Chūji** Judge of the Court of Cassation; b. '66 in Tokyo; graduated from the Law College of the Tokyo Imp. Univ. '88; appointed Probationary Judge '90; after successively served as Judge of Osaka District Court, of Nara District Court and of Tokyo Appeal Court, promoted to present post in '05. Add. Zōshigaya, Tokyo. — p. 57 ([scan leaf 106](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n106/mode/1up))

**Fujii, Koichi** Adm., mem. of Admirals' Council b. '57 in Okayama-ken; Sub-Lieut. '83 to be rapidly promoted to the present rank in '09; took part in Russo-Japanese war as Com. of the Azuma, chief staff of the 2nd and 1st squadrons. Attaché to the Japanese Legation, Berlin; Com. Saseho Adm'ty in '14; Com.-in-Chief of 1st Fleet '15; Com. of Yokosuka Adm'ty '15-16; present post Dec. '16. Add. Naval Office. — p. 57 ([scan leaf 106](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n106/mode/1up))

**Fujii, Shigeta** Lieut.-Gen. (ret.), ex-Com. of 12th Div.; b. '60 in Hyogoken; Ensign of Artillery in '79; entered the Military Staff Col. in '84; studied tactics in Germany in '90; Staff Officer to the 2nd Army in the Japan-China War; Major-Gen., '02 and Dir.-Military Staff College, Chief Staff to Gen. Kuroki's Army in the '04-5 war, Dir. of Art. and Engineering School, '06; Com. of Tokyo Bay Forts '09; the above position in '11, retired in '14. Add. Oban-cho, Yotsuya, Tokyo. — p. 57 ([scan leaf 106](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n106/mode/1up))

**Fujimura, Yoshiro** Baron, Bar. of Middle Tem., Sec. Chief of the Mitsui Bussan; b. in Kumamoto in '70; proceeded to England after finishing secondary education in Japan, and grad. Law at Cambridge, returning home '03, when he entered the Mitsui, and was its London agent till '10, then removed to the head office. Add. Nakano, near Tokyo. — p. 57 ([scan leaf 106](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n106/mode/1up))

**Fujinami Kototada** Viscount (cr. '84), b. at Kyoto '53; Chamberlain to late Emperor from childhood, and from '80 Superintendent of the Im- perial Mews, Deputy Pres. of Horse Adm. Bureau which he has resigned. Add. Iigura, Tokyo. — p. 57 ([scan leaf 106](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n106/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Fujioka, Ichisuke** Dr. Eng. (Japan), Pres. Iwakuni Elec. Tram. and Tokyo Elec. Cos., b. in Choshu, '56; grad. from Tokyo Engineering College in '81; appointed its Professor from '84 to '86 when he retired to start electric business; chief electrician to the Tokyo Electric Light Co. in '92 and founder of the Tokyo Street Railway Co. which with two other street railway Co.'s was fused '06; he has been to Europe and America four or five times for study, on business etc. and was under Edison for somewhile. Add. Higashicho, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 58 ([scan leaf 107](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n107/mode/1up))

**Fujisawa, Ikunosuke** Lawyer, M.P. (Doshikai) and Parl. Secy. of Home Dep't; b. '59 in Sendai; studied law, to become lawyer in '80 and distinguished himself in connexion with the Fukushima affairs in '83. Elected mem. of Municipal Assembly, Sendai, then of Miyagi Prefectural Assembly, of which he was once Chairman; M.P. for 7th time in '15; nominated Parliamentary Secy. of Communication Dep't, July '15, removed to Home Dep't, same year. Add. Ichibancho, Sendai. — p. 58 ([scan leaf 107](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n107/mode/1up))

**Fujisawa, Rikitaro** Dr. Sc. (Japan), Prof. of Mathematics at Tokyo University; b. in Niigata-ken, '61; pursued his special study in England and Germany, '83-87. He has written several works on Mathematics. Add. Suwacho, Koishikawa, Tokyo. — p. 58 ([scan leaf 107](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n107/mode/1up))

**Fujise, Masajiro** Mng.-Dir. of the Mitsui Bussan Kaisha; b. '67 at Nagasaki city; grad. Tokyo Higher Commercial School '85 and entered present firm to be promoted to present position, meanwhile in charge of branches at London, Singapore, Hongkong, etc. Add. Imazatocho, Shirokane, Tokyo. — p. 58 ([scan leaf 107](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n107/mode/1up))

**Fujita, Heitaro** 2nd Baron, one of the millionaires in Osaka, b. in Osaka, '69; son of late Denzaburo Fujita who started in Osaka the Fujita Firm to undertake contract business with the Government, beside undertaking mining and engineering works. Educated at Cambridge Univ. Add. Au ijima, Kika-ku, Osaka. — p. 58 ([scan leaf 107](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n107/mode/1up))

**Fujita, Koriki** Dir. of South Manchuria R'ly 1914-15, ex-Administration Dep't Imp. R'ly Board '13-14; was b. '66 in Tottori; grad. Law at the Imp. Univ. of Tokyo in '99; President of a local school the same year; transferred to the Dep't of Communications in '03; is now Dir. of Shantung Rly, China. Has been twice despatched abroad on official business. Add. Tsingtao. — p. 58 ([scan leaf 107](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n107/mode/1up))

**Fujita, Shiro** Member of the House of Peers, b. at Tokyo, '61; attaché to Japanese Legation in Vienna, '86, then in Berlin; afterwards Private Secretary to the Minister of Communications and of Agriculture and Commerce, &c.; Vice-Minister of Agriculture and Commerce, '18; now President of the Nippon Fire Insurance Co. and of other concerns. Add. Mita, Shiba, Tokyo. — p. 58 ([scan leaf 107](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n107/mode/1up))

**Fujita, Takasaburo** ex-President and Chief Judge of the Nagoya Appeal Court; b. '56 at Uwajima, Iyo; grad. Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ. '78; Judge in '81; for sometime a jun. Sec. and Vice-Dir. Commercial Bureau, Foreign Office; returned to the Bench and occupied the post in '93, relieved of the post in Apr. '13. Add. — p. 58 ([scan leaf 107](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n107/mode/1up))

**Fujiwara, Ginjiro** Mng. Dir. of the Oji Paper Mill; was b. in '68 in Nagano-ken; graduated from Keiogijuku '91 and then took to journalism in the provinces. He next entered the Mitsui Bank and was transferred to the Mitsui Bussan and had charge of the Branches at Shanghai and Taihoku '15. Add. Shin-ami, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 58 ([scan leaf 107](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n107/mode/1up))


## Who's Who in Japan

**Fujiyama, Raita** President of the Nippon Sugar Refinery Co. etc.; b. in '65 in Karatsu; grad. Keiogijuku in '90; then entered the present Co. after the notorious scandal culminating in the suicide of the former President. Add. Shirokane, Shiba, Tokyo. — p. 59 ([scan leaf 108](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n108/mode/1up))

**Fukai, Eigo** Bureau Dir. the Nippon Ginko, b. 71 in Gumma-ken; on the staff of the Kokumin Shimbun 1898-05; Private Secretary to Gov. of the Bank '06-12. Add. Aoyama Minamicho Rokuchome, Tokyo. — p. 59 ([scan leaf 108](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n108/mode/1up))

**Fukuda, Tokuzo** D.C.L. (Japan), Prof. in the Keio Gijuku Univ., b. '74 in Tokyo, being the 1st son of Tokubei Fukuda; graduated from the Tokyo Higher Commercial School, class '94; was dispatched by the Educational Dep't to Germany, France, and Italy to make further study on Economics; Prof. at his Alma Mater on his return home, but resigned '06; wrote works on Economics. Add. Sendagaya, near Tokyo. — p. 59 ([scan leaf 108](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n108/mode/1up))

**Fukuda, Umanosuke** D. Eng., Inspector-Gen. of Naval Construction, Sec. Chief of the Naval Technical Office; b. '55 in Aichi-ken; Lieut.-Constructor in '88 and promoted to the present rank some time ago; served meanwhile as Mem. of the Naval Board for Materials, and as Chief of the Construction Dept. of Maizuru Admiralty. Add. Nakano, nr. Tokyo. — p. 59 ([scan leaf 108](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n108/mode/1up))

**Fukuhara, Arinobu** Pres. of the Teikoku Life Insurance Co., b. '48 in Awa, Chiba-ken; promoted with others in '72 the Shiseido drug store, one of the leading establishments in Tokyo in this line, and has since been Chairman of Board of Directors. Travelled through Europe and U. S. A. several years ago to inspect the insurance affairs there. Add. Izumocho, Ginza, Tokyo. — p. 59 ([scan leaf 108](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n108/mode/1up))

**Fukuhara, Ryojiro** mem. House of Peers (nom. '16), b. '69 in Ise; grad. Law, Imp. Univ., Tokyo '92. Held various junior posts at central Government and then transferred to local Offices as Councillor or Chief Police Commissioner; removed to the Education Dep't '98 as Councillor and Inspector; dispatched to Europe '99 to investigate affairs of educational administration, the Bureau Director in '04, and the Vice-Minister '11-13. Add. Kitacho, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 59 ([scan leaf 108](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n108/mode/1up))

**Fukui, Kikusaburo** Mng. Dir. of the Mitsui Bussan; was b. '66 in Tokyo; grad. from Tokyo Higher Commercial School in '83 and then entered the service of the Mitsui, to be attached to the Branches at Shanghai, Singapore, Hongkong, Osaka, etc., and then the agent at New York for several years. Add. Mitsui & Co., Tokyo. — p. 59 ([scan leaf 108](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n108/mode/1up))

**Fukuoka, Takayoshi** Viscount (cr. '84), Privy Councillor, b. in Tosa, '35; after the Restoration of '67 entered civil service and became Minister of Education, '81-82. Appointed in '88 to the present post. Add. Kanatomicho, Koishikawa, Tokyo. — p. 59 ([scan leaf 108](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n108/mode/1up))

**Fukushima, Namizo** head of Fukushima & Co., a leading firm in the line of Bonds, Shares, etc., Trust & Financial Agents; was born in 1860. He started as a Stock Exchange Broker in 1887, and after a stormy life for twenty years, has risen to affluence. He has retired from the membership of the Tokyo Stock Exchange, to devote himself to negotiable securities business both at home and foreign markets. This kind of business had been formerly in the hand of foreign firm in Japan, but he is now a foremost Japanese in this special line, pushing it with unusual vigor and activity. Add. Aomonocho, Nihombashi, Tokyo. — p. 59 ([scan leaf 108](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n108/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Fukushima, Yasumasa** Baron (cr. '07), General (ret. '14), holder of 2nd Class Golden Kite and Grand Cordon of Rising Sun (war honor), ex Governor-General of Kwantung; b. '53; in Matsumoto, Nagano-ken; Lieutenant '78; Captain '83; Military Attaché to Japanese Legation at Berlin '87-92; relieved and thence he returned home in June '93 via Siberia on horseback. Major-General in '00, and acquired international fame as Commander of the Japanese contingent on the occasion of the Boxer trouble, and was knighted by the late King Edward. Was on the staff of Marshal Oyama in the 1904-5 War, in which he lost his son. Appointed the Vice-Chief of the General Staff Office in '05, then to the last post in '12-14. Add. Zoshigaya, Tokyo. — p. 60 ([scan leaf 109](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n109/mode/1up))

**Fukuzawa, Ichitaro** Prof. at the Keio-gijuku; b. '63, eldest son of the late Y. Fukuzawa; educated at Yale Univ. after he finished the Keio. Add. Mita, Tokyo. — p. 60 ([scan leaf 109](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n109/mode/1up))

**Fukuzawa, Momosuke** ex-M.P., brother-in-law of the Fukuzawa brothers being the husband of their sister; b. in '84 at Kawagoye; studied at Keio-gijuku; and proceeded to U.S.A. after grad. from that institution and studied at a Commercial Col. there. Returning home '9, he first entered Tokyo Ice Works, Oji Paper Mill and others, and finally the Hokkaido Colliery and Steamship Co., of which he was Agent for Tokyo, besides sitting on the boards of directors of several other concerns. Add. Shibuya, Tokyo. — p. 60 ([scan leaf 109](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n109/mode/1up))

**Fukuzawa, Sutejiro** born in Tokyo '63, second son of the late celebrated Fukuzawa; after grad. Keio Univ. studied eng. at Yale, U.S.A. '83; returned home in '90, and has since been managing the Jiji and the Osaka Jiji, the latter started '05. Add. Mita, Shiba, Tokyo. — p. 60 ([scan leaf 109](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n109/mode/1up))

**Funakoshi, Mitsunojo** 2nd Baron, Crown Mem. of House of Peers; b. '67 in Hiroshima; studied in Germany '86-93, passed the diplomatic and consular service examination in '94, éleve Consul at Vladivostock the same year, at Fusan and then at San Francisco in '95, Legation Sec. at Washington in '97; transferred to London in '99, Secretary at head office '02, Sec. at Vienna in '03, Sec. at Berlin '06-09, Chief of Cor. Sec., For. Office and then councillor of Embassy at Berlin '12-14. Add. Motozonocho, Tokyo. — p. 60 ([scan leaf 109](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n109/mode/1up))

**Furuichi, Koi** Dr. Eng. (Japan), Member of the House of Peers, Emeritus Prof. Imp. Tokyo Univ.; was b. at Himeji, '54; pursued his engineering study in France '75-80; filled the posts of the Directorship of the Public Works Bureau, Home Office; Vice-Minister of Communications, and Director-General of the Government Railways, which he resigned in '03 in order to take up the post of Directorship of Seoul-Fusan R'ly till '07. He is one of the best amateur "no" singers and actors. Add. Yumi-cho, Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 60 ([scan leaf 109](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n109/mode/1up))

**Furukawa, Sakajiro** D. Eng., Vice-Pres. of the Imp. Gov. R'lys since '14; b. '58 in Yamaguchi; graduated from the former Engineering College in '80; next entered the Gov. R'ly Bureau as its Engineer; was connected with the field r'ly during the '04-05 war, re-instated to the old post in '08; Chief of Central District in '09, Eng.-in-Chief in '13. Add. Hirakawa-cho, Akasaka, Tokyo. — p. 60 ([scan leaf 109](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n109/mode/1up))


## Who's Who

**Furukawa, Toranosuke** Baron (cr. '15). President of the Furukawa Mining Co., son of the late celebrated Ichibei Furukawa and inherited his father's immense properties including more than twenty mines. He was b. in '82 and graduated Mining at Columbia, U.S.A., class '07; was granted 3rd class Treasure, 1911, for his munificent gifts to various schools. Add. Setomonocho, Nihombashi, Tokyo. — p. 81 ([scan leaf 110](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n110/mode/1up))

**Furuya, Hisatsuna** M.P., ex-Master of Cerem. & Steward to Prince Yi kong of Chosen; b. '74 in Ehime-ken; studied at Brussels after he finished Doshisha College; Priv. Sec. to late Prince Ito '01-09 and accompanied him to Harbin on the fatal tour; elected in 1915. Add. Hinokicho, Akasaka, Tokyo. — p. 81 ([scan leaf 110](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n110/mode/1up))

**Ga, Reishi** Member of the House of Peers since 1891 and Lord-in-Waiting of the Kinkei Hall; b. at Nagasaki in '40; a pioneer scholar of Dutch; held several important posts in the Government service. Add. Nakashibuya, near Tokyo. — p. 81 ([scan leaf 110](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n110/mode/1up))

**Gejo, Masao** Paymaster-Inspector of the Navy (ret.), Mem. of House of Peers; b. in Yonezawa '42. Began his career in the Navy as jun.-clerk '73 then successively held posts of Sectional Chief of Superintendence for Accounts of Yokosuka Admiralty, Chief Paymaster of Sasebo Adm. and Pres. of the Naval Paymaster's College. He is a noted amateur painter and connoisseur. Add. Motozenocho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 81 ([scan leaf 110](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n110/mode/1up))

**Go, Daigoro** See Kure, Daigoro. — p. 81 ([scan leaf 110](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n110/mode/1up))

**Go, Seinosuke** 2nd Baron, Mem. House of Peers, President of Iriyama Colliery Co., Tokyo Stock Exchange and others; . '65 in Tokyo, son of late Junzo Go; learned in Europe; after several years of Gov't services he entered business circle. Add. Kaminibancho, Tokyo. — p. 81 ([scan leaf 110](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n110/mode/1up))

**Goto, Shimpei** Baron (cr. '06), mem. of House of Peers, Minister of Home Affairs and Pres. of Imperial Railways; b. '56 in Iwate-ken; studied medicine in Japan and Germany; was chief of the Nagoya Hospital. Subsequently appointed junior Dir. of the Sanitary Bureau, Home Office; his chivalry implicated in the notorious Soma scandal but was acquitted. He got appointment as Sanitary Commissioner at the time of the Japan-China War, and then re-entered the Sanitary Bureau as its Director. Was singled out '97 by the late Vis. Kodama, newly appointed Governor-General of Formosa, as Dir. of the Civil Adm. Bureau. Pres. of S. Manchuria Railway '06, and then the Minister of Communications in '08, combined the office of Deputy-Pres. of the Colonial Bureau, Cabinet, cr. '10. Retired from the posts in Aug. '11 and again held it with an additional of Pres. of I. G. R. in the 3rd Katsura Cabinet; formed the Doshikai with late Prince Katsura, but left it in '13. Occupied the same posts in Terauchi Cabinet formed Oct. '16. Add. Miyamuracho, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 81 ([scan leaf 110](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n110/mode/1up))

**Gwassan, Yagoro** master sword-smith, Art Commissioner to the Imp. Household (app. '06); b. '46 in Omi and learned craft under Teikichi Gwassan in Osaka by whom he was ultimately adopted as son and heir; is the greatest master in producing swords of Soshu and Ichimonji style, besides creating a new mode of tempering. He has made swords for the late Emperor Meiji and the reigning Emperor. Add. Yariyacho, Osaka. — p. 81 ([scan leaf 110](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n110/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Hachisuka, Shigeakira** Marq. (cr. 1884), formerly daimyo of Tokushima. Privy Councillor; b. '46; represented Japan in France about '81; was appointed President of the House of Peers '91-96; Minister of Education, Sept. '96-Nov. '97; was appointed to the present post following year. Add. Mita-tsunamachi, Shiba, Tokyo. — p. 62 ([scan leaf 111](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n111/mode/1up))

**Haga, Yaichi** Dr. Lit. (Japan), Prof. in the Imp. Tokyo Univ.; b. '61 in Fukui-ken; grad. Lit. Col. in the Imp. Tokyo Univ. in '92 to study at the University Hall afterward; appointed Professor at the 1st Higher School, and that at the Higher Normal School; Assis.-Prof. in his alma mater in '98 and full Prof. in the following year; then transferred to the Higher Normal School; sent to Germany to prosecute further study; was given a chair in the University when he returned home in '02. Add. Otowa, Koishikawa, Tokyo. — p. 62 ([scan leaf 111](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n111/mode/1up))

**Hama, Koichi** Departmental Chief of the Board of Audit; was b. in '48; entered official service first as a junior official in the Board of the Financial Dep't to be afterwards promoted to the present post. Add. Otsuka, Koishikawa, Tokyo. — p. 62 ([scan leaf 111](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n111/mode/1up))

**Hamaguchi, Takayuki** M.P., Crown Mem. of House of Peers, b. '70 in Tosa; grad. Law, Imperial Tokyo Univ. '95; Director of the Tobacco Monopoly Bureau '07-12; ex-Vice-Min. of Communications '12-13; Vice-Min. of Finance in 2nd Okuma Cabinet, '14; resigned '15 and now a whip of the Doshikai. Add. Zoshigaya, Tokyo. — p. 62 ([scan leaf 111](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n111/mode/1up))

**Hamao, Arata** Baron (cr. '07), L.L.D. (Harvard), Lord Steward to the Crown Prince, Privy Councillor; ex-Pres. Imp. Univ. Tokyo; b. in Tajima, '49; Councillor of the Department of Education, '78; Vice-President of Tokyo University in '84; Director of the Special Education Bureau, '88; President of the Tokyo Imperial University, '93-97; Minister of Education from Nov. to Dec. of 1897. Reappointed Pres. of the Univ. '05-13 in consequence of the celebrated "Strike movement" of the Professors. Add. Kanatomicho, Koishikawa, Tokyo. — p. 62 ([scan leaf 111](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n111/mode/1up))

**Hamaoka, Kotetsu** Chairman of Kyoto Chamber of Commerce, ex-M.P.; b. '53 in Kyoto; elected mem. Kyoto Prefectural Assembly '80, then its Vice-Pres. After having filled several important posts in business circle, he was raised to present post in '11. Add. Shimochoja-machi, Kamikyo, Kyoto. — p. 62 ([scan leaf 111](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n111/mode/1up))

**Hanabusa, Naosaburo** D.C.L. (Japan), ex-Dir. Statistic Bureau of the Cabinet; b. '57 in Okayama, brother of the below; appointed official of the Cabinet, then Secretary of the Privy Council, Personal Secretary to Marquis Ito, Secretary of the Cabinet, and finally appointed to the present post in '98. He travelled abroad to investigate statistic affairs, '02-03, being one of the authorities in this line. Add. Aoyama Minami-cho, Tokyo. — p. 62 ([scan leaf 111](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n111/mode/1up))

**Hanabusa, Yoshikata** Viscount (Baron in '00 and Viscount in '07), President of the Japan Red Cross Society; b. in Okayama, '42; Secretary of Foreign Affairs '70; chargé d'affaires at Seoul, '76-77; promoted to Minister Resident '80-81, and narrowly escaped from being killed by the anti-Japanese Korean mob that rose there. Appointed Minister to Petrograd, '83-86; Vice-Minister of Agriculture and Commerce, '87-88, and then Min. of Imperial Household, resigned in '09. Appointed to the present post in Jan. '13. Add. Shirokane Daimachi, Tokyo. — p. 62 ([scan leaf 111](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n111/mode/1up))


## Who's Who

**Hanai, Takuzo** D.C.L. (Japan), Barrister, M.P.; b. '68 in Hiroshima-ken; grad. with honour from an English Law School in Tokyo when he was only eighteen years old; finished the University Course at the Hogaku-in with honour in '87; passed Barristers' Examination a year or two after and soon attracted public notice for his scholarship and eloquence, especially in criminal affairs. Has written several popular essays on Criminal Law; elected Vice-Pres. of House of Representatives in 36th session but resigned it in 37th session. Add. Nishiki-cho, Kanda, Tokyo. — p. 63 ([scan leaf 112](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n112/mode/1up))

**Hanihara, Masanao** Consul-Gen. at San Francisco; b. '76 in Yamanashi-ken; grad. from Waseda '97; attache at Seoul Legation '99; at Washington, '01; Secy. at the same, '02; then Sec. Chief at Foreign Office; promoted to present post '16. Add. Japanese Consulate-General, San Francisco, U.S.A. — p. 63 ([scan leaf 112](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n112/mode/1up))

**Hara, Rokuro** a prominent business-man; b. '44 in Tajima and played some distinguished part at the time of the Restoration, having fought on the Imperial side in the civil war; studied in England and U.S. '71-77. Entered and founded with a few others the One Hundredth Bank and has been instrumental in pushing it to the present flourishing condition; filled the post of President of the Yokohama Specie Bank '83 to '90; is acknowledged as a power in business circles, and is a director for a number of companies. Add. Goten-yama, near Tokyo. — p. 63 ([scan leaf 112](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n112/mode/1up))

**Hara, Takashi** M.P.; '54 at Morioka; entered the now defunct Law College, Justice Dep't, but left before grad.; then took to journalism and was on the staff of the Hochi; followed late Marquis Inouye as newspaper correspondent when he was despatched to Korea '82 as Special Envoy, and afterward an official in the Foreign Office; Consul in Tientsin; Secretary and chargé d'affaires in Paris, 86; the Marquis' Personal Secretary when he was Minister of Agriculture and Commerce; next acted in same capacity when late Count Mutsu filled the chair '90-92; followed his chief to the Foreign Office to be made Director of the Commercial Bureau, and promoted to the Vice-Minister in '95 and transferred to Minister to Korea, '96-7, then chief editor of the Osaka Mainichi. He was one of the right-hand men of late Prince Ito when he raised the banner of the Seiyu-kai, '00, and filled as the late Hoshi's successor the chair of the Minister of Communications Dec.. '00 to May, '01. Then chief editor of the Osaka Shimpo, and appointed in Jan. '08 to the Ministerial post, which he resigned with the fall of the Seiyu-kai Cabinet '08. Visited Europe and America '08-09, re-entered the Cabinet as Min. of Home Affairs '13-14. Is a Leader of Seiyukai. Add. 4, Shiba Park, Tokyo. — p. 63 ([scan leaf 112](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n112/mode/1up))

**Hara, Tomitaro** Pres. of 2nd Bank, Head of the Hara Firm, Yokohama; b. in Shinano '70; studied at Waseda Univ.; was adopted into the present millionaire family as husband of its daughter, is a Director of the Yokohama Fire Insurance Co. and a leading businessman in Yokohama. His fine collection of art objects is widely reputed. Add. Bentendori, Yokohama. — p. 63 ([scan leaf 112](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n112/mode/1up))

**Hara, Yoshimichi** lawyer, D.C.L. (Japan); b. '67 in Nagano-ken; grad. the Tokyo University in '89; Councillor, Dep't Agri. & Com. in '09; Chief of the Tokyo Mine Inspection Office and that of Osaka '01-03; has since been practising law. Add. Iida-machi, Koji, Tokyo. — p. 63 ([scan leaf 112](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n112/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Harada, Jiro** Mng.-Dir. of Konoike Bank, Osaka, Vice-Pres. of Osaka Warehouse Co.; b. '50 at Matsuzaka; early entered Finance Dept.; left it to become Pres. of 74th Bank; but fell sick for nearly ten years. In '93 again he was given a post in the Konoike Bank and has done much service in it. Add. 89/2 Ichibeicho, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 64 ([scan leaf 113](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n113/mode/1up))

**Harada, Tasuku** Pres. of Doshisha, in Kyoto, since '07; b. '63, in Kumamoto; studied at the Foreign Language School in Kumamoto, and later at the Doshisha; entered Yale and grad. from Divinity School, class '91; the following year he travelled through Europe; was Pastor in Tokyo, Kyoto and Kobe; Editor of Rikugo Zasshi, and "The Christian World." Pres. of the Christian Endeavor Union of Japan '93-96 and was present in the world convention in London, '00. Visited India, '06, by the invitation of the National Union of Y.M.C.A.; attended World Missionary Conference at Edinburgh and lectured at Harvard, Yale and other Universities in America '09. Received LL.D. from University of Edinburgh and D.D. from Amherst College. Add. Doshisha, Kyoto. — p. 64 ([scan leaf 113](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n113/mode/1up))

**Haraguchi, Kaname** Dr. Eng. (Japan), Mng.-Dir. of Fuji Paper Mill; b. '51 in Nagasaki-ken; was one of the pioneer students sent abroad for study by the Government, having studied Engineering at Yale. '75-79; served long in the Gov. R'ly service; Dir. of the Hakata Bay R'ly Co., '02; held the post of the Adviser to the Viceroy of Hukwang Provinces, China, '06-10. Add. Shiba Park, Tokyo. — p. 64 ([scan leaf 113](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n113/mode/1up))

**Haraguchi, Kensai** Baron (cr. '07), Lieut.-Gen. (retired), Mem. House of Peers; b. '47 in Bingo; Sub-Lieut., '72; Maj.-Gen., '97, meanwhile having filled the posts of Com. of 1st Reg. (Inf.), Body Guards; Chief Staff to the 4th Division and Director of the Toyama Mil. School. Commanded the Saghalien Exp. Army in the '04-5 War. He was dispatched to Germany, in '90 to investigate military affairs. Add. Sanaizaka, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 64 ([scan leaf 113](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n113/mode/1up))

**Haruki, Ichiro** D.C.L. (Japan), Prof. at the Coll. of Law, Imp. Univ. of Tokyo; b. in '70 at Tokyo; graduated from the Imp. Univ. of Tokyo in '94, and further studied Roman Law in Germany, 1900-04, and appointed Prof. Kyoto Imp. Univ. on his return in '05, transferred to Tokyo later. Add. Nishisugacho, Nezu, Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 64 ([scan leaf 113](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n113/mode/1up))
