**Haruta, Gennojo** Councillor of the N.Y.K.; son of a bannerman of the Tokugawa Shogun, and adopted by Mototaro Haruta; began his business career in Mitsubishi Co., as a clerk, to be promoted to the present post, having once had charge of the Tokyo, Kobe and Osaka Branches of the N.Y.K. Add. Oban-cho, Yotsuya, Tokyo. — p. 64 ([scan leaf 113](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n113/mode/1up))

**Hasegawa, Kinsuke** Chief of Central District of the Imp. Railway Board; b. '55, 2nd son of Tomenobu Hasegawa; was for years in the Formosan Gov't R'ly as its Chief Engineer, then Director, and appointed to Chief of Kobe District, Imp. Gov. R'ly in '09 then of the Central '15. Add. Imperial Government Railway Board. — p. 64 ([scan leaf 113](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n113/mode/1up))

**Hasegawa** Seiya, nom de plume "Tenkei." author, and managing edit. of the Hakubun-kan, largest publishing house in Japan; was b. in 1877 in Echigo; graduated from the Waseda Univ. in '93; travelled in Europe and America in '09-10. Has written, a number of literary works. Add. Hakubun-kan, Tokyo. — p. 64 ([scan leaf 113](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n113/mode/1up))


## Who's Who in Japan

**Hasegawa, Tameji** Lord-in-Waiting of the Kinkei Hall; b. '48 in Yamaguchi; entered Financial Office '69 and transferred to the Mint; travelled through Europe and America, '86-7. Had charge of the Mint '93-13 as its Chief. Add. — p. 65 ([scan leaf 114](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n114/mode/1up))

**Hasegawa, Yoshimichi** Marshal, Count (Baron, '95; Visc., '07; Ct., '16), Governor-General of Chosen, 1st class Golden Kite and Grand Cordon of Rising Sun and Paulownia (war honor); b. '50 in Suwo; Major, '72; Major-Gen. '86; Lieut.-Gen. '98; General in '04. He commanded a Brigade in the Japan-China War and took a distinguished part in the battle of Port Arthur and Wei-hai-wei. In the '04-5 war he commanded the Imp. Guards Division under Gen. Kuroki and led it to success and honour at the Yalu and the battle of Liaoyang, and then to Com.-in-Chief of Korean Garrison and transferred to the Mem. of Supreme War Council in '09 and Chief of Gen. Staff '12-16; promoted Marshal, Jan. '15; present post Oct. 1916. Add. Seoul, Korea. — p. 65 ([scan leaf 114](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n114/mode/1up))

**Hashimoto, Juko** Mng.-Dir. of Nippon Life Ins. Co.; b. '80 in Nagasaki-ken; grad. '95 Imp. Tokyo Univ. and entered the present Co.; sent to Germany to study next year by the Co., raised to Dir. in '08. Add. Sumiyoshi, Hyogo-ken. — p. 65 ([scan leaf 114](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n114/mode/1up))

**Hashimoto, Katsutaro** Lieut.-Gen., Com. of 9th Div.; b. in '62, in Gifu; grad. from the Military Coll. '86; Capt. '94; Col. '06; Maj.-Gen. '11. Lieut.-Gen. 15; Pres. of the Mil. Coll. before appointment to Com. of Gendarmie in '15. Add. Kanazawa. — p. 65 ([scan leaf 114](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n114/mode/1up))

**Hashimoto, Keizaburo** Mem. House of Peers; b. '65 in Niigata; grad. College of Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ. in '90; Secretary of the Privy Council '92; Chief of Accountant's Bureau and then Vice-Min. of Finance '11-12; Vice-Min. of Agr. and Com., '13-14. Add. Aoyama, Tokyo. — p. 65 ([scan leaf 114](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n114/mode/1up))

**Hashimoto, Masa-aki** Vice-Adm. (ret.), ex-Com. of the Naval Adm. at Port Arthur; b. '58, Kagoshima; educated at the Naval Academy '71; Lieut. '83; Capt. '94 and Chief Staff Officer of the Sasebo Admiralty, promoted '02 to Rear-Adm. with post of Chief of Personnel Bur.; Vice-Adm. '06; placed on retired list '09. Add. Nakano, near, Tokyo. — p. 65 ([scan leaf 114](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n114/mode/1up))

**Hata, Ryotaro** Minister to Brazil. b. '87 in Nagano; entered diplomatic service after graduating from Imp. Tokyo Univ. in '90; Councillor of Embassy at Berlin '06, Secretary of Foreign Office '08 and finally to the present post in '13. Add. Japanese Legation, Brazil. — p. 65 ([scan leaf 114](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n114/mode/1up))

**Hata, Sahachiro** M.D., co-worker of Dr. Erlich, Frankfort-on-Meine, in the discovery of No. 606 or Salvarsam; b. '72 in Iwami; graduated '95 with honour Medical Dept. of Okayama High School; became Dr. Kitazato's assistant at the Epidemic Investigation Laboratory '98-07; studied at Berlin and Frankfort '07-11. Add. Nakameguro, near Tokyo. — p. 65 ([scan leaf 114](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n114/mode/1up))

**Hatano, Shogoro** Officer of Mitsui Firm, Aud. of Mitsui Bank, Mining Co., etc.; b. '59 in Tokyo; grad. from the Keio Gijuku (the Keio Univ.) 1876; was on the staff of the Jiji when it was started; entered the Foreign Office and was appointed Consul at Tientsin, next Sec. at the head office, and then he entered the Mitsui. Add. Kami-Nibancho, Koj., Tokyo. — p. 65 ([scan leaf 114](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n114/mode/1up))

**Hatano, Yoshinao** Baron (cr. '07), Min. of Imp. Household; b. Hizen '50; entered the Department of Justice, '71; Councillor '87; Secretary '88; afterwards Pres. of the Kyoto Local Court and Judge of the Supreme Court, &c.; Vice-Minister of Justice in '00 ; Minister of Justice in '03 and resigned it in Jan., '06 ; nom. Mem. House of Peers ; Lord Steward to the Crown Prince '11-14 ; present post '14. Add. Ukyomachi, Yotsuya, Tokyo. — p. 65 ([scan leaf 114](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n114/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Hattori, Bunshiro** Ph. D. (Princeton), Prof. at Waseda University ; b '73 in Shiga-ken ; grad. from Waseda in '02, and further studied economics at Princeton and Berlin '02-08. Works: Local Finance (in English), Commercial Education in University, Principle of Banking, etc. Add. Waseda University. — p. 66 ([scan leaf 115](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n115/mode/1up))

**Hattori, Ichizo** Mem. House of Peers and Lord-in-Waiting of the Kinkei Hall, b. '52 in Choshu, and early took to learning English, first at Nagasaki, and then in America from which he returned as B.S. (Rutgers); appointed official of Edu. Dept. with which he was connected for 15 or 16 years as President of the University Prep. Course, Tokyo ; manager of the University ; Dean of the Law Course ; Councillor and Secretary at the Head Office. Was a Japanese Commissioner in the New Orleans Exhibition, '83. Transferred to Iwate-ken as Gov., '91, of Hiroshima, '98, then of Nagasaki-ken, and finally of Hyogo-ken in 1900, resigned it in '16. Add. Tokyo. — p. 66 ([scan leaf 115](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n115/mode/1up))

**Hattori, Kintaro** Prop. of Seikosha Clock Factory, Auditor of Korean Industrial Co., Mem. of the Tokyo Chamber of Commerce ; b. '60 in Tokyo ; Pres. of the Tokyo Watch-sellers' Guild, his being one of the most extensive stores in the line. Add. Ginza, Tokyo. — p. 66 ([scan leaf 115](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n115/mode/1up))

**Hattori, Unokichi** D. Lit., Prof. Imp. Tokyo Univ.; b. '67 in Fukushima-ken ; studied philos. in Litt. Coll., Imp. Tokyo Univ. '87-90 ; Chinese classics in China and method of teaching in Germany, '99-07 ; Prof. at alma mater on return ; Prof. at Chinese Univ., Peking, till '09 ; Prof. at Harvard, U.S.A. '15. Add. 1825 Shimo Shibuya, Tokyo. — p. 66 ([scan leaf 115](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n115/mode/1up))

**Hayakawa, Senkichiro** Chairman Tokyo Bankers' Asso., Mng.-Dir. Mitsui Bank, Mitsui Bussan etc.; b. at Kanazawa, '63 ; grad. Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ., '87 ; further studied at Univ. Hall ; served at Finance Dep't '90-00, holding post of Councillor, Secretary and Per. Sec. to the Minister ; Sec. of the Currency Reform Com. '93 ; despatched to England in '95 to take charge of the Chinese indemnity ; sent abroad in '98 to raise a loan, and for these special services he was duly rewarded ; resigned the Gov. service '00 and entered the Mitsui. Add. Nibancho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 66 ([scan leaf 115](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n115/mode/1up))

**Hayakawa, Tetsuya** ex M.P., Patent attorney ; b. Okayama, '63; grad. Sapporo Agri. Col., '84 ; studied law in U.S.A. and Germany ; entered the diplomatic service, '85 and attached to the Imperial Legation at Seoul until '88 ; to Consulate at San Francisco, to the Legation at Berlin, '89-92 ; Private Secy. to the Minister of Agriculture and Commerce, '92-97 ; appointed Minister Resident, '97-98 ; Secy. of the Cabinet, '98 ; then Dir. of the Political Bureau, Foreign Office in the Okuma Cabinet whose reform program he chiefly undertook. Succeeded in procuring foreign loan for the Hokkaido Tanko in '06 ; is a prominent patent attorney in international patent affairs. Add. Shimo-Shibuya, Tokyo. — p. 66 ([scan leaf 115](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n115/mode/1up))

**Hayami, Soji** M.P. for Hiroshima city and ex-Vice-Pres. of House of Representatives, Chief Editor of the Geibi, Hiroshima, Chairman of Hiroshima Chamber of Commerce since its est. in '01 ; b. '68 in Hiroshima-ken ; grad. from Waseda Univ. and became teacher ; after two years entered Hakubunkwan Publishing House, then started present daily; and is also an influencial business-man in the city; has much interests in marine transportation in the Island Sea; elected M.P. for sixth time, and appointed Parliamentary Secy. of the Navy '15, then Vice-Pres. Dec., '15. Add. Otemachi, Hiroshima. — p. 66 ([scan leaf 115](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n115/mode/1up))


## Who's Who

**Hayashi, Aisaku** Mng. of the Imperial Hotel; b. '73 in Gumma, studied in U.S.A. for some years and entered business after his return home; Mng. of the hotel since '07. Add. Shinosaka, Akasaka, Tokyo. — p. 67 ([scan leaf 116](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n116/mode/1up))

**Hayashi, Gonsuke** Barou (cr. '07), Minister to China since '16, b. at Aizu, '60; grad. Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ. '86; appointed the same year attache at Foreign Office. Eleve Consul at Cheefoo, '87, at Ninsen '88, promoted full Consul '90, Consul at Shanghai '92, removed to similar post at London, '93, to be appointed six years later Secretary of the Legation. Recalled home he was appointed Director of the Commercial Bureau, '98, and to Minister at Seoul the following year. Minister at Peking, '06-'08. Ambassador to Rome, June, '08. Add. Japanese Legation, Peking. — p. 67 ([scan leaf 116](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n116/mode/1up))

**Hayashi, Ken** Mng.-Dir. of the Toshin Warehouse Co.; b. '63 in Oita-ken; grad. law at Imp. Tokyo Univ.; entered Mitsui Bank and was the agent at Shimonoseki, Nagasaki, etc. Add. Fujimicho, Koji., Tokyo. — p. 67 ([scan leaf 116](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n116/mode/1up))

**Hayashi, Kiroku** M.P. (Chuo Club) and Prof. at Keiogijuku; b. 1872 at Karatsu; grad. Literature Course, Keiogijuku, class '95, and further Diplomacy at Paris 1901-04. Has written "Russian Empire," "Modern European Diplomacy," etc. Add. Shimo-Shibuya, Tokyo. — p. 67 ([scan leaf 116](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n116/mode/1up))

**Hayashi, Tamio** Mng.-Dir. of the N.Y.K.; b. '65 in Tokyo; entered the Company soon after finishing his education in U.S.A.; once had charge of the Hongkong and Yokohama Branches, to be transferred to Chief of the Passenger's Section about '03. Add. Ichibeicho, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 67 ([scan leaf 116](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n116/mode/1up))

**Hayashi, Yozo** Chief of Fukushima Branch of the Bank of Japan; b. in '68 in Yamaguchi-ken; grad. Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ. '93; Per. Sec. to Minister of Finance '95, entered the Bank '96. Add. Fukushima city. — p. 67 ([scan leaf 116](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n116/mode/1up))

**Hayashi, Yuzo** ex-Cabinet Minister, was born in Tosa, '42; Councillor for Kochi-ken, '69; Secretary of Foreign Office, '73-74. then entered the political career under Count Itagaki and fought for liberty and popular right; was thrown in prison for ten years on charge of complicity in the Satsuma rebellion. Sat twice in the Cabinet, chair of Communications in the Okuma-Itagaki Cabinet, '98, and of Agriculture and Commerce in the Seiyukwai (4th Ito) Ministry. In '03 he seceded from the Seiyukai and organized the "Liberal Party" as ministerial supporters, but it soon collapsed. He has lost much of his influence in consequence and is now almost forgotten. Add. Otsuka, Tokyo. — p. 67 ([scan leaf 116](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n116/mode/1up))

**Hayashida, Kametaro** ex-Chief Secy. of the House of Representatives; b. '63 in Kumamoto; pursued study while fighting against adversity. Graduated Law, Imp. Univ., Tokyo, '87; was an official in the Legislative Bureau, Privy Council, and Dep't of Agriculture and Commerce. Promoted in '97 to Chief Secy. of the Lower House, which post he resigned in '15 in connexion with the political scandal '14-5. Add. Haneda, Tokyo. — p. 67 ([scan leaf 116](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n116/mode/1up))

**Hibi, Osuke** b. at Kurume, '62; grad. from the Keio-gijuku, '83; a clerk in the Navy; entered the service of the Muslin Factory, Tokyo; entered the Mitsui Ginko several years ago, to be transferred to the Mitsui Dry Goods Dept. a few year after; now Man. Dir. of the Dept. widely known under the title Mitsukoshi Dry Goods Store. Made a trip through Europe and America, '06. Add. Kitashinagawa, near Tokyo. — p. 67 ([scan leaf 116](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n116/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Hibiya, Heizaemon** millionaire, Pres. of Kanegafuchi Cotton Spinning Co. and Tokyo Woolen Cloth Mfg. Co., and Dir. of several other concerns, is a leading cotton yarn merchant in Tokyo; b. '54, son of a small innkeeper in Echigo; adopted into the Hibiya family, '77, as husband of its daughter and started cotton and yarn shop; and restored its credit after it was declared bankrupt. He has undertaken similar worthy services for other tottering establishments. Add. Hibiya Firm, Horidome, Nihombashi, Tokyo. — p. 68 ([scan leaf 117](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n117/mode/1up))

**Hidaka, Sōnojō** Baron (cr. '07), full Admiral (ret.); b. '49 at Kagoshima, 4th son of a retainer of the fief and adopted by T. Hidaka of the same clan when '24; midshipman, '77, Commander of cru. Hashidate in the Japan-China War, for which decorated with 4th Order of the Golden Kite; subsequently Dir. Naval Academy and Com. of Takeshiki Naval Port; Vice-Adm. '00 and Com.-in-Chief of Combined Fleet '02, which post he exchanged with Adm. Togo, on the eve of outbreak of the '04-5 War, then Com. Maizuru Adm. Holder of 2nd Class Golden Kite and Grand Corden of Rising Sun (war honour). Add. Shimbori, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 68 ([scan leaf 117](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n117/mode/1up))

**Hidehara** —See Shidehara. — p. 68 ([scan leaf 117](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n117/mode/1up))

**Higashizono, Motoyoshi** Viscount (cr. '84), Chamberlain to His Majesty and Councillor to the Court; b. in '53 in Kyoto, being of the family of a hereditary courtier. Add. Ichiban-cho, Koj., Tokyo. — p. 68 ([scan leaf 117](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n117/mode/1up))

**Hijikata, Hisamoto** Count (cr. '95; b. in Tosa, '33; distinguished himself in the cause of Restoration; was appointed Secretary of the Imp. Household soon after that memorable event of '68; Chief Secretary in Count Ito's (late Prince) Cabinet in '85; Minister of Agriculture and Commerce, July '87- Sept. '87 and Minister of the Imperial Household from September '87 to November '98. Add. Hayashi-cho, Koishikawa, Tokyo. — p. 68 ([scan leaf 117](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n117/mode/1up))

**Hijikata, Kyucho** Dir. of the Bank of Japan; b. '70; graduated from the Law Col. of the Imp. Univ. of Tokyo, '93; then entered the Bank of Japan with which he has been connected ever since. Add. Nishi-Daikucho, Fukagawa, Tokyo. — p. 68 ([scan leaf 117](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n117/mode/1up))

**Hijikata, Yasushi** D.C.L. (Japan). Dean and Prof. of the Law College of Imp. Tokyo Univ.; was b. in '59; grad. Law at the Univ. in '82, and has since been connected with it as Prof., with a short break occasioned by his study in England from which he returned in '94; Dean in 1911. Add. Ichibancho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 68 ([scan leaf 117](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n117/mode/1up))

**Hinata, Terutake** ex-M.P., Aud. of Japan Telegraph News Agency; b. '70 in Gumma; went to U.S.A. for study after graduating from a private law college; returned home '96; married Miss Kin, daughter of a noted restaurant house in Tokyo, well known for partiality to snakes. Add. Shin-Sakanacho, Kyobashi, Tokyo. — p. 68 ([scan leaf 117](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n117/mode/1up))

**Hioki, Eki** Mem. House Peers, ex-Min. to China, b. in Ise; grad. from Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ., '88, attaché at New York in '89, Washington in '91, Petrograd in '92, Seoul in '94, Peking in '00, Washington in '03, Germany in '06, decorated with 3rd class Rising Sun and grant of money in '06 for his service in connection with the war; Minister to Chile '08-14, then to China, '14-16. Add. Tokyo. — p. 68 ([scan leaf 117](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n117/mode/1up))


## Who's Who in Japan

**Hirai, Masamichi** M.D., Surg.-Gen., Pres. of Red Cross Hospital ; b. in Wakasa in '63 ; grad. Medicine, Tokyo Univ. '89 ; attached to Otsu Regiment '90 ; took part in Japan-China War as Surgeon-Capt. of 5th Div., studied in Germany '97-00, Director of Tokyo Garrison Hos., Vice Pres. of Red Cross Hos. '06 ; was installed in the present post in '09, on the death of the late President, Vis. Hashimoto. Add. Red Cross Hos., Tokyo. — p. 69 ([scan leaf 118](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n118/mode/1up))

**Hirai, Seijiro** D.E. (Japan), Rly. Eng. ; b. '55 at Kanazawa ; despatched by Gov. to study civil eng. at Troy, U.S.A., '75-77, and stayed in America some years after to study practical side. Was engaged as Eng. of Tanko Rly. Eng. by Hok. Gov. on his return '80, then Chief Rly. '94 when he entered Gov. Rlys. in which he steadily rose till he was appointed to the Presidency. Travelled through Europe '10. Adviser to Chinese Govt. since 1913. Add. Communication Dept., Peking, China. — p. 69 ([scan leaf 118](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n118/mode/1up))

**Hiranuma, Kiichirō** D.C.L., Procurator-Gen. of the Supreme Court since '12 ; b. '65 in Tsuyama ; grad. Law, Imp. Univ., Tokyo, '88 ; then appointed to the Bench, having filled chair of sectional chief '95 in the Tokyo Appeal Court, Procurator of the same court, was finally transferred to be head of a Bureau '06-11 ; Vice-Minister of Justice, '11-12. Add. Nishi-Okubo, Tokyo. — p. 69 ([scan leaf 118](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n118/mode/1up))

**Hiraoka, Ki** pioneer of rolling-stock makers, in Japan. Vice-Pres. Osaka Loco. Works; b. '55 in Tokyo ; went to U.S.A. in '71, and entered a rolling stock shop, N. Y., returning home about '80 ; appointed overseer of the works at Shimbashi, till about '90, and started the Hiraoka Rolling-Stock Works at Honjo, Tokyo, which was fused with Osaka Loco. Works. Add. Nagatacho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 69 ([scan leaf 118](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n118/mode/1up))

**Hiraoka, Teitaro** ex-Gov. of Karafuto ; b. '63 at Fukuoka ; grad. Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ., Secretary of Home Office and also House of Representatives. Governor of Nara-ken '06-09, transferred to Karafuto '09-14. Add. Higashikatamachi, Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 69 ([scan leaf 118](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n118/mode/1up))

**Hirata, Tosuke** Viscount (Baron '02, Vis. '11), ex-Minister of Interior ; b. '49 in Yamagata-ken ; studied at Germany while young ; official of Imp. Tokyo Univ., '77 ; Sec. of Cabinet at Treasury '78 ; Sec. of Cabinet '84 ; Chief Sec. of Privy Council '88 ; Dir. of Legislation Bureau '89-91 ; Member of House of Peers '91-97 ; Minister of Agriculture and Commerce, June '01-July '03 ; was given the Cabinet chair of the Home Minister by late Premier Katsura '08-11. He is a trusted follower of Marshal Prince Yamagata, and is active propagandist of thrift and diligence and for preaching the importance of co-operation for promoting communal prosperity. Add. Surugadai, Kanda, Tokyo. — p. 69 ([scan leaf 118](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n118/mode/1up))

**Hirayama, Narinobu** Court Councillor, Member of the House of Peers b. '50, in Kagoshima ; was once Bureau chief in the Dep't of Finance and of Home Affairs. Nominated member of House of Peers in '93 ; appointed the Steward to Prince Arisugawa, in '07. Add. Hara-machi, Koi., Tokyo. — p. 69 ([scan leaf 118](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n118/mode/1up))

**Hirayama, Shin** Dr. S. (Japan), Prof. at the College of Science of the Imp. Tokyo University ; b. in '67 in Tokyo. Grad. Science Col. of the Univ., in '90 ; further studied at the University Hall and in England and Germany ; was appointed Prof. at his alma mater on his returning home in '94. Add. Nagasaka-cho, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 69 ([scan leaf 118](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n118/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Hiroi, Isamu** Dr. Eng. (Japan), Prof. Engineering, Imp. Univ., Tokyo; b. '62 in Kochi; grad. Sapporo Agr. College, class '81; proceeded to America, Dec. '83, and was employee of the Mississippi River Work Office and some other eng. offices, '84-87; ordered by the Hokkaido Government to study civil eng. at Karlsruhe and Stuttgart, '87-89. Prof. at the alma mater '89 and Eng. to the Hokkaido Government, and had charge of the harbour works of Hakodate and Otaru; present post in 1900. Add. Nakano-cho, Ichigaya, Tokyo. — p. 70 ([scan leaf 119](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n119/mode/1up))

**Hiromi, Jisaburo** marine product dealer; b. in Kaga, '54; promoted the Japan Marine Insurance Co. and appointed its President, and one of the largest ship-owners in Japan; once "highest tax" member of the House of Peers for Osaka. Add. Enokojima, Higashicho, Nishiku, Osaka — p. 70 ([scan leaf 119](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n119/mode/1up))

**Hirosawa, Kinjiro** Count, b. '71 at Yamaguchi; proceeded to England for study '86; a Member of House of Peers on returning home; Private Secretary to the Premier of the time; is a son of the celebrated Sangi Heizo Hirosawa who took active part in the work of Restoration, and was assassinated and for which his son was rewarded with the patent of Count in '84. Add. Zaimoku-cho, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 70 ([scan leaf 119](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n119/mode/1up))

**Hirotsu, Ryuro** one of the leading novelists (Thomas Hardy of Japan), b. in Kurume '60; first studied Medicine; then clerk of the Osaka Chamber of Com. and next a clerk in Dept. of Agr. and Com. About '87 his first story sent to a Tokyo daily won wide fame, and he resigned Gov. service to devote himself to literature. Has since produced about twenty novels and novelettes, his strength lying in depicting tragic side of woman's character. Add. Kasumi-cho, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 70 ([scan leaf 119](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n119/mode/1up))

**Hodzu—** See Hozu—. — p. 70 ([scan leaf 119](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n119/mode/1up))

**Hojo, Tokiyuki** Pres. of the Imp. Northeastern Univ., Sendai; b. 1859 in Kaga; grad. from the Science Col. (Mathematics), Imp. Univ., Tokyo, '85; next sent to Hall, Germany, for further studies; Prof. of the 4th High School '88; the 1st High School '91; Yamaguchi High School '94; Principal of the same '96; Principal of the 4th High School '93; Principal of the Hiroshima Higher Normal School '02-13; then to the present post. Add. Sendai. — p. 70 ([scan leaf 119](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n119/mode/1up))

**Hojo, Ujiyasu** Viscount, Court Councillor, b. '45, 7th son of Masahira Hotta, then of the Sakura daimiate; adopted by the family of Hojo, then daimyo of Sayama. Add. Shimo-Nibancho, Tokyo. — p. 70 ([scan leaf 119](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n119/mode/1up))

**Honda, Kosuke** Dr. Agr. (Japan), President of the Model Industry Laboratory in Chosen; was b. '64 in Kagoshima-ken; grad. from the Agri. Col. in '89; sent to Germany for study '01-04; Prof. Agr. Col. of Tokyo Imp. Univ. in '96. Add. Suigen, Korea. — p. 70 ([scan leaf 119](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n119/mode/1up))

**Honda Seii** Baron (cr. 1900), Mem. of the House of Peers; was b. in '64 in Kaga, in one of the leading families of hereditary councillors to the House of Mayeda; studied English and Mathematics under an Englishman early in Meiji, and devoted himself to banking and industrial affairs in the locality, having been President of the Local Hypothec Bank in Kaga for many years. Took interest in politics and was a member of the Organizing Committee when the late Prince Ito formed the Seiyukai. Add. Teramachi, Yotsuya, Tokyo. — p. 70 ([scan leaf 119](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n119/mode/1up))

**Honda, Seiroku** Dr. Dendrology, Prof. at the Agr. Col. of Imp. Univ., Tokyo; b. '66 in Saitama-ken; grad. Dendrology, Agr. College, '86; further studied in Germany; Prof. since '00. Add. Agr. Col. Komaba, Tokyo. — p. 70 ([scan leaf 119](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n119/mode/1up))


## Who's Who in Japan

**Hongo, Fusajiro** Lieut.-Gen., Com. of 1st Div.; b. '60 in Tamba; grad. Mil. Cadet School '79; Sub-Lieut. of Infantry '89; Maj.-Gen. '05; was attached to the Berlin Legation during Japan-Russia War. was commissioned with exchange of prisoners. Dir. Personnel of War Office in '09; transferred to Div. Chief of Milry. Education Board '10, and Vice-Min. of Army Dept., '13-14. Add. Tokyo. — p. 71 ([scan leaf 120](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n120/mode/1up))

**Hori, Keijiro** Pres. of Osaka Shosen Kaisha; b. '67 in Kanazawa and grad. '93 from Law Coll., Imp. Tokyo Univ.; entered the same year Russian Oil Co.; then present Co. in '95 and was promoted the foll. year Chief of Chemulpo branch, etc.; Dir. in '08; Vice Pres. '11; Pres. '15. Add. 39/4, Edobori Kitadori, Nishiku, Osaka. — p. 71 ([scan leaf 120](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n120/mode/1up))

**Hori, Tohru** Mng.-Dir. of the Nippon Yusen Kaisha, was b. '60 in Kaga; edu. at the Imp. Tokyo Univ., class '80; entered the Mitsubishi S.S. Co. in '81. Add. Shimo Takanawa, Shiba, Tokyo. — p. 71 ([scan leaf 120](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n120/mode/1up))

**Horikoshi, Zenjuro** President of Horikoshi Firm; b. in '63 at Ashikaga; crossed over to America when he was young to help Mr. Mason in carrying on business in Japanese silk goods; returned home and organized the Horikoshi Shokwai which has now branches at New York, London, Paris, Lyon, etc. Add. Tsukiji, Tokyo. — p. 71 ([scan leaf 120](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n120/mode/1up))

**Hoshi, Hajime** Pres. of Hoshi Pharmaceutical Co.; b. '74 in Fukushima; studied at Tokyo Commercial School and Columbia Univ., N.Y.; after grad. published monthly Japan and America in N.Y.; dispatched by Govt. to Europe to inspect industry. Returned '07 and started present Co.; once elected M.P. Add. Gotenyama, Shinagawa, Tokyo. — p. 71 ([scan leaf 120](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n120/mode/1up))

**Hoshino, Hisahi** D. Litt. (Japan), Prof. of Literature at the Imp. Tokyo Univ. and Mem. of Imp. Acad.; b. in Niigata in 1839; appointed compiler of the Historiographical Bureau in '81, Prof. at the Univ. in '88, where he fills chairs of Chinese history and literature. Add. Omotecho, Koishikawa, Tokyo. — p. 71 ([scan leaf 120](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n120/mode/1up))

**Hoshino, Kingo** Lieut.-Gen., Com. of 17th Division since '16; b. '63 in Niigata; chief of staff of the 1st Div. in Russo-Japanese War; promoted to Chief Staff of the Govt.-Gen., Kwantung in '07, Inspector of Field Artillery '12; and rewarded with 3rd Class Golden Kite (war honor). Add. Bentencho, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 71 ([scan leaf 120](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n120/mode/1up))

**Hoshino, Seki** Member of the Tokyo Chamber of Commerce, Mng.-Dir. of the Tokyo Printing Co. and other concerns; b. '54 in Tokyo, started printing business in '74; went to America in '87 to investigate printing business, and was a leading promoter of the Oji Paper Mill; Mem. of House of Rep. in '12. Add. Hamacho, Nihombashi, Tokyo. — p. 71 ([scan leaf 120](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n120/mode/1up))

**Hosokawa, Junjiro** Baron (cr. '00), D. Litt. (Japan), Privy Councillor since '93; b. '34 in Kochi-ken; Vice-Pres. of the House of Peers, '91-93; once held posts of President of the Peer's Girls School, and Chief of the Secretariat Bureau of the Imp. Household; wrote a number of literary works. Add. Surugadai, Kanda, Tokyo. — p. 71 ([scan leaf 120](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n120/mode/1up))

**Hozumi, Chincho** Baron (cr. '15), Privy Councillor since 1916, Emeritus Prof. of Imp. University of Tokyo; b. in Uwajima in '57; studied Law at the Gov.-Univ., Tokyo; sent to England and Germany to study his speciality '79-81; appointed Prof. at the alma mater., and a Dean shortly afterwards; member of the House of Peers, '00; resigned the two posts to devote himself to his speciality; was sent to Italy to attend the International Conference held in '02. Add. Minamicho, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 71 ([scan leaf 120](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n120/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Hunter, Reutaro** B. Sc. (Glasgow), Prop. of Hunter Firm, Dir. of Osaka Iron Works, etc., b. in '71 at Kobe, of a Scotch father and Japanese mother; studied at Glasgow Univ., and afterward assisted his father in various undertakings. Add. Ajikawa, Osaka. — p. 72 ([scan leaf 121](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n121/mode/1up))

**Ibuka, Kajirosuke** M.A. (Princeton), D.D. (Rutger), President of the Meiji Gakuin, Chairman, National Committee, Y.M.C.A. Union of Japan; b. in Aizu; took part in the civil war of the Restoration; studied at Dr. Brown's School in Yokohama and was converted then at the Union Theological Seminary, New York, U.S.A.; has represented Japan in the Worlds Y.M.C.A. and other religious conferences held abroad. Mrs. Ibuka is B.A., (Mount Holyyork, Mass.) Add. Meiji Gakuin, Shiba, Tokyo. — p. 72 ([scan leaf 121](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n121/mode/1up))

**Ichijo, Saneteru** Prince, Mem. House of Peers, one of the pr'oud Fujiwara houses and formerly a gosekke (five privileged houses entitled to furnish candidates to the posts of "Chief" and "Right Minister"); originally a scion of the Kujo family. The present head was b. 1860, is a Captain of the Navy and was attaché to the Embassy 1902-06. Add. Fukuyoshi-cho, Akasaka, Tokyo. — p. 72 ([scan leaf 121](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n121/mode/1up))

**Ichiki, Kitokuro** D.C.L. (Japan), Member of House of Peers, Minister of Education and next Minister for Interior in 2nd Okuma Ministry; b. '65 at Shizuoka; studied the science of constitution, administrative politics, etc. in Germany '90-93. Chief Councillor of the Home Office; Professor at the Tokyo University '94; nominated member of the House of Peers in 1900; held the Vice-Ministerial post '08-11, Chief of Bureau of Legislation '12-13 in 3rd Katsura Ministry. Add. Hayashicho, Koi., Tokyo. — p. 72 ([scan leaf 121](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n121/mode/1up))

**Ichiku, Masakata** 1st Secretary of Japanese Legation at Madrid since '05; b. '59 in Kagoshima; chancellor at Rome in '82, attaché in '86, transferred to the Hague in '90, Per. Sec. to the Minister of Agriculture and Commerce in '92. Secretary at Berlin in '95, at Rome in '97. Add. Japanese Legation, Spain. — p. 72 ([scan leaf 121](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n121/mode/1up))

**Ichiku, Otohiko** Vice-Minister of Finance since '16; b. '72 in Kagoshima; graduated from the Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ. in '96; appointed Revenue officer of Kumamoto Revenue Adm. Bureau; secretary to Land Adjust. Bureau of Okinawa-ken; Secretary to the Formosan Government, Secretary to the Finance Dept., then Chief of Accountant Bureau of Dep't of Finance. Add. Surugadai, Tokyo. — p. 72 ([scan leaf 121](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n121/mode/1up))

**Ichinohe, Hyoe** Gen., Pres. of Military Education Board; of Panlungshan fame in the 1st general assault delivered on Port Arthur in '04; b. '55 in Hiroshima; Sub-Lieut. '77; Capt. '83; Major '88; Lieut.-Col. '94; took part in the Japan-China War at the head of a reg. and was rewarded with 4th order of Golden Kite; Col. '97; Chief Staff of 6th Div. '98; Maj.-Gen. '02; Com. of 4th Army Div. in '07 and of 1st Div. in Dec. '12. Holder of 2nd Class Golden Kite, and of Rising Sun (war honour). Promoted to the present rank and post in '16. Add. Kamishibuya, Tokyo — p. 72 ([scan leaf 121](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n121/mode/1up))

**Igarashi, Shusuke** D. Eng., ex-Electrician of the Department of Communications, in charge of telephone service '94-14; b. '58 in Yonezawa ; grad. from the former Imp. Engineering Col. in '82; assist.-electrician at the then Public Works Dep't; teacher in the former Tokyo Post and Telegraph School '89; visited America to inspect electric business in '89-90; electrician at the the Communication Dep't; resumed duty at the above school soon after; was also examiner of the Patent Bureau as additional post. Add. Roppongi, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 72 ([scan leaf 121](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n121/mode/1up))


## Who's Who in Japan

**Iguchi, Seigo** Gen., member of Military Council; b. '55 in Shizuoka-ken; entered Mil. Academy '75 and Mil. Staff Col. '84; despatched to Germany to study tactics, '87-90; Instructor at Mil. Staff Col. and Major in '91; Staff Officer of 2nd Army in the Japan-China War; Lieut.-Col. '95; Sec. Chief at War Office '01; Maj.-Gen. '02 took part in the Japan-Russia War as a staff officer, was awarded 2nd class Golden Kite; Lieut.-Gen. '09 and transferred to Pres. of Mil. Staff Coll. Com. of 15th Army Division in '12; Com. Chosen Garrison in '15; full Gen. '16 and to present post. Add. Tokyo. — p. 73 ([scan leaf 122](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n122/mode/1up))

**Iida, Giichi** ex-Mng. Dir. Mitsui Bussan; b. '51 in Yamaguchi, early entered the service of the establishment, in which he rose to the high position; was involved in the Naval scandal in '14. Add. Shin-ryudo, Tokyo. — p. 73 ([scan leaf 122](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n122/mode/1up))

**Iida, Seiichi** Dir. of Kabuto Beer Co., Onoda Cement Co., etc.; b. '72 in Yamaguchi-ken; grad. '06 Law Coll., Imp. Tokyo Univ.; entered Communication Dep't and soon after despatched to Germany to inspect postal business; afterwards retired from official service to enter business; once returned M.P. Add. Nishinomiya, Hyogoken. — p. 73 ([scan leaf 122](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n122/mode/1up))

**Iida, Shinshichi** dry goods merchant, head of the Iida Firm, one of the largest dry goods stores in Japan; b. '59 in Kyoto; has been granted medals and decorations for his service in the cause of public utility; has branches in Tokyo, Osaka, Yokohama, Tientsin, Lyons, London, etc. Add. Karasumaru, Kyoto. — p. 73 ([scan leaf 122](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n122/mode/1up))

**Iida, Tatsumi** Aud. of the N.Y.K. and Dir. Japan Brick Company; b. '42 in Aomori-ken; Junior Secretary of the Department of Finance in '79, which he resigned to be made Dir. of the Bank of Japan for some while; then entered the Imperial Household where he had charge of the Treasury but resigned in '93. Add. Edogawa, Koi., Tokyo. — p. 73 ([scan leaf 122](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n122/mode/1up))

**Iijima, Kametaro** 1st Secy. and charge d'affairs of Legation to Chili since '14; b. '65 in Tokyo; graduated from the Imp. Tokyo Univ. in '93; clerk at the Foreign Office in '93; appointed élève consul at Fusan in '94; transferred to Ninsen in '95, to Vancouver the same year, attaché in Holland in '96, transferred to London, to St. Petersburg '97, Consul at Townsville '99, at Odessa in '01, transferred to Rome in '04, to Odessa in '06 then to Calcutta; Con.-Gen. at New York in '13. Add. Japanese Legation, Chili. — p. 73 ([scan leaf 122](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n122/mode/1up))

**Iimori, Teizo** a German scholar who was first educated at the Tokyo Foreign Language School and next at Freiburg '84-86, where he got Ph. D. Has taught German at the Imperial Tokyo Univ., Women's Higher Normal School, Tokyo, etc. Was born in 1850 at Suga. Add. Yumicho, Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 73 ([scan leaf 122](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n122/mode/1up))

**Ijichi, Suetaka** Vice-Adm., Mem. of Admirals' Council. b. '57 in Kagoshima; Sub-Lieut. in '83. In the war with China, '94-95, fought first as chief gunner of the Fuso or Comdr. of the Musashi or as staff officer. Capt. in '01, Commander of the flagship of the Kamimura Squadron in Russo-Japanese War; Com'der of the Kashima during and after she was built in England; Rear-Adm. as chief of the Staff of the Maizuru Admiralty '07; Vice-Adm. '11 and transferred to Kure; Com.-in-Chief of Yokosuka Admiralty '14, of Kure '15; present post Dec. '16. Add. Tokyo. — p. 73 ([scan leaf 122](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n122/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Ijuin, Goro** Admiral, Baron (cr. '07), and Mem. Supreme Council of War; b. '52 in Satsuma; took part in the Restoration War while he was still a minor; entered the Naval Academy '81; despatched to England for study, '78-9; Capt. and was on board the Naikyo Maru in the Battle of Yellow Sea '94; in charge of duty of Vice-Chief of Naval Staff '98; Rear-Adm. '99; invented the Ijuin fuse '00 and was duly rewarded; represented Japan with the Asama and Takasago in the Coronation Ceremony of the late King Edward; Vice-Adm. '03 to be appointed Vice-Chief of Naval Staff; transferred to commander of 2nd Standing Squadron Nov. '06; represented Japan in Jamestown Exhibition, U.S.A., April '08 with the Tsukuba and Chitose. Granted 1st Class Golden Kite for his service in the '04-5 war, Com. 1st Squadron '08, Chief of Naval Staff '09-14, Mem. Supreme Council in '14. Add. Nagata-cho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 74 ([scan leaf 123](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n123/mode/1up))

**Ijuin, Hikokichi** Ambassador at Rome since Jan., '16; b. '64 in Kagoshima; grad. Law, Imperial Univ. in '90; attaché in '90, élève consul at Chefoo in '90, sec. at London in '94, transferred to Vienna in '95, consul at Fusan in '96, at Tientsin '01, Consul-General in '02; councillor of Japanese Embassy at London '08. Decorated with 3rd class Rising Sun and grant of money in '02 in connection with Boxer trouble and with 2nd class Rising Sun with grant of money '06 in connection with the Russo-Japanese War. Minister Ple. and Envoy Extr. at Peking '08-13. Add. Rome, Italy. — p. 74 ([scan leaf 123](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n123/mode/1up))

**Ikebukuro, Hidetaro** Dir. of the Imp. Mint; b. '72 in Kagoshima; grad. Law Col. of Imp. Tokyo Univ., '97; entered to the service of Finance Dept., and promoted Sec., Tobacco Monopoly Bureau and then to present post in '13. Add. Mint, Osaka. — p. 74 ([scan leaf 123](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n123/mode/1up))

**Ikeda, Kensai** M.D., Baron (cr. '99), Surg.-Col., Court Councillor; b. '41 in Niigata-ken; pursued his medical study in Germany '70-73; appointed Surgeon-Colonel in '76; physician-in-ordinary and councillor at the Court in '02. Add. Surugadai, Kanda, Tokyo. — p. 74 ([scan leaf 123](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n123/mode/1up))

**Ikeda, Kenzo** banker, b. '56 in Tajima; Pres. of the "Dai Hyaku Ginko" (One Hundredth Bank) since '83, Chairman of the Tokyo Clearing House, Vice-Pres. of the Japan Traders' Association; reputed as being one of the most wide-awake businessmen. Add. Tsukiji, Tokyo. — p. 74 ([scan leaf 123](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n123/mode/1up))

**Ikeda, Kikunae** chemist, D. Sc., Prof. at the College of Science in the Imp. Univ. of Tokyo; b. '64 in Kagoshima; grad. the Science College of the Imp. Tokyo Univ. in '86; Prof. at the Higher Normal School, but afterward to that of his alma mater; sent to Germany '89, to be made again Prof. at the same Univ. on returning home. Has lately secured patent for a dietary power for increasing taste of foods and drinks, called "Aji-no-moto." Add. Fujimicho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 74 ([scan leaf 123](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n123/mode/1up))

**Ikeda, Nakahiro** Marquis, head of the house of the former daimyo of Tottori with the fief of 320,000 koku (founded by one of the sons of Teru- masa, vid. foll.) ; b. in 1877 ; is 5th son of late Prince Tokugawa Keiki, adopted into the family. Add. Harajuku, near Tokyo. — p. 74 ([scan leaf 123](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n123/mode/1up))


## Who's Who

**Ikeda Narimasa** Marquis, former daimyo of Okayama with the fief of 310,000 koku, the house having been founded by one of the sons of Terumasa (1564–1613), one of the generals of Hideyoshi. The present head was b. in 1865. Add. Yoychata, near Tokyo. — p. 75 ([scan leaf 124](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n124/mode/1up))

**Ikeda Ryuichi** Man. Dir. of the Nisshin Life Insur. Co., Prof. at the Waseda University; b. '72 in Ehime-ken; studied law at the Waseda Univ. and the Meiji Univ.; and further at Berlin and Leipzig; Prof. at the Waseda Univ. combining with it the post at the Nisshin which was started in '05. Add. Nando-machi, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 75 ([scan leaf 124](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n124/mode/1up))

**Ikeda Seihin** Mg. Dir. Mitsui Bank; b. '68 at Yonezawa; studied at the Keio and the Harvard; was assist. Dir. of Mitsui Bank's Osaka Branch '98-00; toured through Europe and America to inspect banking business, '03. It was at his suggestion that the Bank formerly Ltd. partnership concern was converted into one of joint stock system in '09. Add. Shin-Ryudo, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 75 ([scan leaf 124](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n124/mode/1up))

**Ikeda Torajiro** Mng.-Dir. of Hoden Oil Co., Dir. of Chuo Life Ins. Co., Toa Flour Mfg. Co., Teikoku News Agency, etc.; b. '64 in Okayama-ken; studied Pol. Econom. at Keio Univ. and went to America to inspect industry. On return he entered the Murai, then famous tobacco dealers in Kyoto, and when tobacco business became Gov. monopoly he was appointed official of the Bureau and consequently raised to a Dir.; but soon left it, to enter the Toa, then Hoden, etc. Add. Kanasugi-Hamacho, Shiba, Tokyo. — p. 75 ([scan leaf 124](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n124/mode/1up))

**Ikegami Shiro** Mayor of Osaka; b. '57 in Fukushima-ken. Came to Tokyo and entered Metropolitan Police Board '77, and this paved the avenue to his promotion, Chief Inspector of Osaka '00-13. Mayor in Sept. 1913. Add. Enokojima, Osaka. — p. 75 ([scan leaf 124](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n124/mode/1up))

**Ikuta Sadayuki** Dir. of National Debt Bureau, Bank of Japan; b. at Tosa in '70; grad. Keio Univ. '90 and entered present bank; studied in America, the bank furnishing the expense. Add. Takajo-machi, Ichigaya, Tokyo. — p. 75 ([scan leaf 124](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n124/mode/1up))

**Imamura Shigezo** Pres. of the Imamura Bank, is the 2nd son of the late Seinosuke Imamura; b. in Tokyo in '77, inherited father's estate in Sept. '02 and became the Director of the Bank started by his father. Add. Tamachi, Shiba, Tokyo. — p. 75 ([scan leaf 124](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n124/mode/1up))

**Imanishi Rinzaburo** Chairman of Osaka Sampin Exchange, Vice-Ch. of Osaka Chamber of Commerce, Pres. of Hanshin Elec. Railway Co., etc.; M.P. for Ehime-ken; b. '53 in Ehime and studied at Mitsubishi Commercial school, to enter subsequently Mitsubishi Firm; then left it to start Osaka Kyodo S.S. Co. (present N.Y.K.); Mng.-Dir. of San-yo Railway Co. '92, etc. Add. Heden-Nibancho, Nishi-ku, Osaka. — p. 75 ([scan leaf 124](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n124/mode/1up))

**Inagaki Ippei** Agr. D., Prof. Imp. Tokyo Univ.; b. '63 in Nagano-ken; and adopted into the family; grad. '85 in Agr. Coll., Imp. Tokyo Univ. and studied in the Hall; Prof. at Higher Normal School; further studied in Germany '00-3; Prof. at Morioka Agr. and Forest Sch. '03, at alma mater '06. Has written many works on agricultural meteorology, physics, land improvement, nutrition of plants, and so forth. Add. Shimo-Megro, near Tokyo. — p. 75 ([scan leaf 124](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n124/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Inokuty, Ariya** M.E., D. Eng. (Japan), Mem. of Imperial Academy of Japan, Prof. of mechanical engineering and of applied mechanics at Col. Eng., Imp. Univ. Tokyo since '86; b. in Kanazawa in '53; graduated in '82 from the former Eng. College, Tokyo; went on scientific tour in '94 to England, France, etc. staying abroad for two years; wrote (in English) many papers on mechanical science as an essay on the theory of centrifugal pump which has wide attention in Europe and America; invented several mechanical devices of very high practical utility, such as universal coupling for line shafting, valveless pump, forced vortex pump, steam boiler, printing dynamometer, etc. Add. Nishi-Hara, Koishikawa, Tokyo. — p. 76 ([scan leaf 125](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n125/mode/1up))

**Inouye, Enryo** Litt. D., authority of pure philosophy; b. '58 in Niigata-ken; grad. Philosophy at Imp. Tokyo Univ. '85 and studied abroad; founded Tetsugakkan (present Toyo Univ.) in '87 himself as Pres. and had occupied chair of Ph.; left the Univ. to the care of Dr. Mayeda a few years ago. Has many valuable publications on philosophy, especially about the supernatural. Add. Fujimaye, Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 76 ([scan leaf 125](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n125/mode/1up))

**Inouye, Jukichi** Secretary, Foreign Office, but more widely known as one of the best Japanese writers of English; b. '62 in Tokushima; proceeded to England in '73; finished Rugby '81 and then the course of metallurgy at Royal School of Mines, returning home '83; was Prof. of English at Tokyo High School till '73, Japanese editor on the staff of the Japan Gazette; Secretary of Legation at Brussels and Washington; remaining at the head office since '02. Has compiled Jap.-Eng. and Eng.-Jap. dictionaries. Add. Nakano, near Tokyo. — p. 76 ([scan leaf 125](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n125/mode/1up))

**Inouye, Junnosuke** President of Yokohama Specie Bank, since '13; b. in Oita, '69; grad. Law, Imp. Univ., Tokyo, class '95; entered the Bank of Japan the same year and was promoted to be Dir. Banking Bureau in '05; was the Bank's Agent in London '08-11, Vice-Pres. of Y.S.B. '11-13. Add. Fukiteeho, Nishinokubo, Shiba, Tokyo. — p. 76 ([scan leaf 125](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n125/mode/1up))

**Inouye, Koji** Chief Mng. of Furukawa Mining Co. and Chief of Ashio Mining Office; b. '63 in Tokyo. Studied at Keiogijuku Univ.; entered the Furukawa in '88 and has been ever since in the service. Add. Minami-cho, Takanawa, Tokyo. — p. 76 ([scan leaf 125](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n125/mode/1up))

**Inouye, Kakugoro** ex-M.P., Dir. of Japan Gas Co. etc.; b. in Hiroshima, '59; stayed in U.S.A. for some years; started a paper at Seoul and was appointed adviser to the Korean Government in '84; gallantly cut his way when in that year the Japanese Legation was assaulted by a Korean mob. From '90 to '15 he had represented Hiroshima-ken in the House of Representatives, where his sarcastic eloquence attracted wide attention; Pres. of the Hokkaido Coal & Steamship Co. in '10; was Vice-Pres. of the Tokyo Chamber of Commerce. Add. Ichibancho, Tokyo. — p. 76 ([scan leaf 125](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n125/mode/1up))

**Inouye, Katsunosuke** 2nd Marquis (suc. '15), Mem. House of Peers, b. in Choshu, '60; an adopted son and heir of the late Marquis Inouye (d. '15) who was his uncle; was in the service of the Nippon Ginko before he got appointment at Foreign Office in '85, to be sent to Berlin the following year as secretary of Legation there; was back to the Foreign Office as secretary and councillor, '92; and next Envoy Extraordinary and Minister Plenipotentiary in Germany 1898-07; Envoy to Chile in '10; Ambassador to St. James' Court '13-16. Add. Uchidayama, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 76 ([scan leaf 125](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n125/mode/1up))


## Who's Who

**Inouye, Keijiro** Director of the Electric Dep't of Tokyo Municipality; b. in Higo Province in '61; took part in the Saigo Affair (civil war of '77) and was once imprisoned for political offence; subsequently took to business under patronage of the late Mr. Hoshi. Add. Tokyo City Office. — p. 77 ([scan leaf 126](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n126/mode/1up))

**Inouye, Kinosuke** Geologist of Dep't of Agr. and Com.; was b. in Yamaguchi-ken in '73; grad. the Col. of Science Tokyo Imp. Univ. in '96. Add. Shikokucho, Shiba, Tokyo. — p. 77 ([scan leaf 126](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n126/mode/1up))

**Inouye, Kyoshiro** 2nd Viscount, D. Eng., Member of House of Peers, Prof. at Tokyo Imp. Univ.; b. Kumamoto, adopted son of late Viscount Ki; grad. Mining in '97 at Tokyo, Imp. Univ. studied abroad '98-04, Prof. Osaka Higher Tech. School and Kyoto Imp. Univ.; then transferred to Tokyo. Add. Shimo Osaki, Tokyo. — p. 77 ([scan leaf 126](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n126/mode/1up))

**Inouye, Michiyasu** M.D., Oculist. Proprietor and Head of Inouye Hospital; b. '67 in Hyogo-ken; grad. Medical Col. of Imp. Univ. of Tokyo '90 and appointed Assist.-Prof. at that College; Junior-President of the Himeji Hospital '93; Prof. at the 3rd Higher School in Kyoto '95, which he left and established his own Hospital '04. Is also noted as an uta poet of the old school and attached to the National Verse Office of the Court. Add. Uchisaiwai-cho Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 77 ([scan leaf 126](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n126/mode/1up))

**Inouye, Tatsuji** M. D., Oculist, was b. 1881 in Tokyo, and studied his speciality at the Imperial Tokyo Univ., Leipzig and Loyal Ophthalmic Hospital, London. He has published a number of monographs on eye-diseases and maintains his own hospital. Add. Higashi-Kobai-cho, Surugadai, Tokyo. — p. 77 ([scan leaf 126](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n126/mode/1up))

**Inouye, Niro** Lieut.-Gen., Commander of Communication Corps; b. '64 in Ehime-ken; Sub-Lieut. of Eng. '85; sent to Germany for study; was at one time the Chief of Military Affairs Bureau and of Eng. Affairs, dispatched to Europe to inspect Aeronautics in April, '14. Lieut.-Gen. '15. Add. Naka-cho, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 77 ([scan leaf 126](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n126/mode/1up))

**Inouye, Ryokei** Fleet Admiral, Visc. (Baron in '87 and Visc. in '07); b. '45 in Satsuma; studied at Annapolis '77-81; and rendered distinguished services in Korean affairs in '74, civil war of '73 and in Sino-Japanese war of '94-5; promoted to Admiral in '08, Fleet Adm. in '11. Holds 2nd Class Golden Kite. Add. Hiro-o, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 77 ([scan leaf 126](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n126/mode/1up))

**Inouye, Tatsukuro** D.C.L. (Japan); b. '68 in Shidzuoka-ken; graduated from Law College of the Imp. Univ. of Tokyo, in '87, and studied Practical Economics in the Imp. Univ. of Tokyo, in '87, and in the postgraduate Course of the University. Taught Economics for several years in his alma mater, and also in Peer's School as well as some other schools. Entered the Bank of Japan '97; Agent at New York '09-11. Add. Sekiguchi Suido-cho, Tokyo. — p. 77 ([scan leaf 126](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n126/mode/1up))

**Inouye, Tetsujiro** D. Litt. (Japan); b. '55 in Fukuoka-ken; pursued philosophical study in Germany and France, '84-90; Professor in College of Literature, Imperial Tokyo Univ. since '90, of which he was once Dean. Among his numerous works may be mentioned the "Life of Buddha," the Oriental Philosophy, etc. Add. Omote-cho, Koishikawa, Tokyo. — p. 77 ([scan leaf 126](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n126/mode/1up))

**Inouye, Tomoichi** D.C.L. (Japan), Gov. of Tokyo; b. in Kaga, '71; grad. Law Col., Tokyo Imp. Univ. in '93, and then entered Home Office as secretary and Councillor; was next Director of Shrine Bur., and transferred to the present post in '15. Add. Hisakata-cho, Koishikawa, Tokyo. — p. 77 ([scan leaf 126](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n126/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Inouye, Toyotaro** Oculist. Dir. of Tokyo Oculist Hospital; b. '61 in Matsuye, when he finished the local medical school in '85, also studied at Tokyo and Germany '91-95, and established his hospital at the present address. Add. Iida-machi, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 78 ([scan leaf 127](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n127/mode/1up))

**Inukai, Ki** M.P., a leader of the National Party, and an M.P. of unbroken record; born at Okayama '55; early began political career; was editor of the "Hochi Shimbun" till the opening of National Diet, '90 and has distinguished himself by his incisive eloquence and great power of political manoeuvre. On the occasion of the Okuma-Itagaki Cabinet in '98, he occupied the chair of the Ministry of Education for a short while. In the internal trouble of the Party in '09, he at the head of the non-coalition section, came out triumphant. He is rather frail in health. Add. Babashita, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 78 ([scan leaf 127](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n127/mode/1up))

**Inuzuka, Katsutaro** Lord-in-Waiting of the Kinkei Hall and M.P.; b. in Tokyo in '68; grad. Law, Imp. Univ. '89, Per. Sec. to Home Minister '90; Sec. of Nagasaki-ken '91, of Communication Dept. '92, dispatched to Europe and America to inspect railway affairs '95. Dir. of R'ly Bureau '99, Gov. of Aomori '04, Dir. of Public Works Bureau (Home Office) a few months after, sent to Europe and America June '10, removed to Governor of Nagasaki '10 and of Osaka '11-12; Vice-Min. of Communications '13-14. Add. Iida-machi-Gochome, Tokyo. — p. 78 ([scan leaf 127](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n127/mode/1up))

**Iiiye, Tamemori** Viscount, Mem. of House of Peers, Chief Chamberlain to the Crown Prince, amateur painter of "Tosa" style picture; b. '63 in Kyoto; appointed present post, '14. Add. Sankocho, Shiba, Tokyo. — p. 78 ([scan leaf 127](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n127/mode/1up))

**Ishibashi, Hajime** Vice Adm., Pres. of Nautical College; b. '62 at Kanazawa; early entered the Navy; grad. '83 Naval Academy. Fought in both wars, '94-5 and '04-5. Promoted Rear-Adm. '10 and to present post. Add. Kasumicho, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 78 ([scan leaf 127](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n127/mode/1up))

**Ishiguro, Isoji** D. Eng. (Japan), Mem. House of Peers and Lord-in-Waiting of Kinkei Hall; b. '55 at Kanazawa; studied engineering in England, '79-83; was appointed Naval Engineer in '85 to supervise the the construction of a dockyard; also took part in laying the waterworks of Tokyo; had charge of the Shipbuilding Section of the Navy. Add. Daimachi, Akasaka, Tokyo. — p. 78 ([scan leaf 127](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n127/mode/1up))

**Ishiguro, Tadanori** Baron (cr. '95), Surgeon-General (ret.); b. '45 in Echigo; Chief Surgeon of the Osaka Military Hospital on the occasion of the Civil War of Kagoshima in '77; promoted to Surgeon-Col. next year and to Surgeon-Gen. in '90; retired in '96. Since that time has devoted himself to Red Cross work, of which he is a chief promoter and represented it in the international conference held in Germany in 1890. Nom. member House of Peers in '02; decorated with the Grand Cordon of Rising Sun in connection with Russo-Japanese War. He widely enjoys the reputation of being an "all round man." Add. Agebacho, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 78 ([scan leaf 127](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n127/mode/1up))

**Ishii, Kikujiro** Visc. (cr. '16; Baron '12), mem. of House of Peers (nom. '16); b. '65 at Chiba; grad. Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ., '90; attaché to the French Legation '91; 3rd Sec. of the Legation '93; Consul at Ninsen '96 ; 2nd Sec, of the Chinese Legation '97, to be promoted to the 1st Sec. the following year and was one of those who sustained the siege in the Boxer trouble; Sec. at the Head Office and Chief of Telegraph Section 1900; Director of Commerce Bureau in '04; was despatched to San Francisco and Vancouver in '07 in connection with the anti-Japanese riot there. Vice-Minister of Foreign Affairs, '08; then Ambassador to Paris; recalled as Min. of For. Affairs, '15-16. — p. 78 ([scan leaf 127](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n127/mode/1up))


## Who's Who in Japan

**Ishikawa, Chiyomatsu** D. Sci., zoologist, Prof. at the Agr. Col., Imp. Univ. of Tokyo since '90; b. '60 in Shidzuoka; graduated from the Science Col. of the University '82; further studied in Germany; made a tour through Europe in '09 on an official mission, was appointed Member of Imperial Academy Japan, April, '11. Add. Obancho, Yotsuya, Tokyo. — p. 79 ([scan leaf 128](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n128/mode/1up))

**Ishikawa, Shokin** Chief Abbot of the Shinsho-ji Temple, headquarters of a Shingon sub-sect, Narita; b. '69 in Chiba-ken; entered the service of the temple where his uncommon ability won the confidence of the late chief abbot who nominated him as successor; graduated from a Buddhist college in Tokyo; further studied in India and Europe for several years; installed in the present seat in '93. Several educational and charitable institutions have been founded by him at Narita. Add. Narita, Chiba-ken. — p. 79 ([scan leaf 128](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n128/mode/1up))

**Ishimaru, Shigeyoshi** D. Eng., ex-Superintendent-Engineer of the Railway Board; b. '64 in Oita-ken; graduated from the Eng. College of the Tokyo Imp. Univ. '90; appointed Assist.-Engineer to the Home Dept. the same year; transferred as Engineer of Akita-ken '91; subsequently Rly. Eng. to the Dep. of Com'tions; was despatched to Europe and America on an official mission '07-08; retired from Imp. Railways in '16. Add. Aoyama Minamicho, Tokyo. — p. 79 ([scan leaf 128](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n128/mode/1up))

**Ishiwara, Kenzo** Vice Minster of Imp. Houshold; b. '64 in Okayama; grad. '89 law at Imp. Univ. of Tokyo and entered Justice Office; app. judge in '91 which he left the following year to be councillor to local govts. and Home Office; governors of Yamanashi, Chiba, Kochi, Hokkaido, &c. lastly of Kanagawa, from which he was transferred to present post in '15. Add. Shimo Osaki, near Tokyo. — p. 79 ([scan leaf 128](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n128/mode/1up))

**Ishiwata, Bin-ichi** D.C.L. (Japan), Mem. House of Peers; b. '59 at Tokyo; studied law in Germany, '86-90; Judge of Tokyo Local Court; Councillor of Dept. of Justice; Public Pro. of Tokyo Appeal Court; Lecturer of Law at Imp. Tokyo Univ.; Dir. of Civil and Criminal Bureau; promoted to Vice-Min. of Justice in Sept., '03, then to Chief Sec. Cabinet in Jan., '06--April, '08. Add. Echizembori, Kyobashi, Tokyo. — p. 79 ([scan leaf 128](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n128/mode/1up))

**Ishizuka, Eizo** Pres. Oriental Development Co. since '16, b. '65 at Aizu; appointed Legal Adviser in '95 to the Korean Govt. for a time; then held the post of Chief Councillor for the Government-General of Formosa; transferred to post of Governor of the Occupied Districts in Manchuria, '05; appointed Chief Councillor of Chosen Gov. in Sept., '07; Dir. of Com. Agr. & Eng. Dept. Korea, in '12. Add. Seoul. — p. 79 ([scan leaf 128](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n128/mode/1up))

**Isobe, Masaharu** ex-Dir. of the Mining Bureau, Agr. and Com. Dept.; b. '64 in Choshu; graduated Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ., '90, to enter the Department where he filled offices of Councillor, Patent Comptroller, Commissioner of St. Louis Exhibition. Dir. of Patent Bureau, and then Mining Bureau. Add. Yarai, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 79 ([scan leaf 128](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n128/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Isobe, Shiro** D.C.L., Crown Mem. of House of Peers (nom. 1914), ex-chairman of the Tokyo Barristers' Association; b. '51 in Toyama-ken, studied law in France, '75-77; Judge in '79, Public Procurator of the Court of Cassation '86, then Judge of the same Court '90, and was returned from his native place to the House of Representatives, resigned the official post about that time and has since been practising law. He is noted amateur singer of "gidayu." Add. Kamezawacho, Honjo, Tokyo. — p. 80 ([scan leaf 129](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n129/mode/1up))

**Isobe, Yaichiro** Principal and Prop. of the Kokumin Ei-Gakkai (the National English Language Association) in Tokyo; b. '61 in Oita-ken; studied English at Keio Gijiku for several years: organized the above English School in conjunction with a certain foreigner in '88, which was finally brought under his sole control. Add. Sambancho, Tokyo. — p. 80 ([scan leaf 129](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n129/mode/1up))

**Isomura Toyotaro** Dir. of Hokkaido Colliery and S.S. Co.; b. '68 in Oita-ken; grad. from Keio Gijuku in '90; and official in the Dep't of Communications; journalist for a short while before he entered the Bank of Japan in '94; a clerk in the Mitsui Bussan, its sectional chief and later promoted to the London Agent. Add. Takanawa, Tokyo. — p. 80 ([scan leaf 129](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n129/mode/1up))

**Itagaki, Taisuke** Count (cr. '87); b. in Tosa '37; a retired statesman after long years of heroic struggle in the cause of freedom and liberty. Is one of the few remaining Generals who took distinguished part in suppressing the civil war of the Restoration; was appointed after return of peace a Cabinet Minister which he resigned in '73 in connection with the famous Korean question, in which the subject of the sketch advocated the warlike policy with the elder Saigo. After several years of retired life in native place in 1881 he organized the Liberal Party, and the democratic movement started by him exacted from the Government a solemn promise of inaugurating the parliamentary system ten years hence. It was while on political tour at that time that he was stabbed by a young reactionary. A few months after, in '83, he with the late Count Goto travelled through Europe and America; Home Minister in the 3rd Ito Ministry; same chair in the Okuma-Itagaki Coalition Ministry; resigned leadership of the Liberals in favour of late Prince Ito when the latter formed the Seiyukai in '00, and is now studying social policy. Add. Shiba, Tokyo. — p. 80 ([scan leaf 129](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n129/mode/1up))

**Itakura, Matsutaro** Public Proc. of the Supreme Court; b. in '38 at Yamanashi-ken; graduated Law at the Imperial Tokyo Univ. in '8; judge of the Kofu Local Court in '90, of Chiba Loc. Court in '94, of Tokyo Appeal Court in '97; next transferred to Hakodate Appeal Court; and to the supreme Court in '03. Add. Osaki, Ebara-gun, Tokyo. — p. 80 ([scan leaf 129](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n129/mode/1up))

**Ito, Chojiro** ex-Member of the House of Peers; was b. in Hyogo-ken in '73; one of the wealthy merchants and great land owners in the same perfecture which he represented in the House; director of various banks and Cos. in Kwansai districts. Add. Fujimicho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 80 ([scan leaf 129](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n129/mode/1up))

**Ito, Chuta** D. Eng., architect and Prof. at Imp. Univ., Tokyo since '05; b. '64 in Yamagata-ken; grad. Architecture at the said University '92; despatched to China, India and Turkey in '02-05 to study his special subject. Add. Nishikatamachi, Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 80 ([scan leaf 129](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n129/mode/1up))

**Ito, Daihachi** ex-Vice Gov. of S. Manchuria Rly.; b. '58 in Nagano-ken and adopted by the present family; studied Pol. and Eco. in Tokyo, returned M.P. four times since the 1st session; has also occupied several high posts in Govt. Rlys. etc., as. Dir. of Rly. Bur., Promoter and Dir. of Enoshima Rly., Committee on Broad Gauge System. Is a noted collector of Ukiyoye prints. Add. Gobancho, Tokyo. — p. 80 ([scan leaf 129](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n129/mode/1up))


## Who's Who

**Ito, Eizaburo** Prof. of Applied Chemistry at Col. Eng., Imp. Tokyo Univ. since '02; b. in Tokyo in '76; graduated from said University with honour '01; secured patent for some dyes he invented. Add. Col. Eng., Tokyo Univ. — p. 81 ([scan leaf 130](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n130/mode/1up))

**Ito, Hayazo** M.D., Prof. Imp. Univ., Kyoto; b. '65 in Tottori; graduated Medicine, Imp. Univ., Tokyo, class '90, had charge of his adopted father's hospital at Tottori, then of the Sapporo Hospital '90-00, meanwhile studying in Germany for two years; is also Pres. of the University Hospital, Kyoto. Add. Imp. Univ., Kyoto. — p. 81 ([scan leaf 130](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n130/mode/1up))

**Ito, Hirokuni** (former name Yukichi), 2nd Prince, Deputy Master of Ceremonies; b. '70, 4th son of Gorosaburo Inouye, of the former Choshu clan, and was adopted as a son and heir by the late Prince Ito. Add. Omori, near Tokyo. — p. 81 ([scan leaf 130](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n130/mode/1up))

**Ito, Jirozaemon** Banker and millionaire in Nagoya where his house is one of the oldest; was b. in '48, and though once elected by the fellow highest-tax payers of Aichi-ken as member of the House of Peers he declined; besides running the Ito Bank the house keeps a large drapery store styled Matsuzaka-ya at Nagoya. Tokyo and other places. His son and heir Morimatsu b. in '78 joined the Business-men's party that visited U. S. in 1909. Add. Chayamachi, Nishiku, Nagoya. — p. 81 ([scan leaf 130](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n130/mode/1up))

**Ito, Jujiro** Ph. D. (Pennsylvania), Prof. Waseda Univ.; b. '78 in Hyogo-ken; grad. from Waseda, class 1903; graduate student Univ. of Chicago '05-06; same '06-08, (Harrison scholar, U. Penn., '06-07; Harrison, jur. fellow, same U., '07-08). Has written "Carriage by Sea," and "Study of Commerce," '11, etc. Add. Waseda University, Tokyo. — p. 81 ([scan leaf 130](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n130/mode/1up))

**Ito, Kinsuke** editor of the Nippon, born '65 in Yamaguchi; graduated Keiogijuku and for several years was on the staff of the Jiji; served at the Nippon Ginko from '98 to '06 and had charge of the Secretariat Section; visited Europe and America '05 on official business, but left the Bank soon after his return, and with some of his friends he purchased the Nippon '06. Add. Fujimi-cho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 81 ([scan leaf 130](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n130/mode/1up))

**Ito, Miyoji** Viscount (cr. Baron '95, Visc. '07), Privy Councillor; b '57 at Nagasaki; started his career in civil service from the lowest ladder; was the late Prince Ito's Private Secretary '90-95; then Chief Secy. of the Cabinet also under the Prince. It was he that was chiefly instrumental in bringing the Prince and the Liberals into an understanding, for which he was rewarded with admission into the ministerial circle as Minister of Agriculture and Commerce in '98. But he had to leave in less than four months owing to the rupture between the Ministry and its political supporters. Was appointed Privy Councillor in '99. On the occasion of the Japan-China War he was sent to Chefoo to exchange the signed Treaty of Peace and was created a Peer for this service. Add. Nagata-cho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 81 ([scan leaf 130](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n130/mode/1up))

**Ito, Otojiro** Vice-Adm., Com. of Kure Naval Arsenal; b. in Nagoya in '68; grad. from Naval Cadet School in '83; student of the Naval College '90-91, chief navigating officer of the cr. Hiyei (now defunct) '94. Lieut.-Commander '97, Commander '00, Naval Attache at the Japanese Legation at Berlin '02-04, commanded the Taishu-maru in the Russo-Japanese War, Chief of Hydrographical Bureau '08-11, Com. of Sasebo Arsenal, Vice-Adm. in '15. Add. Kure. — p. 81 ([scan leaf 130](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n130/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Ito, Shunkichi** Baron (cr. '95), Vice-Admiral (ret.), member of House of Peers (nom. in '99); b. at Maizuru, '40, and entered the Navy in '69; Lieut.-Commander in '71; Rear-Admiral in '82; Vice-Adm. in '88; and Vice-Minister of the Navy from '89 to '99; was created a Peer in connection with the Japan-China War. Add. Shinagawa, Tokyo. — p. 83 ([scan leaf 131](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n131/mode/1up))

**Ito, Sukehiro** Viscount; his house was formerly hereditary Lord of the feudatory of Obi, Hyuga, and he, 12th of the line, is eldest son of Yuki, 11th chief of the family; was born in '80 and succeeded to the family title in '94; grad. from Law Col., Imp. Tokyo Univ. '07 and studied in Europe and America; elected mem. House of Peers '11. Add. Hitotsugi, Akasaka, Tokyo. — p. 83 ([scan leaf 131](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n131/mode/1up))

**Ito, Teiji** ex-Judge of the Supreme Court; b. in Niigata-ken in '54; grad. Law, Imp. Univ. Tokyo in '83, and took to the Bench until transferred to the Supreme Court in '94. Add. Shinryudo, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 83 ([scan leaf 131](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n131/mode/1up))

**Ito, Tokutaro** D. Sc. (Japan), M.L.S. (London), noted botanist, son of the late Keisuke Ito, a Lineaus of Japan; was b. '68 in Nagoya; studied at the Imp. Univ., Tokyo and in England; has discovered several new species in Japan. Add. Haramachi, Koishi., Tokyo. — p. 83 ([scan leaf 131](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n131/mode/1up))

**Ito, Yuchu** Dir. of the Toyo Kisen Kaisha, was b. in Miye '70, eldest son of Yuken Ito; grad. from Law Course, Imp. Univ. Tokyo; was Inspector or Judge of a Marine Court from '97 to '07; attended the World's Congress of Maritime Affairs held at Brussels in '05; left the Government service in 1910 to enter the above Company. Add. Okubo, Tokyo. — p. 83 ([scan leaf 131](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n131/mode/1up))

**Ito, Yukei** Auditor of the Board of Audit, and was b. in 1850 in Nagano-ken, 3rd son of Nobutoshi Nakajima, but he was adopted by the present family. Add. Kaga-cho, Ichigaya, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 83 ([scan leaf 131](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n131/mode/1up))

**Iwade, Sobei** Mg. Dir. of the 20th Bank, Vice-Pres. of the Tokyo Chamber of Commerce, Dir. of the 1st Mutual Life Ins. Co.; was b. '48 in Chiba-ken. Started a retail business of manure, oil and rice by which he made a fortune. Add. Higashidaiku-cho, Fukagawa, Tokyo. — p. 83 ([scan leaf 131](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n131/mode/1up))

**Iwahara, Kenzo** ex-Mg.-Dir of the Mitsui Bussan; was born at Daishoji, Kaga, in '63; finished the Mitsubishi Nautical School '83; private secretary to Mr. E.W. Irwin, adviser of the Union Transportation Co., then on a similar service to Mr. Masuda, of the Bussan; had charge of the Co's London Office '86-90, Manager of the Osaka and Kobe branches '90-95, manager of the newly established branch Office at New York '96 to undertake export of Japanese raw silk, and he had extended business field of the Co. in America before he returned home in '06. Was involved in the Naval Scandal '14. Add. Miyamura, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 83 ([scan leaf 131](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n131/mode/1up))

**Iwai, Jutaro** Pres. Tokyo Shogyo Bank; b. in '71 at Kyoto; edu. at Keiogijuku and Yale; was once Dir. of Industrial Bank of Japan. Add. Aoyama Kitacho, Tokyo. — p. 83 ([scan leaf 131](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n131/mode/1up))

**Iwakawa, Tomotaro** biologist, Prof. of the Tokyo Female's Higher Normal School; b. '5[illegible] in Hirosaki; graduated from the former Tokyo Daigaku; appointed successively Prof. of the Higher Normal School, and Prof. of the Tokyo Female's Higher Normal School. Add. Sarugaku-cho, Kanda, Tokyo. — p. 83 ([scan leaf 131](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n131/mode/1up))


## Who's Who

**Iwakura, Tomoaki** 4th Prince, is a minor, the house having been founded by the late Prince Tomomi Iwakura who was one of the principal builders of the work of the Restoration and was created Prince. The father of the present head was b. '78 and graduated from the Peers' School in '91, married in '95 Lady Sakurako, daughter of the late Marquis Saigo and was once in the Dept. of Imp. Household. He was declared bankrupt in '13. Add. Nagatacho, Tokyo. — p. 83 ([scan leaf 132](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n132/mode/1up))

**Iwakura, Tomotsuna** Chief Ritualist of the Imperial Household; was born in '42, an adopted son of the late Prince Tomomi Iwakura, one of the veteran Statesmen of the Restoration. Add. Nagata-cho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 83 ([scan leaf 132](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n132/mode/1up))

**Iwamura, Toru, Baron** Prof. of the Tokyo Academy of Arts; b. in '70 in Tokyo; eldest son of late Baron Takatoshi Iwamura; studied at Paris and New York. Appointed Prof. of the Tokyo Acad. of Arts in '02; was dispatched to foreign exhibitions as Inspector several times; married Eso, 2nd daughter of Baron Michitoshi Iwamura. Add. Tatsuoka, Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 83 ([scan leaf 132](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n132/mode/1up))

**Iwasa, Shin, Baron** physician, and Director of the Kokusci-do Hospital; b. in Tokyo in '65, eldest son of the above; studied Medicine in Tokyo and Berlin; established the above hospital. Add. Kakigara-cho, Nihombashi, Tokyo. — p. 83 ([scan leaf 132](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n132/mode/1up))

**Iwasaki, Hisaya, Baron (cr. '00)** Pres. of Mitsubishi Co.; b. '66; eldest son of the great Yataro Iwasaki, the "Sea King" of his time; entered the Keio-gijuku, then the University of Pennsylvania, Philadelphia, U.S.A. of which he is LL.B., class '83; was installed that year as head of the great house of the Mitsubishi. Billiards and hand-net fishing are his favorite pastime. He has travelled Europe and America a number of times. Add. Yushima Kiridoshi, Tokyo. — p. 83 ([scan leaf 132](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n132/mode/1up))

**Iwasaki, Koyata** Vice-Dir. of Banking Dept. of Mitsubishi Co.; b. '79; 2nd Baron and succeeded in '09 to the title on the decease of his father Yanosuke, 1st Baron and brother of the "Sea King;" was educated at Tokyo Imp. University and Cambridge. Add. Surugadai, Tokyo. — p. 83 ([scan leaf 132](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n132/mode/1up))

**Iwasaki, Tatsundo** Rear-Adm. (ret.); director of the Uraga Dockyard Co.; b. '59 in Fukushima-ken; Second-Lieut. in '82; Rear-Adm. in '08; in succession commanded the Akitsu, Takasago, Hiyei (defunct); was Chief of the 1st section of Materials in the Navy. Add. Kasumicho, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 83 ([scan leaf 132](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n132/mode/1up))

**Iwatare, Kunihiko** electrician and Pres. of the Nippon Electric Co. (joint undertaking between Japanese and foreign capitalists in which the Western Electric Co., U.S.A., are prominent); b. '54 in Fukuoka; grad. from the former Gov. Engineering Coll. '82, and entered the present Co. on its formation about '93. Add. Fujimicho, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 83 ([scan leaf 132](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n132/mode/1up))

**Iwaya, Suyeo** author, novelist, and an Andersen of Japan; b. '70 in Tokyo, 3rd son of the late Iwaya Ichiroku, a noted Chinese scholar and calligraphist. Studied in Germany '98-03, filling at the same time chair of Japanese in the Oriental Language School, Berlin. Has written and translated quite a large number of stories for juvenile readers, and is connected with the Hakubunkwan, publishers, and also the Imperial Theatre. Nom de plume "Sazanami." Add. Takanawa, Tokyo. — p. 83 ([scan leaf 132](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n132/mode/1up))

**Izawa, Shimpei** dentist; b. at Shizuoka in '60; studied dentistry in U.S.A. and established a hospital in Kyobashi; has been appointed dentist attached to the Bureau of Court Physician. Add. Toriizakacho, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 83 ([scan leaf 132](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n132/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Izawa, Shuji** Mem. House of Peers; b. '51 at Takato, Shinano; was in the Department of Education almost without a break '72-97; as Secretary, Director of the B.D.D. School, Music School, Higher Normal School, etc., also Bureau Dir. Has rendered distinguished services for curing stammering, and was rewarded in '11 with 3rd class Rising Sun for it. Add. Dairokuten, Koishikawa, Tokyo. — p. 84 ([scan leaf 133](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n133/mode/1up))

**Izawa, Takio** b. '69, in Nagano-ken; graduated from Law College of Imp. Tokyo Univ.; Metropolitan Police Inspector '06; Governor of Wakayama '07, of Ehime '08-12, of Niigata '12-14; Metropolitan Chief Police Commissioner; '14-15. Add.
