Tokyo. — p. 84 ([scan leaf 133](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n133/mode/1up))

**Jimbo, Kotora** geologist, D. Sc., Prof. at the Col. of Science of the Imp. Tokyo University; b. '67 in Tokyo; grad. the above Col. in '89; Prof. at the 1st Higher School, etc., further studied in Germany; Prof. at the Tokyo University since '98, and has frequently been commissioned with geological survey in Korea, Saghalien, etc. Add. Higashikatamachi, Komagome, Tokyo. — p. 84 ([scan leaf 133](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n133/mode/1up))

**Jo, Kazuma** Pres. of Appeal Court, Seoul; b. in '64 in Tokyo, graduated Law, Imperial Tokyo University in '88, and at once began practice in which he has risen to the front. Has repeatedly been M.P. Add. Seoul. — p. 84 ([scan leaf 133](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n133/mode/1up))

**Kabayama, Aisuke** Pres. of Anglo-Japanese Hydro Elec. Co., Dir. of Horai Life Ins., Hakodate Dockyard, and Copper Mfg. Cos.; b. '65; is son of Count Kabayama; studied economics in England. Add. Nagatacho, Tokyo. — p. 84 ([scan leaf 133](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n133/mode/1up))

**Kabayama, Sukenori** Full Admiral (ret.), Count (cr. Vis. '84 and Count '95), holder of the 2nd Golden Kite, Privy Councillor; b. '37 in Satsuma; entered the Army while young and on the occasion of the Civil War of '77 was on the Staff of Major-General Tani (then Commander of Kumamoto Army Division) as Lieut.-Col.; transferred to the Navy '84; promoted Vice-Admiral '85; appointed Minister of Navy and Chief of Naval Staff at the time of the Japan-China War, and on board the transport Saikyō-maru was present in the battle of the Yellow Sea of Sept. 16th, '94. He was the first Governor-General of Formosa; then appointed Minister of Home Affairs Sept. '96-Dec. '97; Minister of Education Nov. '98—Sept. 1909; Privy Councillor in '04, Chairman of the Education Investigation Committee in '13. Add. Nagata-cho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 84 ([scan leaf 133](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n133/mode/1up))

**Kadono, Ikunoshin** Dir. of the Chiyoda Mutual Life Assurance Co., and of the Kyōdō Fire Ins. Co., etc.; was b. '56, in Miye-ken; was long connected with the Keio-Gijuku, being one of its pioneer graduates; took to business about '01, severing connection as instructor with the institution. Add. Aoyama, Tokyo. — p. 84 ([scan leaf 133](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n133/mode/1up))

**Kadono, Jukuro** Vice Pres. of Okura Firm, Ltd.; b. '68 in Ise; and is brother of I. Kadono; grad. Civil Eng., Imp. Univ. Tokyo; has since been connected with the Firm and was its London Agent '00-07, later Mag. Dir. Add. Shinsaka, Akasaka, Tokyo. — p. 84 ([scan leaf 133](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n133/mode/1up))

**Kadono, Rempachiro** Chief of Yokohama branch of the Mitsui Bank; was b. in Miye-ken, in '68; entered the service of the bank in its early days and was promoted to the present post. Add. Kanasugi-hamacho, Shiba, Tokyo. — p. 84 ([scan leaf 133](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n133/mode/1up))


## Who's Who

**Kadono, Tominosuke** Vice-Pres. of Nagoya Chamber of Commerce, Mem. of Nagoya Municipal Assembly, Dir. of Meiji Bank, Nagoya Elec. Railway, etc.; b. '59 in Akita-ken; graduated from the Waseda and joined the staff of the Iochi, afterwards entered into business; was once Chairman of Nagoya Municipal Assembly; Vice-President of the Chamber of Commerce of the same City; visited America in '10 representing business men in Nagoya. Add. Meiji Ginko, Nagoya. — p. 85 ([scan leaf 134](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n134/mode/1up))

**Kagami, Kenkichi** Manager of the Tokyo Marine Insurance Co., was b. in Tokyo in '68 and is brother of Koichiro Kagami, as below. He graduated from the Tokyo Higher Commercial School in the early eightieth. Add. Otsuka, Koishikawa, Tokyo. — p. 85 ([scan leaf 134](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n134/mode/1up))

**Kagami, Koichiro** Partner of Oriental Emigration Partnership, Ltd., Aud. of Brazil Colonization Co., Inawashiro Hydro-Electric Co., etc. b. '60 in Tokyo; studied at Tokyo Higher Commercial School and early entered the service of the Nippon Yusen Kaisha. Add. Fujimicho, Koji., Tokyo. — p. 85 ([scan leaf 134](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n134/mode/1up))

**Kaino, Kozo** ex-M.P. and Director of South Man. Ely., was b. in '57 took active part in local politics before he was returned to the House where as a member of the Seiyukai he enjoyed great influence. Add. Tsunacho, Mita, Shiba, Tokyo. — p. 85 ([scan leaf 134](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n134/mode/1up))

**Kakehi, Katsuhiko** D.C.L., Prof. Law Coll., Imp. Tokyo Univ.; b. '72 in Nagano-ken; grad. Law Coll. Tokyo Imp. Univ.; studied at Berlin Univ., Germany, '98-03; on returning home appinted Prof. at alma mater '03. Add. Kitamachi, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 85 ([scan leaf 134](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n134/mode/1up))

**Kakchi, Mototada** Auditor of the Japan Beer Brewery Co.; b. in '43 at Takasu; was for many years judge or procurator at the Appeal Court and the Court of Cassation; retired from the bench in '98 and has entered business. Add. Araki-cho, Yotsuya, Tokyo. — p. 85 ([scan leaf 134](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n134/mode/1up))

**Kakinuma, Tanizo** Aud. of First Mutual Life Insurance Co., Dir. of To-a Flour Mill, etc.; b. in Joshu, '54 of a well-to-do fish merchant, is now one of the few millionaire yarn merchants in the City. Add. Koamicho, Nihombashi, Tokyo. — p. 85 ([scan leaf 134](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n134/mode/1up))

**Kakizaki, Kingo** Lawyer, Special Mem. of Osaka Chamber of Commerce, b. '63 in Numazu; grad. Law Coll., Imp. Tokyo Univ. '88; Judge at District Tokyo, then Osaka, '91; Chairman of Osaka Lawyers' Association '09-11. He has since filled many important posts in business world of Kansai Districts. Add. Tosabori, Nishiku, Osaka. — p. 85 ([scan leaf 134](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n134/mode/1up))

**Kakizaki, Tomisaburo** Lt.-General, Commander of the 11th Division, b. in '61 Fukushima-ken; Sub-Lieut. in '83; Capt. in '93; Lieut.-Col. in '03; Maj.-Gen. in '09; was military attache to Japanese Embassy in Russia '13-15. Add. Zentsuji, Sanuki — p. 85 ([scan leaf 134](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n134/mode/1up))

**Kaku, Sagataro** Chief of Monopoly Bureau, Formosa; b. '74 in Okinawa; grad. from Imp. Tokyo Univ. '99; entered Dep't of Agr. and Comm. and was transferred to Formosa where he has risen to present office. Add. Taipeh, Formosa. — p. 85 ([scan leaf 134](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n134/mode/1up))

**Kamada, Eikichi** Mem. House of Peers, and of the Highest Edu. Council, Pres. of Keiogijuku University and Chairman of Dirs. of the Kojunsha; b. '58 in Kii, from which he was returned once to the House of Representatives, March '94; studied at the Keiogijuku '74–76; Principal of Kagoshima Zoshikan High School '81–83; taught for the third time at the Keiogijuku, to be given a post at the Home Office '86, which however he soon resigned, and was appointed Director of Oita Middle School, but returning once more to his alma mater '89, by which he was sent abroad to inspect educational affairs in Europe and America, to be appointed its head on returning home, is reputed as one of the best after-dinner speakers in Japan. Add. Mita, Shiba, Tokyo. — p. 86 ([scan leaf 135](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n135/mode/1up))

**Kamada, Katsutaro** Mem. House of Peers as highest-tax payer; b. in Kagawa in '82; occupied posts of Director or Chairman of various Cos. and assemblies in his localtties. Add. Sakadecho, Kagawa-ken. — p. 86 ([scan leaf 135](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n135/mode/1up))

**Kamiki, Jisaburo** Director of the Kamiki Partnership, Ltd. established by this family, was b. in '70 in Tokyo. Add. Hashiba Asakusa, Tokyo. — p. 86 ([scan leaf 135](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n135/mode/1up))

**Kamimura, Osuke** Vice-Adm., b. '70 in Tokyo; grad. Naval Academy; Com. '02, promoted to Rear-Adm., and appointed Staff officer to Yokosuka Admiralty in '12, transferred to Com. of 2nd Squadron, Aug. '14, then 4th Squadron; Com. of Maizuru Naval Arsenal '15-16, Vice-Adm. '16. Add. Maizuru Admiralty. — p. 86 ([scan leaf 135](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n135/mode/1up))

**Kamiya, Denbei** well-known wine-brewer, President of Pharmaceutical Co., Dir. of Kyushu Colliery and S.S. Co., Fuji Tanning Co., etc.; b. '55 in Ibaragi; started a provision store and made fortune by selling a cheap sweet wine; possesses a large vineyard near Mito. Add. Hanakawacho, Asakusa, Tokyo. — p. 86 ([scan leaf 135](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n135/mode/1up))

**Kamiyama, Mitsunoshin** Vice-Min. of Agr. and Commerce; b. '70 in Yamaguchi; graduated Law at Imperial Tokyo University, class '95; was Secretary and Judge at the Administrative Litigation Court; Director of Forestry Bureau, Dep't of Agriculture and Commerce before his transfer to the Gov. of Kumamoto '12-13; app. to present post April '14. Add. Enokizaka, Akasaka, Tokyo. — p. 86 ([scan leaf 135](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n135/mode/1up))

**Kanai, En** D.C.L. (Japan), Prof. Imp. Tokyo Univ.; b. '65; in Shizuoka-ken, graduated from Col. of Literature of the Tokyo Imp. University in '85; the next year he went to England and Germany to study law and economics; Professor at his alma mater '90; dispatched to Europe '11. Add. Tennoji-machi, Yanaka, Tokyo. — p. 86 ([scan leaf 135](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n135/mode/1up))

**Kanda, Naibu** 2nd Baron, Emeritus Prof., Tokyo Higher Commercial School and Mem. of House of Peers; b. '57 in Tokyo adopted son of the late Baron Kohei Kanda, a pioneer Dutch scholar. Proceeded in '71 to U.S.A. for study and returned home about '84 after having grad. Amherst Col., Mass., was appointed Prof. in the Tokyo Imp. Univ., 1st High School, and Prof. at the Higher Com. School of Tokyo. Add. Nakano, near Tokyo. — p. 86 ([scan leaf 135](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n135/mode/1up))

**Kaneko, Kentaro** Viscount (Baron '95, Viscount '07), Privy Councillor, hon. LL.D. (Harvard). President of the America's Friends Society; b. '53 at Fukuoka; grad. Harvard University, U.S.A. class '78; private Secretary to the late Prince Ito when he was Premier '85-86, and President of the Privy Council '88-'90; Chief Secretary of the House of Peers '90; Vice-Minister of Agriculture and Commerce '94-96, and finally Minister of Agriculture and Commerce Apr.-June '98 and Minister of Justice Oct. 1900 - May '01. On the outbreak of the Russo-Japanese War he was sent to U.S.A. to represent Japan non-officially, and returned home soon after the restoration of peace. Add. Ichibancho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 86 ([scan leaf 135](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n135/mode/1up))


## Who's Who in Japan

**Kaneko, Naokichi** partner of the Suzuki Firm (importers and exporters of sugar, camphor, etc. in Kobe), member of Kobe Chamber of Commerce; b. '56 and is a prominent figure in business circles in Kwansai. Add. Kumoidori V., Kobe. — p. 87 ([scan leaf 136](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n136/mode/1up))

**Kanematsu, Fusajiro** Head of Kanematsu Firm and Dir. of various Cos., is known as pioneer in Japan-Australia trade, having established his branch office at Sydney in 1888. He was b. in Owari in 1844 and entered an export firm while young; next he became clerk of the Mitsui Bank and then removed to Osaka to serve as Dir. of Rice Exchange and Osaka Shosen. He established himself at Kobe in 1885 and started export and import business. Add. Kobe. — p. 87 ([scan leaf 136](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n136/mode/1up))

**Kanno, Katsunosuke** Dir. of the Finance Bureau, b. in '67 in Gifu-ken; grad. law at the Imperial Tokyo Univ. in '96; clerk in the Finance Dep't in that year and passed the higher civil service examination the following year; secretary of the Dep't of Agr. and Com., to be transferred soon after to a similar post in Treasury; attached to the Embassy in London in '07 and next transferred to Printing Bureau. Add. Fujimicho, Koj., Tokyo. — p. 87 ([scan leaf 136](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n136/mode/1up))

**Kano, Hisanobu** Viscount, Mem. House of Peers; b. '48. Began his career as official of the Edu. Dep't and Dir. of a Normal School; next entered juridical service and held posts of Judge and Public Procurator, lastly Gov. of Kagoshima. Since his retirement from active work, he has been associated with various enterprises intended to promote industry. Add. Omori, near Tokyo. — p. 87 ([scan leaf 136](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n136/mode/1up))

**Kano, Jigoro** President of Tokyo Higher Normal School; b. in Higo, '60; Director and Professor of the Peers' School '83; Director of the Special Education Bureau '98; President of Tokyo Higher Normal School since '01. He is a renowned master of the art of Jujitsu and most of the noted young Jujitsu experts have studied at the Jujitsu Institute founded by the subject of the sketch. Add. Tokyo Higher Normal School. — p. 87 ([scan leaf 136](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n136/mode/1up))

**Kano, Kyokichi** D. Litt. (Japan), b. '66 in Tokyo, grad. Col. Sc. '84 and next Col. Lit. '89; Prof. of 4th High School, etc., Pres. 1st High School, transferred to the Dean of Col. Literature, Imp. Univ., Kyoto, '07 which he resigned. Add. Imp. Univ., Kyoto. — p. 87 ([scan leaf 136](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n136/mode/1up))

**Kan-o, Mitsuomi** Baron (cr. '16), Lieut.-Gen., (on waiting list '16), b. '55 in Nagano-ken; entered Non-Com. Officers' School in '74; Sub-Lieut. '79; Lieut.-Col. '95; attache to Legation at Peking both before and after the China-Japan War, during the war on the staff of late Marshal Prince Oyama; Col. in '97, Maj.-Gen. in '02; in the Russo-Japanese War he led a Division in besieging Port Arthur and in the battle of Mukden; Lieut.-Gen. in '08; Com. of 9th Division till '12, then of 18th Div. Commanded the Expedition against Tsingtao in 1914; Com. of Tokyo Garrison '15-16. Add. Sendagaya, near Tokyo. — p. 87 ([scan leaf 136](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n136/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Kano, Tokusaburo** Dir. of Gov't Monopoly Bureau, b. '75 in Hyogo-ken; grad. Law Coll., Imp. Tokyo Univ., '95; Revenue officer, 1900; Dir. of Revenue Admi'tion Office, Aomori, Matsuyama; Dir. of Customs Office, Nagasaki, Yokohama; present post in '16. Add. Takehayacho, Koishikawa, Tokyo. — p. 88 ([scan leaf 137](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n137/mode/1up))

**Kano, Tomonosuke** Mng.-Dir. of the Tokyo Branch of the Sumitomo Bank; b. '72 in Ibaraki-ken; was for a while Secretary of the House of Representatives and Councillor to the Dep't of Agr. and Com.; entered the Sumitomo Bank '00. Add. Sumitomo Bank, Tokyo. — p. 88 ([scan leaf 137](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n137/mode/1up))

**Kanokogi, Kogoro** Gov. of Wakayama-ken; b. '67 in Tokyo; became Prob. Judge '88, then Chief of Communication Bureau, Formosa, Chief of Industry Bureau of Agr. and Comm. Dep't and Gov. of Kagawa-ken '10; to present post '14. Add. Wakayama. — p. 88 ([scan leaf 137](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n137/mode/1up))

**Kanzaki, Naozo** Prop. and edit. of the Anglo-Japanese Commercial Magazine, now suspended publication, was b. '61 in Saitama and studied for a while as the Imp. Tokyo University. Was a Commissioner of the St. Louis World's Fair and the Anglo-Japanese Exhibition at Shepherd Bush. Add. Oi, near Tokyo. — p. 88 ([scan leaf 137](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n137/mode/1up))

**Kasai, Shinichi** Gov. of Okayama-ken; b. '64 in Shizuoka-ken; grad. law, Tokyo Imp. Univ. '92; appointed Councillor or Yamagata-ken, Chief Police in Iwate-ken Sec. Kochi-ken, etc.; then the present post '13. Add. Okayama. — p. 88 ([scan leaf 137](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n137/mode/1up))

**Kashima, Fusajiro** Mayor of Kobe, b. in '68 in Hiroshima-ken and adopted into the family of Machi Kashima in '97; was formerly Deputy Mayor of Kobe. Add. Motomachi, Kobe. — p. 88 ([scan leaf 137](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n137/mode/1up))

**Kasugai, Joemon** dry goods merchant, Pres. of Nagoya Bank and of Nagoya Sav. Bank, Dir. of Gifu Silk Weaving Co., Imperial Twisted Thread W. Co., etc.; b. '44 in Nagoya; Mem. of Nagoya Municipal Assembly '88; Mem. of the Nagoya Chamber of Commerce '91-96. Add. Tamayacho, Nagoya. — p. 88 ([scan leaf 137](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n137/mode/1up))

**Kataoka, Naoharu** M.P., Pres. of Nippon Life Ins. Co.; b. '59 in Kochi-ken; grad. at the Toya Gakko School; entered civil service as a district administrative official; afterwards entered Nippon Life Ins. Co. He was a delegate to the 5th International Actuaries Conference in '06 and elected its Vice-Pres.; is a leading business man in western Japan, and a leader of the Kensei-kai. Add. Kawaracho, Kamikyoku, Kyoto. — p. 88 ([scan leaf 137](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n137/mode/1up))

**Kataoka, Naoteru** Pres. of Osaka Gas Co., Hankai Elec. Railway, Sakaye Gas Co.; b. '56 in Kochi-ken; grad. from Naval Paymasters' School '78; began his career as naval paymaster in '78; then transferred to civil service in '92; appointed Private Sec. to Home Minister and then Edu. Minister; entered the Bank of Japan and appointed Dir. of its Osaka Branch in '96, etc. Add. Tanimachi, Higashiku, Osaka. — p. 88 ([scan leaf 137](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n137/mode/1up))

**Kataoka, Shichiro** Baron, Adm., Mili. Sup. Councillor; b. '53, son of retainer of the Lord of Satsuma; Midshipman '77; held posts of Com- mander of various cruisers, chief Staff Officer to a Standing Squadron, etc. Commanded the 3rd Squadron in the 1904-5 war; Chief of Naval Construction Board in '05; Commander of Maizuru Adm. '10; present post '11 ; holder of 1st Golden Kite. Add. Onden, near Tokyo. — p. 88 ([scan leaf 137](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n137/mode/1up))


## Who's Who

**Katayama, Horin** Act.-Chief Court Physician ; b. '53 in Nagano-ken ; grad. Medicine, Imp. Tokyo Univ. '81 ; Assist.-Prof. of the University '83 ; appointed Court Physician, 1888. Add. Yagembori, Tokyo. — p. 89 ([scan leaf 138](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n138/mode/1up))

**Katayama, Kuniyoshi** D.M., Prof. Imp. Tokyo Univ.; b. '55 in Shizuoka-ken ; studied medical jurisprudence in Austria and Germany '84-88, and has been Prof. at the Col. of Med. of Imp. Tokyo Univ. since that time. He is at present vice-chairman of the Dojin-kai, a medical association for befriending China and Korea. Add. Nishikata-machi, Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 89 ([scan leaf 138](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n138/mode/1up))

**Katayama, Toguma** D. Eng., Court Councillor ; b. '53 in Yamaguchi-ken ; finished in '79 Architecture at the now defunct Engineering College ; commissioned to build the late Prince Arisugawa's residence '81 ; built the Japanese Legation at Peking '86. Has undertaken the construction of the Crown Prince's Palace which was completed after about ten years' work ; long held the post of Chief, Architectural Bureau, Imp. Household, till his appointment to present office in '16. He has frequently gone abroad to study his speciality. Add. Onden, near Tokyo. — p. 89 ([scan leaf 138](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n138/mode/1up))

**Kato, Masayoshi** Member of Tokyo Chamber of Commerce ; b. in '54 ; was for some while a clerk at Local Offices ; entered '85 the newly formed (by amalgamation of the Mitsubishi and the Kyodo Un-yu) Nippon Yusen Kaisha with the late Mr. Morioka, its first President ; was elected its Director '89, and Managing Director '93, then the Vice-President which he resigned in Nov., '15. Was Pres. of the Nisshin S.S. Co. organized by Japanese capitalists, and travelled abroad '06-07 to inspect shipping business. Add. Motozonocho, Koj., Tokyo. — p. 89 ([scan leaf 138](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n138/mode/1up))

**Kato, Teikichi** Baron (cr. '16), Vice-Admiral Com. of Kure Adm'ty ; b. '61 in Tokyo-fu; naval 2nd Sub-Lieut. in '86 ; Captain '02 and Rear-Adm. '07 ; was at one time Chief of Yokohama Naval Arsenal ; Com. of 2nd Squadron in '13 ; Com.-in-Chief of 2nd Fleet that blockaded Tsingtao in the war of 1914 for which he was awarded with 2nd class Golden Kite ; Chief of Naval Education Board in Feb. '15 ; present post Dec. '16. Add. Admiralty, Kure. — p. 89 ([scan leaf 138](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n138/mode/1up))

**Kato, Taka-akira** Visc. (cr. '16 ; Baron, '11), Mem. House of Peers, ex-Minister of Foreign Affairs ; b. '59 at Nagoya ; grad. Law, the Imp. Tokyo Univ., class '81. After a year or two in the Mitsubishi, he entered the Foreign Office '87 and appointed Personal Secretary to the Foreign Minister (then Count Okuma) '88 ; on the resignation of his chief, he was transferred to the Finance Office to be promoted in time to be the Director of the Banking Bureau, that of the Taxation Bureau, etc. Soon he returned to the Foreign Office and from '94 to '99 he represented Japan in England ; Minister of Foreign Affairs in 1900-01 ; sat twice in the Lower House, once for Kochi-ken elected Aug. '02, unsolicited and next in March '03 from Yokohama. Entered the Saionji Cabinet as For. Minister, but to resign, being opposed to the Railway Nationalization. Ambassador at London and then For. Min. Dec. '12-Feb. '13. Was decorated with G.C.M.G. by the late King Edward. He leads the Kenseikai formed by late Prince Katsura; again entered Okuma Cabinet in '14-15; nominated mem. House of Peers in '15. Add. Shimo-Nibancho, Koj., Tokyo. — p. 89 ([scan leaf 138](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n138/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Kato, Terumaro** 2nd Baron, D.M., Court Physician, being the eldest son of Baron H. Kato; b. '63; studied medicine at Tokyo Imp. Univ., and further at Berlin and Vienna, his specialty being children's diseases. Add. Shimonibancho, Tokyo. — p. 90 ([scan leaf 139](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n139/mode/1up))

**Kato, Tomosaburo** Adm., Minister of the Navy; b. 59 in Hiroshima; midshipman '83; Captain '99; Prof. at the Naval Academy; Construction-Supervisor and Sectional Chief at the Navy before he was appointed '02 to be Chief of Staff office of a Standing Squadron; chief of staff of the Kamimura Squadron in the 1904-5 war; transferred to a Bureau Chief, appointed Vice-Minister in '06; Vice-Adm. in '08; Com'der of Kure Admiralty '09-14; appointed Com.-in-Chief of 1st Fleet on the declaration of war against Germany in '14. Add. Navy Office. — p. 90 ([scan leaf 139](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n139/mode/1up))

**Kato, Tsunetaro** Mem. of House of Peers, ex-Minister at Brussels; b. '59 at Matsuyama; was attache at Paris, '86; Secretary, For. Office '91 and was again transferred as Secretary to the Legation at Paris, '02 97. Recalled home to be appointed Chief of Personal Affairs; promoted to be Minister Resident 3 years later, and to full Minister-Plenipotentiary accredited to Belgium '02. Resigned the post in '07 and became editor of the Osaka Shimpo. Add. Osaka Shimpo Office, Osaka. — p. 90 ([scan leaf 139](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n139/mode/1up))

**Katsura, Jiro** Dir. of various Cos.; b. '56 in Yamaguchi, brother of the late Prince Katsura; studied wine brewing in Germany and France and started brewing business at Sapporo and in Yamanashi, but it has failed; has subsequently turned into a business-man. Add. Sakuragawa, Nishi-kubo, Tokyo. — p. 90 ([scan leaf 139](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n139/mode/1up))

**Kawada, Ryokichi** Baron, b. '56, eldest son of the late Baron Kawada, Gov. of the Nippon Ginko and founder of the house; he succeeded to the title on the death of his father in '96. The present Baron having a great interest in mechanical science chose shipbuilding as his profession. Add. Shin-Ogawacho, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 90 ([scan leaf 139](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n139/mode/1up))

**Kawada, Takashi** Pres. of Keio Electric R'ly Co., &c. b. '70 eldest son of Takeshi Kawada (learned scholar of Chinese classics); and studied at Michigan in '96. Add. Wakamiya-cho, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 90 ([scan leaf 139](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n139/mode/1up))

**Kawaguchi, Torao** Dir. and Prof. of Kumamoto Higher Technical School; b. '71 in Fukuoka-ken; grad. Civil. Eng., Tokyo Imp. Univ. '95; sent to U.S.A. and France for study '05; Civil Engineer of the Kumamoto-ken '96; Prof. of the 5th High School '00. Add. Kumamoto Higher Tech. School. — p. 90 ([scan leaf 139](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n139/mode/1up))

**Kawai, Misao** Lieut.-Gen., Dir. of Mil. Staff College; b. '64; Sub-Lieut. of Infantry '86; grad. from the Staff College '97; Maj.-Gen. 1900; Dir. of Personnel Bureau, War Office, in '12; present post and grade in '15. Add. Naitomachi, Yotsuya, Tokyo. — p. 90 ([scan leaf 139](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n139/mode/1up))

**Kawai, Shintaro** Dr. Dendrology, Prof. at Agr. Col. of the Imp. Tokyo University; b. '65 in Aichi-ken; grad. Ag. Col. in '92; Assis.-Prof. at the College '94; was honored with the doctorate in '99; sent to Germany and Austria to prosecute his study for about three years, returning home '03 and made full Prof. at the same College. Add. Harajuku, Tokyo. — p. 90 ([scan leaf 139](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n139/mode/1up))


## Who's Who

**Kawakami, Toshihiko** Mng. of the South Manchuria Rly. since 1914; b. '61 in Tokyo; grated in Russian language from the Tokyo Foreign Language School in '84; clerk at Fusan, Vladivostok, San Francisco and Petrograd in '86-92; Interpreter at last place in '96; Commercial Agent at Vladivostok in '00, Legation Sec. in '04; attached to Commanding Headquarters in Liaotung in '04; Commercial Agent in Vladivostok in '06; Consul-Gen. at Harbin '07-14, was injured by stray shot when late Prince Ito was assassinated in '09. Add. South Manchuria Rly., Dairen. — p. 91 ([scan leaf 140](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n140/mode/1up))

**Kawamura, Jizaburo** ex-Vice-Minister of Justice, Lord-in-Waiting of the Kinkei Hall and mem. House of Peers; b. '59 in Shigaken; grad. the then existing Law Institute of Justice Dep't '84; sent abroad for further study '86-90; appointed Councillor of Justice; Judge at some District Courts and at Tokyo App. Court, a Public Pro. at Supreme Court, Dir. of the Civ. and Cri. Bureau, appointed Vice-Min. of Justice in Jan. '06 and held the post till '11. Add. Minami-cho, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 91 ([scan leaf 140](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n140/mode/1up))

**Kawamura, Kageaki** Visc. (cr. '07), Marshal, holder of 1st class Golden Kite; b. '59 in Satsuma; commanded a Brigade in the Japan-China War; and afterward appointed com. of First Division; he led the Himeji Division in Russo-Japanese War, and then commanded an Army which formed the extreme right in the Battle of Mukden. Appointed Mem. Supreme Military Council Dec. '05; nominated Marshal in Jan., 1915. Add. Aoyama, Tokyo. — p. 91 ([scan leaf 140](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n140/mode/1up))

**Kawamura, Kingoro** ex-Vice-Min. of Imp. Household; b. in '63 in Okayama-ken; grad. with honor from Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ. in '87; secretary at local and central offices and Privy Council till about '08, Chief Sec. in '08 and Vice-Min. in '10-15. Add. Aoyama Minami-cho, Tokyo. — p. 91 ([scan leaf 140](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n140/mode/1up))

**Kawamura, Zen-eki** Chief Proc. of Tokyo Court of Appeal; b. '58 in Ishikawa; grad. law at the defunct Gov. Law School '84; appointed Proc. '86; Judge '90; Dir. of the Fukui Local Court; Sectional Chief of the Osaka Court of Appeal; Judge of the Court of Cassation; Dir. of the Osaka Local Court; sent to Europe '99; Dir. of Hakodate Court of Appeal '06; Proc. of the Court of Cassation; present post '07. Add. Nishi-Okubo, Tokyo. — p. 91 ([scan leaf 140](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n140/mode/1up))

**Kawanishi, Seibei** Pres. of Kobe Chamber of Commerce, Pres. of Japan Woolen Cloth, Hyogo Elec. Tramway, San-yo Leather Cos., Dir. of Un-yu, Kobe Elec. Light, Nippon Shogyo Bank, etc.; b. '65 in Osaka. Add. Kawasaki, Kobe. — p. 91 ([scan leaf 140](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n140/mode/1up))

**Kawasaki, Hachi-emon** Pres. of Kawasaki Bank, and other concerns b. '66 in Tokyo; grad. Tokyo Higher Commercial School and further studied in America. Assumed his father's name Hachiro-emon in '13. Add. Toriizaka, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 91 ([scan leaf 140](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n140/mode/1up))

**Kawasaki, Hajime** President of the Nippon Fire Ins. Co. since '13, and of the Nikka Life Ins. Co., and Dir. of the Kawasaki Sav. Bank; b. '84 in Tokyo; entered the Tokyo Higher Commercial School in '93, and next the Penn. Univ. for Economics, class '09. Add. Daimachi, Akasaka, Tokyo. — p. 91 ([scan leaf 140](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n140/mode/1up))

**Kawasaki, Hiromi** Baron (suc. '06), Prop. of Kawasaki Works, Kobe, b. '63 in Kagoshima; studied at 1st-High School and entered Foreign Office '81; attached at Legations at London, Peking, attended the Coro- nation of the Czar '96; retired from Gov. service and entered Bank of Japan '97; Bank of Formosa '99; etc. Add. Tamachi, Shiba, Tokyo. — p. 91 ([scan leaf 140](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n140/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Kawashima, Chūnosuke** Director of the Yokohama Specie Bank; b. '55 in Tokyo; studied French and naval architecture at the Gov. Dockyard, Yokosuka; was engaged as interp. and clerk '71 by a Dutch firm, Yokohama; appointed agent at Lyons by the Yokohama Specie Bank '79-93; removed to the Head Office '94; Agent at Bombay till '95; Agent in Tokyo, '02-06; then recalled to the Head Office. Add. Fukuyama-cho, Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 92 ([scan leaf 141](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n141/mode/1up))

**Kawashima, Reijiro** Vice-Adm., mem. of Admirals' Council; b. '64 in Ishikawa-ken; Sub-Lieut. 2nd class '88; Captain in '01 and Rear-Adm. in '07. Com. of 3rd Squadron, '11; sent to China as Com. of our detachment during the Chinese revolution '11 and then to the post of Hydrographic Dept. in '12; decorated with 3rd class of Golden Kite for his service in Russo-Japanese War. Com. of Port Arthur Naval Station; then present post '15. Add. Takanawa, Shiba, Tokyo. — p. 92 ([scan leaf 141](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n141/mode/1up))

**Kayetsu, Takako** lady educationist, Superintendent of Nippon Girls' Commercial School; b. '69 in Kumamoto-ken; studied at a private girl's school and has ever since been engaged in education, formerly in her native clan; established present school '03 in Tokyo. Add. Doto Sambancho, Koj., Tokyo. — p. 92 ([scan leaf 141](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n141/mode/1up))

**Kazan-in, Chikaiye** Marquis, of a former courtier family which was founded in the 12th century. The present head b. in '78, 7th son of Vis. Horikawa and adopted into the family. Add. Okubo, near Tokyo. — p. 92 ([scan leaf 141](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n141/mode/1up))

**Keto, Katsumoto** D.C.L., lawyer in Osaka, b. '74 in Hyogo-ken; grad. from Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ. '98; appointed Ass.-Prof. at Kyoto 1900; studied in England, France, Germany; Prof. of Law College, Imp. Kyoto Univ., '05, Dean '14; left it in spring of '16 to practise law in Osaka. Add. Mikage, near Kobe. — p. 92 ([scan leaf 141](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n141/mode/1up))

**Kido, Takamasa** Marquis, ex-Chief Chamberlain to the Crown Prince; b. '56, adopted son of the late Kido Koin, one of the great personage in the work of the Restoration and created Peer for the service. The present head studied mining in U.S.A. '70-74, afterwards at the Tokyo Univ. and also Osaka Technical Institute. Add. Shinsaka, Akasaka, Tokyo. — p. 92 ([scan leaf 141](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n141/mode/1up))

**Kikoshi, Yasutsuna** Baron (cr. '07), Lieut.-Gen. (ret.), ex-Min. of War (Dec. '12 Jun. '13); 2nd Order of Golden Kite and Grand Cordon of Rising Sun (war honor); b. '54 at Kanazawa; entered the Military Academy '75; Ensign '77; sent abroad for study in Germany '82; Major-Gen. '98 and Com. of 23rd Brigade '01. Took part in the Japan-China War, which procured him 4th Order Golden Kite, and also in the expedition occasioned by the Boxer trouble; fought under Gen. Oku at the head of his brigade in Russo-Japanese War and succeeded his Chief on the latter's removal to a higher post. Attended Jamestown Mil. and Nav. Exhibition. Add. Kagacho, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 92 ([scan leaf 141](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n141/mode/1up))

**Kikuchi, Choshiro** Pres. Tokai Bank, b. '53; prop. of a big dry-goods store in the business quarter of Tokyo; is a Director of various companies, once sat in the House of Peers as highest tax payer representative for Tokyo Prefecture. Add. Takecho, Shitaya, Tokyo. — p. 92 ([scan leaf 141](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n141/mode/1up))

**Kikuchi, Dairoku** Baron (cr. '02), M.A. (Cambridge), D. Sc. (Japan), Privy Councillor ('12), Emeritus Prof. Imp. Tokyo Univ. and ex-Pres- ident of the Imperial Kyoto University '08–12, Pres. of Teikoku Gakushi-in (Imperial Academy); b. '55 in Okayama-ken; was one of those sent '66 by the Tokugawa Gov. to England for study, to return home with its downfall, but again sent '70 by the Imp. Gov.; graduated mathematics, at Cambridge '77 as one of the wranglers; was next Prof. of mathematics, then President of the Imp. Univ. and lastly Minister of Education June 1901—July '03; President of the Peers' School in '04–05. Delivered lectures on Japanese education at the University of London, 1909 and at Civic Forum, U.S.A., '10. Add. Takehayacho, Tokyo. — p. 92 ([scan leaf 141](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n141/mode/1up))


## Who's Who

**Kikuchi, Ganji** lawyer, ex-M.P.; b. '50 at Kanazawa; called to bar at Osaka; was elected Mem. of Osaka Municipal Assembly, Osaka Prefectural Assembly; once Governor of Osaka. Add. Kitahama, Osaka. — p. 93 ([scan leaf 142](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n142/mode/1up))

**Kikuchi, Takenori** M.P., b. in Aomori '67; edu. at Keio Gijuku, was journalist connected with the Jiji '90–95; General Affairs Chief of the Kyushu R'ly '95–02; M.P. for Moji '03, and then for Aomori, at present for Hirosaki City. Add. Minami Odawara-cho, Kyobashi, Tokyo. — p. 93 ([scan leaf 142](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n142/mode/1up))

**Kikuchi, Tsunesaburo** Surg.-Gen. (ret.), M.D., Dir. of Kwaisei Hospital at Nishinomiya, near Osaka; b. '55 in Sagaken; grad. at Medicine, Imp. Univ. Tokyo, '81, and entered Army as surgeon, and attained the highest post of Surg.-Gen. in '07. Took part in the Japan-China and Russia-Japan Wars as Chief Surg. Add. Nishinomiya, Hyogo-ken. — p. 93 ([scan leaf 142](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n142/mode/1up))

**Kikutei, Kocho** Marquis, head of the former house of a courtier; b. '89 in Hokkaido and studied at Peers' Sch. Add. Okubo, Tokyo. — p. 93 ([scan leaf 142](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n142/mode/1up))

**Kimbara, Meizen** a millionaire and public benefactor; b. in Enshu '32; has undertaken several works of public utility, for which he has repeatedly been rewarded by the Government. Early in '08 he retired to his native village and became its headman; owns a bank, stationer and toilet oil shops. Add. Wada, Hamana-gun, Shizuoka. — p. 93 ([scan leaf 142](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n142/mode/1up))

**Kimotsuki, Kenko** Baron (cr. '07), Vice-Adm. (ret.), Mem. of House of Peers, Mg. Imp. Lifeboat Society; b. '53 in Kagoshima; Sub-Lieut. '72; held posts of Hydrographical Section, Pres. of Naval College, etc.; was Mayor of Osaka '12-13. Created Baron for the service in Russo-Japanese War. Add. Shimo Nihancho, Tokyo. — p. 93 ([scan leaf 142](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n142/mode/1up))

**Kimura, Kusuyata** Mng. of General Affairs Dep't, Mitsubishi Firm, was b. in '65 in Kochi, and after graduating from the Higher Commercial School in '82 he entered the Mitsubishi where he was steadily promoted till he was appointed Chief of its Kobe Branch in 1900 and then to Chief of General Affairs and in 1915 its Director. Add. Benten-cho, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 93 ([scan leaf 142](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n142/mode/1up))

**Kimura, Riemon** b. '34, is one of the millionaires of Yokohama, who, by foresight and shrewdness, rose from obscurity to the present opulence by selling raw silk to foreigners early in Meiji era; is connected with the Yokohama Electric Light Co., the Specie Bank and other important establishments; once sat in the House of Peers as high-tax paying member for Kanagawa-ken. Add. Benten-dori, Yokohama. — p. 93 ([scan leaf 142](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n142/mode/1up))

**Kimura, Sakaye** D. Sc. (Japan), M.R.A.S. (Eng.), Dir. of the Gov. Geodetic Observatory at Mizusawa, since '00; b. '68 in Kanazawa; grad. Astronomy, Imp. Univ., Tokyo, '92; attended the World's Geodetic Conference '98, also next conference held in '06, when he read an essay pointing out error in the formula relating to the axis, and his discovery has been acknowledged as Kimura term by the world's astronomers; received first prize by the Imperial Academy, Japan, in '11. Add. Mizusawa, Iwate-ken. — p. 93 ([scan leaf 142](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n142/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Kimura, Seishiro** Department Dir. of the Bank of Japan, b. '61 in Okayama Prefecture. Had once been an editor of the "Chugai Shogyo," next he entered the present bank in '03, where he began to distinguish himself and was appointed its director soon afterwards. Add. Minami Sakuma-cho, Shiba, Tokyo. — p. 94 ([scan leaf 143](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n143/mode/1up))

**Kimura, Shunkichi** ex-Naval Eng. and wireless expert, b. '66; grad. course of physics at the Imp. Univ., Tokyo, '89; instructor at Tokyo and Sendai High Schools '93-96, studied at Harvard and Yale specially in the line of quaternions and its physical applications; founder with Prof. J. Molenbrack in Holland, of the International Association for promoting the study of quaternions and allied subjects. Prof. at the Naval Academy '00-13 and member of the Wireless Telegraph Committee; dispatched to America and Europe '01 to investigate the wireless telegraph service. He elaborated a new process of wireless telegraphy and completed it early '06, his system having been used exclusively during the Russo-Japanese War by the Imperial Navy. Add. Nakano, near Tokyo. — p. 94 ([scan leaf 143](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n143/mode/1up))

**Kinkozan, Sobei** celebrated potter of Awada, Kyoto, and 7th of the line; b. '68, 2nd son of Kinkozan VI. by whom the export of Awada ware was started. The business at one time suffered decline, but by indefatigable effort and untiring perseverance the present head has restored and further carried the business to great prosperity, his exhibits having won high-grade medals at various exhibitions both at home and abroad. Add. Awada, Kyoto. — p. 94 ([scan leaf 143](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n143/mode/1up))

**Kinoshita, Kenjiro** M.P. for Oita-ken, and Parl.-Secy. to Communication Dept.; b. '69 in Oita-ken; grad. Chuo Univ. '92; first elected M.P. in '03; Parliamentary-Secy. in '15. Add. Tansumachi, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 94 ([scan leaf 143](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n143/mode/1up))

**Kinoshita, Yoshio** Dir. of Traffic Bureau, Imp. Govt. Rlys.; b. '74 in Tamba; grad. Civil Engineering Course in Tokyo Imp. Univ., '98; entered Traffic Dept., Imp. Govt. Rlys.; studied practical and theoretical side of railway traffic in Europe and America '05-07. Add. Imp. Govt. Rly., Tokyo. — p. 94 ([scan leaf 143](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n143/mode/1up))

**Kirishima, Soichi** Mng. of Real Estate Dept., Mitsubishi Firm, was b. '64 in Kochi, and grad. from Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ., '90 and then entered the Firm. Toured abroad in '13. Add. Haramachi, Koishikawa, Tokyo. — p. 94 ([scan leaf 143](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n143/mode/1up))

**Kishi, Seiichi** D.C.L. (Japan), barrister, Dir. of the Osaka Gas Co., legal adviser to several Gov. and private institutions; b. '67 in Matsuye; grad. Law at Imp. University of Tokyo in '87 and began practice in Tokyo and soon grew to be one of the foremost lawyers. Add. Isarago, Shiba, Tokyo. — p. 94 ([scan leaf 143](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n143/mode/1up))

**Kishigami, Kenkichi** zoologist, D. Sc.; Prof. Imp. Tokyo Univ. and expert to the Fishery Bureau, Dept. of Agr. and Com.; b. '67 in Aichi-ken; grad. Science Col. of Imp. University of Tokyo in '94; appointed technical expert of the said Dep't '95; sent to Europe and America in '97. Add. Ichibancho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 94 ([scan leaf 143](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n143/mode/1up))

**Kitagawa, Reisuke** Mng.-Dir. of Chiyoda Mutual Life Ins. Co., Dir. of the Tamagawa Electric Rly. Co.; b. '61 in Fukui-ken; grad. from Keio Univ. in '81 and was once editor of the Jiji. Add. Mita, Shiba, Tokyo. — p. 94 ([scan leaf 143](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n143/mode/1up))


## Who's Who

**Kitamura, Kyotaro** Mng.-Dir. of Osaka Cement Co., Ltd. ; b. '63 in Aichi-ken ; grad. from Engineering College, Imp. Tokyo Univ. '89 ; Civil Eng. of Onoda Cement Mfg. Co., Ltd. ; then of the Osaka Cement Co., Ltd. '96 ; sent to Germany '97-98 ; present post '99. Add. Kita-Momodanicho, Minamiku, Osaka. — p. 95 ([scan leaf 144](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n144/mode/1up))

**Kitamura, Shigemusa** Prop. of the Seiyoken Hotels (at Ueno Park and Tsukiji) ; b. '72 ; grad. from the Meiji Gakuin in '93 and further studied at a private law school. The Seiyoken at Tsukiji, the first foreign restaurant in Tokyo, was established in '71 by his grandfather with the support of the late Prince Sanjo and some others. The Ueno Seiyoken was established in '73. Add. Unemecho, Kyobashi, Tokyo. — p. 95 ([scan leaf 144](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n144/mode/1up))

**Kitazato, Shibasaburo** M.D., President of Kitazato Pathological Laboratory and the Saisei-kai Hospital in Tokyo ; b. '59 at Kumamoto ; studied in Germany under Prof. Koch '85-91 ; discovered the germs of tetanus, diphtheria, pest (in conjunction with Dr. Aoyama), etc., and is regarded as one of the foremost bacteriologists of the world ; had charge of the Gov. Blood-serum Laboratory ; was granted the Red Eagle by the Kaiser in '09 in appreciation of his distinguished scientific achievement ; retired from Gov't service to open his own in '14. Add. Nakanocho, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 95 ([scan leaf 144](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n144/mode/1up))

**Ki-uchi, Jushiro** D.C.L., Crown Mem. House of Peers (nom. '11), ex-Director of Agr. and Ind., Korea ; b. '65 in Chiba-ken ; grad. Law, Imp. Univ., Tokyo, '88, and further studied at the Univ. Hall. Subsequently held posts at Legislative Bureau, House of Peers and finally appointed Dir. Commercial Bureau, Dep't of Agri. & Com., but owing to the opposition of businessmen against reorganizing the Stock Exchanges along the line of Bourse carried out by him, he had to resign in '03 ; was a Bureau Dir., Korean Residency-Gen. '06-11. Add. Yobancho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 95 ([scan leaf 144](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n144/mode/1up))

**Kiyosu, Iyenori** Court Councillor and Mem. of the House of Peers, Dir. of the Japan Red Cross Society, born '62 in Kyoto and is 12th son of the late Prince Kuniiye Fushimi. In '88, he was ordered to create the Kiyosu family and was appointed Lord Abbot of Bukkoji in Kyoto '73, but afterward he turned a layman ; Mem of the House of Peers '90 ; Governor of Yamanashi '97, that of Ibaraki '98, and afterwards of Niigata-ken. Add. Shimo-Roku-bancho, Koji., Tokyo. — p. 95 ([scan leaf 144](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n144/mode/1up))

**Kiyoura, Keigo** Visc., Privy Councillor ; b. '50 at Kumamoto ; was at one time a schoolmaster ; then a humble clerk in the central Gov. where his promotion was quite rapid, chiefly in the Justice and Home Offices. In the former he rose to the highest post and twice held the chair of Ministry '98-00 and '01-03 ; was transferred to that of Agri. and Com. in July '03 which he resigned '06. He is one of the trusted lieutenants of Marshal Prince Yamagata ; created Baron in '02 in connection with the conclusion of the Anglo-Japanese treaty and appointed to the present post in '07. Add. Iriarai, Omori, nr. Tokyo. — p. 95 ([scan leaf 144](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n144/mode/1up))

**Koba, Sadanaga** D.C.L. (Japan), Crown Member of the House of Peers ; b. in '59 ; studied in Germany and Austria '82-85 ; Private Secretary to the Minister of Education, &c., '85-92 ; afterwards the Director of the Ordinary Education Bureau and Chief Councillor of Education ; was made Vice-Minister of Education 03, which he resigned '05. Add. Harajuku, Aoyama, Tokyo. — p. 95 ([scan leaf 144](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n144/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Kobashi, Ichita** Dir. of Eng. Bureau, Home Dep't; b. '70 in Kumamoto; grad. law, Imp. Tokyo Univ. '98; Councillor of Yamaguchi-ken, then of Nagasaki-ken; entered Home Dep't as Secy., then became Councillor, sent abroad, promoted to present post '10. Add. Kami-Osaki, Ebaragun, Tokyo. — p. 96 ([scan leaf 145](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n145/mode/1up))

**Kobayashi, Beika** —See under “de Becker.” — p. 96 ([scan leaf 145](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n145/mode/1up))

**Kobayashi, Genzo** M.P., ex-Dir. of the Imperial Railways; b. in 1867 in Yonezawa, and adopted in 1894 by Y. Kobayashi of the same place; grad. from Law Coll., Imp. Tokyo University in '96, appointed a Secretary of the Imperial Railways the following year and continued in the service till '12. He was one of those civilians who were on board the transport Hitachi Maru and taken prisoner by the Rosia in the Russia-Japan War. Add. Reinanzaka, Akasaka, Tokyo. — p. 96 ([scan leaf 145](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n145/mode/1up))

**Kobayashi, Koai** Dir. of Manchuria-Chosen Salt Mfg. Co.; b. in '45 in Tokyo, and served for a long while at the Treasury in which he rose to the position of a Bureau Director, which he resigned '91. He has been on the Board Directors of various companies. Add. Yayoicho, Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 96 ([scan leaf 145](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n145/mode/1up))

**Kobayashi, Tomijiro** manufacturer of the “Lion” tooth powder and other toilets; b. '74 in Niigata-ken; assumed father's name and succeeded to his business; is also a zealous Christian, working in the cause of Temperance Society. Add. Yanagiwaragashi, Kanda, Tokyo. — p. 96 ([scan leaf 145](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n145/mode/1up))

**Kobayashi, Ushisaburo** D.C.L. (Japan), an economist and financier, Dir. of the Japan Steel Foundry, Prof. at the Meiji Univ., etc.; b. 1865 in Gumma; grad. from Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ., '94; Secretary at the Legislative Bureau '96-07; Chief of the Financial Bureau, Formosa, '07-10; has written a number of works on finance and economy. Add. Akebonocho, Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 96 ([scan leaf 145](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n145/mode/1up))

**Koda, Nariyuki** D. Litt. (Japan), one of master novelists, nom de plume “Roban,” a George Meredith of Japan; b. '67 of an artist family. Among his famous works are “Gojunto,” “Futsuka Monogatari,” etc. His elder brother, Lieut. Gunji (ret.) undertook the colonization of northerly islands of Kuriles in '94, and was taken prisoner at Kamchatka during the Russo-Japanese war, but released in '05; his younger brother, Narimoto, is a grad. of the Lit. Col., Imp. Tokyo Univ. Two sisters Miss Nobu, and Mrs. Ando (her younger sister) were b. at Tokyo, the elder in '70 and the younger '75; both showed a great talent for music for childhood; sent abroad by the Gov. to study music, Miss Nobu, to study in U.S.A., Germany and Austria '89-95; then Prof. of the Gov. Tokyo Music Academy. Both have resigned. “Roban” was Lecturer at Col. Lit., Imp. Kyoto Univ., '08-10. Add. Mukojima, Tokyo. — p. 96 ([scan leaf 145](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n145/mode/1up))

**Kodama, Hideo** 2nd Count, Chief Secretary of Cabinet; b. '76 in Tokyo; son of the late celebrated General Kodama, chief of staff in the Russo-Japanese War; grad. Law, Imp. Tokyo University in 1900 and entered in the service of Korean Govt.-Gen., in which he rose to Chief of Gen. Affairs '12; transferred to Home Govt. '16. Add. Nagatacho, Tokyo. — p. 96 ([scan leaf 145](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n145/mode/1up))

**Kodera, Kenkichi** Kobe millionaire, M.P.; b. '77 in Kobe; edu. at Kobe Com. School, Columbia, Johns Hopkins and further in Germany, etc.; went through one-year volunteer service and was sent to the front in the war with Russia 1904-05. Has founded and maintains at his expense a middle school at Sanda, Hyogo-ken. Add. Aoyama Minamicho, Akasaka, Tokyo. — p. 96 ([scan leaf 145](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n145/mode/1up))


## Who's Who in Japan

**Kodera, Shinsaku** b. '55 at Himeji ; grad. Literature, Imp. Tokyo Univ. '80; studied in England and Germany '81-85, Prof. at the Tokyo Higher Normal School till '91, then removed to the Foreign Office; was at the Legation, London '94-95, next Petrograd, Peking, etc. Add. Sugamo-Myogizaka, Tokyo. — p. 97 ([scan leaf 146](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n146/mode/1up))

**Koezuka, Ryu** M.P., ex-Vice-Pres. of the House of Representatives; b. '51 in Hyogo-ken, studied under late Kei-u Nakamura and early took to journalism and political life in Tokyo, Dir. of Mining Bureau when the Kenseito Cabinet was formed in '98, then Governor of Tokyo which he resigned with fall of the Cabinet. Add. Hitotsugi, Akasaka, Tokyo. — p. 97 ([scan leaf 146](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n146/mode/1up))

**Koga, Renzo** D.C.L., Crown Member of the House of Peers; b. '58 in Saga; joined Eto's rebellion raised '74; was captured but acquitted with reprimand. After fighting against adversity in Tokyo '74-80 he succeeded in entering the defunct Law Col., Justice Dep't and graduated from it in '84. He was Public Procurator at various courts, ultimately sat on the bench of Supreme Court '98 and was transferred to Dir. Police Bureau of Home Office in '06, to resign it '08 with the fall of the Seiyukai Cabinet, and again to the same post in '11. Add. Yamabushicho, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 97 ([scan leaf 146](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n146/mode/1up))

**Koganei, Seiryo** M.D. and Prof., Med. Col., Imp. Tokyo Univ., his speciality being diseases of the brains. He was born in '58 at Nagaoka; entered the Imp. Tokyo Univ. '70; studied at Berlin '81 85 and has been connected with the University ever since. Add. Akebonocho, Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 97 ([scan leaf 146](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n146/mode/1up))

**Koike, Chozo** General-Manager of Sumitomo Firm, Osaka, b. '73 at Fukushima; passed the diplomatic and consular service examination in '96; attaché at Seoul in the same year, transferred to London in '97; personal Sec. to Foreign Minister in '00; Legation Sec. at Peking in '01, transferred to London in '02, Consul-General at New York, at San Francisco, at Mukden '08 and Councillor to Embassy at London 1911-13; Dir. of Political Bureau, Foreign Office, 1913-16. In '16 retired from official service and entered into business. Add. Sumitomo, Osaka. — p. 97 ([scan leaf 146](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n146/mode/1up))

**Koike, Kuniso** broker of the Tokyo Stock Exchange, Member of Tokyo Chamber of Commerce; b. in Yamanashi-ken in 1866. Began his career under the late Mr. Wakao, millionaire merchant of Yamanashi and then engaged in the business of banking, silk export, and gold-mining. In 1898, just after the Sino-Japanese war, opened business on his own account as a stock broker in Tokyo and achieved success, converting it in 1907 into semi-partnership firm under the name of Koike & Company. He was one of the Japanese business men, who, invited by the United States Chamber of Commerce of the Pacific Coast, undertook a tour in the States in 1909. Add. Office, 2, Kabutocho Tokyo; residence, Nakanocho, Ichigaya, Tokyo. — p. 97 ([scan leaf 146](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n146/mode/1up))

**Koizumi, Masayasu** Lieut.-Gen. (ret.), ex-Com. of 8th Division, b. '55 in Ibaraki prefecture; grad. from the defunct Mil. Non-Com. Sch., Sub-Lieut. '77; Colonel 97; Maj.-Gen. '03; promoted to the present rank in Aug. '10; fought in the China-Japan war as Brigade Commander and also in the Russo-Japanese war, when he was wounded at the battles of Liao- yang and Saho. Decorated with 2nd class Rising Sun and Golden Kite (war honour); on reserve list in '14. Add. Bizencho, Kamiichi, Mito. — p. 97 ([scan leaf 146](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n146/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Kojima, Kenshi** B. Ar. (Cornell, class '81), Prof. First High School; b. '55 in Tokyo; studied in U.S.A. and appointed Prof. Imp. Tokyo Univ. '82; transferred to High school '86; is a well-known English scholar. Add. 1/23, Yumicho, Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 98 ([scan leaf 147](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n147/mode/1up))

**Komatsu, Kenjiro** ex-Vice-Minister of Communications, and Crown Mem. of the House of Peers; b. '63 in Nagano-ken; grad. from Law Coll., Imp. Tokyo Univ., '83, and had long been connected with the Department having been promoted to the above post in 1911, but resigned in Dec. '12. Was despatched abroad in '97 on official business; was severely wounded by a burglar in Sept. '11 in his official residence. Add. Tsukudo Hachiman, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 98 ([scan leaf 147](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n147/mode/1up))

**Komatsu, Midori** Dir. Foreign Affairs Bureau, Korean Gov.-Gen.; b. '65 in Tokyo; grad. at Michigan Univ., '90; translator and Secretary of the Foreign Office till '07; transferred to Korean Government where he was promoted to the present position in '11. Add. Seoul, Korea. — p. 98 ([scan leaf 147](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n147/mode/1up))

**Komatsu, Teruhisa** 2nd Lieut. (Navy), 1st Marquis, the House being newly created in '10 to succeed the House of the late Prince Komatsu who died without an heir; was b. '88, fourth son of the late Prince Kita-Shirakawa; grad. at Naval Academy '09. Add. Kioi-cho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 98 ([scan leaf 147](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n147/mode/1up))

**Komatsubara, Eitaro** Lord-in-Waiting of the Kinkei Hall, and Privy Councillor; ex-Member of the House of Peers, ex-Minister of Education; b. in '52 at Okayama; took active part in democratic movement and was even imprisoned for some years; appointed a Secretary of Foreign Affairs '81; next Secretary of the Legation at Berlin; Vice-Minister of Justice, and next of Home Affairs Nov. '98-Sept. 1900; and afterwards entered the Osaka Mainichi Shimbun, but resigned the editorship in '03; Minister of Education from '08 till '11. Add. Oicho, Ebara-gun, Tokyo. — p. 98 ([scan leaf 147](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n147/mode/1up))

**Komoto, Jujiro** D.M., Prof. at the Medical Col. of Imp. Tokyo Univ.; b. '59 in Tajima; studied in Germany and Austria '85-89; is one of the most distinguished ophthalmologists of the day. Add. Kudan-zaka, Tokyo. — p. 98 ([scan leaf 147](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n147/mode/1up))

**Komura, Kin-ichi** Marquis (2nd), Secretary of the Foreign Office; b. in '82, eldest son of the late Vic. Jutaro Komura, 1st Marquis, one of the greatest diplomatists in recent Japan; grad. with honor from the Coll. Law, Imp. Univ., Tokyo, 1907; passed the Diplomatic Service Exam. the same year; attache at London '08-10; transferred to the head office '11. Add. Fujimicho, Koj., Tokyo. — p. 98 ([scan leaf 147](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n147/mode/1up))

**Kondo, Hisayoshi** Per. Secretary to the Imperial Household Minister and Court Councillor; b. '52 in Yamaguchi-ken; entered the Household Department in '79, where he has remained ever since. Add. Nakacho, Yotsuya, Tokyo. — p. 98 ([scan leaf 147](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n147/mode/1up))

**Kondo, Jihan** M.D., Prof. at the Imp. Tokyo Univ., b. in '64 in Shinano; grad. from the Coll. of Medicine in 1891; further studied surgery at Strasburg and Heidelberg '95-98. Add. Maruyama Shimmachi, Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 98 ([scan leaf 147](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n147/mode/1up))

**Kondo, Motoki** D. Eng., Inspector General of Naval Construction, Pres. of the Kogyoku-sha, Director of the Modelling Section, Navy; b. '64 in Tokyo; is Director of the Kogyokusha, private school founded by his father who did much in the cause of naval education. Received Hon. Prize from Imperial Academy of Japan '13. Add. Hiratsuka-mura, Tokyo-Fu. — p. 98 ([scan leaf 147](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n147/mode/1up))


## Who's Who in Japan

**Kondo, Rempei** Baron (cr. '11), President of the Nippon Yusen Kaisha (Japan Mail Steamship Co.), b. '50, and is long connected with shipping business, having served under the late Iwasaki whose relative by marriage he subsequently became; was appointed Director on the creation of the present Co.; elected Vice-President at the time of the Japan-China War and finally its President; bears the 2nd Order of the Rising Sun in recognition of his service in transport business during the Russo-Japanese War; M.P. in the 1st session of the Diet, but has lately kept aloof from politics. Add. Ichigaya, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 99 ([scan leaf 148](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n148/mode/1up))

**Kono, Hironaka** M.P. of unbroken record and leader of the Kenseikai ex-Min. of Agr. and Commerce, b. at Fukushima '49; entered early political career, joined the democratic movement started by Count Itagaki, and is one of the pioneer Liberals who fought in the cause of freedom and suffered much from collisions with the authorities. Was the leader of the Fukushima trouble and thrown in prison for several years; seceded '98 from the Liberal Party to join its rivals, the Progressives, and at once sprang into notoriety for a short while when as President of the Lower House he invited its dissolution by submitting an impeachment Reply to the Throne soon after the opening of the session of '03; had previously seceded from the Progressives; was arrested on the charge of sedition in connection with the Anti-Peace violence of Sept. 1905, but was acquitted. Was a leader of the Kokuminto but left in Jan., 1913 and joined late Prince Katsura's party; appointed Minister for Agr. and Com. of the 2nd Okuma Ministry in Dec., '14. Add. Fujimicho, Koj., Tokyo. — p. 99 ([scan leaf 148](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n148/mode/1up))

**Konoike, Zen-emon** Baron (cr. '11), is one of the oldest millionaire families in Japan, dating 3 centuries back; runs the Konoike Bank, and is at the same time the largest shareholder of the Nippon Life Insurance Co., and the Osaka Savings Bank. Add Konoike, Osaka. — p. 99 ([scan leaf 148](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n148/mode/1up))

**Konoye, Fumimaro** Prince, one of the proud Fujiwara houses and a gosseke (see Ichijo); was founded by Motozane (1143-66 A.D.). The present head is still a minor. Add. Shibuya, near Tokyo. — p. 99 ([scan leaf 148](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n148/mode/1up))

**Kosaki, Kodo** famous pioneer Christian and pastor of Reinanzaka Church, Tokyo, Chairman of the Kumi-a-i Churches of Japan, of Sunday School Society of Japan, and of Christian Church Union of Japan; b. '56 in Kumamoto-ken; studied at Kumamoto English School and Doshisha, Kyoto. Pres. of Doshisha Univ. in '90-97; three times represented Japan at International Congress of Religion abroad. Add. Reinanzaka, Tokyo. — p. 99 ([scan leaf 148](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n148/mode/1up))

**Koyama, Junji** ex-Dir. of Imp. Gov. Rlys. '12-13, b. in '70 at Kumamoto; graduated law at the Imperial Tokyo Univ. in '95; was clerk at the Home Office and next Secretary in the provinces and also in Tokyo, having held posts of Personal Secretary to the Minister of Justice and of Agriculture and Commerce, Dir. of Fishery Bureau in the latter Dept. '06; transferred to Governor of Gumma '08. Add. Ryodocho, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 99 ([scan leaf 148](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n148/mode/1up))

**Koyama, Kenzo** Member of Osaka Chamber of Commerce, Pres. of the 34th Bank; b. '58 in Saitama; entered Dep't of Education; appointed Dir. of Tokyo Higher Commercial School; then Dir. of Technical Bureau of Education Dep't and promoted Vice-Minister of Education; on retiring from the Government service became Pres. of the 34th Bank and is one of the most eminent bankers. Add. Otecho, Higashiku, Osaka. — p. 99 ([scan leaf 148](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n148/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Kozai, Yoshinao** D. Agr. (Japan), Ph. D. (Leipsic), Dean and Prof. of Coll. Agriculture, Imp. Univ., Tokyo; b. '65 in Kyoto-fu; grad. from Agr. Col. '85; Prof. at the alma mater '89-95; studied at Leipsic '95-99, and resumed the duty at Komaba; Agriculturist to the Gov. Experimental Farm at Nishigahara, Tokyo '03 and then its chief '06; Dean in '11. Add. Nishigahara, Tokyo. — p. 100 ([scan leaf 149](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n149/mode/1up))

**Kubota, Katsumi** ex-Dir. of the South Manchuria R'ly Co., '05-14; b. '69 in Kagoshima, 3rd son of Den-emon Higuchi; was adopted as son and heir by the late Mr. Kubota; graduated from Law Col. Imp. Univ. of Tokyo in '94; to be soon attached to the Bureau of National Treasury of the Nippon Ginko, and then transferred to the above post in '05. Add. Motomachi, Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 100 ([scan leaf 149](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n149/mode/1up))

**Kubota, Seishu** ex-Vice-Min. of Home Affairs, b. '71 in Hokkaido; grad. Law, Imp. University of Tokyo in '95 and then entered the legal service as Public Procurator till '06 when he was appointed Governor of Yamagata, Dir. of South Manchuria R'ly '06; started for Europe in '08 on an inspection tour of railway affairs, and returned home in '10; Governor of Miye in '11, Dir. of Eng. Bureau, and then of Public Works Bureau of the Home D pt., Dec. '12—Apr. '14; Gov. of Tokyo '14-15. Add. Shiba Park, Tokyo. — p. 100 ([scan leaf 149](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n149/mode/1up))

**Kubota, Seitaro** Judge, Adm. Litigation Court, b. '64 in Okayama; grad. Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ., '91; Sec. at local and the Home Office till '94; was sent abroad to investigate sanitary matters relating to factories '98-99 and was transferred to be Secretary of Agr. and Com. to draw up draft of factory law, retaining as additional post Secretary of the Home Office; Dir. Sanitary Affairs Bureau '03-10. Add. Reinanzaka, Tokyo. — p. 100 ([scan leaf 149](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n149/mode/1up))

**Kubota, Yusuru** Baron (cr. '07), ex-Minister of Education, Mem. House of Peers since '94; b. '47 in Hyogo-ken, entered the civil service in connection with Education in which he became Vice-Minister '94 and finally its chief, had to resign the Ministry Nov. '05 in connection with the University Prof.'s demonstration affair. Holds 1st class of the Order of Sacred Treasure. Add. Kanatomicho, Koishikawa, Tokyo. — p. 100 ([scan leaf 149](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n149/mode/1up))

**Kuga, Michihisa** Marquis, formerly courtier, the house being founded by Prince Tomohira, son of the Emperor Murakami; b. at Kyoto, '41; appointed commander of the Imp. Army at the time of the Restoration, Sec. of the Imperial Household and the Cabinet, then Governor of Tokyo '96-97. Appointed Director of Board of Decorations in '09. Add. Shin-Ogawamachi, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 100 ([scan leaf 149](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n149/mode/1up))

**Kuhara, Fusanosuke** Pres. of the Kuhara Mining Co. at Osaka, the leasee of the Hitachi Copper Mine, etc.; b. 1869 at Osaka, grad. from Keiogijuku '89; a clerk at the Kosaka Copper Mine which was resuscitated by him from the threatened danger of decay. He was made the supervisor of the mine, but resigned the post '98. Bought the Hitachi Mine '05, and has exploited it as one of the best copper mines in Japan. Add. Nakanoshima, Osaka. — p. 100 ([scan leaf 149](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n149/mode/1up))


## Who's Who in Japan

**Kujo, Michizane** Prince, one of the proud Fujiwara houses and formerly a gosekke (see Ichijo) founded by Kanezane (1148–1207). The present head, was b. '96 and is Chief Kitualist on duty at Court, being a brother of the Empress. Add. Fukuyoshicho, Akasaka, Tokyo. — p. 101 ([scan leaf 150](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n150/mode/1up))

**Kuki, Takakazu** Baron (cr. '05). Privy Councillor, b. '52 in Settsu; was for several years a high official in the Dep't of Education; Minister at Washington between '84 and '88. Director of the Imperial Nara Museum in '90, Exhibition Commissioner, etc., is a great virtuoso of Japanese painting. Add. Nagatacho, Tokyo. — p. 101 ([scan leaf 150](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n150/mode/1up))

**Kuma, Tokuzo** Pm.-Gen., Director of Accounts Bureau, War Office since '13; b. '62 in Fukuoka; Sub-Lieut. in 85, Lieut. in '88; removed to the Accountant service in '93, Paymaster-Colonel in '05 and General in '09; Chief of account section, Kwantung Govt., '09-13. Add. Okubo, Tokyo. — p. 101 ([scan leaf 150](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n150/mode/1up))

**Kumamoto, Kenjiro** English scholar and educationist, Prof. at the Peers' School since 1898 and Commissioner of Teachers' License Examination (Eng.) for Middle & Normal Schools; b. '67 in Osaka; studied Law at the Imp. Univ., Tokyo '87-89; English teacher at Himeji Mid. School in '89, then at Osaka Mid. School and next Prof. at 3rd (Kyoto) High School; at Tokyo Higher Normal School '94-98. Add. Zoshigaya, Takata, near Tokyo. — p. 101 ([scan leaf 150](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n150/mode/1up))

**Kume, Keiichiro** historian of the Western painting, b. '66 in Saga, son of the celebrated historian Kume Kunitake; studied in Paris '86-93; appointed Professor of the Tokyo Fine Art School '06; is noted for landscape painting. Add. Harajuku, Sendagaya, Tokyo. — p. 101 ([scan leaf 150](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n150/mode/1up))

**Kume, Kin-ya** ex-Vice-Minister of the Dep't of Agr. and Com., b. '67 in Tokyo; finished Law, Imperial Tokyo Univ., '86, and has since held post of Councillor or Secretary at the Communication Dep't in which he rose to be Dir. Communication Bureau, to be transferred to that of Patent Bureau '04 and then Dir. Forestry Bureau '06-07. Raised to the post of Vice Min. '07-08. Add. Nishiokubo, Tokyo. — p. 101 ([scan leaf 150](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n150/mode/1up))

**Kume, Kunitake** D. Litt. (Japan), historian, Prof. Waseda Univ., b. '44 in Saga; was on suite of the late Prince Iwakura's mission to America and Europe in '72; was next in the compilation Bureau, Cabinet, and afterward Prof. of the 1st High School, but his article about the origin of the Japanese race obliged him to resign; has been connected with Waseda ever since. Add. Meguro, Tokyo. — p. 101 ([scan leaf 150](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n150/mode/1up))

**Kume, Ryosaku** Pres. of the Tokyo Gas Works since '07, b. '67 in Saitama; studied at the Keiogijuku and the Hogaku-in (Chuo Univ.) in Tokyo; was for years Managing Director of the Nippon Railway Co. before its nationalization. Add. Sendagi, Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 101 ([scan leaf 150](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n150/mode/1up))

**Kunizawa, Shimbei** D. Eng., Vice-President of the South Manchuria R'ly Co. since '06; b. '64 in Kochi-ken; grad. in '89 from Eng. Col. of Imp. Tokyo Univ.; engineer of the nationalized Kyushu R'ly Co. '89; served at the Railway Department '92-05; was connected with the field railroads in Manchuria before the South M. R'ly Co. was created. Add. Dairen, Manchuria. — p. 101 ([scan leaf 150](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n150/mode/1up))

**Kurachi, Tetsukichi** Mem. House of Peers, Vice Pres. of Japan-China Industrial Co.; b. '71 in Kanazawa; grad. Law, Tokyo Imp. Univ. in '94; passed higher civil service examination in '96, Councillor of Foreign Office in '87; 3rd class Sec. at Berlin in '99, Councillor at head office '01; attached to the Japanese Envoy to the Peace Conference at the Hague in '07; Vice-Min. of Foreign Dep't, '11-12. Add. Harajuku, Sendagaya, Tokyo. — p. 101 ([scan leaf 150](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n150/mode/1up))


## Who's Who

**Kuratomi, Yuzuburo** D.C.L. Lord-in-Waiting of Kinkei Hall, and Chief Auditor, Imp. Household Dept.; b. '53 in Fukuoka; grad. Law Coll., Justice Dept. '79; Judge '82 and long held post of Councillor, Judicial Dept.; Dir. Civil and Cri'l Bureau '98; Vice-Minister '99; Procurator of the Supreme Court, and Chief Proc. of Osaka and Tokyo Appeal Courts, Bureau Director and Vice-Minister of Justice; Vice-Min. of Justice of the Korean Gov.-Gen. '07-13; Dir. of the Legislative Bureau '13-14; mem. of House of Peers '14-16. Add. Tangocho, Akasaka, Tokyo. — p. 102 ([scan leaf 151](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n151/mode/1up))

**Kure, Bunso** ex-Chief Statist of Dept. of Arg. and Com., b. '51 in Tokyo; clerk in the Cabinet '75 and has since been devoted to statistic work. Was sent abroad to U.S.A. and Europe '00 to investigate statistic affairs. Is teaching his special science at various institutions. Add. Motozonocho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 102 ([scan leaf 151](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n151/mode/1up))

**Kure, Daigoro** Dir. of Chiyoda Dye Stuff Co., b. '62 in Nagasaki, was sent to China by the Foreign Office in '77 to study Chinese language; attaché to the Japanese Consulate at Tientsin '85; Chancellor to the Japanese Consulate at London '86; transferred to Bombay which post he soon resigned to enter the Mitsui Bussan in '96. Add. Shimizukawa, Tozuka, near Tokyo. — p. 102 ([scan leaf 151](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n151/mode/1up))

**Kure, Hidezo** M.D., Prof. at the Imp. Tokyo University, b. '65 in Tokyo; grad. Medicine at the Imp. Tokyo Univ., '90; sent to Germany for study, '97-00; is a specialist on insanity. Add. Yushima Shinhanacho, Tokyo. — p. 102 ([scan leaf 151](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n151/mode/1up))

**Kurino, Shin-ichiro** Viscount, ex-Amb. to France, b. at Fukuoka '52; Commissioner of the Foreign Office '81; Junior Secretary '84; Vice-Chief of the Investigation Bureau '86; transferred to the Communication Dep't to act as Personal Secretary to its Minister, promoted to be Director of the Foreign Correspondence Bureau in the Dep't three years after; returned to the Foreign Office '91 as Director of the Investigation Bureau and then that of the Political Bureau. Next commenced his diplomatic career first as Minister at Washington '94, at Rome '96, at Paris '97, and Petrograd '01, in the stormy time preceding the diplomatic rupture; appointed to Ambassador to France '06. Add. Hinokicho, Akasaka, Tokyo. — p. 102 ([scan leaf 151](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n151/mode/1up))

**Kuroda, Kiyoteru** Prof. at the Gov. Art Academy since '02, b. '66, son of the following; studied painting at Paris under Colin '84-93; is one of the master painters of Western school. Add. Hirakawacho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 102 ([scan leaf 151](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n151/mode/1up))

**Kuroda, Kiyotsuna** Viscount, Privy Councillor, b. '30 in Kagoshima clan; took active part in the Meiji Restoration and was created Viscount in '87. Add. Kogaicho, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 102 ([scan leaf 151](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n151/mode/1up))

**Kuroda, Nagashige** Marquis (cr. '08), formerly Daimyo of Fukuoka, Vice-President of the House of Peers; b. '67 at Fukuoka; studied at Cambridge Univ. '84-89; a Master of Ceremonies '89-90; is President of the Oriental Association. Add. Fukuyoshicho, Akasaka, Tokyo. — p. 102 ([scan leaf 151](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n151/mode/1up))

**Kuroiwa, Shuroku** Edi. and Prop. of the Yorozu Choho, b. '52 in Tosa; was long connected with various papers chiefly as translator of foreign novels; started the present daily in '92, and by his clever management created for it a wide circulation and to-day it is one of the best circulated journals. Add. Kogai-cho, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 102 ([scan leaf 151](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n151/mode/1up))

**Kuroki, It[illegible]n** Gen. (ret.), Count (Baron '95, elevated '07), 1st class Golden Kite and Grand Order of Rising Sun and Paulownia (war honor); b. '44 in Satsuma; commanded the Kumamoto Army Division in the Japan-China War, and achieved a distinguished service at We-hai-wei under Marshal Oyama Feb., '95; promoted General in Nov. '03; commander of the First Army in Russo-Japanese War and won the first great victory at the Battle of the Yalu, succeeded by the brilliant series of successful campaigns; appointed Mem. of the Supreme Military Council Dec. '95. Attended Jamestown Exhibition, U.S.A. as representative of Japan. Add. Aoyama, Tokyo. — p. 103 ([scan leaf 152](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n152/mode/1up))

**Kurose, Yoshikado** Baron ('07), Mem. House of Peers, Lieut.-Gen. (ret.), b. '46 in Okayama; Ensign '71; was Commander of Artillery Corps of the 2nd Army in the Japan-China War and was rewarded 4th Order of Golden Kite; Lieut.-Gen. '01 and put on retired list the following year. He was commissioned Commander of Formosan Garrison in Russo-Japanese War. Add. Nandocho, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 103 ([scan leaf 152](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n152/mode/1up))

**Kurusu, Sobei** Mem. of Yokohama Chamber of Commerce, Dir. of Nisshin Life Ins. Co., Nippon Safe Oil Co., etc.; b. in '55 in Saitama-ken, and has risen to present position by starting export of raw silk at Yokohama when he was still young. Add. Hommachi, Yokohama. — p. 103 ([scan leaf 152](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n152/mode/1up))

**Kusaka, Yoshio** ex-M.P., Pres. of Nippon Live-Stock Co., Dir. of 1st Bank, Tokyo Savings Bank, Toho Fire Ins. Co., Toyo Life Ins. Co., etc.; b. '51 in Fukushima-ken; went over to U.S.A. for study in '71; on returning home appointed Secy. of Home Office, transferred to Agr. and Com. Office, then Chief of Statistic Bureau, Transportation Bureau; Governor of Nagasaki, Fukushima; once Minister-Resident; finally entered into business, made a tour through Europe in '76 in the suite of the late Marq. Inouye. Add. Suzaki, Mukojima, Tokyo. — p. 103 ([scan leaf 152](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n152/mode/1up))

**Kusakabe, Benjiro** engineer, D. Eng. (Japan), Chief Engineer of the Tokyo City Office; b. '61 in Shiga-ken; grad. Eng. Col. of the Imp. Tokyo Univ. '86; was long at the Home Office as its Chief Engineer and once been to Europe; received his doctorate in '01. Add. 53/5 Aoyama Minamicho, Tokyo. — p. 103 ([scan leaf 152](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n152/mode/1up))

**Kushida, Manzo** Chief of Banking Dep't of Mitsubishi Firm, b. '67 in Tokyo; studied in U.S.A. '85-94 and graduated at Pennsylvania Univ.; entered the Mitsubishi on returning home; present post '14. Add. Nishikubo, Shiba, Tokyo. — p. 103 ([scan leaf 152](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n152/mode/1up))

**Kusunose, Sachihiko** Lieut.-Gen., ex-War Minister, b. '58 in Tosa; Sub-Lieut. of Artil. '79; was sent to Europe in suite of late Generals Nogi and Kawakami in '86; Major-General '01, and Lieut.-General in '07; filled in the meanwhile the posts of adjutant to the General Staff, Chief Staff Officer of the 12th Division, Com. of the Tsushima Garrison, Head of the Osaka Arsenal, Head of the Saghalien Government, and Com. of the Yura Fort; Min. of War '13-14. Add. Sanya, Yoyogi, near Tokyo. — p. 103 ([scan leaf 152](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n152/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Kuwada, Kumazo** D.C.L. (Japan), Prof. of Imp. University, Tokyo, Member of the House of Peers as highest-tax-payer in Tottori-ken; b. '63 in Tottori-ken; grad. Law Col. Imp. Tokyo Univ., in '93; Manager of the Kōgo Club; further studied in Europe '96-98, especially Social Politics, and organized the Social-Political Association on his return home; elected Member of the House of Peers the same year. Works, "On Industrial Economy," "The General Trend of Social Problems in Europe," and Factory Law and Labor Insurance. Parl.-Secy. to the Education Dept. July to Aug., '15. Add. Sendagi, Hayashicho, Tokyo. — p. 104 ([scan leaf 153](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n153/mode/1up))
