**Machida, Chuji** M.P. (Doshikai) & Parliamentary Secy. of Agr. and Com. Dept., Dir. of 110th Bank and Kyodo Fire Ins. Co., etc.; b. '63 in Akita-ken; grad. Imp. Tokyo Univ. and further studied in Europe and America; started the "Oriental Economist"; once connected with the Bank of Japan; entered into business ever since. App. Par. Secy. in '15. Add. Minami-Enokicho, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 104 ([scan leaf 153](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n153/mode/1up))

**Mae —** See Maye —. — p. 104 ([scan leaf 153](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n153/mode/1up))

**Maki, Bokushin** Pres. of Toyo Marine Ins. Co. and Chiyoda Rubber Co.; b. '54 in Shimabara, and held various offices in provincial and Central Offices from '75 to '87; President of Sobu Rly. '88; was in Formosa as Governor and in other capacities '85-86; transferred to be Dir. Police Bureau, Home Office, then to that of Fishery Bureau '98, and finally to the Forestry Bureau '06 which he soon left. Once sat in Parliament. Add. Kurumacho, Shiba, Tokyo. — p. 104 ([scan leaf 153](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n153/mode/1up))

**Makino, Nobuaki** Baron (cr. '07), Crown Mem. of House of Peers (nom. '75); second son of the late Okubo, b. '51; Secretary of the Foreign Office '89; Private Secretary to the then Premier Count Ito (late Prince) '88; a local Governor '91-92; Vice-Minister of Education '93; Minister at Rome '97; tranferred to a similar post at Vienna which he resigned in Mar. '03 to be made Min. of Education, Privy Councillor in '09; Minister Agr. and Com. '11-12, and of Foreign Affairs '13-14. Add. Sendagaya, near Tokyo. — p. 104 ([scan leaf 153](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n153/mode/1up))

**Makino, Tomitaro** a botanist and lecturer at the Imp. Tokyo Univ., was b. '63 in Kochi; started the Botanical Magazine in '86 in Tokyo, being the first journal devoted to the science in Japan; started publication of his "Flora Japonica" in '89; has written various other works on his speciality. Add. Nishikata-machi, Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 104 ([scan leaf 153](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n153/mode/1up))

**Makoshi, Kyohei** Pres. of Japan Brewery Co., Dir. of other Cos.; b. '43 at Okayama; reached Tokyo '70 to find work with almost empty purse; started a lodging house; became acquainted with Mr. Takashi Masuda and was appointed clerk of the then existing Senshu Gumi, exp. and imp. firm at Yokohama, subsequently transferred to the Mitsui Bussan, forerunner of present Mitsui Bussan, which he left '88 to start the Yebisu Brewery at Meguro. Twice sat in the House of Representatives, but has since abandoned politics. Add. Sakuragawa-cho, Shiba, Tokyo. — p. 104 ([scan leaf 153](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n153/mode/1up))

**Manabe, Ton** Baron (cr. '07), Lieut.-Gen (ret.), Mem. House of Peers and Parl.-Secy. to the War Dept.; b. '51 in Yamaguchi; became Sub-Lieut. Infantry in '72 and gradually promoted to be Lieut.-Gen. in '05 and on retired list in '07, fought in Japan-China War, Boxer's trouble and Japan-Russia War and decorated with 2nd class Order of the Golden Kite; Elected Parliamentary-Secy. of the War Dept. in '15. Add. Ote-machi, Hiroshima. — p. 104 ([scan leaf 153](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n153/mode/1up))


## Who's Who

**Mano, Bunji** Pres. Imp. Kyushu Univ. since '13, Emeritus Prof. Tokyo Imp. Univ.; b. '61 in Shizuoka; grad. course of Mech. Eng. at the former Eng. College '81; studied his speciality at Glasgow University. Member of the Institution of Mechanical Engineers London, studied practical side of the science at Armstrong Works and returned home '90. Was appointed Prof. at the Imp. Univ.; sent to Paris on the occasion of World Fair; Dir. of Technical Education Bureau '01-13, retaining the chair in the University. Add. Fukuoka. — p. 105 ([scan leaf 154](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n154/mode/1up))

**Maruta, Hideomi** Dir. of the Nagasaki Dockyard, Mitsubishi, b. '59 in Nara-ken; grad. from Naval Academy '72; further studied in England; from '80 to '06 he was Naval Eng., Prof. at Naval Eng. Coll. at Naval Academy, Chief of Yokosuka Shipyard; resigned and became connected with the present yard. Add. Nishi-Okubo, near Tokyo. — p. 105 ([scan leaf 154](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n154/mode/1up))

**Masaki, Naohiko** Dir. Gov. Fine Art Academy, Tokyo, b. 62 in Kyoto; grad. Law, Imp. Univ., '82. After serving at various provincial schools, he was appointed Personal Sec. of the Min. of Edu. '97 combining post of Chief of Art Section. Was dispatched abroad to inspect art business in Europe and America, and after holding posts of Inspector at head office and lecturer of schols, he was appointed to the present post '01; was Commissioner of the Anglo-Japanese Exhibition '10. Add. Yarai, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 105 ([scan leaf 154](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n154/mode/1up))

**Masuda, Eisaku** dealer in art articles, Dir. of the Mitsukoshi Department Store, Ltd.; was b. '65 in Tokyo and is brother of Takashi Masuda. Add. Meguro near Tokyo. — p. 105 ([scan leaf 154](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n154/mode/1up))

**Masuda, Giichi** ex-M.P. (Kokuminto), prop. of various popular magazines, Dir. of several Cos., was b. at Takata in '69; grad. from Waseda Senmon Gakko in 1893; was on the staff of several newspapers. Tour around the world 1914. Add. Haramachi, Koishikawa, Tokyo. — p. 105 ([scan leaf 154](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n154/mode/1up))

**Masuda, Takashi** Adviser to the Mitsui Family, b. '48 in Sado of a petty samurai family; appointed cavalry officer and at the same time interpreter at Shogunate; after the Restoration entered the Senshu Kaisha, an export and import firm in Yokohama, afterward transferred to the Mitsui and has remained in it to this day, drawing the highest salary of any in Japan. He is a noted connoisseur of arts, his collection of Buddhist pictures and images is reputed to be the finest in the country. Add. Goten-yama, Tokyo. — p. 105 ([scan leaf 154](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n154/mode/1up))

**Masuda, Taro** Man.-Dir. of Taiwan Sugar Mfg. Co., Dir. of Dai Nippon Artificial Fertilizer, Manju Life Ins., Imperial Theatre, Chiyoda Fire Insur. Co., etc.; b. in Tokyo '75, eldest son of Takashi Masuda; educated in England and is also a popular amateur playwright. Add. Hama-cho, Nihombashi, Tokyo. — p. 105 ([scan leaf 154](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n154/mode/1up))

**Masujima, Rokuichiro** D.C.L. (Japan), lawyer, b. '57 at Hikone; one of the pioneer graduates of Law, Imp. Uni., Tokyo, in '77, and a graduate of the Middle Temple '83. Was for somewhile Lecturer at the Imp. Univ., but soon resigned to open with several friends the English Law School (now Central Univ.). He has undertaken various important cases and also played distinguished part in business of international description as intermediary and adviser. He is a consulting lawyer to many Japanese and foreign establishments. Add. Uchisaiwai-cho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 105 ([scan leaf 154](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n154/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Matano, Taku** Director of the Imperial Museum, Tokyo, b. '46 in Sano fief which sent him to Yedo for prosecution of study; entered civil service, '71 and was Dir. of then existing Record Bureau, Cabinet, to be transferred '89 to the present post. Add. Nakano-cho, Akasaka Tokyo. — p. 106 ([scan leaf 155](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n155/mode/1up))

**Matsudaira, Yasutaka** Marq., head of the old Daimyo of Fukui, Pres., Japan Agr. Society; b. '67; proceeded to Germany '84 to study and afterwards entered Cirencestor Agr. Col., England; established a model exp. farm at his hereditary estate in Fukui and was rewarded with silver medal at the 5th Domestic Exhibition in Osaka; elected Pres. Japan Agr. Society '04; Mem. House of Peers by right of birth. Add. Sekiguchi, Tokyo. — p. 106 ([scan leaf 155](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n155/mode/1up))

**Matsudaira, Yorinaga** Count, Mem. House of Peers, was b. in '74; grad. from the Waseda Univ. '93; succeeded to the title '03, the House being formerly the Daimyo of Takamatsu. Add. Motomachi, Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 106 ([scan leaf 155](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n155/mode/1up))

**Matsui, Keishiro** Ambassador at Paris, b. '68 at Osaka; grad. Law. Imp. Tokyo Univ. '89; dip. attaché '90 at the Seoul Legation and Sec. '93 at the same place; 2nd Sec. of Legation at Washington '94; 1st Sec. at London '97; transferred to a similar post at Peking '02; again appointed Sec. at London, to be transferred to post of Councillor, Embassy in Paris early Oct., '06; transferred to Washington in '08 and then Vice-Min. of Foreign Affairs, '13. Add Paris. — p. 106 ([scan leaf 155](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n155/mode/1up))

**Matsukata, Iwao** Pres., 15th Bank and Vice-Pres. of Tokyo Bankers' Association etc; b. '62 at Kagoshima; eldest son and heir of Marquis Matsukata; was once a dip. attaché but soon left Gov. service to devote himself to business. Add. Minamisakumacho, Shiba, Tokyo. — p. 106 ([scan leaf 155](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n155/mode/1up))

**Matsukata, Kojiro** Chairman of Kobe Chamber of Commerce, Pres. of Kawasaki Dockyards Co., Kobe Gas Co., etc.; b. '65 at Kagoshima, 3rd son of Marquis Matsukata, grad. Peers' School and further studied in U.S.A., and since his return he has been exclusively devoted himself to business, being at present connected with several other leading concerns in Keihan districts. Was elected M.P. in '12. Add. Yamamoto-dori, Kobe. — p. 106 ([scan leaf 155](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n155/mode/1up))

**Matsukata, Masayoshi** Mraquis (Count '84 and Marquis '07), hon. LL.D. (Oxford), Privy Counsillor, one of the "elder statesmen"; b. '40 at Kagoshima; was connected with the Treasury soon after the advent of Meiji, and when in '81 the Minister of Finance of the day (Okuma) resigned, the subject of this sketch as Vice-Minister succeeded him, continuing to occupy this chair for more than ten years; did much in restoring the credit of paper money which had fallen below par in consequence of excessive issue in the civil trouble '77. In '91 he undertook the task of forming a Cabinet, combining the function of Minister of Finance, but the Ministry fell the following year unable to withstand the united attack of the Liberals and the Progressives; had to assume the portfolio of finance on the occasion of the Japan-China War. His second Ministry '96-97 was memorable in the establishment of gold monometallic standard and also for the conclusion of entente with the Progressives, but it came abruptly to an end, followed by the miserable collapse of the Cabinet; was Finance Minister in the Yamagata Ministry that followed his; a Privy Councillor in July '03 which was an outcome of peculiar circumstance. In '02 he travelled through Europe and America; President of the Japan Red Cross Society in '03, but resigned the post from bad health in '12. Add. Mita, Tokyo. — p. 106 ([scan leaf 155](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n155/mode/1up))


## Who's Who

**Matsukata, Shosaku** Dir. of Inawashiro Hydro-Elec. Co., b. '63 at Kagoshima, 2nd son of Marquis Matsukata; started career as clerk in Foreign Office, to be attached to the Legation at Paris and other places; was ordered to prosecute study at official expense at Brussels Univ. which he finished, '88. Appointed attaché, '93; 3rd Sec. Legation in Holland and Paris '95; Per. Sec. to Foreign Min. '97 combining that to Min. Agr. and Com.; 2nd Sec. at London '98; promoted to be 1st Sec. the same year at the same post of duty; Sec. at head office '02; Minister to Siam '05-07. Add. Takeyacho, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 107 ([scan leaf 156](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n156/mode/1up))

**Matsukawa, Toshitune** Lieut.-General and Commander of Tokyo Garrison, b. '59 in Miyagi-ken; Sub-Lieut. in '82; Major-General in '95, meanwhile serving as Military Attaché to our Legation at Berlin; Chief of the 1st Section of the General Staff Office, etc.; took active part in the Sino-Japanese War as a staff-officer of the 2nd Army; also staff-officer to Marshal Oyama during Russo-Japanese War; Sectional Chief of the General Staff in '07; Lieut.-Gen. '08; Com. of 10th Div. '11-14. Add. Kyoto. — p. 107 ([scan leaf 156](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n156/mode/1up))

**Matsuki, Kan-ichiro** ex-Dir. Electric Bureau, Tokyo City Office, was b. '72 in Iyo; grad. Law, Imp. Univ., Tokyo, class '96; was Secretary of Communication Department '97; Secretary and Director of the Imperial Railways till his transfer to the last post on the municipalization of the Tokyo Street Electric Railways. Add. Shin-saka, Akasaka, Tokyo. — p. 107 ([scan leaf 156](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n156/mode/1up))

**Matsumoto, Bunzaburo** Dr. Lit. (Japan), Dean and Prof., Col. of Lit., Imp. Kyoto Univ., b. '59 in Kanazawa; grad. from Col. of Lit., Imp. Tokyo Univ. in '93 and further studied at Berlin in '99; Lecturer at Waseda, etc., Prof. at Imp. Tokyo Univ., then Prof. of Kyoto Univ. '06, Dean '08. Add. Imp. Univ. of Kyoto. — p. 107 ([scan leaf 156](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n156/mode/1up))

**Matsumoto, Gentaro** Prof. and Chief of the Girls' Section of the Peers School, b. '59 in Fukui Prefecture, graduated from the Col. of Lit., Imp. Tokyo Univ.; taught at several Govt. Schools and finally appointed present post in '07. Add. Nagatacho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 107 ([scan leaf 156](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n156/mode/1up))

**Matsumura, Ninzo** Dr. Sc. (Japan), Prof., Science Col., Imp. Tokyo Univ., Dir. Botanical Garden of the same Univ., b. '56 in Ibaragi; studied in Germany for years, Assist.-Prof. of the Univ. in '83, full Prof. in '90; honoured with the degree of D. Sc. in '90; made an inspection tour through Europe and America '06. Add. Akebonocho, Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 107 ([scan leaf 156](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n156/mode/1up))

**Matsumuro, Itasu** Min. of Justice, b. '52 in Fukuoka; grad. Imp. Tokyo Univ. in '84; entered the judicial service and promoted Pres. of Court of Cassation in '04 with add. chair of Pres. of Administrative Litigation Court; Min. of Justice, Dec. '12-Feb. '13; Chief of Auditor Bureau, Imp. Household, '13-16; again Min. Oct. '16. Add. Ikebukuro, near Tokyo. — p. 107 ([scan leaf 156](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n156/mode/1up))

**Matsunami, Nin-ichiro** Dr. Lit. (Japan), Prof. of Imp. Tokyo Univ., b. '67 in Osaka, graduated from the Imp. Tokyo Univ. in '93 and then studied in Europe for years; Prof., Naval Cadet Sch. and then present post; attended International Conference regarding to maritime law several times. Add. Nakacho, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 107 ([scan leaf 156](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n156/mode/1up))

**Matsuo, Yoshio** Manager of Tokyo Branch of the Yokohama Specie Bank, b. in Nagasaki in '61; entered the bank when it was inaugurated; Manager of the Bombay Branch in '05; present post in '07. Add. Takanawa, Shiba, Tokyo. — p. 107 ([scan leaf 156](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n156/mode/1up))

**Matsuoka, Koki** Pres. of Nippon University and Crown Mem. House of Peers, b. '46 at Tokushima; appointed President of the Tokyo Appeal Court '90; Procurator-General '92. He was twice Vice-Minister of Home Affairs in '94 and '98, and then removed to be President of the Adm. Litigation Court till Jan. '06, when he entered the Cabinet as Min. of Agr. Com., being one of the staunch followers of Prince Yamagata. Add. Zaimokucho, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 108 ([scan leaf 157](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n157/mode/1up))

**Matsu-ura, Atsushi** Count (suc. '08), Mem. House of Peers, formerly of the House that held the fief of Hirado; studied at Imp. Univ., Tokyo, and at Trinity, Cambridge. Add. Mukoyanagiwara, Tokyo. — p. 108 ([scan leaf 157](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n157/mode/1up))

**Matsu-ura, Chinjiro** Dir. of the Special Education Bureau, Department of Education, since '12, was b. in '72 at Uwajima; grad. from the Law Col., Imp. Tokyo Univ. '98; Secretary at Tokyo Prefectural Office and at the Department of Education '98-11; sent to Germany '07 to inquire into education matters. Add. Kobinata-Daimachi, Tokyo. — p. 108 ([scan leaf 157](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n157/mode/1up))

**Matsuzaki, Kuranosuke** D.C.L. (Japan), Prof., at Imp. Univ., Tokyo, b. '62; grad. law, Imp. Univ., Tokyo in '85; further studied in France and Germany; Prof. both at Agr. Col. and Law Col. of the Univ., appointed to the post of the Director of the Higher Com. School which he was compelled to resign the same '08 owing to the student's agitation. Add. Imperial Tokyo University. — p. 108 ([scan leaf 157](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n157/mode/1up))

**Mayeda, Masana** promoter of various business organizations. b. '50 in Kagoshima; sent to France for study '69 and was for somewhile attached to the Legation there, and then acted as Chief Commissioner when Japan participated in the French Exhibition '78. Was Senior Secretary of Treasury and Dep't Agr. and Com.; Vice-Min. of the latter '89; and subsequently transferred to the then existing Senate and with its abolition nominated Mem. House of Peers which he resigned '97 to devote himself to organizing an industrial league. Add. Kioicho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 108 ([scan leaf 157](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n157/mode/1up))

**Mayeda, Takeshiro** Auditor of the Nippon Electric Co., Proprietor of the Technical Magazine, Mem. of the Tokyo Chamber of Commerce; was b. in Niigata-ken in '67 and graduated from the Post and Telegraph School '88; has by self-study and indefatigable diligence risen to eminence in electric business; made tours of inspection in Europe and America in '05 and '13. Add. 21 Shimbori, Shiba, Tokyo. — p. 108 ([scan leaf 157](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n157/mode/1up))

**Mayeda, Toshinari** Marquis, head of the House of the former daimyo of Kaga with the fief of 1,022,700 koku (founded in 16th cent.). The present head was b. '85, son of a scion of the family and was adopted by it which had no male issue. Proceeded to Europe and was attached to the British Army in France '15-16. Add. Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 108 ([scan leaf 157](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n157/mode/1up))

**Mayejima, Hisoka** Baron (cr. '08), Pres. of Japan Seamen's Association; b. '35 in Echigo, and had charge of postal affairs in the initial stage of the service, and laid the foundation of its present perfection. Resigned the post '81 when Count Okuma left the Government, and has ever since led comparatively retired life. When the 30th anniversary of the postal service was celebrated in '03 he, "Father of Japanese Postal Service" was retrospectively rewarded with grant of patent of Baron. He is one of the enthusiastic language reformists and advocates of Roman spelling system. Add. Sekiguchi, Koishikawa, Tokyo. — p. 108 ([scan leaf 157](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n157/mode/1up))

**Mayekawa, Tahei** Pres. of the Tokyo Bank, Tōyo Muslin Co., Minami Nippon Sugar Co., etc., Mng. of the Tokyo Stock Exchange, etc., is a leading cotton draper in Tokyo ; b. in '62 in Yamanashi and was adopted by the family. Add. Tomizawa-cho, Nih., Tokyo. — p. 109 ([scan leaf 158](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n158/mode/1up))

**Megata, Tanetaro** Baron (cr. '07), Member of the House of Peers; b. '51 in Tokyo, one of the first Japanese who grad. Harvard, and for about 25 years ending '04 Mr. Megada continued in the service of the Finance Dep't when he was engaged by the Korean Gov., as Financial Adviser, and occupied the post of Bureau Director. It was abolished in consequence of the new Japan-Korea agreement in Oct. '07; is chairman of the Harvard Club. Add. Haramachi, Koishikawa, Tokyo. — p. 109 ([scan leaf 158](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n158/mode/1up))

**Mikami, Sanji** Historian, D. Litt. (Japan), Prof. at the Imp. Tokyo University since '92, was b. '65; grad. Lit. Col., of the Tokyo Univ. in '89, and subsequently studied in the Post-Grad. Course to be afterwards appointed Lecturer at the same Col. '91; Prof. at the Girls' Higher Normal School '92; sent to Germany to attend a conference, the International Oriental Society, which was held at Hamburg in '02. Add. Sendagi Hayashi-cho, Tokyo. — p. 109 ([scan leaf 158](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n158/mode/1up))

**Mimura, Kumpei** in charge of the Banking Department of the Mitsubishi Firm, was b. in '55 in Oita, and early entered the service of the Firm; is also connected with the Tokyo Warehouse Co., etc. Add. Shinsaka, Akasaka, Tokyo. — p. 109 ([scan leaf 158](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n158/mode/1up))

**Minakami, Hiromi** ex-Mayor of Kobe, b. '63 at Kumamoto; grad. Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ. '88; appointed Councillor of Legislative Bureau and dispatched to Europe to investigate Parliamentary system; appointed Sec. of House of Representatives, Nagasaki Local Office, Customs House at Kobe and that at Yokohama in turn, which last he resigned '05 to take up the Mayoralty; he resigned it in '09. Add. Kobinatadai, Tokyo. — p. 109 ([scan leaf 158](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n158/mode/1up))

**Minami, Hiroshi** Mem. of House of Peers, b. Toyama-ken; grad. Law, Tokyo Imp. Univ. in '96, to be appointed Secretary of the Cabinet where he was steadily promoted till he rose to chief in January, '08; afterward Governor of Fukuoka. Add. Naitocho, Yotsuya, Tokyo. — p. 109 ([scan leaf 158](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n158/mode/1up))

**Minami, Takajiro** Dr. Ag. (Japan), Prof. at College of Agr. (Sapporo), of Tohoku Univ. since '81; b. '59 in Hizen; grad. from the Sapporo Agr. Coll., class '81; visited U.S.A. as a commissioner of the Columbus Exhibition '89 and again in '13 on party of Japanese business-men invited by American Chambers of Commerce. Add. Agr. Coll., Sapporo. — p. 109 ([scan leaf 158](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n158/mode/1up))

**Minobe, Shunkichi** Pres. Bank of Chosen, b. '69 at Takasago, Harima; grad. Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ., '89; Personal Sec'y, and Sectional Chief at the Dep't Agri. and Com. and the Treasury; elected to Pres. of Hokkaido Colonization Bank '93-15; the present post '16. Add. Bank of Chosen, Seoul. — p. 109 ([scan leaf 158](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n158/mode/1up))

**Minobe, Tatsukichi** D.C.L. (Japan), Mem. of Imperial Academy, Prof. Imperial Univ., Tokyo; was b. '73 in Hyogoken, brother of S. Minobe above; grad. Law, Imp. Univ., Tokyo, class '97; studied in Germany, France and England '99-01. Add. Takehayacho, Koishikawa, Tokyo. — p. 109 ([scan leaf 158](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n158/mode/1up))

**Minoura, Katsundo** M.P. (Kenseikai) of unbroken record; b. '54 in Bungo; for a long while was manager of the Hochi. He began political life under then Count Okuma; three times has entered the Gov. service, first as Dir. of the now abolished Commercial Bureau of the Dep't of Agr. and Com. in '97 and second as Vice-Min. of Communications in '98; was once elected Vice-Pres. of the House of Representatives. Min. of Communications '15-16. Add. Higashi-goken-cho, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 109 ([scan leaf 158](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n158/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Mishima, Ki** D. Litt., tutor of Chinese classics to the Emperor, b. in Bitchu, '30, and held a post in the Justice Dep't as Public Procurator of the Supreme Court. Was also a Prof. in the Imp. Tokyo Univ. The institute of Chinese classics founded by him was granted ¥10,000 by Emperor '15. Add. Ichibancho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 110 ([scan leaf 159](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n159/mode/1up))

**Mishima, Yataro** Viscount. Member of the House of Peers, and Gov. of the Bank of Japan; b. '67 in Tokyo; studied economics in America, '84-88; repeated his visit '89-92; President of Yokohama Specie Bank '11, present position '13. Add. Sendagaya, near Tokyo. — p. 110 ([scan leaf 159](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n159/mode/1up))

**Misu, Sotaro** Baron (cr. '07), Admiral (ret.), holder of 2nd Class Golden Kite; b. '54 at Hikone; Captain at the time of the Japan-China War; Rear-Adm. '01, and Director of the Personal Bureau; during Russo-Japanese War he was Com. of the 2nd Squad. under Adm. Kamimura and was slightly wounded in the memorable Battle of the Japan Sea; promoted Vice-Adm., and Chief of Port Arthur Naval Station, Feb., '06; appointed Deputy Chief, Naval Staff, '06; Mem. of the Admirals Council in '09; Com. of Maizuru Admiralty '11-13; Admiral '12; on reserve list in Dec., '14. Add. Omote-cho, Koishikawa, Tokyo. — p. 110 ([scan leaf 159](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n159/mode/1up))

**Mita, Kitsu** Auditor of the Bank of Japan, was b. '51 in Shizuoka; entered the Naval Academy at Numazu when a boy, but gave it up owing to ill health; proceeded to France in '73; and then to England to prosecute his study; he was made a clerk, attached to the Japanese Legation at London; an official in the Financial Office '81 on his return; has since been connected with the Bank. Add. Edogawacho, Koish., Tokyo. — p. 110 ([scan leaf 159](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n159/mode/1up))

**Mitsui family, The** Is one of the oldest millionaire families and the most noted hereditary houses of "business kings" in Japan, managing the big family concerns, the Mitsui Bank, Mitsui Bussan Kaisha (Trading Dep't) and Mitsui Mining Co. They are backed with registered nominal capital of 8½ mil. yen and reserve funds amounting to 17 millions. Baron Hachiroemon Mitsui, the head of the family and Pres. of Mitsui Firm, was b. in '57 in Kyoto, and studied at New Brunswick, U.S.A., '72-74 and England, succeeded to the estate in '85, he being the youngest brother of his predecessor who had no issue; was created Baron '00, in consideration of valuable services rendered by the family. He has also much contributed to the cause of the rescue of the poor and ex-convicts. The houses are Hachirojiro, Baron (Dir. of Mitsui Gomei-Kaisha), Tokuemon (Pres. Oji Paper Co.), Takayasu, Baron (Pres. Mitsui Bank), Gen-emon (Pres. Mitsui Bussan, Mining'), Morinosuke (Pres. Shibaura Eng. Works), Yonosuke, (Pres. of Sakai Celluloid Co.). Add. Imai-cho, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 110 ([scan leaf 159](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n159/mode/1up))

**Mitsukuri, Gempachi** D. Litt., Prof. Litt., Imp. Tokyo Univ.; b. '62, grad. Science at Imp. Tokyo Univ. '85, sent to Germany, where owing to weak eyesight, he changed subject of study to history, returning home '93 and has since been connected with the alma mater. Add. Akebono-cho, Komagome, Tokyo. — p. 110 ([scan leaf 159](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n159/mode/1up))

**Miura, Goro** Privy Councillor since '10, Lieut.-Gen. (retired '88), Visc. (cr. '84), b. '46 in Choshu; fought in the Civil War of '77; promoted Lieut.-General '79; afterward Commander of the Tokyo Army Division, &c. It was while he was at Seoul as Japanese Minister '95 that the memorable tragedy of the assassination of the Queen took place; he was arrested on suspicion of complicity, but the suspicion was cleared on examination and soon set free. He is an earnest votary of the Zen doctrine of Buddhism. Add. Naka-Tomisaka, Koishikawa, Tokyo. — p. 110 ([scan leaf 159](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n159/mode/1up))


## Who's Who in Japan

**Miura, Kinnosuke** M.D., Prof., Imp. Univ. of Tokyo. Physician-in-Ordinary to the Court; b. in Tokyo '64; grad. Med. Col. of Univ. '87; prosecuted his study in Germany and America '89 92; was appointed Assis.-Prof. in his alma mater on returning; full Prof. in '95. Add. Surugadai, Kanda, Tokyo. — p. 111 ([scan leaf 160](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n160/mode/1up))

**Miura, Taisuke** Dir. of Ome R'ly Co. etc., b. in Choshu '55; brother of the late Viscount Aoki, studied agriculture at Berlin and wine brewing '74-79; commenced farming on the field purchased by his brother at Koganehara, Chiba, which however was not a success; was engaged '87-89 on the reclaimed foreshore at Kojima, Osaka, undertaken by the Fujita family. Add. Shimorokubancho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 111 ([scan leaf 160](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n160/mode/1up))

**Miura, Tokuichiro** Surg.-Gen. (ret.), M.P. for Miyazaki-ken, Parliamentary Jun. Under-secy. to the War Dept.; b. '56 in Miyazaki-ken; entered the Army in '81 and became Surg. Sub-Lieut. '83 and gradually promoted to present rank '07 and on retired list '12, meanwhile occupying the posts of Dir. of Garrison Hosp. and Sanitary Section, War Office, Surg. Chief at Kwantung Government, then the same of 3rd Army Div.; holder of 3rd Golden Kite. Elected M.P. and Parl. Under-Secy. in '15. Add. Nishi Okubo, near Tokyo. — p. 111 ([scan leaf 160](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n160/mode/1up))

**Miwata, Masako** one of the few lady educationists of great renown, Pres. Miwata Girls' High School; b. '44 at Kyoto. After she was bereft of her husband by death in '78 she took to learning with undivided attention, attaining great eminence in Japanese and Chinese, and enabling her to devote herself to the work of education. By thrift and diligence she amassed enough to build a new school-house and this cherished wish was carried out a number of years ago. Add. Yobancho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 111 ([scan leaf 160](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n160/mode/1up))

**Miyabe, Hisahi** Dir. of Tokyo Woolen Mill, at Oji, b. '44 in Mito; was confined for about five years by his rival faction in the clan, and was released when he was 23. Then after a short career in civil service, he entered business and was connected with the Kawasaki and the One Hundredth Banks, and the Tokyo Rice Exchange; finally he started the present Woolen Factory, '87. He was twice confined on ungrounded suspicion of complicity in peculation either by his fellow Directors or by clerks. Add. Takecho, Shitaya, Tokyo. — p. 111 ([scan leaf 160](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n160/mode/1up))

**Miyabe, Kingo** D. Sc. (Japan), Dr. Sc. (Harvard), Prof. of the Agr'ture Col. and Dir. of Botanical Garden, Sapporo; b. in Tokyo '59; grad. the College '81, and was soon sent to U.S.A. to study botany under Asa Gray; returned home '87; is one of the foremost specialists in Japan in this line. Add. Sapporo, Hokkaido. — p. 111 ([scan leaf 160](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n160/mode/1up))

**Miyagawa, Kozan** originator of the Makuzu, porcelain ware and Art Commissioner to the Imp. Household, b. '43 in Kyoto, 3rd son of the family of master ceramist; removed to Yokohama '70 at the invitation of a purveyor to the Lord of Satsuma who conceived an idea to produce porcelains for export. Kozan's great feat was the discovery of a simple process of painting the unglazed biscuit, and his wares have frequently won highest prizes both at domestic and foreign exhibitions; appointed Art Com. to the Imp. Household '96. Add. Nishi-Ota, Yokohama. — p. 111 ([scan leaf 160](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n160/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Miyagawa, Kyujiro** Chief of Aud. Sec. of the Yokohama Specie Bank, b. '62, in Tokyo. Early grad. the Higher Commercial School of Tokyo, and subsequently entered the Yokohama Specie Bank, to be gradually promoted to the present post. Add. Sendagi Hayashicho, Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 112 ([scan leaf 161](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n161/mode/1up))

**Miyahara, Jiro** Baron (cr. '07), D. Eng. (Japan), Vice-Adm. (ret.), Mem. House of Peers; b. '58 in Shizuoka-ken; Eng.-Lieut. in '83; filled posts of Inspector of Naval Construction, member of the Board for Naval Construction, etc. Was created peer and granted 3rd Class of Golden Kite in connection with Russo-Japanese War; elected to the House in '11. He is inventor of Miyahara Boiler. Add. Omori, near Tokyo. — p. 112 ([scan leaf 161](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n161/mode/1up))

**Miyake, Hiidzu, M.D** Mem. House of Peers, Mem. Imperial Academy, Emeritus Professor of the Imp. Tokyo Univ.; b. '48 in Tokyo; educated in France and was appointed Prof. and Dean of Med. Coll., Imp. Tokyo Univ. Add. Takehaya-cho, Koishikawa, Tokyo. — p. 112 ([scan leaf 161](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n161/mode/1up))

**Miyake, Yujiro, D. Litt. (Japan)** author and journalist, b. '60 at Kanazawa; grad. philosophy at Imp. Tokyo Univ., '81; visited the South Seas and in '03 made a trip round the world. He is a conspicuous figure in the world of letters and journalism, being a writer of great originality, of powerful style and deep thought, though an awkward stammering speaker; wrote many works chiefly of philosophical and literary interest. He was for long chief editor of the Nippon, but quarrelled with its new proprietor, and resigned with the whole staff, in Nov. '03. The fortnightly magazine, Nippon oyobi Nippon-jin, is now the organ of this clique. Add. Shinsaka-machi, Akasaka, Tokyo. — p. 112 ([scan leaf 161](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n161/mode/1up))

**Miyaoka, Naoki** Vice-Adm. (ret. '11), b. '59 in Kanazawa, Kaga; Sub-Lieut. Navy '62; promoted to the present rank after having held posts in the Naval Staff Board, Attaché to Japanese Legation in America, Captain of the Chokai, Tsukuba, and Hashidate, Commander of the Standing Squadron, etc. Add. Nakacho, Akasaka, Tokyo. — p. 112 ([scan leaf 161](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n161/mode/1up))

**Miyaoka, Tsunejiro** Attorney and Counsellor at Law and Registered Patent Agent, b. '65 in Osaka; grad. from Law College Imp. Univ., Tokyo, qualified for legal profession and entered Diplomatic Service '87; Sec. to Japanese Legation, Washington, '92; Charge d'Affaires to U.S.A. '94; First Secy. at Berlin, '94; Charge d'Affairs to Germany and Belgium '97; Minister Resident and Principal Law Officer of the Foreign Office '00; represented Japan before International Arbitral Tribunal of The Hague '04-05; Counsellor of Embassy at Washington with rank of Envoy Extraordinary and Minister Plenipotentiary '06-08; represented Japan at International Opium Conference, Shanghai '09; resigned Government service and started general law practice in Oct., '09. Add. 6 Takiyamacho, Kyobashi, Tokyo. — p. 112 ([scan leaf 161](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n161/mode/1up))

**Miyazaki, Michisaburo, D.C.L. (Japan)** Prof. at Imp. Univ., Tokyo, b. '55 in Ise; grad. Law Col. of Tokyo Univ. '80, and subsequently entered service of the Education Dep't; went over to Germany in '84 for study; Prof. at his alma mater with an additional office of Councillor to the Legislative Bureau on his return home in '88. Has charge of the chair of History of Legislation. Add. Tamachi, Ichigaya, Tokyo. — p. 112 ([scan leaf 161](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n161/mode/1up))

**Miyoshi, Manabu** Botanist, D. Sc. (Japan), Prof. at the Imp. Tokyo University; b. '61 in Tokyo; grad. Col. Science of Imperial Tokyo Univ. '90 and further studied in its post-grad. course, and in Germany '92-95; Prof. at his alma mater on returning. Add. Nishikata-machi, Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 112 ([scan leaf 161](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n161/mode/1up))


## Who's Who in Japan

**Mizumachi, Kesaroku** Vice-Gov. Bank of Japan, b. '63 in Saga ; grad. Law, Imp. Univ. Tokyo in '89 ; Junior Councillor of the Treasury '91 ; despatched to Europe to investigate financial and economic situation in '98 ; filled chair of civil code at the alma mater as add. post, but resigned it '01 ; Dir. Finance Bureau '04 with add. post of Temporary Pension Adjustment Bureau ; Vice-Minister in '06 ; Agent at London '06-11. Add. Nagatacho, Tokyo. — p. 113 ([scan leaf 162](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n162/mode/1up))

**Mizuno, Rentaro, D.C.L** Crown Member of House of Peers ; b. '68 in Akita ; grad. Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ., in '92 ; entered Home Office '95 as Councillor and Personal Secretary ; sent abroad '96 to investigate copyright affairs and attended as Japanese delegate at the World's Conference for the Protection of Right of Industrial Property held at Brussels ; returned home '98 and framed the draft of the Copyright Law. Dir. of Public Works Bureau '10-12 ; Vice-Minister of Home Office '13-14. Again in '16. Add. Shirokane Sarumachi, Tokyo. — p. 113 ([scan leaf 162](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n162/mode/1up))

**Mochizuki, Kotaro, M.P** Editor and Prop. of an English News-Agency and of a Financial and Economic Monthly, was b. in '65 ; edu. the Keio Gijuku and also Middle Temple, coming home about '98 ; meanwhile he followed Prince Yamagata when he attended the coronation ceremony of the Czar '96. Has been three times returned to the House (Doshikai), visited abroad with his wife in '10. Add. Omori, near Tokyo. — p. 113 ([scan leaf 162](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n162/mode/1up))

**Mogi, Sobei** Pres. of the Mogi Ginko, was b. '67 in Gumma-ken : adopted in '74 by Yasuhei Mogi, millionaire in Yokohama. Add. Bentencho, Yokohama. — p. 113 ([scan leaf 162](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n162/mode/1up))

**Mori, Goro, Baron (cr. '92)** Member of the House of Peers, Aud. of Railway Car Co. and Nichi-Ei Hydro-Elec. Co. etc., b. in '71 in Tokyo ; created a Baron in recognition of his father's service in the grand work of Restoration ; educated in England '88-96. Add. Hommura, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 113 ([scan leaf 162](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n162/mode/1up))

**Mori, Moto-aki, Prince** the House of a former Daimyo founded by Motonari, (1497-1571 A.D.) which enjoyed fief of 369,000 koku at Choshu. The clan, together with the House of Shimazu, was chiefly instrumental in overthrowing the Tokugawa Regency and in consummating the Restoration. The present head was b. in '65. Add. Takanawa, Tokyo. — p. 113 ([scan leaf 162](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n162/mode/1up))

**Mori, Rintaro, M.D., D. Litt. (Japan)** Surg.-Gen., I.A.U.C. and ex-Dir. of Medical Affairs, War Office ; b. in Iwami '60 ; is probably more widely known as litterateur who writes a highly finished style. He is equally famous in his official career, having been connected with military hygiene after he came back '88 from Germany where he had stayed about four years to complete his medical studies. Principal medical officer of the Tokyo Division '03-07 ; occupied the Directorship '08-16. He has translated several German works and has also written a number of literary works. Retired in Mar., '16. Add. Sendagi, Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 113 ([scan leaf 162](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n162/mode/1up))

**Morimura, Ichizaemon, Baron (cr. '15)** banker and exporter ; b. Oct. '39 in Tokyo ; worked his way from obscurity to attain his present eminence in business circles having been engaged in export and import business for two decades and more, maintaining branch stores at New York and other places ; runs the Morimura Bank, and was once a director of the Bank of Japan. Add. Takanawa, Shiba, Tokyo. — p. 113 ([scan leaf 162](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n162/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Morishita, Gannan** President of the Tokyo Mercantile Agency, was b. at Wakayama in '52, graduated from Keio Gijuku in '74, appointed Director of former Mitsubishi Commercial School founded by the Mitsubishi in '78, appointed junior secretary of the Treasury in '81, left it the following year and joined the staff of the Jiji; started the Mercantile Agency in '95, toured in Europe and America '07. Is also Auditor of Imperial Life Ins. Co. Add. Shirokane Sankocho, Tokyo. — p. 114 ([scan leaf 163](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n163/mode/1up))

**Morita, Mokichi** Dir. of Sakai Celluloid Co. etc., b. '66 in Hyogo-ken; grad. Law, Imp. Univ., '90, and held posts at prefectural offices and also Central Government till appointed Director of Sanitary Bureau '02, to be transferred to the Department of Com. and Agr. as Dir. of Ind. and Com. Bur. the following year. Has travelled through Europe and America twice on official business and also visited India in '07. Add. Akasaka Daimachi, Tokyo. — p. 114 ([scan leaf 163](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n163/mode/1up))

**Moriya, Konosuke** lawyer, M.P. (Doshikai), and Dir. of the Keihin Electric Rly. Co.; b. in Okayama in '61; after graduating from the Tokyo Law Col. he passed the Barristers examination in '83 and established his office. Add. Ginza, Tokyo. — p. 114 ([scan leaf 163](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n163/mode/1up))

**Moroi, Rokuro** Consul-General at Honolulu, b. '72 in Saitama-ken; grad. Law College, Imp. Tokyo Univ. '96; passed the diplomatic service examination '99; and dispatched to Shanghai, Suchou, and London; Sec. at Foreign Office '06, at Berlin '12; present post '16. Add. Japanese Consulate-General, Hawaii. — p. 114 ([scan leaf 163](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n163/mode/1up))

**Motoda, Hajime** M.P. of unbroken record, lawyer. b. '58 in Oita; was a leader of the National Association which was organized in '92, by the late Marquis Saigo and Viscount Shinagawa as organ of the clan statesmen; was one of those who rallied round the standard raised by late Prince Ito when he organized the Seiyukai in '00; Vice-President of the House of Rep., '98-02; Director of Colonization Bureau in '11; Minister of Communications, '13-14. Add. Kioicho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 114 ([scan leaf 163](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n163/mode/1up))

**Motoda, Sakunoshin** B.A., M.A., Ph. D. (Philadelphia). Dir. of Rikkyo Daigaku, a prominent Christian teacher; b. '62 in Kurume; stayed in America '86-96; studying Divinity, Philosophy etc. at Kenyon College, University of Penn. and Columbia University and also attending to religious work. He attended the Jubilee Convention of Y.M.C.A. held at Boston '01 as representative of Japan and in '08 travelled through India as a lecturer, attended the General Convention of the Church in America held in '10. Add. Nishi-Okubo, near Tokyo. — p. 114 ([scan leaf 163](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n163/mode/1up))

**Motono, Eikichiro** Mng.-Dir. of the Yomiuri, Prof. at Waseda Univ.; was b. in '64 in Hizen; grad. from the Imp. Univ. in '00. Course of Applied Chemistry; engineer to the Yokohama Gas Works '90-95, examiner at the Patent Bureau, Dep't of Agr. and Commerce '96-00. Add. Mita Tsunamachi, Shiba, Tokyo. — p. 114 ([scan leaf 163](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n163/mode/1up))

**Motono, Ichiro** Visc. (cr. '16; Baron '07), D.C.L. (Japan), Minister for Foreign Affairs; b. '62 in Saga-ken; was translator in the Foreign Office '90, then Councillor '93, transferred to the Secretaryship of Legation at Petrograd '96; appointed Minister-Resident at Brussels '98; transferred to be Minister at Paris '01. He was a junior delegate of Japan in the Peace Conference held at The Hague '99. Ambassador at Petrograd '08-16; Foreign Minister, Nov. '16. Add. Foreign Office, Tokyo. — p. 114 ([scan leaf 163](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n163/mode/1up))


## Who's Who in Japan

**Motoyama, Hakun** sculptor, b. '71 in Kochi, studied the art under Prof. Takamura Ko-un and graduated from the Tokyo Fine Art Academy '95; he filled for a short while the chair of assist. teacher at the alma mater, and at some places. Gave up teaching, having been commissioned by Baron Iwamura Michitoshi to cast bronze images of modern notables who took distinguished part in the grand work of the Restoration, and has already produced images of late Count Goto, Prince Ito and some others. Add. Haramachi, Koishikawa, Tokyo. — p. 115 ([scan leaf 164](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n164/mode/1up))

**Motoyama, Hikoichi** Dir. of the Osaka Mainichi and Tokyo Nichinichi, b. '53 in Kumamoto; grad. of the Keio-gijuku; was once in the service of the Fujita firm; established the Osaka Mainichi in '92; also occupies the chairs of Dir. of Nankai Rly. Co., Meiji Life Ins. Co., &c. Add. the Osaka Mainichi, Osaka. — p. 115 ([scan leaf 164](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n164/mode/1up))

**Mudaguchi, Gengaku** Pres. and Dir. of several Cos., b. '47 in Saga; an official of the Dept. of Education, and of Agriculture and Commerce, but resigned when Count Okuma (then Mr.) left the Govt. in '81. From that time till '93 he was engaged in various undertakings with the Count's backing, but generally ended in failure, but tide of fortune began to set in his favour with his appointment to Pres. of the then Tokyo Horse Tram Co., which soon rose to prosperity under his management. With the conversion of horse trams into electric trams and the amalgamation of the three Tokyo Street Electric Trams, he was for a while Pres. of the new concern in '06. Add. Kami Osaki, near Tokyo. — p. 115 ([scan leaf 164](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n164/mode/1up))

**Murai, Kichibei** Pres. of Murai Bank, b. '64, in Kaga, and adopted by the late Kichiemon Murai who was running a small tobacco shop in Kyoto. The decision to manufacture cigarettes which had begun to come in a large quantity from abroad was a turning point in his career. He learned the art of cigarette-making in America and his brand of cigarettes soon spread all over Japan, and began to go to China and elsewhere in a large quantity. The American Tobacco Co. then offered to form a trust with him and the result was the Murai Brothers & Co. with a capital of yen 12,000,000 of which Murai's assets were valued at yen 5,000,000. The concern was purchased by the Government when it launched tobacco monopoly a year or two after. The Murais then started the Murai Bank, capital yen 1,000,000. They also undertake printing and cotton thread business. Add. Nagatacho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 115 ([scan leaf 164](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n164/mode/1up))

**Murai, Teinosuke** Ph. D. (Yale), Mng.-Dir. Murai Bank, Dir. of Japan Fertilizer Co., etc., was b. '70, in Wakayama-ken, and was adopted by the Murai family. Add. Kami Rokubancho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 115 ([scan leaf 164](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n164/mode/1up))

**Murai, Yasukata** Dir. of the Yokohama Kiito Gomei Kaisha; b. '54 in Hyogo-ken; engaged in foreign trade at Kobe from the opening of the country to foreign intercourse, and was New York agent for several firms; m. an American lady. Add. Tori Itchome, Nihom., Tokyo. — p. 115 ([scan leaf 164](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n164/mode/1up))

**Murakami, Kakuichi** Vice-Adm., Com. of the Third Fleet; b. '61 in Saga-ken. Early entered the Navy and had long served as Adjutant to the Naval Minister before he was promoted to Rear-Admiral in '07; and Sectional Chief at the Education Board and the Construction Bureau '07-12; Vice-Adm. '12, transferred to Kure; placed on waiting list temporarity in connection with the Bribery Case in '14; but was soon reappointed to Chief of Naval Technical Office. Add. Naval Office. — p. 115 ([scan leaf 164](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n164/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Muraki, Masayoshi** Baron (cr. '07). Lieut.-Gen. (ret.). Member of House of Peers; b. '56 in Kochi, Sub-Lieut. of Art. '76, studied in France '80-83, was afterwards Prof. at the Cadets School, Per. Sec. to the War Minister, etc., Lieut.-Gen. in '96. Add. Nakano-cho, Ichigaya, Tokyo. — p. 116 ([scan leaf 165](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n165/mode/1up))

**Murano, Tsune-emon** M.P. (Seiyukai), was b. '59 in Tokyo, and has sat in the House seven times; is on the Board of Directors of Yokohama Railway and Yokohama Warehouse Cos. Add. Aokihirodai, Yokohama. — p. 116 ([scan leaf 165](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n165/mode/1up))

**Muraoka, Han-ichi** D. Sc., Emeritus Prof. Imp. Univ., Kyoto; was b. '53 in Tottori; official of Edu. Dept. '75, studied physics in Berlin '78-81, Prof., Imp. Univ., First Higher School, etc., and finally transferred to Kyoto Imp. Univ., resigned in '13. Add. Tonodanteramachi, Kyoto. — p. 116 ([scan leaf 165](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n165/mode/1up))

**Murase, Haruo** D.C.L. (Japan), Prof. Tokyo Higher Com. School, etc., Mng.-Dir. of the Imp. Marine Transport and Fire Ins. Co.; was b. '71 in Fukui-ken; studied at Tokyo Higher Commercial School, Antwerp Higher Com. School and Leipsic University. Add. Haruki-cho, Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 116 ([scan leaf 165](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n165/mode/1up))

**Murata, Atsushi** Lieut.-Gen. (ret.), ex-Chief of the Fortification Dep't, War Office, '09-14; b. '54 in Shizuoka; Captain '86; studied tactics in France and Italy '86-89; Major '91; Lieutenant-Colonel '95; then Military Attaché to the Japanese Legation in Petrograd; promoted to Lieut.-General in July '09. Add. Nakacho, Yotsuya, Tokyo. — p. 116 ([scan leaf 165](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n165/mode/1up))

**Murata, Tamotsu** ex-Crown Mem. of House of Peers '90-14; b. '42 in Osaka, once an official of Justice Dep't, and was dispatched to England '79 to investigate criminal laws, etc.; has been active as fishery propagandist since '90 when he resigned Govt. service. Add. Shintomicho, Kyobashi, Tokyo. — p. 116 ([scan leaf 165](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n165/mode/1up))

**Murata, Tsuneyoshi** Lieut.-Gen. (retired), Baron, inventor of the Murata rifle, Mem. House of Peers since '90; b. '38 in Satsuma; entered the military service early, and took part in the Civil War of '77 as a Major. Add. Sambancho, Kojimachi-ku, Tokyo. — p. 116 ([scan leaf 165](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n165/mode/1up))

**Murayama, Ryuhei** proprietor of the Tokyo and Osaka Asahi newspapers, b. at Wakayama '50; is a "Harmsworth" of Japan in a small way, having raised by his shrewd business management the two Asahis to the present stage of prosperity and importance. Once sat in the House for Osaka-fu. Add. Osaka Asahi Office, Osaka. — p. 116 ([scan leaf 165](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n165/mode/1up))

**Murota, Yoshibumi** Member of the House of Peers, Lord-in-Waiting of Kinkei Hall, And. of Horai Life Ins. Office, Kanegafuchi Cotton Sp. Co., and Sankyo Co.; b. in '47 at Mito; entered the Foreign Office in which he steadily rose till he was appointed Resident-Minister at Mexico '02-04, resigned the service and then entered business. Add. Nippori, near Tokyo. — p. 116 ([scan leaf 165](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n165/mode/1up))

**Muto, Sanji** Mng. Dir. Kanegafuchi Spinning Co.; b. '67 in Gifu, grad. Keiogijiku about '90 and has since been connected with business; rewarded with Blue Ribbon Medal in '10 for his service in promoting spinning industry and yen 100,000 disbursed for protection of operatives. Add. Tarumi, Akashi, Hyogo-ken. — p. 116 ([scan leaf 165](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n165/mode/1up))

**Mutsu, Hirokichi** Count, ex-Min. to Belgium; b. '69, son of the late Count Mutsu (who distinguished himself in treaty revision and Shimonoseki treaty); the new Count studied in England for several years beginning '87 and returned home as Barrister; passed exam. of dipl. and cons. service '95; 3rd Sec. of Legation (U.S.A.) '96; 2nd Sec. at Rome '00; transferred to be 1st Sec. at Japanese Embassy at London '04, and then appointed Sec. on leave of absence, having returned home in '10-12; transferred to Belgium but soon resigned in '15. Add. Kamakura, Sagami. — p. 116 ([scan leaf 165](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n165/mode/1up))


## Who's Who in Japan

**Nabeshima, Keijiro** mem. House of Peers (nom. '16) and ex-Minister Plen. Env.-Extraordinary to Belgium, b. '60 in Saga; attaché in '85, attached to Legation, London in '87, Sec. at head office in '92, was on suite of Prince Arisugawa in late Queen Victoria's Diamond Jubilee in '97, 1st Legation at Sec. Washington in '00 and at London in '02, Sec. at head office in '03, Chief of Foreign Affairs, Korea, in '07-09. Add. Higashi Toriizaka, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 117 ([scan leaf 166](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n166/mode/1up))

**Nabeshima, Naohiro** Marquis (cr. '84, formerly Daimyo of Saga, Hizen), b. 48; studied in England for several years from '71; Min. at Rome '80-82, Master of Ceremonies at the Court. The Marchioness is Pres. of Volunteer Nurse Association. Add. Nagatacho, Tokyo. — p. 117 ([scan leaf 166](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n166/mode/1up))

**Nagai, Matsuzo** Secretary of Foreign Office, b. in '77 in Aichi-ken; grad. from Imp. Tokyo Univ. '02; passed dip. & con. exam. '02; Eleve Consul at Tientsin, at New York and Washington '03-07, Consul-Gen. at San Francisco, '08-12, appointed Personal Secretary '15 and Chief of Telegraph Section '16. Add. Foreign Office. — p. 117 ([scan leaf 166](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n166/mode/1up))

**Nagai, Nagayoshi** D. Sc. and D. Phr. (Japan), Prof. Med. Col., Imp. Tokyo Univ.; b. '45 in Tokushima; was ordered to study his speciality in Germany '70-33; appointed Dir. of the German Language School, Tokyo. Mrs. Nagai is a German lady. Add. Minami-cho, Aoyama, Tokyo. — p. 117 ([scan leaf 166](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n166/mode/1up))

**Nagai, Ryutaro** Prof. at Waseda University and editor of the Shin Nippon (monthly magazine); was b. in '81 at Kanazawa; grad. from Waseda (politics) in '05, and further studied in England and Germany from '06-09. Add. Waseda University, Tokyo. — p. 117 ([scan leaf 166](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n166/mode/1up))

**Nagamatsu, Tokuboku** Baron, Member of House of Peers, Dir. of Tokyo Fire, Marine Transport Ins. Co.; was b. '64 in Tokyo, eldest son of Chokan, 1st Baron; studied at the Peers' School, Imp. Tokyo Univ., and Berlin, whence he returned in '86; was for a while Prof. at the Peers' School. Add. Aoyama Minami-cho, Tokyo. — p. 117 ([scan leaf 166](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n166/mode/1up))

**Nagamori, Tokichiro** b. in Saga '60; grad. Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ.; long served as Public Proculator at Tokyo District Court and elsewhere, resigned '00; appointed Chief Councillor of the Treasury '01 but soon the post was abolished; he attracted wide attention for his reclamation undertaking in Korea in '04. Add. Nakashibuya, near Tokyo. — p. 117 ([scan leaf 166](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n166/mode/1up))

**Naganuma, Shukei** sculptor, Mem. of Japan Art Committee; b. '57 in Iwate-ken; studied sculpture in Italy; was Prof. in the Tokyo Fine Art Academy after his return home; well known in the art circles. Add. No. 109 Omote-cho, Koishikawa, Tokyo. — p. 117 ([scan leaf 166](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n166/mode/1up))

**Nagaoka, Gaishi** Lieut.-Gen. (ret.'15) and Pres. of the National Aviation Association, b. '58 in Yamaguchi, and adopted into the present family; Sub-Lieut. '79; entered Mil. Staff Col. '83; rewarded with the 4th Order of Golden Kite in the Japan-China War in which he was on the Staff of Gen. Nozu; ordered to proceed to Europe on official tour, '99; Maj.-Gen. '02; Vice-Chief of Staff during Russo-Japanese War before he was succeeded by Lieut.-Gen. Fukushima in Mar., '06; Com. of 1st Brigade Bureau Chief in War Office; Lieut.-Gen. '09, and finally to Com'der of 16th Div. Add. Harajuku, Sendagaya, Tokyo. — p. 117 ([scan leaf 166](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n166/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Nagasaki, Seigo** ex-Court-Councillor and ex-Director-General of the Bureau of Supplies, b. at Kagoshima, '55; studied in America and England; returned home about '80 and served in the Imp. Court till '14; bearer of the First Class Order of the Sacred Treasure, the Grand Cross of Royal Victorian Order, of the Knight Commander of St. Michael and St. George, and of other foreign Order. Add. Shimo-Rokubancho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 118 ([scan leaf 167](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n167/mode/1up))

**Nagashima, Washitaro, M.P. (Seiyukai)** from Chiba-ken and Barrister in Tokyo, b. in Sakushu in '68; grad. Law Imp. Tokyo Univ. '90, appointed junior Councillor of Justice, resigned '93; and then began practice, travelled abroad and delegated at the International Parliamentary conference in '09. Add. Tsukiji, Tokyo. — p. 118 ([scan leaf 167](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n167/mode/1up))

**Naito, Hisahiro** Pres. of Nippon Petroleum Co., Dir. of Central Pet. Co. and Niigata Iron Works; b. '59 in Niigata-ken; elected Mem. of Prefectural Assembly at his 20th year. M.P. in '94. Organized Nippon Pet. Co. in '88; despatched on official mission to inspect petroleum business to America and Europe in '97 and again to U.S.A. in '04. Add. Shimo-Miyabicho, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 118 ([scan leaf 167](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n167/mode/1up))

**Nakada, Keigi** Dir. of Yokohama Cable Mfg. Co. and Taiheiyo Life Ins. Co., b. '58 in Ishikawa-ken; junior Sec. of the Dep't of Foreign Affairs about '85; Sec. of Japanese Legation at Peking; Per. Sec. to the Minister of Foreign Affairs; Sec. of German Legation and others; Chief of the Political Bureau, Foreign Office '95, which he resigned in '98; nominated member of the House of Peers '00. Add. Aoyama, Tokyo. — p. 118 ([scan leaf 167](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n167/mode/1up))

**Nakada, Kinkichi** Director of the Sumitomo General Head Office and Mng.-Dir. of the Sumitomo Bank, b. '60 in Akita; grad. Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ. in '90; Judge at various Courts which post resigned and became Manager of Besshi Copper Mine of the Sumitomo; transferred to the present post in '10. Add. Shimacho, Higashi-ku, Osaka. — p. 118 ([scan leaf 167](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n167/mode/1up))

**Nakagawa, Kenjiro** Pres. of the Women's Higher Normal School, Tokyo, b. '70 in Kyoto-fu; grad. from the Tokyo Kaisei Gakko; Prof. at the Peers' School and the Tokyo Girls' Normal School, etc., with an additional office of Inspector of the Educational Department; Prof. of Sendai Technical School '06-10 and then appointed to present post. Add. Nishikatamachi, Komagome, Tokyo. — p. 118 ([scan leaf 167](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n167/mode/1up))

**Nakahama, To-ichiro** M.D. and Dir. of Kwaisei Hospital, Tokyo, b. '57 in Kochi-ken; is a son of the celebrated fisherman Manjiro who was drifted to America and came back on Perry's ships; is a specialist of internal diseases. Add. Shimo-Nibancho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 118 ([scan leaf 167](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n167/mode/1up))

**Nakahashi, Tokugoro** M.P., ex-Pres., Osaka Shosen Kaisha and Pres. of Ujigawa Electric Co., etc.; b. '60 at Kanagawa, grad. the Imp. Tokyo Univ. '84; was long an official in the Dep't of Com. where he occupied the post of Director of the Railway Bureau, which he resigned about '98 to take charge of the O.S.K. which he has raised to the present state of activity. Add. Tennoji, Osaka. — p. 118 ([scan leaf 167](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n167/mode/1up))

**Nakamatsu, Morio** Patent Attorney and Barrister, b. '65 in Wakayama; grad. Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ. '91; entered the Dep't of Agr. and Com. as Councillor and Patent Examiner, then as Sectional Chief of the Bureau; Dir. of the Patent Bureau '09-13. Add. Omori, near Tokyo. — p. 118 ([scan leaf 167](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n167/mode/1up))


## Who's Who

**Nakamizo, Tokutaro** Baron (cr. '07), Vice-Adm. (ret.), Mem. of House of Peers; b. in Saga in '57; Midshipman in '78; Captain '97; Chief Staff at Maizuru in '01; Naval attache to the Legation in London '02-03, and promoted to be Rear-Adm. in '03; Chief Staff at Kure in '04; placed on retired list '10. Add. Osaki, near Tokyo. — p. 119 ([scan leaf 168](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n168/mode/1up))

**Nakamuda, Kuranosuke** Visc. (cr. '84), Vice-Adm. (ret.), Privy Councillor; b. '31 in Saga; is one of the pioneer naval officers, and did much in laying the foundation of the future fleet of Japan. Add. Aoyama, Tokyo. — p. 119 ([scan leaf 168](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n168/mode/1up))

**Nakamura, Satoru** Baron (cr. '07), General, and Governor-General of Kwantung; b. 54 in Shiga-ken; Sub-Lieut. '75; took part in the Japan-China War as Reg. Commander; Maj.-Gen., '99; commanded the forlornhope at Port Arthur on Nov. 26, when he was slightly wounded; Lieut.-Gen. '06 and appointed Chief of Staff of the Military Education Board, Com. 15th Division '07; Aide-de-Camp to the late Emperor Meiji '08-12; Commander of Tokyo Garrison in '12-15; Gen. in Mar., '15 and transferred to present office. Add. Port Arthur, Manchuria. — p. 119 ([scan leaf 168](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n168/mode/1up))

**Nakamura, Shingo** D.C.L. (Japan), Prof. at the Waseda Univ., etc.; b. '70 in Niigata Prefecture; grad. Law from Tokyo Imp. Univ. in '94 and continued his study at its University Hall; then in England, Germany and France (International Law and History of Diplomacy); one of the "Jingo Professors" in the Russo-Japanese War. Add. Takata, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 119 ([scan leaf 168](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n168/mode/1up))

**Nakamura, Shukei** metal-carver, gold-medalist (Paris) and ex-Prof., Gov. Fine Art Academy; b. '57 at Ichinoseki; studied the art under an Italian master then in the employ of the Government; proceeded to Italy '81 in company of the then Italian Minister and studied the art at Venice, returning home '87; revisited Italy ten years later in charge of the exhibition business of the Japan Fine Art Association. Add. Omote-cho, Akasaka, Tokyo. — p. 119 ([scan leaf 168](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n168/mode/1up))

**Nakamura, Tatsutaro** D.E., Professor of architecture at the Imperial University, Tokyo, b. in Tokyo in '60; graduated from the now defunct Engineering College in '82; was assistant architect in the work of building the Imperial Palace '83-86, then Professor of the present University, besides holding a post in the Home Office; studied in Europe in '92-93; sent in '97 to Assam, India, to study architecture as affected by earthquake, and also to Naples in '09 on a similar mission. Add. Haramachi, Koi., Tokyo. — p. 119 ([scan leaf 168](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n168/mode/1up))

**Nakamura, Yujiro** Baron, Lieut.-General (ret.), Governor of South Manchuria Railway, Mem. of House of Peers; was b. '52 in Miye-ken; Sub-Lieut. in '74 and through gradual promotion, to the present high position. Once held the post of Vice-Minister of War, Head of the Board of Military Affairs, and Chief of the Gov. Steel Foundry in '06-14; the present post since '14. Add. Nakacho, Yotsuya, Tokyo. — p. 119 ([scan leaf 168](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n168/mode/1up))

**Nakamura, Zeko** b. '65 in Hiroshima-ken; graduated from law at the Imperial Tokyo University '93; appointed an official in the Governor-General's Office, Formosa, where he rose to be Director of the Industrial Bureau; transferred in '06 to be the Dir. of Civil Affairs Bureau, the Governor-General of Kwantung succeeded Baron Goto as President of the South Manchuria R'ly, '08-13. Add. Shimo Shibuya, Tokyo. — p. 119 ([scan leaf 168](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n168/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Nakano, Buyei** one of leading business men in Tokyo, Chairman of the Tokyo Chamber of Commerce, Pres. of Nisshin Life Ins. Co., Dir. of Ishikari Colliery, Hakodate Hydro-Elec. Cos., etc.; b. '49 at Takamatsu; before '81 he was a civil official but resigned the post and entered business. Was for a long time Director of Tokyo Tramway Co. He sat in the Lower House from '90 to '02 as a Progressive M.P. He repeatedly visited U.S.A. on the business men's and other missions. Add. Moto-machi, Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 120 ([scan leaf 169](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n169/mode/1up))

**Nakao, Yu** Vice-Adm. (ret.), ex-Dir. of Naval Hydrographic Bureau; b. '56 in Hiroshima; midshipman in '78, and promoted Rear-Adm. in '07; at one time Instructor at Naval Academy; Staff-Officer attached to the Imperial Headquarters and afterward Vice-Com. of the Izumi at the time of China-Japan War; holds 3rd class of Golden Kite for service in Japan-Russia War. Add. Nagasaka-cho, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 120 ([scan leaf 169](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n169/mode/1up))

**Nakashoji, Ren** Crown Mem. House of Peers; b. '66 in Tokushima-ken; studied law at a private school and passed Judge and Procurator Exam.; was Judge in the assassination case of Kim Okun and the Tokyo Waterpipe scandal; appointed Chief Councillor of the Dep't of Justice '01-02; Director, Seoul-Fusan R'ly '03-04; Dir., Police Bureau, Home Office, '04-06; sent abroad '10; Vice-Minister of Communications '06-11; Minister of Agr. and Com. Dec., '12-Feb., '13; and Min. again since Oct., '16. Add. Hiro-o, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 120 ([scan leaf 169](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n169/mode/1up))

**Nakaya, Hirokichi** Pres. of the Japan Dye-Stuff Co., b. in Kyoto '71; grad. Law Col. of Imp. Tokyo Univ. '96; entered the service of the Dep't of Communications; Secretary of the Dep't, Head of Postal and Savings Bureau; Per. Sec. to the Minister of Com., senior Councillor and finally Chief of newly created Electric Bureau, '09-14; Vice-Min. of Communications Apr.-Nov., '14. Add. Hara-machi, Koishikawa, Tokyo. — p. 120 ([scan leaf 169](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n169/mode/1up))

**Nakayama, Hidesaburo** D. Eng., Prof. Civil Engineering at the Imp. Univ. of Tokyo; b. in '67 in Mikawa; grad. from the University with honour in '83; engineer to the Kwansai R'ly Co. for some years; Prof. at the alma mater since '90 down to the present, railway and hydraulic engineering being his speciality which subject he studied further in Germany and other countries in '96-98. Add. Nishikata-machi, Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 120 ([scan leaf 169](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n169/mode/1up))

**Nakayama, Takamaro** Marquis, of a former courtier family, founded in the 12th century; b. '52 and filled till quite recently post of Lord Steward to the Crown Prince. Add. Aoyama Minami-cho, Tokyo. — p. 120 ([scan leaf 169](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n169/mode/1up))

**Nakazawa, Iwata** D. Eng., Chemist and Pres. of Kyoto Higher Technical School; b. '58 in Echizen; graduated chemistry at the Imp. Tokyo Univ. in '79; further studied in Germany '83-91, to be appointed Professor at the alma mater. He has been connected with the Patent Office and also several manufacturing works. Add. Kyoto Higher Tech. School. — p. 120 ([scan leaf 169](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n169/mode/1up))

**Nambu, Kameo** Baron (cr. '96), Privy Councillor, b. '45 in Tosa, 2nd son of a retainer of that clan; took some active part in the work of Restoration; was appointed a junior judge '72, and subsequently rose steadily in the legal service till he occupied post of chief Judge at the Tokyo Appeal Court '91 and promoted to the Pres. of Supreme Court '96. Add. Hommuracho, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 120 ([scan leaf 169](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n169/mode/1up))


## Who's Who

**Nambu, Koshin** Dir. of the Imp. Foresty Bureau, Home Office, was b. '64 in Kyoto, adopted by Baron Kameo Nambu ; grad. Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ. in '90 ; Secretary of the Home Office '91, Director of Public Works Bureau in '07. Add. Hommuracho Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 121 ([scan leaf 170](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n170/mode/1up))

**Nambu, Kyugo** Dr. Eng. (Japan), Director of the Mitsubishi Firm ; b. in '55 in Fukui ; graduated at the Imperial University in '74 ; was among the first batch of students sent abroad by the Gov. for prosecution of study and he studied mining in U.S.A. '75-80 ; was engaged the following year by the Mitsubishi to take charge of Takashima coal mine. Has been connected with this firm ever since. Add. Benten-cho, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 121 ([scan leaf 170](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n170/mode/1up))

**Nambu, Shimpei** Lieut.-Gen., Commander of 2nd Division at Sendai ; b. '56 in Ishikawa-ken ; Sub-Lieut. '77, Col. '99, Maj.-Gen. shortly before Russo-Japanese War in which as Com. of a Brigade of the 3rd Div., he won universal renown for having resisted one of the enemy's corps at Likanpao on Mar. 8, '05 in the great battle of Mukden ; Lieut.-Gen. in '11 and appointed Com. of Gendarmie, Tokyo ; transferred to present post in '15. Add. Sendai. — p. 121 ([scan leaf 170](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n170/mode/1up))

**Nanjo, Fumio** D. Litt. (Japan), b. '49 in Mino ; grad. Oxford Univ. in '84 where he studied Sanskrit under Max Müller ; was appointed to chair of Sanskrit at the Imp. Tokyo Univ. '95-97, visited China and India '97, was abbot of a temple at Echizen, Pres. of the Budd. School at Nagoya '83, Prof. at the Peers' Female School '90, Dir. of Shinshu Mid. School at Kyoto '94, Pres. of Shinshu Univ. '13. Add. Shimo-Niban-cho, Koji-machi, Tokyo. — p. 121 ([scan leaf 170](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n170/mode/1up))

**Naruse, Ninzo** President of the Nippon Women's University, b. '60 in Yamaguchi-ken ; was primary school teacher before he started the Baikwa Girls' School at Osaka '78 ; started a similar school at Niigata '86-90 and with its close proceeded to America where he investigated female education, returning home '96. He then actively canvassed among wealthy classes, representing necessity of establishing a higher female institution. The movement bore fruit and the present school was opened in April '01. Add. Nippon Women's Univ., Mejiro, Tokyo. — p. 121 ([scan leaf 170](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n170/mode/1up))

**Naruse, Seikyo** Dir. of 15th (Peers') Bank, was b. in Sanuki in '68 ; studied at Keiogijuku, then at Bryant and Stratton Bus. School (Chicago), Cornell Univ., returning home '90, to enter the Yokohama Specie Bank, and then the present bank in '98. Add. Sanko-cho, Shiba, Tokyo. — p. 121 ([scan leaf 170](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n170/mode/1up))

**Nashiba, Tokioki** Baron (cr. '07), Vice-Adm. (ret.), mem. House of Peers ; b. '50 in Yamaguchi-ken ; Sub-Lieut. Navy '80 and gradually promoted to Vice-Adm. Took active part in Sino-Japanese war, Boxers' trouble, and Russo-Japanese war. For his services in the last war, in which he was Com. of 1st Squadron, he was created a Peer and decorated with 3rd class G.K. Add. Iriyamazu, near Tokyo. — p. 121 ([scan leaf 170](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n170/mode/1up))

**Nawa, Matahachiro** Vice-Adm., Com.-in-Chief of Maizuru Admiralty ; b. '63 in Fukui ; grad. from Naval Cadet School in '83, Capt. in '05, Rear-Adm. in '09, Vice-Adm. '12 ; fought in Sino-Japanese War, Boxers Trouble and Russo-Japanese War ; Chief of Naval Education Board and Mem. of Admirals Council till his appointment to Com. of 2nd Fleet in Feb., '15 ; present post Dec. '15. Add. Admiralty, Maizuru. — p. 121 ([scan leaf 170](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n170/mode/1up))

**Nawa, Yasushi** entomologist, Pres. Nawa Entomological Institute, at Gifu ; b. '57 in Gifu-ken ; grad. Gifu Agri. school in '82 and devoted him- self to the study of insects besides teaching at schools; started above institute in '96 and his specimens have won high class medals in domestic and foreign exhibitions. Damage of crops caused by cicadula sexnotata in '96 made him and his work famous. Add. Nawa Entomological Institute, Gifu Park, Gifu. — p. 121 ([scan leaf 170](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n170/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Nedzu, Kaichiro** M.P., Pres., of the Tokyo Rice Exchange, Kabuto Beer Brewing Co., Dir. of the Bō-sō R'ly Co., etc.; 2nd son of Toyemon Nedzu, was b. '60 in Yamanashi-ken. Came up for study to Tokyo in his youth, but to return home to attend to family affairs, owing to frail health of elder brother; took active part in village and provincial affairs. Has been returned from his native district in the last two general elections; was one of the business-men who visited U.S.A. in the fall of '09 at the special invitation of American Chambers of Commerce. Add. Aoyama Minamicho, Tokyo. — p. 122 ([scan leaf 171](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n171/mode/1up))

**Nemoto, Sho** M.P. (Seiyukai), Dir. of the Teikoku Oil Co. etc., Inspector of the Dye-Stuff Manufacturing Co.; b. '51 in Ibaragi-ken; went over to America in '79 for study; sent by the Government to Mexico and Brazil to inquire into emigration affairs '03; has sat in the House since '02 and took active part in instituting the free primary education system and anti juvenile smoking and drinking. Add. Mita Shikokumachi, Tokyo. — p. 122 ([scan leaf 171](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n171/mode/1up))

**Nitahara, Shigeyuki** Lieut.-Gen., Com. of the Imperial Bodyguards Division; b. in '61 in Fukuoka; grad. from the Mil. Coll. in '83; Staff Major of the 2nd Div. in Japan-China War; attached to the 5th Draft Div. in Russo-Japanese War; Major-Gen. in '05 and Com. of 7th Brigade; Lieut.-Gen. and Com. of 2nd Div. in '12; of the 4th in '15. Add. Tokyo. — p. 122 ([scan leaf 171](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n171/mode/1up))

**Niho, Kamematsu** D.C.L. (Japan), Prof. of Coll. Law, Imp. Kyoto Univ., was b. in Ise '68; grad. from Law Coll., Imp. Univ., Tokyo, '93; studied in Germany '97-00. He attracted wide attention in connection with the "strike" agitation of the Kyoto Professors in '14. Add. Kyoto University. — p. 122 ([scan leaf 171](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n171/mode/1up))

**Nijo, Motohiro** Prince, one of the five proud gosseke (see Ichijo). The present head was b. '59, 8th son of Prince Kujo and adopted into the family; studied at Cambridge, England, '87-89. Add. Wakamatsucho, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 122 ([scan leaf 171](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n171/mode/1up))

**Nishi, Genshiro** Minister to Siam, b. '62 in Yamaguchi-ken; studied in China '83-85 and in Belgium '85-93; Attaché in '93; Sec. of Legation at Berlin in '93, transferred to Holland in March, '99, to Vienna in Nov., '00 as 1st Secretary, promoted Councillor '08; present post '15. Add. Bangkok, Siam. — p. 122 ([scan leaf 171](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n171/mode/1up))

**Nishi, Shinrokuro** Baron, Vice-Adm. (ret.), ex-Aide-de-Camp to the late Emperor Meiji; b. in '60 in Tokyo and was adopted by late Baron Shu Nishi, celebrated jurist and English scholar early in Meiji era; was educated at the Naval Cadet Sch. and appointed 2nd Sub-Lieut. in '85; took part in China-Japan War as Captain; once attached to the Legation in U.S.A. as attaché; on reserve in '14. Add. Omori, nr. Tokyo. — p. 122 ([scan leaf 171](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n171/mode/1up))

**Nishijima, Sukeyoshi** Baron (cr. '07), Lieut.-Gen. (ret.), b. '48 in Yamaguchi-ken; Sub-Lieut. '73; granted 4th Class of Golden Kite for his services in the Japan-China War; Maj.-Gen. '93; Com. of a Brigade of a Div. in Russo-Japanese War, Commander of the 2nd Army Div. '06, etc.; was placed on retired list '09. Add. Nagasaki. — p. 122 ([scan leaf 171](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n171/mode/1up))


## Who's Who in Japan

**Nishikubo, Hiromichi** mem. House of Peers (nom. '16); b. in '63 at Saga; grad. Law at the Gov. law school; Prof. at Military Staff College; sent to England in '02; Governor of Fukushima till '13; Governor of Hokkaido in '14; Inspector-General of Metropolitan Police Board '15-16. Add. Ichikawa, Chiba-ken. — p. 123 ([scan leaf 172](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n172/mode/1up))

**Nishimura, Seiichi** Baron, Lieut.-Gen. (ret.), ex-Dir. of the Tokyo Military Arsenal '03-12, b. 55 in Yamaguchi-ken; grad. Military Cadet School '76, Sub-Lieut. the following year; Lieut.-Gen. in '06, created Baron in connexion with the '04-05 War. Add. Yoyohata, near Tokyo. — p. 123 ([scan leaf 172](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n172/mode/1up))

**Nishiwaki, Saizaburo** Pres. of Nishiwaki Bank, Dir. of several other banks & Cos., was b. 1880 in Niigata, of a millioniare family; studied at the Peers School and Cambridge (class '08), and has devoted himself to banking since his return. Add. Sekiguchi, Koishikawa, Tokyo. — p. 123 ([scan leaf 172](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n172/mode/1up))

**Nitobe, Inazo** D. Agr. (Japan). D.C.L. (Japan). Professor at Imperial Univ., Tokyo since '06; b. '63 in Iwate-ken and grad. Sapporo Agr. Col. '81; studied political economy at the Johns Hopkin's Univ., and at Bonn. Halle, Berlin; Prof. at the Sapporo College for several years after his return; then official of Formosan Gov., which he left in '04 to fill chair of Economy at the Univ. of Kyoto. His work in English, "Bushido," published at Philadelphia in '98 has been translated into several languages and is a classic about Japan and Japanese; also wrote "Nogyo Honron" (Principle of Agriculture) and others; was selected as a first Japanese professor in the Japan-America exchange lecture arrangement started in '11. Mrs. Nitobe is an American lady by birth. Add. Kobinata Daimachi, Tokyo. — p. 123 ([scan leaf 172](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n172/mode/1up))

**Nitta, Chojiro** Pres. Nitta Leather Belting Co., Osaka, the largest establishment in this line in Far East.
