Tani) with whom he travelled through Europe and America on official business; resigned office next year with his chief and published a political novel written in highly polished Sinico-Japanese, and it had a wide circulation; arrested on the charge of complicity in the Korean Queen assassination affair in '95, to be acquitted on examination; Vice-Min. of Agr. and Com. '93 in the Okuma-Itagaki Cabinet. Add. Nagata-cho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 148 ([scan leaf 192](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n192/mode/1up))

**Shibata, Kamon** Mem., House of Peers, ex-Min. of Education '12-13; b. '61 in Yamaguchi-ken; grad. Law of the Imp. Tokyo Univ. '85; appointed Councillor, Home Office '86; Secretary of the Cabinet '89; Dir. Local Adm. Bureau, Home Office, '95; Chief Sec. of the Cabinet in the Katsura Ministry '01-05; reappointed to the post in '01; then elevated to the President of Colonial Board in '11; Min. Education in Dec., '12. Add. Naka-Rokubancho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 148 ([scan leaf 192](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n192/mode/1up))

**Shibayama, Yahachi** Baron (cr. '07), Adm. (ret), b. '50 in Kagoshima; Sub-Lieut. of the Navy, '74; Rear-Adm. and Com. Sasebo Adm'ty at the time of the Japan-China War and was rewarded for his service with 3rd Class Golden Kite; Vice-Adm. '97 and was Com. of Kure Adm'ty during '04-05 War and was granted 2nd Class Golden Kite, transferred to be Com. of Port Arthur '05; raised to full-Adm., '06 and placed on retired list. Add. Kami-Rokuban-cho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 148 ([scan leaf 192](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n192/mode/1up))

**Shibusawa, Ei-ichi** Baron (cr. '00), Pres. of 1st Bank and Tokyo Savings Bank, Chief of Committee of Tokyo Bankers' Association, Chairman of Council of Tokyo Inquiry Ass. and Dir. of Municipal Orphanage of Tokyo, etc., etc., b. '40 in Saitama-ken, is a Nestor of our business circles. Took service under the Tokugawa in its declining days and visited Europe, '67-68 in company of a Tokugawa Prince; appointed a high official in the Treasury on establishment of the Imperial Government, but left it '73 with the then Vice-Minister of the Dep't (the late Marquis Inouye) in consequence of his opposition to the policy of the Minister. From that time to this day the subject of this sketch consistently kept aloof from Gov. service, though very frequently the chair of Finance has been offered to him. Founded the 1st Bank (Dai-ichi Ginko), a pioneer of national bank in '73; was the chairman of the Tokyo Chamber of Commerce from its inauguration till spring of '05 when he resigned on account of ill-health. Toured in Europe and America in '02; through U.S.A. in '10, and also in '15. Add. Kabuto-cho, Nihombashi, Tokyo. — p. 148 ([scan leaf 192](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n192/mode/1up))

**Shibuya, Ariaki** Lieut.-Gen. (ret. '14), ex-Insp. of Commissariat, Mil. Education Board, b. '56 in Wakayama; appointed Sub-Lieut. of Cavalry '79, entered Military Staff Col. in '84; served for years as Aide-de-Camp to the Crown Prince, etc.; Lieut.-Gen. '08. Add. Gazembo, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 148 ([scan leaf 192](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n192/mode/1up))

**Shida, Kotaro** D.C.L. (Japan), Prof. in Tokyo Higher Commercial School, b. '68 in Chiba Prefecture; grad. '94 from Law Col. of Imp. Univ., and further studied in its post-grad. course, being at the same time Pro fessor in the Tokyo Higher Com. Sch. in '97, sent to Germany '98-01 to study Com. Law; Prof. at Law Coll., Tokyo Imp. Univ. in '06; once special mem. of Tokyo Chamber of Commerce; engaged by the Chinese Govt. as Prof. and Adviser to compile Com. and Civ. Law '08-12. Add. Kobinatadai, Koish., Tokyo. — p. 148 ([scan leaf 192](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n192/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Shidate, Tetsutaro** Gov. of the Industrial Bank of Japan, was b. '64 in Shimane; grad. from Politics, Imp. Tokyo Univ., class '89; served at the Bank of Japan till '89; entered the Kyushu Railway '00; Sumitomo Bank '01-10; joined the editorial staff of the Osaka Mainichi '11-12; present post in '13. Add. Nakashibuya, near Tokyo. — p. 144 ([scan leaf 193](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n193/mode/1up))

**Shidehara, Kijuro** Vice-Minister of Foreign Affairs, b. in '72 in Kawachi; grad. from Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ. in '95 and served in Agr. and Com. Dept.; élève-Consul at Chemulpo '99; transferred to London, Antwerp, &c.; Sec. of Head Office in '11 and Embassy Councillor at Washington in May '12; Minister at the Hague '14-15; finally present post in '15. Add. Foreign Office. — p. 144 ([scan leaf 193](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n193/mode/1up))

**Shiga, Shigetaka** ex-M.P., journalist, traveller, author, and Prof. Waseda Univ.; b. '63 at Okazaki; grad. Sapporo Agr. Col. '84; was once a school teacher and then visited the South Seas, '86, on board a naval training ship and his "Affairs in the South Seas" acquired a wide reputation for its charming style; joined the Progressionists '95; appointed Dir. Forestry Bureau, '97; Chief Councillor of Foreign Office, '98; joined the Port Arthur investing army in the '04-05 war, and also the Saghalien commission. Has written a number of works, chiefly geographical; visited South America, Africa and Europe on board cr. Ikoma; again America '15. Add. 476 Yoyogi, Tokyo. — p. 144 ([scan leaf 193](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n193/mode/1up))

**Shigemi, Kumao** Lt.-Gen. (ret. '16); ex-Com. of Shimonoseki Fortress; b. in Yamaguchi-ken; grad. Mil. Academy, '84; then Mil. College; Departmental Chief of Staff Board etc. Add. — p. 144 ([scan leaf 193](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n193/mode/1up))

**Shijo, Ryuai** Marquis, of a former courtier family that descended from Fujiwara Kamatari; the present head was b. '80, and is Lieut. of Cavalry. Add. Fujimi-cho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 144 ([scan leaf 193](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n193/mode/1up))

**Shimada, Saburo** M.P. (Kensei-kai) of unbroken record, Pres. of House of Representatives since '15; b. '52 in Tokyo; held a post of Secretary in the Dept. of Edu., which he resigned '81 when Count Okuma left the Government; was long one of the ablest lieutenants of the Count and a prominent member of his party, but afterward he severed connection with it and has ever been in the House. He joined the Nationalist Party in '10 but left it in '13 and then went over to late Prince Katsura's party. Wrote several works of historical interest; visited America to deliver lectures '11. He played active part in disclosing the Naval Scandal in '14. Add. Naka-rokubancho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 144 ([scan leaf 193](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n193/mode/1up))

**Shimamura, Hayao** Baron (cr. '16), Adm., Chief of Naval General Staff, Mem. of Admirals' Council since '11; b. in Kochi-ken '58; Midshipman, '81; sent abroad for study in England and Italy '88-89; and staff officer on board the flag-ship Matsushima, in the Japan-China War when he was slightly wounded; Commander soon after and attached to the Naval Board; Prof. at the Naval Staff Col. '96; attache to the Japanese Legation at Rome '96; attached to the Naval Board on returning home the same year; Capt. '99; was Commander of the Suma, and Chief Staff of the Standing Squadron in the Boxer Trouble '00; attached to the Naval Board and also Prof. at the Col. on returning home; Rear-Adm. in '02; was Chief Staff to Adm. Togo in Russo-Japanese War; Vice-Adm. '07 and Pres. of Staff Col. '07-09; attended The Hague Peace Conference held '07; Commander of the Sasebo Adm. '09-14; Com. of the Squadron that attended King George's Coronation Ceremony, promoted Adm. '15. Add. Aoyama-Minamicho, Tokyo. — p. 144 ([scan leaf 193](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n193/mode/1up))


## Who's Who in Japan

**Shimamura, Takitaro** man of letters, dramatist, and Prof. at the Waseda Univ.; was b. in '71 in Shimane; grad. from Waseda in '94; further studied in England; has translated Ibsen and Shaw and also organised a new group of actors. Add. Tozuka, Waseda, Tokyo. — p. 145 ([scan leaf 194](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n194/mode/1up))

**Shimazu, Tadashige, Prince** formerly of the great House of Satsuma, with a fief of 700,000 koku, and 30th head of the line which was founded in 12th century; his predecessor Seihin and uncle Hisamitsu did much for rehabilitating the Imperial power. The present head was b. '86 and is Lieutenant of Navy. Add. Nagatacho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 145 ([scan leaf 194](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n194/mode/1up))

**Shimizu, Cho, D.C.L. (Japan)** Judge of Adm. Litigation; b. '68 in Kanagawa; grad. Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ. '94; Secretary of the Home Office and Prof. at the Peers' School and studied in Germany and Austria '98-99. Add. Aizumicho, Yotsuya, Tokyo. — p. 145 ([scan leaf 194](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n194/mode/1up))

**Shimizu, Ichiro** President of the Appeal Court at Sendai, b. in '58 in Kaga; grad. the former Law College under control of Justice Dept. '84, and has since sat on the Bench in which he was Judge at the Supreme Court, President of Kobe District Court, etc. Add. Appeal Court, Sendai. — p. 145 ([scan leaf 194](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n194/mode/1up))

**Shimoda, Utako** Mad., one of the foremost bluestockings and educationalists; a Court maid of honour when about 16 years old, and again after the death of her husband. On the founding of the Peeress' School she was appointed an instructor, subsequently combining the executive duty of Lady Steward of the School, resigned in Nov. '07, and has since devoted herself chiefly to a private female school she founded about '99. She visited Europe '93-95, and is perhaps the only Japanese lady who was received by Queen Victoria of England in audience in Japanese costume. Add. Kitamachi, Aoyama, Tokyo. — p. 145 ([scan leaf 194](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n194/mode/1up))

**Shimogo, Denbei** ex-Member of the House of Peers, Pres. of the Jinju Life Ins. Co.; b. '72, at Nagahama, Shiga-ken; studied in the Keiogijuku; made an inspection tour in Europe and America '00; has interests in many companies. Add. Mita Koyamacho, Tokyo. — p. 145 ([scan leaf 194](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n194/mode/1up))

**Shimomura, Ko** Dir. of Civil Administration Bureau, Formosa (app. '15), b. '75, eldest son of late Fusajiro Shimomura; grad. Law, Imp. Univ., Tokyo '98, and has since been connected with the postal service; Dir. Postal Money Order and Saving Bureau. Add. Taipeh, Formosa. — p. 145 ([scan leaf 194](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n194/mode/1up))

**Shimo-oka, Chuji** M.P., ex-Vice-Minister of Home Affairs, b. '70 in Hyogo-ken; grad. Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ. '95; was appointed Secretary of Justice Dep't and of Legislative Bureau; Gov. of Akita-ken '05-08; Director of Agr. Bureau '08; Vice-Min. of Agr. and Com '12-13; Chief Sec. of the Cabinet '13-14; appointed Vice-Min. of Home Affairs '14-15; Parliamentary Secy. to the same Dept. July—Aug., '15. Add. Yakoji, Ichigaya, Tokyo. — p. 145 ([scan leaf 194](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n194/mode/1up))

**Shin, Tsuneta** D. Eng., Naval Architect, ex-Mng. of the Ishikawajima Ship-building Co.; was b. '64 in Yamaguchi-ken; grad. from Eng. Col. of the Imp. Tokyo Univ. about '86 and soon entered the said Co. He established his office in '11 representing some General Loco. Works, &c. Add. Aoyama Minamimachi, Tokyo. — p. 145 ([scan leaf 194](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n194/mode/1up))


## Who's Who

**Shiohara, Matasaku** , Mng. Dir. of Sankyo Kabushiki-Kaisha, was b. in Yokohama in '99, and studied at the Yokohama Commercial School and the Yokohama School of English and Japanese Languages; started drugs business in '99; went to America to study drugs business. He began importing drugs, chemicals, etc., and incorporated his firm in '07 as partnership Co. under the name of Sankyo Goshi-Kaisha, which in '13 was consolidated into a joint stock Co., Mr. Shiohara acting as Mng. Dir. Add. Ilgurakata-machi, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 146 ([scan leaf 195](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n195/mode/1up))

**Shiozawa, Shōtei** , Ph. D., D.C.L. (Japan), Prof. at the Waseda Univ.; was b. in '74 at Mito; grad. from Waseda in '95, from Wisconsin '00, and further studied at Berlin and Halle '01-2, his speciality being political economy. Add. Yarai, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 146 ([scan leaf 195](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n195/mode/1up))

**Shiraishi, Motojiro** , Dir. of the Toyo Kisen Kaisha, Dir. of Hinode Life Ins. Co., etc., b. '67 in Niigata-ken. Early grad. from Law Col. of the Imp. Tokyo Univ. and entered the Toyo Kisen Kaisha to be elevated to the present post. Add. Mitakouncho, Tokyo. — p. 146 ([scan leaf 195](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n195/mode/1up))

**Shiraishi, Naoharu** , D. Eng. (Japan), M.P. and Pres. of the Wakamatsu Harbour Works, Mng. Dir. of Inawashiro Hydro-Elec. Co., etc.; b. in '57 in Tosa; grad. Civil Eng. at the Imp. Tokyo Univ. in '80; studied in America and Europe from '83-6, to be appointed Prof. at the alma mater; afterward Pres. of the Kwansai Rly. Co. Add. Iigura, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 146 ([scan leaf 195](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n195/mode/1up))

**Shirani, Takeshi** , Chief Dir. Civil Adm'tive Dep't of Gov.-Gen., Kwantung, b. '63 in Fukuoka; grad. Law, Imp. Univ., '90; appointed at the Home Office in which he held post of Councillor, Section Chief and finally Dir. of Temple and Shrine Affairs Bureau '02, also serving for some years at the Hokkaido Office, and also at the Edu. Dep't. Transferred to Gov. of Tochigi-ken, '03; Dir., Ordinary Education Bureau, '06-08; appointed to the present post in May, '08. Add. Port Arthur. — p. 146 ([scan leaf 195](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n195/mode/1up))

**Shiratori, Kurakichi** , historian, D. Litt. (Japan), tutor to the Crown Prince, and Prof. at the Peers' School; was b. '65 in Chiba; grad. in '90 from Litt. Col. of the Imp. Tokyo Univ., further studied in Germany and Hungary '00-05, and is an authority on Mongolian dynasties and allied subjects. Add. Shimo-Ochiai, suburb of Tokyo. — p. 146 ([scan leaf 195](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n195/mode/1up))

**Shiroyama, Shosai** , master "makiye" artist, Grand Prize recipient at Paris and St. Louis, ex-Prof. of Fine Art Academy; b. 53 in Tokyo; first studied inlaying but subsequently took to "makiye"; was ordered in '87 to make makiye-frame for mirror in the Empress' chamber; organized the Japan Lacquer Art Society with some others '90, and was appointed Prof. of the Academy the following year, resigning the post '01. He is specially famous for "level polish makiye," and has frequently received orders from the Imperial Court. Add. Kobiki-cho Itchome, Kyobashi, Tokyo. — p. 146 ([scan leaf 195](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n195/mode/1up))

**Shisa, Suguru** , Paymaster-General, Chief of Naval Account Bureau; b. '64 in Nagasaki-ken; Dir. of Paymasters school and Chief accountant of Sasebo Admiralty before transferred to present post in '12. Add. Omori near Tokyo. — p. 146 ([scan leaf 195](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n195/mode/1up))

**Shito, Akira** , Chief of Silk Conditioning House, at Yokohama, b. '60 in Kumamoto; grad. '85 from Komaba Agr. Col. and was in the service of Prefectural Offices and Department of Agr. and Com. as agriculturist, having been Chief of the Silk Conditioning House till '10, then commercial agent in New York; reinstated to present post '12. Add. Silk Cond. House, Yokohama. — p. 146 ([scan leaf 195](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n195/mode/1up))

**Sho, Seijiro** Mng. of the Mitsubishi Firm, was b. '62 in Nagasaki; grad. Imp. Tokyo Univ. '84; studied in U.S.A.; and has since been connected with the Firm. Add. Kobinata Suido-cho, Tokyo. — p. 147 ([scan leaf 196](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n196/mode/1up))

**Sho, Ten** Marquis, of the "princely house" of Luchu; b. '64. Add Fujimi-cho, Koj., Tokyo. — p. 147 ([scan leaf 196](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n196/mode/1up))

**Shoda, Heigoro** Dir. of Meiji Fire, Meiji Life, Tokyo Marine Ins. Cos., etc., b. '45 in Oita-ken; was one of the most trusted confidants of the late Mr. Fukuzawa, Japan's greatest educationalist, and was one of his assist-teachers in the inception stage of his famous institution, Keiogijuku. Then entered the Mitsubishi Firm soon after its creation and has done much in building it up to the present greatness; had charge of the Nagasaki Dockyard owned by the Firm for long while. Add. Hayashi-cho, Koi., Tokyo. — p. 147 ([scan leaf 196](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n196/mode/1up))

**Shoda, Kazue** Min. of Finance since '16, Crown Mem. of House of Peers (nom. '14); b. '69 in Ehime-ken; graduated from Law Col., Imp. Tokyo Univ., in '95; passed the higher civil service examination in the following year; inspector of taxation affairs '97; promoted to the Dir. Finance Bureau, Treasury in '07; Vice-Minister in '11-14, having been in the meanwhile Chief of Hakodate Customs House, Sec. at the head Office, etc. Pres. of Chosen Bank in '15, promoted to Vice Minister Oct. '16, full Min. same year. Add. Nakashibuya, Tokyo. — p. 147 ([scan leaf 196](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n196/mode/1up))

**Simla, Gentaro** Gov. of Japan Hypothec Bank; was born in '67 in Yamanashi; grad. from Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ. '89 and entered Agr. and Com. Dept.; Councillor '93; Sectional Chief in '97; Vice-Gov. of the Hypothec Bank '02-12; travelled through Europe and America for inspection '99-01; Gov. in '12; Add. Kanetomicho, Koishikawa, Tokyo. — p. 147 ([scan leaf 196](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n196/mode/1up))

**Soe—.=Soye—**  — p. 147 ([scan leaf 196](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n196/mode/1up))

**Soga, Sukenori** Lieut.-Gen. (retired), Viscount (cr. '84), Privy Councillor, ex-President of nationalized Nippon Railway Co., and Mem. House of Peers '91-15; b. '43 in Saga-ken; early entered the Imp. Army; took distinguished part as Commander of Brigade on the occasion of Civil War of '77; was subsequently a Divisional Commander at various places; held for a while, the post of Vice-Chief of the General Staff; retired from active service about '87, was once chief military tutor and Aide-de-Camp to the Crown Prince. Add. Surugadai, Kanda, Tokyo. — p. 147 ([scan leaf 196](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n196/mode/1up))

**Soma, Hanji, M.A** Pres. of Meiji Sugar Co., Formosa, was b. '69 at Inuyama; non-commissioned officer '85-90; studied at the Tokyo Higher Technical School; then Berlin and Michigan, devoting himself to chemical researches about sugar; taught at his alma mater '03-04; chemist to Formosan Government-Gen.; Dir. of the present Co. which was established '06. Add. Shiba Park, Tokyo. — p. 147 ([scan leaf 196](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n196/mode/1up))

**Soma, Nagatane** Dir. of Yokohama Specie Bank, Prof. of the Senshu Univ.; b. '50 in Hikone, Omi; sent to America to study Laws and Economics at Yale Univ. as early as 71; established the Senshu Gakko (now Univ.) in conjunction with Dr. Vise. Tajiri and Baron Megata on his return home in '79, entered Yokohama Specie Bank, and was Pres. for about ten years till '09. Was granted 3rd Grade of Merit for his service in the Russo-Japanese War. Add. Shimo-Tozuka, near Tokyo. — p. 147 ([scan leaf 196](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n196/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Sone, Tatsuzo** architect, D. Eng., b. '55 in Karatsu, grad. architecture '80 in the defunct Govt. Eng. College; was architect to the Kure Admiralty for some years, architect of the Mitsubishi Firm '90-07 and undertook building a number of brick houses in the extensive open space which the firm owns near the Imperial Palace, Tokyo; started business on his own account in '07. Add. Nakashibuya, near Tokyo. — p. 148 ([scan leaf 197](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n197/mode/1up))

**Sonoda, Kokichi** banker, Dir. of 15th Bank, Yokohama Specie Bank, and of Anglo-Japanese Hydro-Electric Co., etc., b. '48 in Satsuma, and adopted into the family; entered the Kaisei Gakko (present Imp. Tokyo Univ.) '69, first as student, then appointed Assist.; removed to the the Foreign Office as clerk. '71; dispatched to England, '74 as Jap. Exhibition Commissioner and subsequently appointed attache to the Legation; personal Secretary to the Foreign Minister '79; Jap. Consul in London, '82-90; elected Pres. of Yokohama Specie Bank, '90; dispatched to London to bring home the indemnity from China, '95; transferred to Pres. of 15th Bank '99-15. Add. Shirokane, Shiba, Tokyo. — p. 148 ([scan leaf 197](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n197/mode/1up))

**Sonoda, Yasukata** Baron (cr. '97), ex-Governor of Hokkaido '98-06, and ex-Councillor of Court, b. '50 in Satsuma. Was l ng connected with the Police affairs, having held the post of Police-Insp. of Tokyo twice. Add. Dai-machi, Akasaka, Tokyo. — p. 148 ([scan leaf 197](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n197/mode/1up))

**Soyeda, Juichi** D.C.L. (Japan), ex-Pres. of Imp. Gov. Rlys, b. '63 in Fukuoka-ken; reputed as a prodigy of genius when a child on account of his precocious talent; grad. from Pol. Economy of the Imp. Tokyo Univ., '84; further prosecuted his studies at Cambridge, England, Heidelberg, to be appointed a Councillor of the Treasury on his return home, '87; was promoted to post of Vice-Ministership at the time of Okuma-Itagaki Cabinet, '98; appointed Pres. of the Bank of Formosa, '99 when the bank was inaugurated, removed to the chairman of the Organization Com. of the Japan Ind. Bank and appointed its President on its being formally started '02; retired from the post in '12; and visited U.S.A. in connection with anti-Japanese agitation in '13. Has taken active part in the introduction of foreign capital; President of Imp. Gov't Railways in '15-16. Add. Fujimicho, Koj., Tokyo. — p. 148 ([scan leaf 197](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n197/mode/1up))

**Soyejima, Michimasa** 2nd Count (1st Count Taneomi, d. '05 rendered distinguished service for the Restoration); b. in '71 and was educated at the Peers' School, then at Leeds and Cambridge, England, where he studied history under Prof. Seeley, class '95. Add. Omotecho, Akasaka, Tokyo. — p. 148 ([scan leaf 197](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n197/mode/1up))

**Suda, Toshinobu** D.E. (Japan), Dir. of the Nippon Yusen Kaisha; b. in '56 in Hyuga; grad. from defunct Gov. Engineering Coll. in '81; after a short service in the Gov't he entered the present Co. Add. Shio-cho, Yotsuya, Tokyo. — p. 148 ([scan leaf 197](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n197/mode/1up))

**Sugawara, Michitaka** mem. House of Peers (nom. '16), b. '69 in Miyagi-ken; grad. Law Coll., Imp. Tokyo Univ. '96, and entered the service of the Treasury; after holding post of Dir. of Revenue of Customs Office, at Hakodate, Kobe, etc. promoted to Councillor in '04 then Dir. Tax Bureau; Vice-Min. Finance '15-16. Add. Kagocho, Koishikawa, Tokyo. — p. 148 ([scan leaf 197](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n197/mode/1up))


## Who's Who in Japan

**Sugi, Magoshichiro** Viscount (cr. '87), Privy Councillor; b. '35 in Choshu; took active part in the work of the Restoration, and was subsequently in the Imperial Court before he was appointed Privy Councillor, '97. Add. Hirakawacho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 149 ([scan leaf 198](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n198/mode/1up))

**Sugimura, Kotaro** journalist and author, on the staff of the Tokyo Asahi, b. '72 in Wakayama; was school teacher, and translator, U.S. Embassy, before he entered Asahi in '04; travelled several times in the West. Has written a number of books. Add. Omori, near Tokyo. — p. 149 ([scan leaf 198](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n198/mode/1up))

**Sugimura, Toraichi** ex-Ambassador at Berlin, b. '57 at Kanazawa; took service at Justice Dep't '57; removed to the Foreign Office '85; Secretary of the Legation at Rome, '87; removed to Paris, '91, to Petrograd, '92; to Vienna, '94-99; again to Mexico, '02-06; Minister to Sweden, '06-11; to Germany, '11-14. Add. Foreign Office. — p. 149 ([scan leaf 198](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n198/mode/1up))

**Sugino, Kisei** Broker of Tokyo Stock Exchange, Mng. Dir. of Yamaichi & Co., b. in '70, in Hirosaki, Aomori-ken; grad. '89 from the Banking Training Course attached to Treasury and entered the Bank of Japan; Mng. Dir. of Nagoya Ginko L'td. 1901; and came up to Tokyo after 1904-5 War and entered Koike & Co. as its manager and on its voluntary winding up April, '17 created present concern. Add. Office No. 3 Kabuto-cho, Tokyo; resid. No. 815 Sendagaya, suburb of Tokyo. — p. 149 ([scan leaf 198](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n198/mode/1up))

**Sugita, Tei-ichi** Mem. of House of Peers, leader of Seiyukai; b. '51 in Fukui-ken; is one of the dwindling old Liberals who struggled long for the cause of freedom by sacrificing personal interest and comfort; appointed Gov. of Hokkaido in the Okuma-Itagaki Cabinet, '98; was Vice-Pres. of House of Rep. in '06-08; nom. Crown Mem. of House of Peers in '12. Add. Shimoshibuya, Tokyo. — p. 149 ([scan leaf 198](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n198/mode/1up))

**Sugiura, Shigetake** educationist, tutor of Crown Prince and Principal of Nippon Middle School, b. '55 in Shiga-ken; studied at the pioneer Imp. Tokyo Univ. '70-76, at Owen's Coll., Manchester '76-80 chemistry under Roscoe; was afterward Principal of the University Preparatory School, Dir. of Special Edu. Bureau; M.P. in the 1st session; tutor to Crown Prince '14. Add. Hisakata-cho, Koishikawa, Tokyo. — p. 149 ([scan leaf 198](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n198/mode/1up))

**Sumitomo, Kichizaemon** Baron (cr. '11), Pres. of Sumitomo Bank, Osaka; b. '64 at Kyoto, represents one of the oldest millionaire families in Japan; is a younger brother of Marquises Tokudaiji and Saionji, and adopted by the family; once sat in the House of Peers as representative for the highest tax-payers of Osaka. The family runs the Sumitomo Bank, copper mining and melting and also colliery. Add. Chausuyama, Tennoji, Osaka. — p. 149 ([scan leaf 198](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n198/mode/1up))

**Suyematsu, Kencho** Viscount (cr. Baron '95, and Visc. '07), Dr. Lit. (Japan), Privy Councillor; b. '65 in Fukuoka-ken; began public career as writer on the staff of the Nichi Nichi; appointed Chancellor of the Legation at London, where he turned his leisure fully to advantage by attending lectures; returned M.P. from his district, '90-95; Dir. of the Legislative Bureau, '92-95; Minister of Communications in the Ito Cabinet, '98; Minister for the Interior, '00-01 in the Seiyukai Ministry; non-official agent in England during the '04-5 war; appointed Privy Councillor, '06. Is a son-in-law of the late Prince Ito whose eldest daughter is Viscountess Suyematsu; had for a time charge of the education of the Crown Prince of Korea when the latter first arrived in Tokyo in '07. Wrote "Risen Sun Empire," "Japanese Fantasies," etc. Add. Nishinokubo, Shiba, Tokyo. — p. 149 ([scan leaf 198](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n198/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Suyenobu, Dosei** Pres. of Toyokuni Bank and Dir. of Tokyo Marine Ins., Meiji Life Ins. Cos., and of several other important concerns; b. '55 in Kochi-ken and was among the first batch of graduates of the Imp. Univ., Tokyo, and has ever since devoted himself to business. Add. Toriizaka, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 150 ([scan leaf 199](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n199/mode/1up))

**Suzuki, Jubi** barrister, was b. at Kambe, Ise, '54; grad. '81, Law at the Imp. Tokyo Univ., connected with the Peers' School till '85; transferred to the Foreign Office and was Consul in Korea and Hongkong. Resigned in '89 and opened law office in Tokyo. Joined the Liberals; was returned from his native place in the 7th and 8th general elections, and held post of Vice-Min. of Home Office at the time of the Okuma-Itagaki Coalition Cabinet in '98. Add. Zaimokucho, Kyobashi, Tokyo. — p. 150 ([scan leaf 199](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n199/mode/1up))

**Suzuki, Kantaro** Rear-Adm., Vice-Minister of Navy; b. '69 in Chiba-ken; grad. Naval Academy; Lieut.-Commander, '98; Commander, '03; Capt. '08; once Dir. of Torpedo School. Decorations are 3rd Order of R.S., 4th Order of S.T., 3rd Class G.K. Appointed to present post in '14. He also combines a post in the Admiral's Council. Add. Admiralty. — p. 150 ([scan leaf 199](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n199/mode/1up))

**Suzuki, Kisaburo** D.C.L. (Japan), Vice-Min. of Justice; b. in Kanagawa-ken '67; grad. Law Col. of the Imp. Tokyo Univ. in '91, and has sat on the bench since '95; meanwhile occupying the posts of Chief Judge at Tokyo District Court, Dir. Legal Aff. Bur., etc., was dispatched abroad in '08 on a tour of inspection; app. to present post in '14. Also holds a chair in Waseda University, teaching Civil Law. Add. Yamabushi-cho, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 150 ([scan leaf 199](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n199/mode/1up))

**Suzuki, Konosuke** D.M., Surgeon-General of the Navy (ret.); b. '54, in the clan of Tahara; began his career in the Navy first in '83, placed on retired list in '06, established a hospital at Iigura, Tokyo in '11. Add. Iigura Katamachi, Tokyo. — p. 150 ([scan leaf 199](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n199/mode/1up))

**Suzuki, Masaya** Gen.-Mng., Sumitomo family, Osaka; was b. in Kanazawa '62; grad. Law, Imp. Tokyo University, '87; was a Councillor of the Dep't of Agr. and Com. on graduation; then removed to a post of secretary in several local offices, lastly at Osaka; to resign the post and enter the present service about '96. Add. c/o Sumitomo, Osaka. — p. 150 ([scan leaf 199](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n199/mode/1up))

**Suzuki, Saburosuke** Dir. of the Japan Chemical Industry Co., and Proprietor of Suzuki Firm, manufacturer and exporter of Iodine, etc.; was b. in Kanagawa-ken '68. He established the former Co. in '06 and was Mng.-Dir. till May, '09 when resigned at his own convenience and became a Director. His "Ajinomoto" (cooking powder) for giving relish has been patented at home and abroad. Add. Atagocho, Shiba, Tokyo. — p. 150 ([scan leaf 199](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n199/mode/1up))

**Suzuki, Shigemichi** Naval Surgeon-Gen. (ret.), ex-Pres. of the Yokosuka Naval Hospital; was b. '57 at Shiga; Surgeon-Lieut. '97; was once Chief of Medical Dep't of Maidzuru Admiralty, promoted to the present rank of Surgeon-Gen. in '05 and reserve list in '12. Add. Okubo, near Tokyo. — p. 150 ([scan leaf 199](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n199/mode/1up))


## Who's Who

**Suzuki, Sobei** President of the Nagoya Chamber of Commerce and leading banker and businessman in that city, was b. in '56 in a house noted as a wholesale dealer in lumber; has sat in the House of Representatives for a long while. Add. Nagoya. — p. 151 ([scan leaf 200](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n200/mode/1up))

**Suzuki, Umeshiro** M.P. for Tokyo, Dir. of Kyodo Fire Ins. Co., and Japan Colonization Co., etc.; b. '62 in Nagano-ken; grad. from the Keiogijuku '85 and after having been connected with the Jiji, turned to business. Add. Yobancho, Tokyo. — p. 151 ([scan leaf 200](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n200/mode/1up))

**Tachibana, Koichiro** Lieut.-Gen., Com. of 19th Div. in Korea; b. '61 at Fukuoka; grad. Cadet School '83, Cap. '94, Major '98, Colonel '05, and Adjutant to the War Minister, Maj.-Gen. '09, Chief Staff of the Korean Garrison and Com. of Gendarmerie in Korea in 14; present post in '16. Add. Ranan, Korea. — p. 151 ([scan leaf 200](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n200/mode/1up))

**Tadokoro, Yoshiharu** Vice-Minister of Edu., b. '71 in Tokyo; grad Law at Imperial Tokyo Univ. '95; passed the higher civil service examination the following year; Councillor at various local offices and at Edu. Dep't; was in Europe '03-05 to investigate educational administration; Commissioner of Anglo-Japanese Exhibition '10; Dir. General Education Bureau, Dep't of Education, '11-16; the present post '16. Add. Kobinatadaimachi Tokyo. — p. 151 ([scan leaf 200](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n200/mode/1up))

**Tagawa, Daikichiro** Edit. of the Miyako, ex-Parl.-Secy. of Justice Dept., ex-Deputy-Mayor of Tokyo; b. in '69 in Nagasaki-ken; grad. from the Waseda Semmon Gakko (now Univ.) in '90; took part in Sino-Japanese and Russo-Japanese Wars as interpreter; is a prominent Christian thinker. Add. Kobinatadaimachi, Tokyo. — p. 151 ([scan leaf 200](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n200/mode/1up))

**Tajiri, Inajiro** Viscount (cr. Baron '95, Visc. '07), D.C.L. (Japan), Chief of the Board of Audit, ex-Mem. of the House of Peers; b. at Kagoshima '50; grad. from Yale Univ., U.S.A. in '79; Councillor of the Finance Dep't '81; afterward Dir. of Loan and other Bureaux; twice Vice-Minister of Finance, '92-97 and '98-01 and then transferred to the present post. He is teaching political economy at the Imp. Tokyo Univ. and other institutions, and enjoys as great a reputation as a scientist as he does as an executive officer; has written several works on political economy. Add. Kanetomicho, Koishikawa, Tokyo. — p. 151 ([scan leaf 200](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n200/mode/1up))

**Takagi, Kenkwan** Baron (cr. '05), M.D., D.S.Hon. (Columbia and Penn.), LL.D. (Durham, Eng.), Mem., House of Peers since '92; b. in Satsuma '49; studied medicine in England; was connected with the Naval Surgery from which he retired '92. He first adopted barley-rice-mixed ration in place of rice alone for the Fleet as a preventive against the leg dropsy. On retiring from the service, he with others organized the Tokyo Charity Hospital and also the Tokyo Hospital as his own private enterprise. Visited England and U.S.A. '05-06. Add. Higashi Toriizaka, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 151 ([scan leaf 200](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n200/mode/1up))

**Takagi, Masutaro** M.P. and barrister, b. in '69 in Tokyo; grad. from the Tokyo Hogaku-in in '91; passed the Judge and Procurators' Examination and Barristers' in '95, and has since been practising in Tokyo. Owns and edits a law journal, and is legal adviser to several business establishments. Add. Hon-Shirokane-cho, Nihombashi, Tokyo. — p. 151 ([scan leaf 200](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n200/mode/1up))

**Takahashi, Kazutomo** Editor of the Japan Times (Tokyo), Prof. Keio-gijuku Univ.; was b. '62 in Kawagoye; entered Col. of Law, Imp. Univ., Tokyo, but left in '86, and proceeded to U.S.A., where he grad. from Michigan State Univ.; attended to business in the States and Canada before he returned home '97 and joined the staff of the Japan Times. Add. Tsunohazu, Tokyo. — p. 151 ([scan leaf 200](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n200/mode/1up))

**Takahashi, Korekiyo** Baron (cr. '07), Mem. of House of Peers; b. '54 in Tokyo; sent to America for study, '67; was for several months treated as a slave through the treachery of his knavish American "guardian"; returned home the following year; appointed an official of the Dep't, Agr. and Com., '81; ultimately rising to be Dir., Patent Bureau, but this post he had to abandon owing to the notorious fraud of a "gold mine" in Peru, he and his friends who formed a company to exploit, having been victimized by a German swindler. Soon he found a post in the Bank of Japan, promoted to a Director having charge of the western section of Japan; removed to the Yokohama Specie Bank, '95 to be elected its Vice-Pres., '97; elected Vice-Gov., Bank of Japan, then made Pres. once more of the Specie Bank '06, retaining the former post. He was financial Agent for raising foreign loans in England and America, and was dispatched abroad twice on the important mission '04 and '06; was Gov. of the Bank of Japan in '11; Minister of Finance '13-14. Add. Omote-cho, Akasaka, Tokyo. — p. 152 ([scan leaf 201](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n201/mode/1up))

**Takahashi, Sakuye** D.C.L. (Japan), Prof. of Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ. and Mem. of Peers (nom. '16); was b. '65 in Nagano-ken; grad. Politics at Imp. Tokyo Univ., '94; legal adviser to Adm. Ito, Commander of the Combined Squadron in the Japan-China War; proceeded to England and France to study International Laws '97-01; Prof. at his alma mater on his return home; wrote several works on internat. law. Dir. of Legislative and Pension Bureaux '14-16. Add. Komagome Akebonocho, Tokyo — p. 152 ([scan leaf 201](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n201/mode/1up))

**Takahira, Kogoro** Baron (cr, '07), ex-Ambassador to Washington; b. '54 in Iwate-ken; began his career as official of the former Public Works Dep't, to be transferred to Foreign Office as translator, '76; Legation Secy. at Washington, '81; removed to Seoul '84; Consul at Shanghai, '87; Secy. at the Head Office, '90; Consul-Gen. at New York, 91; Minister at the Hague, '92; at Rome, '94, to be transferred to Vienna shortly after; appointed Vice-Minister '99; and the following year appointed Min. at Washington where he was Peace Plenipotentiary in '05; relieved of the post in '05, Mem. of the House of Peers; Amb. to Rome '07, to Washington '08-09; put on unattached list in '09; was on staff of Prince Fushimi when he visited England '10. Add. Naka-Meguro, near Tokyo. — p. 152 ([scan leaf 201](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n201/mode/1up))

**Takakura, Tobei** Pres. of Kitahama Bank, Teikoku Land Co., Naniwa Fire Ins. Co., Yurin Life Ins. Co., Chairman of Bd. of Dirs. of Dojima Rice Exchange, Osaka, etc.; b. '74 in Kishiwada; succeeded to father's cotton dealing and next he became a stock-broker in '94; elected Dir. of Dojima Exchange '07, then its chairman '12, and Pres. of Naniwa Fire Co.; purchased Yurin Life Co. '12. Add. Tennoji, Osaka. — p. 152 ([scan leaf 201](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n201/mode/1up))

**Takakusu, Junjiro** M.A. (Oxf.), D. Litt. (Japan), Prof. at Imp. Tokyo Univ.; was b. in Bingo '65; entered Oxford '88 and studied Sanskrit; further studied in Germany to return home '97, and appointed Prof. at the Tokyo Univ.; Personal Secretary to the Minister of Communications (Viscount Suyematsu); President of Foreign Language School, '00-08; translated several Sanskrit and Pali works. Stayed in London under the Viscount during the Russo-Japanese War; sent abroad '11-12. Add. Sendagaya, Tokyo. — p. 152 ([scan leaf 201](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n201/mode/1up))


## Who's Who in Japan

**Takamatsu, Toyokichi** D. Eng., Emeritus Prof. of Imp. Univ., Tokyo, Dir. of State Industrial Laboratory, Special Mem. of Tokyo Chamber of Commerce; b. 55 in Tokyo, grad. course of Chemistry at the Imp. Univ., in '73; further studied at Owen's Col., Manchester, and at Berlin Univ., returning home in '82; held chair of applied chemistry at his alma mater and other institutions before he retired from official career in '05; the Pres. of Tokyo Gas Co. '05-15. Add. Nishikata-machi, Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 153 ([scan leaf 202](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n202/mode/1up))

**Takamine, Jokichi** D. Eng. and D. Phar. (Japan), inventor of "Taka Diastase," "Adorinalin," etc.; b. in '53 in Kaga; completed Applied Chemistry at the former Engineering Col. in '79; sent to England to prosecute further studies, returning home '81 and appointed chemist to Dep't of Agr. and Com., where he did much to improve sake brewing and indigo making. Attended World's Fair at New Orleans in '84 as Japanese Commissioner, and since then he has settled in U.S.A. where he married a daughter of a well-known druggist in Chicago and a relative of Henry George. He invented a powerful alcoholic ferment; established the Takamine Ferment Co. in Chicago. Add. c/o Japan Club, New York. — p. 153 ([scan leaf 202](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n202/mode/1up))

**Takamura, Ko-un** master-artist in wood carving, Prof. in Gov. Fine Art Academy; b. '52 in Tokyo; studied under Takamura To-un, a celebrated Buddhist image carver in Tokyo who died '79; undertook wood-model for the bronze images of Nanko elected in front of the Imp. Palace and of the Great Saigo at Uyeno Park. Won Gold medal at the Paris Fair '00 and has frequently been honoured to carve in presence of Their Majesties. Art Commissioner to Imp. Household. Add. Komagome Hayashicho, Tokyo. — p. 153 ([scan leaf 202](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n202/mode/1up))

**Takarabe, Hyo** Vice-Adm. and mem. of Admirals' Council, b. '67 in Hyuga; graduated from the Naval Cadet School '90; was on staff at the headquarters in the '94-95 and '04-05 wars; Capt. '95, Rear-Adm. '09, Vice-Adm. '13; Vice-Min. of Navy, '13-14. Put on waiting list for a while in connection with the Naval Case in '14; Com. of 3rd Squadron early in '15 and transferred to Com. of Port Arthur Naval Station '15-6; present post, Dec. '16. Add. Reinanzaka, Tokyo. — p. 153 ([scan leaf 202](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n202/mode/1up))

**Takasaki, Chika-aki** Mem. House of Peers, Lord-in-Waiting of the Kinkei Hall, and Pres. of the Iron Works at Muroran; b. '53 in Kagoshima and was one of those who were roughly treated as spies by the Kagoshima rebels in '77 and narrowly escaped death. Subsequently served as Chief Police Inspector at various districts, then Provincial Governor at Osaka, Nagano, Okayama etc.; also Bureau Director at the Home Office; Mem. of House of Peers in '11. Add. Tennoji, Osaka. — p. 153 ([scan leaf 202](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n202/mode/1up))

**Takashima, Kokinji** Pres. of Niitaka Sugar Mfg. Co., Dir. of the Okura Firm, Dir. of the Nippon Shoemaking Co.; was b. '81 in Tokyo; studied at the Keiogijuku and for years on the staff of the Jiji; then entered the firms, having married a daugher of its proprietor. Add. Nagatacho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 153 ([scan leaf 202](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n202/mode/1up))

**Takashima, Tokuzo** artist of Japanese painting, Mem. of Hanging Committee of Fine Arts, Mem. of Art Society of Japan; nom de plume "Hokkai"; b. '50 in Yamaguchi-ken; learned Japanese painting from his father; was Gov. forestry expert before he became artist in '03; won several medals at art exhibitions. Add. Motozono-cho, Tokyo. — p. 153 ([scan leaf 202](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n202/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Takasugi, Shin** Dir. Nippon Beer Brewery Co., was b. in Okayama in '68. Add. Hommura, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 164 ([scan leaf 203](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n203/mode/1up))

**Takata, Koan, M.D** Proprietor of Takata Hospital, Tokyo, and Nanko-in Hospital, Chigasaki, was b. '61 in Kyoto; grad. from Med. Col., Imp. Univ. of Tokyo '90 and subsequently commenced practice. Add. Sambancho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 164 ([scan leaf 203](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n203/mode/1up))

**Takata, Motojiro** Director of the Industrial Dept., Formosan Govt.-Gen., b. in '64 in Niigata; grad. from Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ., and entered Communication Dept. in '93; Councillor of Imp. Govt. Rlys. in '07; toured through Europe and America on official inspection '07-8; transferred to present post '10; was given additional post of Dir. Arisan Timber Dept. in '13. Add. Taipeh, Formosa. — p. 164 ([scan leaf 203](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n203/mode/1up))

**Takata, Sanae, D.C.L. (Japan)** Crown Mem. of House of Peers, (nom. '15), honorary President of Waseda Univ. and ex-Minister of Education; b. '60 at Tokyo; grad. Law at the Imp. Tokyo Univ. '82, and had since been connected with Waseda Univ., started the same year by Marquis Okuma; was once one of the leading members of the Progressists but for years past kept aloof from active politics, devoting himself to affairs of the Univ.; was Director of the Commercial Bureau, Foreign Office, '96-97 and Senior Councillor of Education, '98. He was repeatedly returned to the House from Saitama-ken; toured in Europe and America '14; and entered Okuma Cabinet in Aug., '15, resigning Presidency of Waseda. Now a leader of Kenseikai. Add. Sekiguchi, Tokyo. — p. 164 ([scan leaf 203](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n203/mode/1up))

**Takata, Shinzo** Prop. of well-known Takata Shokai, exp. and imp. firm, b. in Sado, '55; student-interpreter at Ebisu Customs House '69; reached Tokyo in '70 to seek fortune; entered the Allen's Firm, Yokohama the following year; started business on his own account '82 which, by his untiring perseverance and extreme prudence, has been carried to present state of prosperity. Add. Yushima, Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 164 ([scan leaf 203](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n203/mode/1up))

**Takatsukasa, Hiromichi, Prince** formerly one of the five proud houses of Fujiwara extraction and Gosekke (see Ichijo), founded by Kanehira (1228-94 A.D.), scion of the Princely House of Konoye. The present head was b. in '55 and is Maj.-Gen., Grand Chamberlain to His Majesty. Add. Kami-Nibancho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 164 ([scan leaf 203](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n203/mode/1up))

**Takayama, Keizo** Mng.-Dir. of Kwansai Trust Co., Osaka, etc.; was b. '62 in Fukuoka-ken; grad. from Waseda Special School '85; a journalist in Tokyo and Osaka for several years; Chief of Osaka Branch of Mitsui Drygoods Store for about ten years; Mg.-Dir. of Kyodo Fire Ins. Co. '05-12 Add. Momodani, Osaka. — p. 164 ([scan leaf 203](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n203/mode/1up))

**Takekoshi, Yosaburo** ex-M.P., author and journalist; b. '65 at Honjo, Tokyo, and adopted into the present family; studied at the Doninsha and Keiogijuku; began his journalistic career about '88 as writer on the staffs of the Osaka Koron and Kokumin, then that of the Jiji; started his own paper in Tokyo, the short lived Sekai-no-Nippon, '96; chef de Cabinet of Marquis Saionji '98. Has written "History of 2,500 Years of Japan" and other works. He travelled abroad in '06; next the South Pacific '09. Add. Higashi-Okubo, near Tokyo. — p. 164 ([scan leaf 203](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n203/mode/1up))

**Takemura, Yoshisada** Proprietor of the Teikoku News Agency, b. in '61 in Niigata-ken; started the news agency in '96; sat for years in the Tokyo Municipal Council; M.P. for his native district in '08. Add. Fujimi-cho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 164 ([scan leaf 203](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n203/mode/1up))


## Who's Who

**Takenouchi, Ko** Pres. of Sakae Bank, Tokyo, Dir. of Takenouchi Mining Co., etc.; b. Tosa in '39, and once took active part in politics, having been a chief lieutenant of Count Itagaki in his democratic agitation before the advent of constitutional regime, and sat in the House in the first few sessions. Add. Kogaicho, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 155 ([scan leaf 204](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n204/mode/1up))

**Takenouchi, Naoya** Man.-Dir. of Nisshin S.S. Co., b. '67 in Toyama-ken; grad. Law Coll., Imp. Tokyo Univ. '94 and entered Communication Dept. that year; resigned it to join in the Osaka Shosen Kaisha '96; travelled Europe and America, '04. Is one of the promoters of the Nisshin. Add. Kami-Osaki, Tokyo. — p. 155 ([scan leaf 204](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n204/mode/1up))

**Takeo, Jiemon** Pres. of Settsu Cotton Sp. Co. and Nippon Cot. Sp. Co., Dir. of 34th Bank etc.; b. 54 in Hyogo-ken; succeeded to a dry goods business. Organized the Settsu Cotton Sp. Co. in '89, and Osaka Railway Co. the same year; Japan Cotton Co. in '92; the Nippon in '93; which together with the Settsu is a leading Cotton Mill in Japan. Add. Minami-Hommachi, Higashi, Osaka — p. 155 ([scan leaf 204](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n204/mode/1up))

**Taketomi, Hotei** Vice-Adm. (ret.), ex-Chief of Naval Affair Bureau; b. '52 in Saga-ken; Sub-Lieut. in '79; Capt. in '98, meanwhile Com. of the Akagi; Aide-de-Camp to H.H. the Crown Prince; Com. of the Iwate and some others. Add. Takagicho, Aoyama, Tokyo. — p. 155 ([scan leaf 204](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n204/mode/1up))

**Taketomi, Tokitoshi** M.P. and leader of Kenseikai, ex-Min. of Finance; b. '55 in Saga-ken; from obscure politician of local reputation he suddenly came to the front as soon as he began to sit in the House where he has been returned almost without a break from the first session '90; Dir. of the Com. and Ind'al Bureau, Dept. of Agr. and Com. in the Matsukata-Okuma Ministry, '96-97; Chief Secretary of the Cabinet in the Okuma-Itagaki Ministry; Minister of Communications in 2nd Okuma Ministry; occupied chair of Finance in Aug., '15-16. Add. Kaga-cho, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 155 ([scan leaf 204](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n204/mode/1up))

**Takeuchi, Toru** Lieut.-Gen. and Com. of Communication Corps; b. b. '67 in Fukui-ken; grad. Mil. Staff College; Sub-Lieut. Eng. '87; Lieut.-Gen. '16; has held for a long time posts in War Dept. and Staff Board; present post '16. Add. Ichigaya, Tokyo. — p. 155 ([scan leaf 204](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n204/mode/1up))

**Takeuchi, Tsunekichi** nom de plume Seihō, Painter-in-Ordinary to the Court, Prof. at Kyoto Fine Art Academy, is one of the living master painters of the Japanese school; was b. '64 at Kyoto and studied under the late Kōno Baisei; visited Europe '00; has frequently won 2nd prizes at the Gov. Art Exhibition. Add. Miike-dori, Yanagikoji, Kyoto. — p. 155 ([scan leaf 204](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n204/mode/1up))

**Takezoye, Shin-ichiro** D. Litt., a noted Chinese scholar; b. in '40 at Amakusa, and held a post of histographer, in the Department of Education; was the Japanese Minister in Korea '84-85, and had narrow escape when a mob rose there; a similar post at Peking and next a chair in the Lit. Coll., Imp. Tokyo Univ. after which he has retired from active work; was rewarded with the Japanese Academy prize in 1914. Add. Otsuka Sakashitacho, Tokyo. — p. 155 ([scan leaf 204](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n204/mode/1up))

**Takikawa, Benzo** Chairman Kobe Chamber of Commerce, is a leading manufacturer of matches in Japan; was b. '51 at Chofu; engaged in match industry in Kobe since '80 and has done much in restoring credit of Japanese matches abroad. Add. Kusunoki-cho, Kobe. — p. 155 ([scan leaf 204](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n204/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Tamura, Shinkichi** Prop. of Tamura Trading Firm, Kobe, and Vice-Pres. of Kobe Chamber of Commerce, Pres. of Japan-Canada Bank and Nippon Rice Cleaning Co., M.P.; b. '63 in Osaka; when 25 years old he proceeded to Canada and took to trade after years of hardship; afterwards opened above bank and has done much in promoting trade between Japan and Canada. Add Nakayamatedori Gochome, Kobe. — p. 156 ([scan leaf 205](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n205/mode/1up))

**Tanabe, Sakuro** D. Eng. (Japan), Prof. of Engineering at the Imp. Kyoto Univ. since '00; b. '62 at Tokyo; grad. '84 from Gov. Engineering Col.; undertook the work of connecting lake Biwa with the river Kamo by canal which was successfully carried out '90; Prof. in the Eng. Col. of the Imp. Tokyo Univ., then an Engineer to the Hokkaido Government. Add. Imp. Univ. Kyoto. — p. 156 ([scan leaf 205](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n205/mode/1up))

**Tanabe, Teikichi** Pres. of Kyodo Fire Ins. Co. and Japan Celluloid and Artificial Silk Co., Dir. of Sakura Cement Co., Settsu Elec. Co., etc.; b. '47 at Numazu; early mastered Western knowledge and became teacher, but left it to start in business, entering the Sumitomo's service, Osaka, in '79. It was his suggestion, that present Sumitomo Bank was organized. He later resigned the service and has devoted himself to business generally. Add. Sumiyoshi, Hyogo-ken. — p. 156 ([scan leaf 205](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n205/mode/1up))

**Tanahashi, Aya** b. '39 at Osaka, is one of the most noted lady educationalists; besides teaching at the Gov. Higher Girls' Normal School and other places she keeps her own private girls' school. Her son, Mr. Ichiro Tanahashi, ex-M.P., maintains the tradition of the family keeping a flourishing private boys' school of secondary education. Add. Maruyama Shimmachi, Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 156 ([scan leaf 205](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n205/mode/1up))

**Tanaka, Gentaro** Chief Dir. of Kyoto Stock Exchange, Pres. of Kyoto Elec. Light Co. and Kyoto-Shoko and Kameoka Banks etc.; b. '53 in Kyoto-fu; is a prominent citizen of Kyoto which he frequently represented in the House of Commons, and a Mem. of the House of Peers as representative of the highest tax-payers of the prefecture. He is connected with most leading business establishments in Kyoto. Add. Kyoto. — p. 156 ([scan leaf 205](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n205/mode/1up))

**Tanaka, Giichi** Lieut.-Gen. and Vice-Chief of the General Staff Board; was b. '63 in Yamaguchi; Sub-Lieut. '86; grad. from the Staff Coll. '98; Dir. of Military Affairs Bureau '10-13; despatched abroad '13; then Com. of the 2nd Brigade, Infantry; promoted to Lieut.-Gen. and present post, Oct. '15. Add. Funacho, Yotsuya, Tokyo. — p. 156 ([scan leaf 205](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n205/mode/1up))

**Tanaka, Ginnosuke** Director of Tanaka Mining Co. and Auditor of the Taihoku Savings Bank, was b. '73 in Tokyo; is grandson of the late Hcihachi Tanaka, widely known as "Raw-silk-king" of his time; studied at Leeds High School and Trinity, Camb., England and is a well-known figure in social circles. Add. Ichibei-cho, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 156 ([scan leaf 205](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n205/mode/1up))

**Tanaka, Hozumi** D.C.L. (Japan), M.A. (Columbia), Prof. at Waseda Univ.; was b. in '76 in Nagano-ken; grad. from Waseda '96 and further studied '01-03 in America, England and Germany. Was once connected with the Yomiuri and was also the editor-in-chief of the Nichi Nichi. Has written several works on finance. Add. 170 Benten-cho, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 156 ([scan leaf 205](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n205/mode/1up))

**Tanaka, Inagi** Chief Librarian of the Imperial Library. b. '56 in Suwo; grad. '81 course of Japanese and Chinese Literatures, Imp. Univ.; was subsequently appointed Lecturer at the Univ. combining other duties at the Edu. Dep't; visited Europe and America '88-90 to study the science of library management and was appointed in '93 to the present post. Add. Akebonocho, Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 156 ([scan leaf 205](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n205/mode/1up))


## Who's Who in Japan

**Tanaka, Mitsu-aki** Count (cr. Visc. '37, Count '90), ex-Minister of the Imp. Household; b. '43, in Tosa; has had a varied career, having been connected with the Army till '81 when he held the rank of Quartermaster Gen., and then retired from the service; Chief Commissioner of the Metropolitan Police, '89-91; transferred the following year to the post of President of the Peers' School, to be shortly after removed to the office of Vice-Min. of the Imp. Household, where he succeeded Count Hijikata in the Ministry '98; widow in '05; resigned the Ministry in '09. Add. Iwabuchi, Tokaido. — p. 157 ([scan leaf 206](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n206/mode/1up))

**Tanaka, Shohei** D. Sc. (Japan), b. in Awaji '62; studied acoustics at Berlin, '84-98, and received a reward from the Kaiser for his invention of a wind instrument while studying at Berlin; is an consulting engineer of the Imp. Railways and is also doing researches on Western and Japanese musics to improve the latter. Add. Tsukishima, Tokyo. — p. 157 ([scan leaf 206](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n206/mode/1up))

**Tanaka, Son** Dir. of the Oriental Compressor Co., adopted son of Count Tanaka, ex-Minister of Imperial Household; was b. '64 in Fukushima, brother of Sei Iwagami; studied law and economics in France '91-00; lecturer at the Peers' School '01; was returned to the House from Kochi in '02. Add. Motozono-cho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 157 ([scan leaf 206](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n206/mode/1up))

**Tanaka, Tsunenori** Dir. of Kirin Brewery Co., Imp. Theatre Co. etc., was b. '60 in Tokyo. Early entered the Bank of Japan and arose to be the Chief of the Bureau of Supplies. Add. Naka-Rokubancho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 157 ([scan leaf 206](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n206/mode/1up))

**Tanaka, Ryuzo** Dir. of Osaka Shosen Kaisha, and Electric Trust Co.; b. '64 in Akita-ken; grad. Law Coll., Imp. Tokyo Univ. '89 and entered Dept. of Agr. and Com. in which he to be Dir. of Mining Bureau '92; became lawyer '93; appointed the Secretary of the House of Representatives '95; retired from Gov. service to enter Fujita-gumi; promoted Man.-Dir. of the Firm. Dir. Kosaka Rly. Co. '10; once M.P. for Akita-ken. Add. Nakanocho, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 157 ([scan leaf 206](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n206/mode/1up))

**Tanaka, Zenryu** M.P. and Par. Jun.-Secy. of Navy Dept.; b. '74 in Aichi-ken; when 13 years old entered Buddhist priesthood; grad. Budd. Philos. at Tetsugakkwan (now Toyo Univ.) '95; teacher at Midd. Sch. till '98; went over to South China '99 to propagate Budd., where he stayed about 13 years, consequently is known as specialist on China. Par. Jun.-Secy. in '15. Add. Tokiwamura, Aichi-gun, Aichi-ken. — p. 157 ([scan leaf 206](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n206/mode/1up))

**Tanakadate, Aikitsu** D. Sc. (Japan), b. '56 in Iwate-ken; after grad. Col. of Science of Imp. Tokyo Univ., was sent to England and Germany to complete his studies in physics. Appointed professor of physics '91 and then member of the Committee of Earthquake Investigation of the Imp. Geodetic Commission, and of the Imperial Academy; Committee of Mil. Aviation since '12 and also occupies a chair on aeronautics in Imp. Tokyo Univ. Was granted 2nd Class Rising Sun for his service about military baloon during the Russo-Japanese War. Add. Yayoi-cho, Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 157 ([scan leaf 206](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n206/mode/1up))

**Taniguchi, Fusazo** Pres. of Osaka Godo Cotton Sp. Co., Izumi Cot. Sp. Co., Nagoya Textile Co. and Toyota-shiki Weaving Machine Co., etc.; b. '61 in Osaka-fu; came to Osaka to start as a dealer in cottons in '88; Dir. of Meiji Cot. Co., '95; organized Osaka Godo Co. '00. Since then he has occupied several important posts in Cot. Sp. and weaving circles in Kansai Districts. Add. Kitakyutaro, Higashi, Osaka. — p. 157 ([scan leaf 206](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n206/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Tashiro, Yoshinori** M.D., Prof. in Med. Col. of Imp. Tokyo Univ.; b. '64; grad. the Med. Col. in '89; was given a chair at his alma mater '92; established the Tashiro Hospital in '95; was entrusted with the duties of the President of the Mitsui Charity Hospital opened in '08. Add. Neribei-cho, Shitaya, Tokyo. — p. 158 ([scan leaf 207](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n207/mode/1up))

**Tatsuma, Han-emon** millionaire sake brewer in Japan, was b. '77 at Naruo, Hyogo-ken, the largest brewing centre in Japan, the family having kept up the business for two centuries; owns a number of steamers, and is connected with various business undertakings. Add. Nishinomiya, Hyogo-ken. — p. 158 ([scan leaf 207](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n207/mode/1up))

**Tatsuno, Kingo** D.E. (Japan), Emeritus Prof. Imp. Tokyo Univ., b. '56 at Karatsu, is a pioneer architect having grad. the now defunct Eng. College; studied in England '80-83, and had charge of the building of the Bank of Japan, Parliament and other large edifices. At present he practises his profession, and is a consulting architect to many important offices. Add. Shinsaka, Akasaka, Tokyo. — p. 158 ([scan leaf 207](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n207/mode/1up))

**Tejima, Seiichi** Emeritus Prof. of Higher Technical School, Tokyo; b. Kazusa; studied in America, '70-74; held various posts as Bur. Dir., Dep't of Edu.; Chief Commissioner of Japan at the Chicago and St. Louis World's Fairs. As Pres. of Higher Technical School he rendered great services to promote technical education. Add. Komagome, Tokyo. — p. 158 ([scan leaf 207](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n207/mode/1up))

**Terada, Yukichi** Principal of the Seika School and the Nihombashi Girls' School, Manager of the Japan Athletic Association; b. '53 in Tokyo; studied German and Mining in Kaisei Gakko as early as '70; sent to Europe and America in '89; appointed Councillor to the Edu. Dept.; Secretary, and then School Inspector of that Dept.; Pres. of the Tokyo Higher Commercial School '02-04. Add. Iida-machi, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 158 ([scan leaf 207](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n207/mode/1up))

**Teragaki, Iso** Vice-Adm. (ret. '12), b. '57 in Kanazawa; Sub-Lieut. in '82; Captain in '99; and Vice-Adm. in '12; also served as Adjutant of the Yokosuka Adm., Com. of the Yoshino, Matsushima and Akagi, Chief Staff of the said Adm. in '11. Add. Shimoshibuya, Tokyo. — p. 158 ([scan leaf 207](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n207/mode/1up))

**Terajima, Seiichiro** 2nd Count, was b. '70 in Tokyo, eldest son of the late Count Terajima who in his days occupied chair of Foreign Office; studied at Oxford and next at Paris, returning home in '04 to be appointed Per. Secretary to Count Hayashi, then Foreign Minister; was elected Mem. of House of Peers '07 and '11. Add. Shirokane, Shiba, Tokyo. — p. 158 ([scan leaf 207](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n207/mode/1up))

**Terao, Seiichi** D. Eng., Prof. of Shipbuilding at the Imp. Tokyo Univ., since '92, having graduated from it in '90, and further studied in England '94-97. He was b. in '67 in Nagoya. Add. Hisakata-machi, Koishikawa, Tokyo. — p. 158 ([scan leaf 207](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n207/mode/1up))

**Terao, Hisashi** D. Sc. (Japan), Prof. in Col. of Science of the Imp. Tokyo Univ., Dir. of the Gov. Astr. Observatory of Tokyo, and Mem. of Imp. Acad. of Japan; b. '54 at Fukuoka; studied mathematics and astronomy in France, '70-88. Add. Kashiwagi, near Tokyo. — p. 158 ([scan leaf 207](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n207/mode/1up))

**Terao, Toru** D.C.L., ex-Prof. in Imp. Tokyo Univ.; b. '58 at Fukuoka; connected with the Bench for some years after grad. from the Imp. Tokyo Univ., transferred to the Col. of Law of his alma mater, '91; studied at Brussels, '92-95 to finish his study of international law, his special line; resigned his post as Councillor of the Foreign Office, June, '05, on account of his being one of the "Seven Univ. Jingoes." Was Legal Adv. to the Southern Chinese Army '12. Add. Reinanzaka, Akasaka, Tokyo. — p. 158 ([scan leaf 207](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n207/mode/1up))


## Who's Who

**Terauchi, Masakata** Count (Vis. cr. '07; Count '11), Field-Marshal, Prime Minister; b. '52 in Choshu; Sub-Lieut. '71; was wounded in the right arm in the Civil War of '78 and its free use was permanently lost; Maj. '79; ordered to study in France, '82, and on his return was given post at the Mil. Cad. School; Per. Sec. to the War Min.; Vice-Dir. Toyama Mil. School, Dir. Cav. School, Chief Staff Officer to an Army Div.; Sectional Chief of the General Staff Board, etc.; supervised transport affair in the Japan-China War and rewarded with 3rd Class Golden Kite; Lieut.-Gen., '97, and appointed Inspector-General of Military Education; transferred to be Vice-Chief of the General Staff; appointed War Min. '02 in the Katsura Cabinet, and resigned it in '11. He was granted G.C.V.O. by the late King Edward. Gov.-Gen. of Chosen '11-16. Formed his Cabinet in Oct. '16. Field-Marshal '16. Add. Nagata-cho, Tokyo. — p. 159 ([scan leaf 208](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n208/mode/1up))

**Tochinai, Shojiro** Vice-Adm., ex-Com. of 1st Squadron; was b. in '66 at Morioka; Midshipman in '83; was the Personal Secretary to the Minister '04-08; Com. of the Training Squadron, Chief of the Naval Affairs Bureau, etc., Com. of 1st Squadron in '14. Add. Nagasaka, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 159 ([scan leaf 208](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n208/mode/1up))

**Toda, Ujitomo** Count (cr. '84, formery Daimyo of Ogaki). Grand Master of Ceremonies, b. '29; studied in America, '71-76; Legation Councillor, '86; Min. Plenip. accredited to the Court of Vienna; removed to the Imp. Household Dep't as Vice-Chief of the Board of Ceremonies; was on suite of Prince Higashifushimi in King George's Coronation. The Countess, grandaunt of Prince Iwakura, is a well-known figure in society. Add. Surugadai, Tokyo. — p. 159 ([scan leaf 208](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n208/mode/1up))

**Tozuka, Kankai** Surgeon-Gen. (ret.) of the Navy, Director of the Tedzuka Surgeon Hospital; was b. '54 in Ehime-ken; appointed Normal 2nd Assist. Surgeon '81, and was finally promoted to the high rank in service, after having filled the posts of Head of the Naval Medical College, Chief Surgeon of the Yokosuka and Kure Adm., Instructor in the Naval Staff Col., etc. Add. Minami-Shinagawa, near Tokyo. — p. 159 ([scan leaf 208](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n208/mode/1up))

**Togo, Heihachiro** Count (cr. '07), Admiral of Fleet, O.M. (Br.), 1st Class Golden Kite and Grand Order of Chrysanthemum, Mem. of Supreme Mil. Council, hero of the memorable battle of the Japan Sea, Lord Tutor to the Crown Prince; was b. in '47, a son of petty retainer of the Lord of Kagoshima. He commenced sailor's career at 15 and at 21 first came under fire in fighting with the late Enomoto's Kwaiten. In '71 he with 13 young officers were sent to England for study, of whom he is the only survivor, prosecuting studies till '73, when he returned home, on board the Hiyei just completed at an English yard. In the Japan-China War, he commanded the cruiser Naniwa, and sank the Chinese transport Kowshing, a British steamer flying the British flag. On the return of peace Togo was promoted Rear-Adm.; Vice-Admiral in '00 with the command of Maizuru Admiralty; Commander-in-Chief of the Combined Fleet on the eve of the outbreak of hostilities with Russia, culminating in the virtual annihilation of the Baltic Fleet in the historic Battle of Japan Sea fought on 27th May, '05. Togo was promoted full Admiral, June '04; app. Chief of Naval Staff, '05, and transferred to Mem. Sup. Council of War '09; Adm. of Fleet in '12. Attended King George's Coronation on the suite of Prince Higashi-Fushimi. The Adm.'s wife is a daughter of the late Viso. Kaeda, Privy Councillor; two sons and a daughter. Add. Kami-rokuban-cho, Tokyo. — p. 159 ([scan leaf 208](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n208/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Tokonami, Takejiro** M.P., b. in Kagoshima '63; graduated Imp. Tokyo Univ. '90; entered civil service at the Home Office, either at head office or local office, in which he rose to be Governor of Tokushimaken '01, to be transferred to Dir. Local Administrations, Home Office in '03, and to the Vice-Minister of Home Affairs in '11-12; Pres. of Imp. Govt. Rlys. '13; now leader of Seiyukai. Add. Mikawadai, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 160 ([scan leaf 209](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n209/mode/1up))

**Tokudaiji, Sanenori** Prince (cr. 11), formerly courtier, ex-Grand Chamberlain and Keeper of the Privy Seal; was b. '39 at Kyoto. Has had an unvaried career, and was connected with the Imperial Household from the inauguration of the Imp. Gov.; at one time as Min. of the Household, but principally as the Grand Chamberlain which post he resigned soon after the death of the late Emperor. Was granted Grand Order and Grand Cordon of Chrysanthemum for his service during the Russo-Japanese War. The Prince is an elder brother to Marquis Saionji and Baron Sumitomo. Add. Sendagaya, Tokyo. — p. 160 ([scan leaf 209](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n209/mode/1up))

**Tokugawa, Iyesato** Prince (cr. '84). President of the House of Peers, since '03; was b. '63 at Tokyo; is a third son of Yoshiyori Tokugawa, the Tayasu branch of the Tokugawa, and was adopted as heir in '63 by the last of the Tokugawa Shoguns, himself an adopted heir, in the very next year of the surrender of Regency to the Imp. Court. Studied in England, '73-77. Toured abroad '10, the Princess is a sister of the late Prince Konoye. Add. Sendagaya, near Tokyo. — p. 160 ([scan leaf 209](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n209/mode/1up))

**Tokugawa, Kuninori** Marquis, head of the clan of Mito, one of the three Houses of Tokugawa which was founded by Yorifusa, 11th son of Iyeyasu, the fief of 350,000 koku. The Marquis was b. '86. Add. Komme, Honjo, Tokyo. — p. 160 ([scan leaf 209](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n209/mode/1up))

**Tokugawa, Rairin** Marquis, formerly one of the three great Houses of Tokugawa which enjoyed at Kishu the fief of 555,000 koku; founded by Yorinobu, 10th son of Iyeyasu. The Marquis, brother of Prince Tokugawa Iyesato and 5th son of Tayasu branch of Tokugawa, was b. '72; studied for several years in England, and has founded the Nanki Library. Add. Iigura, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 160 ([scan leaf 209](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n209/mode/1up))

**Tokugawa, Satotaka** Count (cr. '84), of the Tayasu branch of the Tokugawa; was b. '65 in Tokyo; made a tour through Europe and America '89-90; nom. Mem. of the Peers' House in '98; is now Jr.-Lord Chamberlain to H.I. Majesty. Add. Mita, Tokyo. — p. 160 ([scan leaf 209](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n209/mode/1up))

**Tokugawa, Yoshihisa** Prince (suc. '13), Mem. of House of Peers, succeeded in '13 the late Yoriyoshi Tokugawa (d. '13) the last Shogun of Tokugawa Government. Add. Dairokuten, Koishikawa, Tokyo. — p. 160 ([scan leaf 209](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n209/mode/1up))

**Tokutomi, Ichiro** Journalist, chief editor and proprietor of the Kokumin Shimbun, Crown Mem. House of Peers (nom. '11); was b. '63, at Kumamoto; edu. at the Doshisha, Kyoto; published about '86 "The Future Japan." Issued '87 a monthly magazine entitled "The Friend of the Nation," then started in '90 the "Kokumin" (Nation), a daily paper and an English monthly the "Far East"; Councillor of the Home Office in '97. He wrote many books, among which the "Life of Yoshida Sho-in" may be mentioned. He visited Europe and America in '96. Add. Aoyama, Tokyo. — p. 160 ([scan leaf 209](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n209/mode/1up))


## Who's Who

**Tokutomi, Kenjiro** (Pen-name "Eokwa"), younger brother of the above, was long on the staff of Kokumin. Wrote '99 "Namiko," a highly popular novel intended to depict collisions between old and modern ideas of Japan and was translated into English and other languages; "Omoide-noki," somewhat autobiographical; "Kuroshiwo" (Black Current), incomplete and of socialistic tendency; "Yadorigi," a real story written by a retired officer and touched up by the novelist; "Mimizu no Tawagoto" (Fantasies of the Earthworm) in '13. He undertook pilgrimage to Jerusalem and to Tolstoy in '06, and issued a 4-page fortnightly as his organ. Add. Kasuya, near Tokyo. — p. 161 ([scan leaf 210](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n210/mode/1up))

**Tomatsu, Magotaro** Baron (cr. '07), Crown Mem. House of Peers, Paymaster-Gen., Army (ret. '09); was b. '47 in Wakayama; took part in the expedition of Choshu by the Tokugawa; studied for a short while at Kobe and Tokyo, but soon returned home and took to farming; a local school inspector '75, then an official of industrial section of Wakayama Kencho; clerk of Military Store Headquarters at Kobe in the Civil War '77, and had long been connected with the supply dept'ment of the Army, ultimately rising to the top of the service. Was granted 2nd Class Golden Kite (war honour). Add. Tozuka, suburb of Tokyo. — p. 161 ([scan leaf 210](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n210/mode/1up))

**Tomi-i, Masa-akira** D.C.L. (Japan), Mem. House of Peers, Emeritus Prof. of Imp. Tokyo Univ.; b. '58 at Kyoto; grad. Lyon Univ., France '83; Prof. of the College of Law of the Imp. Tokyo Univ. '85, once Dean of the College. Add. Ichigaya Yakoji-machi, Tokyo. — p. 161 ([scan leaf 210](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n210/mode/1up))

**Tomizu, Hiroto** ex-M.P. (Seiyukai), D.C.L. (Japan); b. '61 at Kanazawa; studied law in England and Germany '89-94; was one of the "Seven University Jingoes," and his compulsory resignation occasioned the resignation of President Yamakawa, next the famous strike of the Faculty, and finally resignation of the Min. of Education. The agitation was finally settled by the reinstallment of the Prof. to his former seat. Add. Iida-machi, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 161 ([scan leaf 210](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n210/mode/1up))

**Tomono, Otoya** Dir. of the Nishiwaki Bank and Taiyo Life Ins. Co., b. '67 in Tokyo; grad. in '92 from the Law Col., Imp. Tokyo University under adverse circumstances; then served at the Financial Department, but soon resigned to enter the Industrial Bank of Japan on its establishment. Add. Kaga-cho, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 161 ([scan leaf 210](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n210/mode/1up))

**Toshima, Yozo** Lieut.-Gen. (ret.), was b. '52 in Hiroshima-ken; Sub-Lieut. of Art. in '79; further studied at the Artillery and Engineering School; Com. of the Tokyo Bay Forts in '02 and in the attack of Port Arthur he had charge of siege batteries as Maj.-Gen.; Lieut.-Gen. in '08; Mayor of Hiroshima in '14. Add. Rokkon-cho, Aoyama, Tokyo. — p. 161 ([scan leaf 210](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n210/mode/1up))

**Toyama, Mitsuru** Political boss, ex-Pres. of the Gen-yo-sha (a political club); was b. '55 in Fukuoka-ken; was thrown in prison for having sided with the rebels in the Civil War of '77. On his release he played active part in democratic movemenent as wire-puller, but has lately stood aloof from politics. Is a great friend of the Chinese reform agitators. Add. Reinanzaka, Akasaka, Tokyo. — p. 161 ([scan leaf 210](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n210/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Toyama, Shūzō** Pres. of Osaka Commercial Information Agency and Mng.-Dir. of Osaka Savings Bank, b. '43 in Niigata-ken; studied at Keio Univ. and Kaisei Gakko. Had been in service of Finance Dept., Yokohama Specie Bank, Bank of Japan, etc.; M.P. in '92. Afterwards devoted himself to business and had been Pres. of Naniwa Bank, Hanshin Elec. Railway Co., Osaka Chamber of Commerce, Osaka Syrup Co., Osaka Bankers' Association; Aud. of Osaka Warehouse, Dai Nippon Brewery, Kawasaki Dockyard, etc. Add. Nakanoshima, Osaka. — p. 162 ([scan leaf 211](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n211/mode/1up))

**Toshimitsu, Tsurumatsu** Pres. of Kinugawa Hydro-Elec. Co., Dir. of Tokyo Gas Co. etc.; b. '63 in Oita-ken; came to Tokyo in '87, to study law at Meij Univ.; became lawyer and struggled his way with success; once M.P., but soon resigned it to enter into business; Dir. of then Tokyo Railway Co.; established Kinugawa Hydro-Elec. Co. and Chiyoda Gas Co., the latter being amalgamated with Tokyo Gas Co. later. Add. Tomikawacho, Fukagawa, Tokyo. — p. 162 ([scan leaf 211](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n211/mode/1up))

**Toyokawa, Ryohei** mem. House of Peers (nom. '16). Adviser to Toyokuni Bank, Vice-Chairman of Bankers' Association of Tokyo, Chairman of the Committee of Tokyo Clearing-House, etc.; b. '52 in Tosa, and since his grad. from the Keiogijuku has been connected with the Mitsubishi with whom he is related by blood. Had charge of the Mitubishi Banking Dep't, and resigned it in '12; a prominent figure in business circles of Tokyo. Add. Suidocho, Koiah., Tokyo. — p. 162 ([scan leaf 211](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n211/mode/1up))

**Toyoshima, Naomichi** D.C.L., Dir. of Bureau, Justice Dept.; b. '71 in Tokyo; grad. '95 from Law Coll., Imp. Tokyo Univ.; Pub. Proc. in '97; attached to the Supreme Court '07, Councillor of Justice Dept. '11. Has also been Lecturer at his alma mater, Waseda Univ., etc. Add. Akebonocho, Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 162 ([scan leaf 211](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n211/mode/1up))

**Tsuboi, Kumazo** D. Lit. (Japan); Professor of the Imp. Tokyo Univ. (Lit. Col.), was born in '58 at Osaka; grad. from Lit. of the Univ., and was sent by Govt. to Europe for study; Prof. at his alma mater in '91; then Dean of Col. Add. Yayoicho, Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 162 ([scan leaf 211](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n211/mode/1up))

**Tsubouchi, Yuzo** D. Litt. (Japan), author and a man of letters; b. '59 in Aichi-ken; is connected with the Waseda Univ., from its inception '82, being now its Emeritus Prof.; grad. Col. Lit. of the now Imp. Tokyo Univ. '82; is more widely known in public as the pioneer novelist of modern education; is the foremost Shakespearian scholar of Japan and translated several of his plays; has also written several dramas and works on ethics; organized in '11 the Literature and Drama Society but dissolved in '13. Add. Yochomachi, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 162 ([scan leaf 211](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n211/mode/1up))

**Tsuboya, Zenshiro** Dir. of Hakubunkan, Mem. Tokyo City Council, journalist and traveller; b. '62 in Echigo; grad. from Waseda '88, and connected with the Firm ever since; toured through Europe '19; has written a number of works on travel, etc. Add.
