Kita-Yamabushi-cho, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 162 ([scan leaf 211](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n211/mode/1up))

**Tsuchiko, Kinshiro** D.C.L., Pres. of the Yokohama Fire and Transport Insurance Co.; b. '63 at Mino; grad. Imp. Tokyo Univ. '85; studied for several years in America and England, and went through practical side of banking business as an extra-employé of a certain bank in America; entered the Yokohama Specie Bank about '90 and held post of junior agent at its London Branch; entered the present Co., '96. Add. Yumi-cho, Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 162 ([scan leaf 211](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n211/mode/1up))


## Who's Who in Japan

**Tsuchiya, Kokin** Vice-Adm., Com. of Ominato Naval Station; b. in Okazaki in '65, eldest son of Gen. Baron Tsuchiya; Midshipman '86. Commander '00. Captain '05 Rear-Adm. and Com. of Maizuru Torpedo Corps '11, Com. of the Kure Adm. Squadron '12–14; Com. of 3rd Squadron, and of 1st Torpedo Flotilla, then Com. of the 2nd Torpedo Flotilla; present post and Vice-Adm. '15. Add. Okubo, Tokyo. — p. 163 ([scan leaf 212](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n212/mode/1up))

**Tsuchiya, Mitsuharu** Baron (cr. '07), Gen. (ret.), b. in '54 in Okazaki, Aichi-ken; early entered Military service and app. Sub-Lieut. in '72; took part in Japan-China War as Staff Officer of Headquarters, and also Com. of 11th Army Div. in the Russo-Japanese War and was wounded at Port Arthur; rewarded with 2nd Order of Golden Kite. Add. Okubo, near Tokyo. — p. 163 ([scan leaf 212](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n212/mode/1up))

**Tsuchiya, Tamotsu** Vice-Adm. (ret), ex-Chief of Naval Personnel Bureau; b. in Tokyo-fu '50; Sub-Lieut. in '85; Mem. of the 1st Section of Naval Staff Board and Dir. of Bureau for Naval Affairs; Rear-Adm. '07; Com. of Ominato Station '12. Add. Kami-Ochiai, suburb of Tokyo. — p. 163 ([scan leaf 212](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n212/mode/1up))

**Tsuda, Umeko** lady educationist and proprietor of the Girls' English School founded by herself in Tokyo; b. '65 in Tokyo; daughter of the late Tsuda Sen, an agriculturist; was among the first batch of girls sent '71 to U.S.A. for study by the Imp. Gov.; stayed there till '82, and on returning home was appointed teacher in the Peeress' School and lecturer in the Women's Higher Normal School; has frequently visited America. Add. Goban-cho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 163 ([scan leaf 212](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n212/mode/1up))

**Tsujimura, Kusuzo** Paymaster-Gen. (ret. '14), b. in '65 in Kochi; grad. from Mil. Paymasters' School '93; was Paym. to Guards Division and instructor of Pay'ter' Sch., before he was transferred to War Office in '02; subsequently appointed Pres. of Pay'ters' Sch., Chief Paym. of 10th Army Div., Garrison of Lioatung, Guards Div., Dir. Mil. Account and Supply Bur. etc. Add. Ichigaya Nakano-cho, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 163 ([scan leaf 212](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n212/mode/1up))

**Tsukamoto, Katsuyoshi** Baron, Lieut.-Gen. (ret.), b. '47 in Gifu; Sub-Lieut. '72, has subsequently served as Reg. Com., Dir. Military Coll., Chief Staff, of 9th Division and took part in '94–95 and '04–05 Wars and won 2nd class Golden Kite. Add. Kanagawa. — p. 163 ([scan leaf 212](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n212/mode/1up))

**Tsukamoto, Yasushi** D. Eng., Prof. of Architecture, Imp. Tokyo Univ., b. '69 in Kyoto; grad. in '93 from University, in which he was appointed lecturer; further studied in England, Germany and France '99–02; Prof. at the alma mater on his return. Add. Hisakata, Koishikawa, Tokyo. — p. 163 ([scan leaf 212](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n212/mode/1up))

**Tsukuda, Ichiyo** Dir. of the South Manchuria Rly. (app. 1913), ex-Vice-Pres. of the Yokohama Specie Bank; was b. '63 in Ehime-ken; grad. Law at the Imp. Tokyo Univ. in '90, and subsequently entered the Finance Dept. in which he served as Secretary, Chief of Customs House, etc.; Adv. to Yuan Shihkai '03–06. Add. Takagicho, Aoyama, Tokyo. — p. 163 ([scan leaf 212](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n212/mode/1up))

**Tsukui, Shigeru** Dir. of Stock and Shares Bureau, Bank of Japan; b. '69 in Hiyogo-ken; grad. from Imperial Tokyo Univ. (Law), '93, and for some years practised law. Add. Ichigaya Kaga-cho, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 163 ([scan leaf 212](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n212/mode/1up))

**Tsunashima, Kakichi** Pastor of Bancho Congregational Church, Tokyo, b. '60 in Okayama-ken; studied at Doshisha, Kyoto and Yale Univ. U.S.A.; Pastor of the present church since that time. Peace Envoy to U.S.A. in '13 representing Christian churches of Japan, and to Constance in '14 representing Japan; is a vigorous preacher. Add. Nakarokubancho, Tokyo. — p. 163 ([scan leaf 212](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n212/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Tsuneto, Noritaka** Agr. D., Pres. of Rasa Island Phosphate Co.; b. '57 in Oita-ken; grad. Agr. Coll., Imp. Tokyo Univ. '83; Expert at Dept. Agr., Prof. at Morioka Agr. and Forest School, then at Kagoshima; Expert Formosan Govt; retired from official service '04. Add. Wakamiyacho, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 164 ([scan leaf 213](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n213/mode/1up))

**Tsuruta, Teijiro** Surg.-Gen., ex-Chief Surg. of 1st Division; b. '65 in Tokyo; Surg.-Sub-Lt. '90, Surg.-Col. '05, Surg.-Gen. '10; President of Red Cross Hospital of Japan '10-14; last post in '14. Add. Sendagaya, Tokyo. — p. 164 ([scan leaf 213](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n213/mode/1up))

**Tsutsui, Yaoju** M.D., Dir. of Okayama Medical College and Hospital of Okayama Prefecture; b. '63, in Miye-ken; graduated from Med. College, Imp. Tokyo Univ. in '90 and appointed Prof. of Chiba Med. College the same year; Dir. of Hospital of Chiba Prefecture for 23 years; transferred to Okayama in '13; while in Chiba he proceeded to Germany for study and became M.D. in '03. Add. Okayama. — p. 164 ([scan leaf 213](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n213/mode/1up))

**Tsutsuki, Keiroku** Baron (cr. '08), D.C.L., Privy Councillor since '07; b. '62 in Tokyo; studied in Germany '82-85, after grad. from the Imperial Tokyo University; appointed Sec. of Legation and Per. Sec. to the Foreign Min., '88; travelled abroad '89 on the suite of Prince Yamagata; entered the Home Office in which he subsequently appointed Dir. of Public Works Bureau, '94; again travelled with Prince Yamagata when the latter was dispatched to Moscow to attend the Tsar Nicholas' Coronation Ceremony; Vice-Min. of Edu., '97; Extr. Min. Plen. unattached '98; Vice-Min. of Foreign Affairs '99 under the late Vis. Aoki, but left it owing to some disagreement of views with his chief. Was on the suite of the late Prince Ito when he visited Europe, '01-02. Appointed Special Amb. to attend The Hague Peace Conference of '07; was created Baron for the service. Add. Maminana, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 164 ([scan leaf 213](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n213/mode/1up))

**Uchida, Kakichi** Vice-Min. of Communications, b. '66 in Tokyo; graduated Law, Imperial Tokyo Univ. '91, after which he entered the Department of Communications, in which he held post of Dir. of Shipping Bureau till '10; Dir. of Civil Affairs, Formosa, '11-15, present post, Mar. '17. Add. Kyobashi, Tokyo. — p. 164 ([scan leaf 213](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n213/mode/1up))

**Uchida, Sadatsuchi** Minister in Sweden since '11; was b. '65 at Kokura; appointed attaché in '89; élève Consul at Shanghai in '90; transferred to Seoul in '93, Consul-General in '02; Min. and Con.-General in Brazil in '07; decorated with 3rd Class Rising Sun with grant of money in '06 for his service during the Russo-Japanese War. Grad. Law, Tokyo Imp. Univ. in '89. Add. Jap. Legation, Sweden. — p. 164 ([scan leaf 213](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n213/mode/1up))

**Uchida, Yasuya** Viscount (Baron '07, Vis. '11), Ambassador at Petrograd since '16; b. '65 in Kumamoto-ken; was attaché of the Legation at Washington '87; Per. Sec. to the Min. of Agr. Com. (C't Mutsu), '90; held a similar post at the Foreign Office when his chief was transferred to it; Sec. of Legation at London '93; transferred to a similar post at Peking '95-98; Dir. of Political Bureau, to be promoted Vice-Min. of Foreign Affairs '00 and removed to the Legation at Peking, '01-06; nominated Amb. at Vienna in Feb. '07; Amb. to U.S.A. '09; Minister of Foreign Affairs in '12. Mrs. Uchida is a daughter of Mr. Do- gura and was educated at Vassar College, U.S.A. Add. Petrograd, Russia. — p. 164 ([scan leaf 213](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n213/mode/1up))


## Who's Who in Japan

**Uchimura, Kanzo** Christian teacher and essayist, was b. '61 in Saitama; grad. from Sapporo Agr. Col. '81; clerk at the Dep't of Agr. and Commerce, studied at Amerhest Univ. '83-88; instructor at the Higher School, Tokyo, and by his refusal to bow before the Emperor's portrait from religious scruple he had to resign; has subsequently devoted himself to journalism, educationist and non-Sectarin Christian teacher, and is publishing a religious monthly. Wrote "How I became a Christian" and others. Add. Tsunohazu, near Tokyo. — p. 165 ([scan leaf 214](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n214/mode/1up))

**Uchimura, Tatsujiro** Mech. E., Mem., A.S.R.E., Patent Solicitor; was b. '67 in Tokyo; grad. from the Tokyo Higher Technical College in '90; examiner of the Gov't Patent Bureau till '05; Chief Expert of the Imp. Refrigerating Co., travelled through Europe and America to inspect cold storage business in '07; is the first introducer and erector of cold storage and refrigerating plant in Japan. Add. Yariyacho, Kyobashi, Tokyo. — p. 165 ([scan leaf 214](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n214/mode/1up))

**Uchiyama, Kojiro** Gen., Chief Aide-de-Camp to his Majesty since '12, was b. '59 in Tottori; Sub-Lieut. (Art.) '70; attached to the Legations in Russia and France; rewarded with 4th Class Golden Kite in the Japan-China War, and was Com. Art. Corps attached to the 2nd Army during Russo-Japanese War. He was transferred on return of peace to attache to Japanese Legation at Petrograd where he remained till '07; Commander of Yura Fort '08 and of 15th Division (Toyohashi) and 12th (Kokura) later; General '15. Add. Harajuku, Sendagaya, Tokyo. — p. 165 ([scan leaf 214](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n214/mode/1up))

**Ue—** See Uye—. — p. 165 ([scan leaf 214](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n214/mode/1up))

**Ukita, Kazutami** D.C.L. (Japan), historian and educationist, Prof. Waseda Univ., Chief Editor of the Taiyo (monthly magazine published by the Hakubunkan); was b. '60 in Kumamoto; studied English under Capt. Janes of U.S.A. engaged by the Lord of the Fief and was converted into a Christian; entered the Doshisha '76 and grad. it '79; was for some while engaged in religious work and journalism; Prof. at his alma mater; entered Yale '92 and studied philosophy and politics; was again Prof. at the Doshisha and next at present institution '97. Add. Takata, near Tokyo. — p. 165 ([scan leaf 214](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n214/mode/1up))

**Umezawa, Michiharu** Lieut.-Gen. (ret. '15), ex-Commander of 6th Division; b. '53 in Miyagi-ken; Sub-Lieut. '72; Colonel in '99; Maj.-Gen. in '05 and to the present rank of Lieut.-Gen. in '11; took part both in the Sino-Japanese and Russo-Japanese Wars and his gallant feat at Penchihu, in the battle of Mukden was highly reputed. Attended Jamestown Exhibition, U.S.A. in '08. Add. — p. 165 ([scan leaf 214](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n214/mode/1up))

**Uno, Hogara** D.M., Emeritus Prof. Imp. Tokyo Univ. and Prop. of the Kakuzando Hospital for skin diseases; was b. in Shidzucka in '50; grad. Medicine at the Imp. Tokyo Univ. in '75; Assist.-Prof. in the Col. in '81, and subsequently appointed Vice-Pres. of University Hospital; was sent to Germany in '89 for study. Resigned the chair in '01 to establish the hospital. Add. Yumicho, Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 165 ([scan leaf 214](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n214/mode/1up))

**Uryu, Shin** Dir. of the Kirin Beer Brewing Co., Auditor of the Tokyo Warehouse Co., etc.; was b. '53 in Fukui-ken; first studied English at Nagasaki and then at Tokyo in '67; was on suite of late Ambassador, Prince Iwakura; was made to stay in Europe '72-75 and was made an official of the Railway Bureau on returning home; resigned it in '77, got into the service of the Mitsubishi firm. Add. Kami-rokuban-cho, Tokyo. — p. 165 ([scan leaf 214](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n214/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Uryu, Sotokichi** Baron (cr. '07), Admiral (ret.), holder of 2nd G. K.; b. '54 at Kanazawa; entered the Navy early and was dispatched to Annapolis, to complete study; Cap., '91; naval attache at the French Legation till '96; appointed Commander of the Akitsushima, Fuso, Yashima and others; Rear-Adm. '00 with a post at the Naval Staff Board; on the outbreak of the Japan-Russia War, commanded a detachment and sank the Yaryag and Koreetz at Ninsen on Feb. 9th; took part in the battle of Aug. 14th (off Shantung) and in that of the Japan Sea, meanwhile promoted to Vice-Adm. and appointed Commander of Takeshiki, and next Sasebo; which he was relieved in '09 and made Member of the Admirals' Council '09; Com'der Yokosuka Admiralty '09-12; full Adm. and placed on retired list in Dec. '12. He and his wife, who was educated in America, attended the Annapolis anniversary function in '09. Add. Nippori, near Tokyo. — p. 166 ([scan leaf 215](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n215/mode/1up))

**Usagawa, Kazumasa** Baron (cr. '07), Lieut.-Gen. (ret.), ex-Pres. of Oriental Development Co., Seoul (1908-13); was b. '49 in Yamaguchi and adopted by K. Usagawa of the clan; Lieut. '89 staff officer to the 1st Army in the Japan-China War which gained him the 4th Class Golden Kite; Maj.-Gen. '01, Lieut.-Gen. '06. Add. Sendagaya, near Tokyo. — p. 166 ([scan leaf 215](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n215/mode/1up))

**Usami, Shofu** Dir. Internal Affairs, Korean Govt.-General, b. '69 in Yamagata; grad. Law Imp. Univ., Tokyo, class '96; was in the service of the Home Office in the provinces and the head office, appointed Gov. of Toyama in '80; transferred to the Residency-Gen. of Korea, '00. Add. Seoul, Korea. — p. 166 ([scan leaf 215](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n215/mode/1up))

**Ushiba, Takuzo** Dir. of Chiyoda Mutual Life Insurance Co., b. '50 in Miye-ken; grad. from Keio Univ. '74; Secretary of Home Dept. '76-80; adviser to Korean Govt. '82-3; Revenue officer '83; but resigned it to enter business. Founder and Manging Dir. of Nippon Civil Eng. Co. '92; Gen.-Man. of Sanyo Ely. Co., '94, its Pres. '02. Add. Tarumi, Akashi, Hyogo-ken. — p. 166 ([scan leaf 215](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n215/mode/1up))

**Ushijima, Kinji** potato planter in Cal., U.S.A. and Pres. of Japanese Association of San Francisco; b. '65 at Kurume; studied at the Keiogijuku; went over to U.S.A. and began potato plantation, from which he made a fortune; is reputed as "Potato King" along Pacific coast. Add. Barclay, San Francisco, Cal., U.S.A. — p. 166 ([scan leaf 215](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n215/mode/1up))

**Utsunomiya, Taro** Lieut.-Gen., Com. of the 4th Division; b. in Saga '51; Sub-Lieut. of Infantry in '85; Col. '05 and Major-Gen. in '09. Was attached to the Legation in London '04, Dep'tal Chief of the General Staff, Com. of 7th Div., then of 4th. Add. Osaka. — p. 166 ([scan leaf 215](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n215/mode/1up))

**Uyeda, Mannen** D. Litt., Philologist, Dean and Prof. Litt. Col., Imp. Tokyo Univ.; b. '67 in Aichi-ken; grad. Lit. Col. of the Univ. in '88; sent to France and Germany to prosecute further study; was once appointed acting Dir. of the Tokyo Foreign Language School; Councillor and Bureau Chief of the Edu. Dept. Add. Yanaka Shimizucho, Shitaya, Tokyo. — p. 166 ([scan leaf 215](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n215/mode/1up))

**Uyeda, Yutaku** Baron (cr. '07), General (ret. '12), ex-Commander of Korean Garrison; b. '50 in Tokushima; Captain '72, chief staff of the 5th Division in the Sino-Japanese War; Maj.-Gen. '97, and Dir. of Staff Col., Lieut.-Gen. '02; Chief of the Staff at the Military Inspection Board, full Gen. in '12; took part in '04-5 war as Com. of 5th Division; Com. of 7th Division '07; Com. of Guards Div. '09-11. Add. Yoyohata, near Tokyo. — p. 166 ([scan leaf 215](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n215/mode/1up))


## Who's Who in Japan

**Uyehara, Yusaku** Baron (cr. '97), General, Chief of General Staff Board, b. '56 in Hyuga; Sub-Lieut. (Eng.) '79; was ordered to study in France '89; staff officer to the 1st Army in the Sino-Japanese War which gained him 4th Class Golden Kite; attended the coronation ceremony of the Tsar and also The Hague Peace Conference; chief staff to Marshal Nozu in the Russo-Japanese War; Com. of 7th Division '08; Minister of War '12-13; Chief, Mil. Edu. Board, '14. Add. Daimachi, Akasaka, Tokyo. — p. 167 ([scan leaf 216](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n216/mode/1up))

**Uyemua, Chōzaburo** Mng.-Dir. Japan Beer Brewery Co., etc.; was b. '62 in Tokyo; was for some years an official of the Departments of Agr. and Com. and of Communications. Add. Aoyama Minamicho, Tokyo. — p. 167 ([scan leaf 216](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n216/mode/1up))

**Uyemura, Masahisa** a noted Christian preacher and Prof. at the Meiji Gaku-in, pastor of the Fujimicho Church, Tokyo; was b. '59 in Tokyo; is one of the prominent Christians brought up by the late Dr. Niijima. Add. Nakarokuban-cho, Tokyo. — p. 167 ([scan leaf 216](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n216/mode/1up))

**Uzawa, Sōmei** D.C.L. (Japan), barrister, M.P. (Seiyukai), Dir. of the Meiji Univ.; b. '72 in Chiba; where his precocious ability attracted wide attention; grad. Law, Imp. Univ., Tokyo, class '99, and has been practising since then, being reputed as an expert in criminal cases. Add. Tsukiji, Tokyo. — p. 167 ([scan leaf 216](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n216/mode/1up))

**Wada, Eisaku** painter of Western style, Prof. in the Tokyo Fine Art Acad., was b. '74 in Kagoshima-ken; grad. from the Academy in '96 and after having served as Assist.-Prof. at his alma mater for some years he was sent to France where he studied under Raphael Collin; is a Mem. of the Hanging Committee of Imperial Art Society. Add. Kasumi-cho, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 167 ([scan leaf 216](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n216/mode/1up))

**Wada, Hikojiro** Crown Mem. House of Peers (nom. '11), b. '59 in Hiroshima-ken; took active part in the democratic movement under Count Itagaki about '80; visited Europe and America, '89-90; member of the former Nationalist Association; appointed Dir., Agr. Bureau, Dept., Agr. and Com.; transferred to the Com. and Ind. Bureau; and then raised to be Vice-Minister; the Chief Commissioner of the Grand Exhibition '08; resigned in '11. Add. Hikawacho, Akasaka, Tokyo. — p. 167 ([scan leaf 216](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n216/mode/1up))

**Wada, Toyoji** Mng.-Dir. of the Fuji Spinning Co., eldest son of Kunroku Wada, was b. '61 in Oita-ken; proceeded to America as Agent of the Kai Shoten about '99 and returned home about five years later; entered the Mitsui Bussan Kaisha, and Kanegafuchi Spinning Co., which was started in '96; next the present Co. Add. Mukojima, Tokyo. — p. 167 ([scan leaf 216](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n216/mode/1up))

**Wada, Tsunashiro** Dr. S. (Japan), Mng. of Association of Mine-Owners of Japan; was b. in '58 at Wakasa, and first studied at the Kaiseiko, pioneer of the present Imp. Univ., Tokyo, where he became interested in geology of which he is a Nestor in Japan. He was next appointed assist.-Prof. at the University; transferred to the Department of Agriculture and Commerce in which he rose to Director of Mining Bureau and President of the Imperial Steel-Works when it was started in '95. Add. Yakōjimae, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 167 ([scan leaf 216](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n216/mode/1up))

**Wadagaki, Kenzo** Economist, D.C.L. (Japan). Professor at the Imperial Tokyo University, Principal of Japan Girls' Commercial School, etc.; was b. '60 in Hyogo-ken; grad. Lit. Col. of the Univ. in '80; further studied Political Economy at Cambridge, England in '81; and afterward in Germany; visited Europe '12-13. Add. Kobinata Dai-machi, Koishikawa, Tokyo. — p. 167 ([scan leaf 216](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n216/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Wakatsuki, Reijiro** ex-Minister of Finance, Crown Mem. of House of Peers, was b. '66 in Shimane-ken, grad. from Law, Tokyo Imp. Univ., '92; was appointed Councillor of the Treasury soon after graduation, where he rapidly rose and was appointed to be made a Bureau Dir. '03, and then to be the Financial Agent in London in '06; recalled home and appointed for the second time Vice-Minister of Finance '08 and resigned in '11; Minister of Finance Dec. '12—Feb. '13 and again April '14—Aug. '15. Add. Nakarokuban-cho, Tokyo. — p. 168 ([scan leaf 217](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n217/mode/1up))

**Wakao, Ikuzo** Pres. of Wakao Bank, Dir. of Yokohama Fire, Marine etc. Co., Y'hama Elec. Co., Toyo Muslin Co., Y'hama Warehouse Co., Nisshin Cotton Sp. Co., Tokyo Gas Co., etc.; b. '57 in Yamanashi-ken; early came to Y'hama with his father to open a shop. Since his est. of Y'hama Kyodo Transportation Co. with late S. Hiranuma, he took part in organization of over thirty business Co.s, and is now reputed as the 'star' of business circle in Y'hama. Add. 64/4 Honcho, Y'hama. — p. 168 ([scan leaf 217](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n217/mode/1up))

**Watanabe, Chiaki** ex-Minister of Imperial Household, was b. '43 at Suwa; was clerk at a local office '69; Secretary and then Governor at Kagoshima and others till about '00; Gov. of Shiga then Hokkaido '01; Vice-Min. of the Household '02; Gov. of Kyoto '04-'05; Treasurer of the Household '05; Vice-Min. of the Household '08 and Minister '10-14. Add. Takanawa, Tokyo. — p. 168 ([scan leaf 217](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n217/mode/1up))

**Watanabe, Chifuyu** Mng.-Dir. of the Franco-Japanese Bank (Tokyo Branch), ex-M.P., son of Chiaki Watanabe and adopted as heir by his uncle Viscount Kunitake Watanabe; b. '75; grad. from Law, Imperial University (Tokyo) '98; further educated in France, Germany and America; Manager of Industrial Bank of Japan for some years; then Mng. Director of Nippon Seikosho and Hokkaido Colliery and Steamship Co.; was for a while proprietor and editor of a Tokyo daily. Add. Nishi-machi, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 168 ([scan leaf 217](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n217/mode/1up))

**Watanabe, Chiharu** 2nd Count (succeeded his father Chiaki in '14), Director of the Bank of Japan; b. in '72 in Tokyo; grad. from Coll. Literature, Imp. Tokyo University '95, and succeeded in '14 the title of his father. He has been connected with the Bank since his graduation. Add. Takanawa Minami-cho, Tokyo. — p. 168 ([scan leaf 217](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n217/mode/1up))

**Watanabe, Kaichi** D. Eng., C.E. B.S., Pres. Ishikawajima Shipbuilding & Eng. Co., Dir. Keihan Elec. Rly.; b. in Shinano '53; grad. in '83 Gov't Eng. College; conferred C.E. and B.S. by Glasgow Univ. which he finished in '86, then studied practical side under Sir John Fowler and other noted British engineers; elected member of Civil Eng'ing Society, London. Add. Omote-cho, Akasaka, Tokyo. — p. 168 ([scan leaf 217](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n217/mode/1up))

**Watanabe, Kunitake** Viscount (cr. '95), ex-Minister of Finance; b. '46 in Nagano-ken, was long connected with the Treasury till he rose to be the Vice-Min. '89-92 and next to the principal post '92-94; was temporarily transferred to the chair of Communications, but soon restored to the original chair of Finance which he left '95; was Finance Min. in the late Prince Ito's party Ministry in '00, but the Ministerial rupture over the question of financial adjustment and the retrenchment insisted upon by the Viscount culminated in the collapse of the Cabinet. The Visc. is a great votary of the Buddhist doctrine of asceticism, and wrote a work on Indian philosophy. Add. Hommura-cho, Az., Tokyo. — p. 168 ([scan leaf 217](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n217/mode/1up))


## Who's Who

**Watanabe, Jiemon** Pres. of 27th Bank, Watanabe Warehouse Co., Asahi Life Ins. Co., and of Watanabe Hozen Kaisha, Dir. of Tokyo Bay S.S. Co. etc.; b. '70 in Tokyo; is brother of Jiemon Watanabe. (Pres. Akaji Bank and Sunto Elec. Co., Dir. of about ten other companies. Add. Nishikubo Shiroyama, Shiba, Tokyo.) Add. Honzaimokucho, Nih., Tokyo. — p. 169 ([scan leaf 218](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n218/mode/1up))

**Watanabe, Katsusaburo** Dir. of Local Adm. Bureau, Home Dept.; b. '72 in Okayama-ken; grad. Law Coll., Imp. Tokyo Univ. '96 and had been in service of local gov'ts, ultimately, Gov. of Tokushima-ken; present post, '07. Add. Minami Enokicho, Ush., Tokyo. — p. 169 ([scan leaf 218](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n218/mode/1up))

**Watanabe, Osamu** M.P. (Seiyukai), Dir. of Osaka Electric Lamp Co., etc.; was b. in '59 in Ehime; educated at the Keiogijiku; has held the post of Councillor in the Foreign Office; Mayor of Sasebo etc. Add. Hirakawa-cho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 169 ([scan leaf 218](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n218/mode/1up))

**Watanabe, Wataru** D. Eng., Dean of Eng. Col., Imp. Univ. Tokyo; b. '67 in Nagasaki; studied metallurgy in the Univ. (class '89); further studied at Freiburg, returning home in '75; had charge of Sado gold mine when it belonged to the Court, and then back to the alma mater. Add. Sendagi-Hayashi-cho, Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 169 ([scan leaf 218](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n218/mode/1up))

**Watase, Shozaburo** Ph. D., D. Sc. (Japan), Prof. of Zoology, College of Science, Imperial University of Tokyo; grad. Imperial College of Agriculture (Sapporo) '84; studied Zoology at Imp. Univ. Tokyo, '84-88; Johns Hopkins Univ. '85-90; Clark Univ. '90-92. Was Lecturer at the University of Chicago, '92-1900; travelled extensively in America, Europe and in Tropics. Add. 9 Minami-cho, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 169 ([scan leaf 218](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n218/mode/1up))

**Watase, Torajiro** elder brother of the above, was b. at Numazu '60; grad. Sapporo, Agr. Col. '84. Was connected with educational work at Tokyo and in the provinces; then started business as seed-grower and importer and exporter of agricultural instruments and requisites. Sat in the Municipal Council, in which he had charge of the Grand Exhibition Section. Add. Tame-ike, Akasaka, Tokyo. — p. 169 ([scan leaf 218](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n218/mode/1up))

**Yagyu, Kazuyoshi** was b. in 1864 at Nagoya; grad. from Law Imp. Tokyo Univ. '91; secretary to the Treasury '91-00; Vice-Pres. of the Formosan Bank '09-12 and Pres. of the same '12-15. Add. Wakamatsu-cho, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 169 ([scan leaf 218](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n218/mode/1up))

**Yajima, Eisuke** ex-Mem. House of Peers, Dir. of 10th Bank and Kofu Gas Co., Pres. of Yajima Silk Filature Co., etc. dealer in silk and cocoons; b. '69 in Yamanashi-ken. Add. Midoricho, Kofu. — p. 169 ([scan leaf 218](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n218/mode/1up))

**Yajima, Kajiko** Mrs., lady educationist, Pres. of W.C.T.U. of Japan, b. '34 in Kumamoto-ken; entered a Teachers' Training Institute at her 38th year; founded present W.C.T.U. in '86, Chrittenden Home in '95 and ever since has devoted herself the social reform of girls; had long been Dir. of Joshi Gakuin. Add. Tamcike, Tokyo. — p. 169 ([scan leaf 218](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n218/mode/1up))

**Yamaba, Torakusu** Pres. Japan Musical Instrument Co. (Hamamatsu), etc., was b. in '51 in Wakayama and was long engaged as a dealer in watches and medical instruments. His invention of the Yamaba organ was a turning point in his career. Add. Hamamatsu, Shizuoka-ken. — p. 169 ([scan leaf 218](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n218/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Yamada, Eitaro** Mng. of the Iwakura Railway School, Councillor of Waseda Univ., Nisshin Life Insurance Co., Ltd., etc.; was b. in '62 in Aichi-prefecture; graduated from the Aichi Normal School; and afterwards from the Tokyo Semmon Gakko (forerunner of the present Waseda Univ.); took to journalism; entered business in '92 and was connected with the Tokyo Electric Railway, the Narita Railway, and the Kinki Railway, the Nippon Railway Co., and Official Receiver of the Nippon when it was nationalized. Add. Shirokane Daimachi, Tokyo. — p. 170 ([scan leaf 219](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n219/mode/1up))

**Yamada, Hikohachi** Vice-Admiral (ret.), ex-Com.-in-Chief, Yokosuka Admiralty, was b. in Kagoshima '55; began his career in the Navy when young, and was promoted to the Com. of the 1st Squad. in '08 and Mem. Admirals' Council in '12-15. Was honoured with 3rd Class of Golden Kite. Add. Higashi-Gokencho, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 170 ([scan leaf 219](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n219/mode/1up))

**Yamada, Naoya** Dr. Eng., Director of Mitsui Mining Firm and Hokkaido Colliery and S.S. Co; was b. in '60 in Tokyo; graduated from Mining and Metallurgy at Imperial Tokyo Univ. '85; Mining Superviser of Gov., before he entered the Mitsui. Add. Nakarokuban-cho, Tokyo. — p. 170 ([scan leaf 219](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n219/mode/1up))

**Yamada, Saburo** D.C.L., Prof. in the Law Col. of the Imp. Tokyo Univ.; b. '70 in Nara-ken; first studied at the Tokyo Semmon Gakko (now Waseda Univ.) and after grad. from the Law Col., Imp. Tokyo Univ. in '96; studied International Private Law at the Univ. Hall and in Germany, England, etc. '97-01; Prof. at the Univ. on his return; is also Prof. in the Waseda Univ. and is editor of a law magazine. Add. Benten-cho, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 170 ([scan leaf 219](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n219/mode/1up))

**Yamagata, Aritomo** Marshal, Prince (Count '84, Marquis '95, Prince '07), Pres. of Privy Council, holder of Grand Cordon, O.M. (Br.), one of the elder statesmen; was b. '38 in Choshu, and took a distinguished part in the civil strife attending the Restoration; appointed Major-Gen. and a junior Vice-Minister of War by the Imp. Government; Lieut.-Gen. '72 and the Minister of War '73; was chief of Staff of the Army of Subjugation commanded by the late H.I.H. Prince Arisugawa in the civil war '77. After having sat frequently in the chair of Chief of the General Staff and of War, circumstances obliged him to undertake various civil posts, chiefly connected with Home Affairs and Justice Departments. Visited Europe and America '88-89 to study the local self-government system and the constitution of civil corporations; commander of the 1st Army in the Japan-China War, but soon compelled by ill-health to return home. Attended the coronation ceremony of Tsar Nicholas '96, and brought home Yamagata-Lobanow Convention in regard to Korea. Formed a Ministry '89-91, and had rather stormy time with the freedom-loving people's representatives, and also in his second Ministry '08-00. The Marshal's political career outwardly ended with the second Premiership, but he still continues to wield potential power in active politics, for the late Prince Katsura was believed as the Prince's successor in politics. On the outbreak of the Russo-Japanese War he succeeded Marshal Oyama as Chief of the General Staff, and was transferred to the Presidency of Privy Council on the late Prince Ito's departure for Korea as Resident-Gen. in '09. Add. Sekiguchi, Tokyo. — p. 170 ([scan leaf 219](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n219/mode/1up))

**Yamagata, Isaburo** Director-General of Political Affairs, Korean Government-General, Mem. House of Peers; b. '57 in Choshu, an adopted son and heir of Marshal Prince Yamagata, being a son of his sister. Studied in France; held various subordinate posts in the Central Government and as Governor in several provinces; Minister of Communications soon after the formation of the Saionji Ministry; resigned the chair early '03; appointed to present post '10. Add. Seoul, Korea. — p. 170 ([scan leaf 219](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n219/mode/1up))


## Who's Who

**Yamaguchi, Einosuke** D. Sc. (Japan), Director of the Imperial Archives and Mausolea; was b. '69 in Shimane-ken; grad. Physics at the Science Col. of the Tokyo Univ. in '84; Prof. in the First Higher Sch., and then in the Imp. Kyoto Univ. '97; President of the Peers' Sch. in '05; transferred to the present post in '07. Add. Iidamachi, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 171 ([scan leaf 220](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n220/mode/1up))

**Yamaguchi, Sogi** Auditor of the Bank of Japan, b. '51 in Shimane-ken, had long served in the Financial Dep't as Bureau Dir., and entered the Nippon Ginko about twelve years ago. He is elder brother of Mr. Einosuke Yamaguchi. Add. Haraikata-machi, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 171 ([scan leaf 220](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n220/mode/1up))

**Yamaji, Yakichi** a journalist, historian and essayist, "Aizan" by nom de plume; was b. '66 in Tokyo; has been on the staff of the Kokumin, to which he is a regular contributor; Chief Editor of the "Independent Reviews"; has many publications. Add. Nakashibuya, near Tokyo. — p. 171 ([scan leaf 220](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n220/mode/1up))

**Yamakawa, Gitaro** D. Eng., Prof. of Electric Eng. at Imp. Univ. Tokyo since '87; b. at Kawagoe in '60; graduated electricity from the Gov. Engineering Col. in '82; visited Europe for study '96-99, has written several works on his speciality. Add. Kagomachi, Koishikawa, Tokyo. — p. 171 ([scan leaf 220](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n220/mode/1up))

**Yamakawa, Kenjiro** Baron (cr. '15), D. Sc. (Japan), Emeritus Prof. and President of Imperial Tokyo Univ., Mem. House of Peers; b. '54 at Aizu; studied physics in Russia and America, was connected with the Tokyo Imp. Univ. after his return in '72; ultimately rising to the President. He resigned the post and was nom. Mem. House of Peers '04. Since '06 he had charge of the Tech. Col. founded near Wakamatsu by Mr. Yasukawa, millionaire coal-miner; Pres. of Imp. Kyushu Univ. on its inauguration in '11; Pres. of Imp. Tokyo Univ. in '13, combining ad interim the same of Kyoto in '14. Add. Ikebukuro, near Tokyo. — p. 171 ([scan leaf 220](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n220/mode/1up))

**Yamakawa, Yuki** Vice-Pres. of Yokohama Specie Bank; b. '55 in Ishikawa-ken; an official of the Treasury '76-78; entered the Specie Bank in '80 and has continued in its service, having had charge of the Kobe Branch and of London Branch at the time of Russo-Japanese War. Add. Nishitobe, Yokohama. — p. 171 ([scan leaf 220](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n220/mode/1up))

**Yamamoto, Gombei** Admiral (ret.), Count (cr. Baron '02, Count '07), ex-Premier, ex-Mem. of Supreme Mil. Council; b. '52 in Satsuma and fought in the War of the Restoration on the Imperial side; one of the pioneer graduates of the Naval Academy, class '77; was on board a naval training vessel that visited America and then made a voyage round the world on board a German warship; promoted Lieut. '80, and appointed Vice-Commander of the cruiser Asama; was one of the commissioners sent to England '85 to bring home the cruiser Naniwa built there; Commander of gunboat Amagi '86; was dispatched to Europe and America in '87 on suite of the then Vice-Minister of the Navy (Adm. Kabayama); Captain '90 and Commander of the Takao, and next that of the Takachiho. He was soon transferred to the Naval Office first as Personal Secretary to the Minister; Director of a Bureau and was gazetted Rear-Admiral '95; Vice-Minister '96, then Vice-Adm. and Minister from '98 to Jan. '06; full Admiral '04. Dispatched to England and U.S.A. on suite of Prince Fushimi '07 and was decorated with G.C.M.G. by King Edward; formed Cabinet '13-14 succeeding 3rd Katsura Ministry. The Naval Scandal involved his Cabinet in great difficulty and it fell in '14. Add. Takanawa, Tokyo. — p. 171 ([scan leaf 220](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n220/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Yamamoto, Jotaro** ex-Executive Dir. of the Mitsui Bussan, was b. in '67 in Fukui; entered the service of the Company in '82, and was attached to the Branch at Shanghai, where he soon rose to its chief and remained till '04, transferred to the head office in '12. Add. Shinsaka, Akasaka, Tokyo. — p. 172 ([scan leaf 221](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n221/mode/1up))

**Yamamoto, Tatsuo** Mem. House of Peers, ex-Gov. of Hypothec Bank of Japan; b. '56 in Oita-ken; connected with educational work from '80 to '82 when he entered the Mitsubishi Firm and was appointed a Director of the Yusen Kaisha, formed by amalgamation of the Mitsubishi with the rival company Kyodo; entered in '90 the Bank of Japan in which he was appointed Dir. of Banking Bureau, to be nominated Governor '98; relieved of the post '03; Gov. of Hypothec Bank of Japan '10; appointed to the Minister of Finance in '11, resigned in '12; again Minister of Agr. and Com. '13-14. Add. Kami-Niban-cho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 172 ([scan leaf 221](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n221/mode/1up))

**Yamamuro, Gunpei** Lt.-Col. and Chief Secy. of Salvation Army, b. '71 in Okayama-ken. Raised himself from a printer's apprentice and studied at Doshisha, to enter Salvation Army in '95; is known as the most influential preacher as well as an able social reformer. Has written a number of christian literatures. Add. Salvation Army, Ginza, Tokyo. — p. 172 ([scan leaf 221](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n221/mode/1up))

**Yamanaka, Nobuyoshi** Baron, Lieut.-Gen. (ret.), ex-Commander of 16th Army Division at Kyoto; was b. '52 at Yamaguchi; Sub-Lieutenant in '71, Captain in '73, Major in '80, Lieut.-Colonel in '98, Colonel in '94, Major-Gen. in '99, Lieut.-Gen. in '05; was awarded 2nd Class Golden Kite and created a Peer in '07 in consideration of his service in Russo-Japanese War; placed on ret. list in Dec. '12. Wakamiya-cho, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 172 ([scan leaf 221](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n221/mode/1up))

**Yamanaka, Rinnosuke** Dir. Tokyo Card-Board Co., Fuji Paper Mill, etc.; was b. '40 in Saitama; M.P. in the 1st Session, but has since kept aloof from politics. Add. Nakarokuban-cho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 172 ([scan leaf 221](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n221/mode/1up))

**Yamane, Takesuke** Baron, Lieut.-Gen. (ret.), holder of 2nd Golden Kite; b. '53 at Yamaguchi; Sub-Lieut. of Eng., '77; Capt., '84; Maj., '90; Col., '95; was appointed chief adjutant of 6th and 12th Divisions; Major-Gen., '00 and Com. Sasebo Fort; Lieut.-Gen. '06 and Com. Shimonoseki Fort; Baron, '07; Com. 6th Div., '08; transferred to Com. Guard Division, '12; relieved and put on reserve list, '15. Add. Sendagaya, near Tokyo. — p. 172 ([scan leaf 221](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n221/mode/1up))

**Yamanouchi, Ichiji** Crown Mem. House of Peers (nom. '14) and Lord-in-Waiting of the Kinkei Hall; b. in Kagoshima in '65; grad. Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ. in '88; Secretary and next Sectional Chief of the Police Bureau, Home Office, Governor of Aomori '02-4, Dir. of the R'ly Bureau '04-6, Vice-Pres. of the Imp. R'lys, and then Director of R'ly Adm. Dept., '09-12; Governor of Hokkaido '12; Chief Secy. to the Cabinet in Yamamoto Ministry. Add. Fujimicho, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 172 ([scan leaf 221](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n221/mode/1up))

**Yamanouchi, Masuji** Baron ('07), Vice-Admiral (retired), Mem. House of Peers; b. '60 in Hiroshima; grad. from the Naval Academy in '77; Midshipman in '79; Sub-Lieut. '84; dispatched to France and Germany '84 to study naval arsenal affairs; on commission to bring home the built in France '90; Naval Constructor-Commander '07; Rear-Adm. '02; Vice-Adm. '03; Commander of Kure Admiralty '05-09; Pres. of the Japan Steel Works '10-14. Add. Omori, near Tokyo. — p. 172 ([scan leaf 221](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n221/mode/1up))


## Who's Who in Japan

**Yamanouchi** Toyokage, Marquis, Capt. of Infantry (ret.) ; was b. in '75 of the former fief of Tosa ; graduated from the Military Cadet School in '00 ; to France for study '09 to return home ill in '10, married in '03 Princess Sachiko, eldest daughter of Prince Fushimi. Add. Yoyogi, near Tokyo. — p. 173 ([scan leaf 222](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n222/mode/1up))

**Yamao** Yozo, Viscount (cr. '87), was b. '37 in Yamaguchi, and was one of those young men who with late Prince Ito and Marquis Inouye proceeded to England on the eve of the Restoration for study ; returned home in '70 and appointed Secretary at the Public Works Department and finally rose to be its Minister in '80 ; subsequently appointed Vice-President of the former Senate ; Councillor at the Court ; President of the Legislative Bureau ; Lord Steward to the House of Princes Arisugawa and Kitashirakawa, etc. Resigned the official service in '93 and has since been leading retired life. Add. Toriizaka, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 173 ([scan leaf 222](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n222/mode/1up))

**Yamashita** Gentaro, Vice-Admiral, Com.-in-Chief of Sasebo Admiralty, b. in '63 in Yamagata-ken; graduated from the Naval Academy '83; 2nd Sub-Lieutenant '85; Commander '98; Captain '03; Rear-Admiral '08; Vice-Admiral '12. Was Member of the Naval General Staff, on the Staff of the Imperial Headquarters, Chief of Staff to the 1st Squadron and then to the Sasebo Naval Station, Chief of the 2nd Section of the Naval Materials Dep't. etc.; President of the Naval Academy '10-14. Deputy Chief of the Naval Staff Board and Mem. of the Adm. Council in '14-15. Add. Sasebo. — p. 173 ([scan leaf 222](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n222/mode/1up))

**Yamashita** Kamesaburo, Pres. of Yamashita Steamship Co. which has sprang into fame since the outbreak of the War, was b. 1866 in Iyo, and came up to Tokyo while under 20 to seek employment. In '98 he became a small coal-dealer; began shipping business in '03, next lumber and warehouse business. The war and unusual activity of shipping business has established his fortune. Add. Takanawa, Tokyo. — p. 173 ([scan leaf 222](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n222/mode/1up))

**Yamawaki** Gen, D.C.L., L. Mem. House of Peers and ex-Pres. of Adm. Litigation Court ; b. in Fukui '49 ; studied in Germany '70-72. He afterward filled various high civil posts and occupied the Presidency Adm. Liti. Court '06-13. Mrs. Fusako Yamawaki is a lady educationist and Dir. of Girls' High School. Add. Hinoki-cho, Akasaka, Tokyo. — p. 173 ([scan leaf 222](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n222/mode/1up))

**Yamawaki** Haruki, Governor of Yamanashi-ken since 1916 ; b. '71 in Kyoto adopted son and heir of the above ; grad. Law, Imp. Univ. in '99 and passed the higher civil service examination the same year ; official in the Dep't of Agr. and Com., first as Per. Sec. to the Minister, then as Secretary and Sectional Chief ; Commissioner of Anglo-Japanese Exhibition in '10 ; the Dir. of Monopoly Bureau, Formosa, Chief Commissioner to Panama Exhibition in '14-15. Add. Hirakawacho, Koj., Tokyo. — p. 173 ([scan leaf 222](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n222/mode/1up))

**Yamaya** Tajin, Vice-Admiral, Vice-Chief of Naval Staff Board ; b. in '66 in Tokyo ; graduated from the Naval Academy '86 and then the Naval College '97 ; Navigating-Lieutenant of the transport Saikyo Maru during the Japan-China War ; Captain '05 ; Chief of Staff to the 4th Squadron during the Russo-Japanese War ; Rear-Admiral '09 ; Chief of the Bureau of Personnel '09–13; Pres. of Naval College '13-14; Com. of 1st Fleet Aug., '14 and partook in the war, then transferred to Com. of 3rd Flotilla Feb., '15; present post Dec., '15. Add. Hommura, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 173 ([scan leaf 222](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n222/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Yamazaki, Shioroku** Treasurer, Imp. Household; b. in '68 at Saga; grad. from Imp. Tokyo Univ. (Law) '96, and at the same time passed the higher civil service exam.; Tax Inspector '97 attached to Nagasaki Taxation Office; transferred to Finance Office to be made its Sec. '00; Dir. of Finance Bur., then Debt Bur. Add. Yoyohuta, near Tokyo. — p. 174 ([scan leaf 223](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n223/mode/1up))

**Yanagisawa, Yasutoshi** Count, Mem. House of Peers, Mem. of Japanese Census Commission, Consulting Statist to the Cabinet, Mem. of International Statistical Society, Hon. Mem. of Royal Statistic Society (England), etc.; b. '70 in Echigo, son of Lord Mitsu-aki, scion of the House of Yanagisawa which hereditarily held the fief of Koriyama, Yamato; adopted '86 as son and heir of the main house, and succeeded to the title '93; grad. at the Peers' College with honour '94; sent by the Imperial Court to Germany for study and attended the Universities of Berlin, Vienna, Strassburg, Brussels and Paris, studying statistics; meanwhile appointed on the suite of H.H. Prince Kan-in who was sent to attend the Diamond Jubilee of Emperor Francis Joseph; returned home in '00; attended the International Statistical Conference six times as representative of Japan, from that at Christiania in '99 to the latest at the Hague '11. He held Presidency of the 1st Mutual Insurance Co. till '15, and is now its adviser. Add. Tamachi Hatchome, Shiba, Tokyo. — p. 174 ([scan leaf 223](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n223/mode/1up))

**Yanagiya, Usaburo** Paymaster-Lieut. (ret.), Vice-Gov. of Hypothec Bank of Japan; b. in '67 in Nagasaki-ken; graduated from the College of Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ., '90. Entered the Bank of Japan immediately after graduation; travelled Europe in '96; was appointed Chief of the Issue Dep't, Auditor to the New York Agency, Chief of the National Loan Bureau; resigned '13, and appointed Dir. of the Hypothec Bank; present post '13. Add. Harajuku, Tokyo. — p. 174 ([scan leaf 223](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n223/mode/1up))

**Yano, Fumto** Dir. of Kinjo Middle School, author, and retired diplomat; b. '50 in Oita-prefecture; graduated from the Keiogijuku, '70; entered the central Government as Secretary to the Treasury, '78; resigned with Count Okuma in '81 and took charge of the Hochi Shimbun, in company with the late Mokichi Fujita; took part in the formation of the Kaishinto (Progressive party); made tour in Europe and America, '86-88; Master of Ceremonies and Chief of the Bureau of Imperial Tombs, '90-96; Envoy Extraordinary and Minister Plenipotentiary to China, '97-98; placed on the retired list in '99. He has written "Keikoku Bidan" (story of ancient Greece), "Ukishiro Monogatari" (a marine novel); "News Society," etc. Add. Harajuku, Tokyo. — p. 174 ([scan leaf 223](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n223/mode/1up))

**Yato, Tsuneta** Pres. and Mng.-Dir. of the 1st Mutual Life Insur. Co., was b. '65 in Okayama; grad. the Third Higher School in '89; entered the Japan Life Insur. Co., and in '93 was made manager of the Kyosai Life Insur. Co.; went over to Belgium to attend the International Congress of Actuaries held in Sept. '95; and afterward entered a German Life Insur. Co. to receive its business training; was appointed an official in the Dep't of Agr. and Com., but soon resigned to start the present Co. Add. Omori, near Tokyo. — p. 174 ([scan leaf 223](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n223/mode/1up))

**Yashiro, Rokuro** Baron (cr. '16), Vice-Adm., Com. of 2nd Fleet; b. '60 in Aichi; Naval Ensign in '85, and repeatedly promoted was elevated to Captain in '01 and finally to the present rank. Long served as Staff-Officer of the Standing Squadron, then Attache to the Legation at Petrograd, and Vice-Com. of the Yashima, commanding the Asama in Russo-Japanese War; Dir. of Naval Staff College '12-13; Com. of Maizuru Admiralty '13-14; Minister of Navy '14-15, present post Dec. '15. Add. Navy Office. — p. 174 ([scan leaf 223](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n223/mode/1up))


## Who's Who in Japan

**Yasoshima, Chikanori** Manager of the Shibusawa's Office, Dir. of the Shinagawa Fire Brick Co., Shibusawa Warehouse Co. etc., was b. '73 in Uwajima, Iyo; grad. Tokyo Higher Commercial School in '94; appointed Per. Sec. to Baron Shibusawa. He travelled once in Europe and America with the Baron. Add. Shirokane-Daimachi, Shiba, Tokyo. — p. 176 ([scan leaf 224](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n224/mode/1up))

**Yasuda, Zenjiro** a millionaire who sprang from obscurity to the present affluence, greatest perhaps next to the Mitsui and the Mitsubishi; b. '38 in Toyama-ken; and was an apprentice at a shop in Tokyo and finally opened an exchange shop on his own account. This was enlarged in scope and developed into a bank. He has founded several other banks and has moreover lent his powerful banking in the starting of the Tokyo Fire Insurance Co., the Imp. Marine Insurance Co. and Kyosai Life Insurance Co. Being a public-spirited man he does not spare to furnish fund to any useful undertakings. The fund required in the Osaka Harbour Works was almost wholly supplied by him. He is a business man of extraordinary integrity and diligence; is Mem. of House of Peers. Add. Yokoami-cho, Honjo, Tokyo. — p. 176 ([scan leaf 224](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n224/mode/1up))

**Yasuhiro, Ban-ichiro** Member of Privy Council since 1916, was b. '59 in Fukuoka-ken. Grad. the Keiogijuku, the Chuô Shoin at Hongkong and finally from Cambridge Univ. in England; Prof. in the 3rd Higher Sch. in '89; Secretary to the Cabinet; Councillor to the Legislation Bureau; Director of General Education Bureau in '97; Chief Secretary to the Cabinet '98; Member of House of Peers from '00 to '16; Vice-Min. of the Dep't of Agr. and Com. in '01, resigned in '11. Add. Sadowaracho, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 176 ([scan leaf 224](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n224/mode/1up))

**Yasukawa, Kei-ichiro** highest tax-payer, mine-owner, Dir. of several Cos.; b. '49 in Chikuzen; studied at the Keiogijuku '72; has made a fortune in coal-mining; founded single-handed the Meiji Semmon College in 1908 by contributing 3,000,000 yen, and has been rewarded for it with 3rd class of the Sacred Treasure. Add. Wakamatsu, Fukuoka. — p. 176 ([scan leaf 224](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n224/mode/1up))

**Yoda, Hirotaro** Lieut.-Gen. (ret. '14), ex-Commander of the 11th Division; b. '53 at Kyoto; Sub-Lieut.; studied in France '86-90; Major '92 and instructor at the Cadet School; Col. '97 and Dir. of Toyama Military School till '03; Major-Gen. '03 and took part in Russo-Japanese War as Com. of the 4th Brigade, Infantry; Lieut.-Gen. '10. Add. Nishinada, Hyogo-ken. — p. 176 ([scan leaf 224](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n224/mode/1up))

**Yokoi, Jikei** Dr. Agr., Prof. at Agr. Coll., Imp. Tokyo Univ. and Pres. of Tokyo Agr. Univ. (private); b. 60 in Kumamoto; grad. from above Coll. '83; studied in Germany '99-02; Expert to the Agr. and Com. Dep't; Prof. in '94. Add. Aoyama Minamicho, Tokyo. — p. 176 ([scan leaf 224](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n224/mode/1up))

**Yokota, Hideo** D.C.L. (Japan), Judge of Supreme Court since '01; b. '62 in Nagano-ken; grad. from Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ., class '88; sat at Tokyo, Chiba and Hakodate District Courts, and Tokyo and Hakodate Appeal Courts before his transfer to the present post; teaches at the Waseda Univ., being an authority on claims; was sent abroad on official mission '09. Add. Nakano, near Tokyo. — p. 176 ([scan leaf 224](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n224/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Yokota, Kuniomi** Baron, (cr, '15), Pres. of the Supreme Court since '06 ; was b. '50 in Oita-ken ; entered the judical service '76, Public Procurator '80, the acting Dir. of Criminal Bureau and Sec. of the Dep't which post he resigned to study law in Europe '86-91 ; was appointed on return Pub. Proc. of the Tokyo Appeal Court, Councillor, then Vice-Min. of Justice '96, Proc.-Gen. '98, Chief Pub. Proc. of Tokyo Appeal Court '99. Add. Kagurazaka, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 176 ([scan leaf 225](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n225/mode/1up))

**Yokoyama, Matajiro** Geologist, D. Sc. (Japan), Prof. in the Imp. Tokyo Univ.; was b. '60 in Nagasaki; grad. Geology in the Imp. Tokyo Univ. and was subsequently appointed an official at the Dep't of Agr. and Com. in '82 ; sent to Germany to prosecute further study in '86 ; appointed Prof. at his alma mater on returning home. Add. Higashi-Goken-cho, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 176 ([scan leaf 225](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n225/mode/1up))

**Yokura, Toryu** veterinarian, principal and proprietor of the Azabu Veterinary School ; was b. '61 in Kagoshima ; studied veterinary science at the Agr. Col., Imp. Tokyo Univ., and further in America ; appointed Prof. in the Agr. Col. of the Imp. Tokyo Univ. '87, which he resigned some years ago to start his own veterinary school in Tokyo. Add. Sanko-cho, Shirokane, Tokyo. — p. 176 ([scan leaf 225](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n225/mode/1up))

**Yonei, Genjiro** Prop. of the Yonei Firm, Dir. of the Meiji Gum Co., the Kirin Beer Brewing Co., etc.; was b. '61 in Okayama-ken; grad. the Keiogijuku; then entered the business circle and established an import business at Ginza, the "Yonei Shoten." Add. Kaminibancho, Tokyo. — p. 176 ([scan leaf 225](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n225/mode/1up))

**Yoshida, Choket** Mng.-Dir. of Kobe Marine, Transport and Fire Ins. Co., and of Nisshin Fire and Marine Ins. Co., b. '66 in Ishikawa-ken; became a lawyer '87; elected Vice-Chairman of Osaka Lawyer's Association '99; gave up the profession and became Mng.-Dir. of Kobe Marine Ins. Co. '07; organized Nisshin Co. '11. Add. Kyomachi Hondori, Nishi, Osaka. — p. 176 ([scan leaf 225](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n225/mode/1up))

**Yoneyama, Umekichi** Mng.-Dir. of Mitsui Bank, was b. '68 at Numazu; studied in America '90-95, and entered the Nippon R'ly Co., removed to the Mitsui Bank which sent him abroad '00 to inspect banking business '02; Chief of the Yokohama Branch 1903 and next that at Osaka '08; Mng.-Dir. '10. Add. Aoyama Minami-cho, Tokyo. — p. 176 ([scan leaf 225](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n225/mode/1up))

**Yoshida, Heitaro** Maj.-Gen., Dir. of Remounting Dep't; was b. '68 in Oita; Sub-Lieut., cavalry '88; held posts of Prof. at Staff Coll., Chief Staff at 7th Div., Dir. of Riding School, Com. of 2nd Cavalry Brigade, etc. Add. Meguro, near Tokyo. — p. 176 ([scan leaf 225](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n225/mode/1up))

**Yoshida, Jun-ichi** Chief of Police Bureau, Household Dep't, was b. '45 in Ishikawa; held local posts at Kagoshima etc.; Dir. Finance Bureau, Imp. Household. Add. Shimonibancho, Tokyo. — p. 176 ([scan leaf 225](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n225/mode/1up))

**Yoshida, Kenryu** Pres. of 7th High School, b. '70 in Ishikawa; grad. from Philosophy, Imp. Tokyo Univ. '97; was Dir. of Middle Schools, Prof. at Waseda, etc. Add. Kagoshima. — p. 176 ([scan leaf 225](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n225/mode/1up))

**Yoshida, Sakuya** ex-Minister to Siam; b. '59 in Kumamoto-ken, attaché in '83, appointed to The Hague Legation in '88, Per. Sec. to Minister of Education in '93 and then Prof. in Third High School. 2nd Legation Sec. at Vienna in '98, removed to The Hague in '00, and transferred to Vienna in '01 till '04; Minister to Siam in '08, relieved in '14. Add. Foreign Office. — p. 176 ([scan leaf 225](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n225/mode/1up))


## Who's Who in Japan

**Yoshida, Togo** Dr. Lit. (Japan); historian and historiographer, Prof. at Waseda University; b. '64, Niigata; wrote on historical subjects to the Yomiuri and completed "The Dictionary of Japanese Geography" in '09 after 16 years work; "The Reverse History of Japan" (with pyramidal for basis modern time summit for ancient time) in '14. Add. Yarai, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 177 ([scan leaf 226](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n226/mode/1up))

**Yoshida, Yosaku** Per. Sec. to Foreign Minister since '96 and attaché at the Board of Ceremonies, Imp. Court, b. '50; clerk at Foreign Office in '77-86, attache in '87, élève Consul at Manila in '92 and then attaché in Berlin. Add. Foreign Office, Tokyo. — p. 177 ([scan leaf 226](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n226/mode/1up))

**Yoshizawa, Kenkichi** Secretary of Foreign Office, was b. '74 in Niigata-ken; grad. from Coll. Literature (English Lit.), Imp. Tokyo Univ. '99; passed the Dip. and Con. Service the same year; attache at Seoul '99; Eleve-Consul at Amoy, Shanghai, London, etc. '00-03; 1st Secy. at London '10; Consul-Gen. at Hankow. Add. Hiroo, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 177 ([scan leaf 226](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n226/mode/1up))

**Yoshii, Kozo** Commander (ret.), 2nd Count, Mem. House of Peers, Pres. Japan Lifeboat Asso., was b. '55; studied in England, and then grad. from Naval Cadet School in '80. Add. Sagacho, Fukagawa, Tokyo. — p. 177 ([scan leaf 226](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n226/mode/1up))

**Yoshii, Yukei** Dir. of the Bank of Japan, was b. '63 in Kagoshima. Early entered the Bank of Japan in which he has gradually distinguished himself to be given an import position of its Director. Add. Kita-igacho, Yotsuya, Tokyo. — p. 177 ([scan leaf 226](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n226/mode/1up))

**Yoshikawa, Akimasa** Count (cr. Vis. '96 and Count '07), ex-Vice-President of Privy Council '12-16; b. '41 at Tokushima; entered the civil service early in the Meiji era and after having held various posts in the Treasury, Home Office and elsewhere was appointed the Gov. of Tokyo-fu and then Vice-Min. of Home Affairs. Then began his Ministerial career, first as Minister of Edu., '90; Justice, '91-3; Home Affairs '93-96, Communications twice '98-00 and '01-3; Home Affairs '04, which last he had to resign in connection with the Sept.-5th-'05 anti-Peace Agitation in Tokyo. Add. Miyamura-cho, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 177 ([scan leaf 226](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n226/mode/1up))

**Yoshimatsu, Shigetaro** Adm., Com.-in-Chief of 1st Fleet; b. '59 in Kochi-ken; appointed Sub-Lieut. in '83; was at one time Staff Officer of the Kure Adm., Sec. Chief, Staff Office; Chief Staff of Sasebo Adm., Com. of the Naniwa, and Takasago, and some others, Rear-Adm. in '07, Vice-Adm. '09; Pres. of Naval Staff Col. in '10; Com. of 2nd Squad. in '11-12, Chief of Edu. Board '12-14; Com. of the Kure Adm. '14-17; Com.-in-Chief of 1st Eleet, '15 and full Adm. '16. Add. Navy Office. — p. 177 ([scan leaf 226](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n226/mode/1up))

**Yoshioka, Kyoho** Pres. of 5th High School; b. 75 in Yamaguchi; grad. from Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ. '99; was Prof. at 2nd High School, School Inspector, etc. Add. Kumamoto. — p. 177 ([scan leaf 226](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n226/mode/1up))

**Yu-asa, Shichiza-emon** millionaire dealer in iron and copper and importer and exporter in Kyoto, b. in Kanazawa '77, brother of Mr. S. Hayakawa, banker, and was adopted by the Yuasa of Kyoto; grad. from Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ. '91. Add. Takakura Higashi-iru, Gojodori, Kyoto. — p. 177 ([scan leaf 226](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n226/mode/1up))

**Yu-asa, Sōhei** mem. House of Peers (nom. '16), b. 74 in Fukushima; grad. from Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ. '98; held posts of Secretary at Shiga, Hyogo and other provinces; then Secretary and Dir. of Local Government Bureau, Home Office; Gov. of Okayama Gov. of Shizuoka in 1914; Dir. of Police Bur., Home Office '15-16. Add. Shizuoka. — p. 177 ([scan leaf 226](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n226/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Yu-asa, Takenosuke** general exporter and importer in Kobe, Aud. of Masuda Flour Mill Co. and Mem. of Kobe Chamber of Commerce; b. '69 in Wakayama-ken; was Mng. of the well-known Masuda Firm. Add. Yamamoto-dori Gochome, Kobe. — p. 178 ([scan leaf 227](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n227/mode/1up))

**Yugawa, Moto-omi** Vice-Min. of Communications since '14; was b. '65 in Shizuoka; grad. Law Coll., Imp. Univ. '93, and has been connected with the Dep't ever since, having been Secretary, Dir. of Maritime Bureau, etc. Add. Nakarokubanch, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 178 ([scan leaf 227](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n227/mode/1up))

**Yuhara, Gen-ichi** Dir. of Imp. Music Academy since '08; b. '63 in Saga; first studied Medicine at Imp. Tokyo Univ., but left it unfinished in '84; held posts of Prof. at various Gov. Schools, Dir. of Middle Schools, etc. Add. Ichigaya, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 178 ([scan leaf 227](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n227/mode/1up))

**Yuhi, Mitsuye** Lieut.-Gen., Com. of 15th Division; b. in Kochi-ken; Sub-Lt., '82; graduated from Military Staff College; studied in England, Vice-Chief of Gen. Staff of 2nd Army in Russo-Japanese War; Com. of 27th Brigade '09; Sec. Chief of General Staff till '14, when transferred to Dir. of Mil. Staff College; present post in Jan. '15. Add. Toyochashi. — p. 178 ([scan leaf 227](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n227/mode/1up))

**Zumoto, Motosada** Prop. and Edit. of the Herald of Asia, started by him in '16, an English weekly review of life and progress in the Orient; b. '62 in Hoki Province, and grad. Sapporo Agrl Col. '84; was long on the staff of the Japan Mail; entered civil service for a short while and was twice appointed Per. Sec. to the late Prince Ito when he was Premier; frequently visited Europe and America; was during the Russo-Japanese War a resident correspondent of the London Standard in Tokyo, accompanied the late Prince Ito to Seoul as non-official mem. of the Residency-Gen., and in January '06 he took over the Seoul Press formerly owned by an Englishman. Withdrew from it in '09, to start the Oriental Information Agency in New York City. Returned to Tokyo in December '10 to assume the control of the Japan Times, from which withdrew in '14. Made a tour to U.S.A. with Baron Shibusawa in '15. Add. Hommuracho, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 178 ([scan leaf 227](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n227/mode/1up))

**Zushi, Taniyoshi** Director of the Taiwan Sugar Co., was b. '54 at Kagoshima; studied at an American Col. '73-79; Sec. of the former Public Works Dep't '79; transferred to the Ely Bureau '83 and remained in the service as Dep't Dir. till '09 when he was placed on retired list. Add. Ochiai-mura, near Tokyo. — p. 178 ([scan leaf 227](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n227/mode/1up))


## Arts and Crafts

**Kano, Hogai** d. '88, of the Kano school and one of the master painters of the Meiji era. — p. 201 ([scan leaf 250](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n250/mode/1up))

**Kano, Yeichoku** d. '91, of the Kano school and Art Com. to Court. — p. 201 ([scan leaf 250](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n250/mode/1up))

**Kawabata, Gyokusho** d. 1912, master painter of Shijo school, Painter in Ordinary to the Court. — p. 201 ([scan leaf 250](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n250/mode/1up))

**Kawabe, Mitate** d. '05, Tosa school and high authority in antiquities. — p. 201 ([scan leaf 250](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n250/mode/1up))

**Kawamura, Oshin (Ukoku)** d. '06, ex-Judge and a master of Chinese Southern school. — p. 201 ([scan leaf 250](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n250/mode/1up))

**Kawanabe, Gyosai** d. '89, master painter who originated a new style of the Popular school. — p. 201 ([scan leaf 250](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n250/mode/1up))

**Kawasaki, Chitora** d. '93, Tosa school and high authority in antiquities. — p. 201 ([scan leaf 250](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n250/mode/1up))

**Kikuchi, Yosai** d. '78, originated the Yosai style chiefly depicting warriors and war scenes. — p. 201 ([scan leaf 250](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n250/mode/1up))

**Kishi, Chikudo** d. '95; Kyoto painter who was Art Com. to the Court. — p. 201 ([scan leaf 250](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n250/mode/1up))

**Kobayashi, Yeitaku** d. '90, noted artist of the Popular school. — p. 201 ([scan leaf 250](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n250/mode/1up))

**Kodama, Kwatei** d. 1913, a master painter of the Southern school. — p. 201 ([scan leaf 250](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n250/mode/1up))

**Kono, Bairei** d. '05, Kyoto painter who was Art Com. to the Court. — p. 201 ([scan leaf 250](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n250/mode/1up))

**Kubota, Beisen** d. '05, one of the most versatile painters of the periods pupil of Suzuki Shonen. — p. 201 ([scan leaf 250](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n250/mode/1up))

**Kumagaya, Naohiko** d. 1913, master landscapist of the Shijo school. — p. 201 ([scan leaf 250](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n250/mode/1up))

**Musugi, Seikin** d. '10, lady painter, pupil of Kumagaya Naohiko. — p. 201 ([scan leaf 250](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n250/mode/1up))

**Matsumoto, Fuko** d. 1911, historical painting, the Yosai's school. — p. 201 ([scan leaf 250](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n250/mode/1up))

**Miyagawa, Shuntei** d. '14. Ukiyoye painter. — p. 201 ([scan leaf 250](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n250/mode/1up))

**Mochizuki, Gyokusen** d. 1911, painter in ordinary to the Court; Tosa and Chinese schools. — p. 201 ([scan leaf 250](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n250/mode/1up))

**Mori, Kwansai** d. '94, one of the recent masters of Okyo's school painter in ordinary to the Court. — p. 201 ([scan leaf 250](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n250/mode/1up))

**Morikawa, Sobun** d. '02, Kyoto painter of Shijo school. — p. 201 ([scan leaf 250](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n250/mode/1up))

**Morizumi, Awangyo** d. '92, painter in ordinary to the Court and master of Sumiyoshi school. — p. 201 ([scan leaf 250](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n250/mode/1up))

**Murase, Gyokuden** d. 1910, Chinese Southern school. — p. 201 ([scan leaf 250](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n250/mode/1up))

**Nakajima, Raisho** d. '71, Okyo's school. — p. 201 ([scan leaf 250](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n250/mode/1up))

**Nakanishi, Koseki** d. '83, Prof. in Kyoto Art Academy. — p. 201 ([scan leaf 250](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n250/mode/1up))

**Noguchi, Shohin** d. 1917, master painter of Southern school, a lady. — p. 201 ([scan leaf 250](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n250/mode/1up))

**Noguchi, Yukoku** d. '98, Southern Chinese school and painter in ordinary to the Court. — p. 201 ([scan leaf 250](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n250/mode/1up))

**Nomura, Bunkyo** d. 1911, landscapist of the Shijo school. — p. 201 ([scan leaf 250](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n250/mode/1up))

**Ochiai, Yoshiiku** d. '04, Nishikiye, painter who improved style of newspaper illustrations. — p. 201 ([scan leaf 250](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n250/mode/1up))

**Okuhara, Seiko** d. '03, lady painter of the Southern school. — p. 201 ([scan leaf 250](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n250/mode/1up))

**Saigo, Kogetsu** d. '12, one of the best pupils of Gaho. — p. 201 ([scan leaf 250](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n250/mode/1up))

### Leading Dailies in Tokyo and Osaka

**Chugai Shogyo Shimpo** (est. Dec. '76)—economic and commercial paper. Man. - H. Nozaki. Office.—Kitajimacho, Nihombashi, Tokyo. — p. 312 ([scan leaf 361](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n361/mode/1up))

**Chu-o Shimbun** (est. '90)—organ of the Seiyu-kai, issues an evening edition. Pres.—S. Yoshiue. Ed.- U. Wakamiya. Office.—Yamashita-cho, Kyobashi, Tokyo. — p. 312 ([scan leaf 361](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n361/mode/1up))

## The Japan Year Book

**The Yokohama Specie Bank** —Founded in 1880 for furnishing financial facilities to the foreign trade of the country, capital ¥30,000,000 paid up. The Bank gets its foreign bills of exchange rediscounted by the Bank of Japan to an amount not exceeding yen 20,000,000 a year at the rate of 2 per cent. Board of Directors.—President Junnosuke Inouye. Vice-Pres. & General Manager: Yuki Yamakawa. Directors: Yuki Yamakawa, Masunosuke Odagiri, Chunosuke Kawashima, Nagatane Soma, Kokichi Sonoda, Rokuro Hara, Riemon Kimura, Baron K. Iwasaki, K. Tatsumi, J. Inouye. Head Office.—Minami Nakadori, Yokohama. Branches.—Tokyo, Osaka, Kobe, Nagasaki, London, Lyons, New York, San Francisco, Los Angeles, Honolulu, Sydney, Bombay, Calcutta, Hongkong, Shanghai, Tsingtau, Hankow Tientsin, Peking, Newchwang, Dairen, Port Arthur, Mukden, Tiehling, Changchun, Antung-Hsien, Harbin, Liaoyang, Tsinan. — p. 383 ([scan leaf 437](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n437/mode/1up))

**The Hypothec Bank of Japan** —Founded in 1896, the Bank furnishes long-period loans (within limit of 50 years) at a low rate of interest for improvement and development of agriculture and industry and to serve as a central banking organ for agricultural and industrial enterprises on a larger scale throughout the whole country; cap. ¥25,000,000 paid up. The Bank is authorized, when at least one-fourth of its nominal capital is paid up, to issue mortgage debentures to an amount not exceeding ten times its paid up capital. These debentures shall be redeemed at least twice a year by means of drawings and for each issue of debentures premiums of various amounts varying from five to one thousand yen are allotted to a certain number of debentures to be determined by drawings. Board of Directors.—Governor Gentaro Simla. Vice-Gov. Usaburo Yanagiya. Directors: Keiju Ario, Keishi Igarashi, Naonosuke Kawakami. Head Office.—Yamashita-cho, Hibiya, Park, Tokyo. — p. 383 ([scan leaf 437](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n437/mode/1up))

**Provincial Hypothec Banks (Noko Ginko)** —These banks exist one in each prefecture to act as local organ of credit for agriculture and industry, each with a capital of two hundred thousand yen or upwards. The lines of business transacted by these banks are similar to those dealt with by the Hypothec Bank, only in a smaller scope. — p. 383 ([scan leaf 437](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n437/mode/1up))

**The Colonial Bank of Hokkaido** —The Bank was established in 1900 for promoting colonization and exploitation of Hokkaido; capital ¥5,000,000 p.u. The Bank is authorized to issue mortgage debentures to an amount not exceeding five times its paid-up capital, though such debentures may not exceed the total amount of outstanding loans redeemable in annual instalment. Nominal capital was increased to ¥10,000,000 in '17. Board of Directors.—President and Managing-Director Riyo Mizukoshi. Directors: Yuichi Akahane, Wataru Majima, Muneki Seki. Head Office.—Sapporo, Hokkaido. — p. 383 ([scan leaf 437](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n437/mode/1up))
