## Learned & Social Institutions

### Section I. Learned Institutions

**Agricultural Association, Japan** Founded 1831; publishes a monthly organ Dai Nippon Noai-ho, and maintains Tokyo Agricultural University (private) in Tokyo as its educational institution. Members number 15,500, and are Honorary, Special, Ordinary and Associate. Patron Prince Fushimi; Pres. Marquis K. Matsudaira; Vice-Pres. Dr. T. Yokoi, the Pres. of the University. Add. Tameike, Akasaka, Tokyo. — p. 722 ([scan leaf 791](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n791/mode/1up))

**Asiatic Society of Japan** Pioneer association of the kind in Japan and was est. about 1880 by the late Sir Earnest Satow and others in Japan for studying and investigating Japanese customs, history, literature, etc. List of members 350 including residing abroad. Annual subscription ¥12; Meeting held quarterly and proceedings published occasionally and distributed to members gratis. Pres. Sir William Conyngham Greene; Hon. Secy. Ernest Wilson Clement. Add. Keio University, Tokyo. — p. 722 ([scan leaf 791](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n791/mode/1up))

**Bankers' Association, Tokyo** Founded 1900; funds ¥150,000 to be created. Subscription in proportion to the capital paid up of the associated banks, ranging from ¥200 to ¥1,000. The members number 69 banks. Pres. Baron E. Shibusawa; Directors, S. Hayakawa, T. Matsukawa; Chief Secy. T. Kuma. Add. Sakamotocho, Nihombashi, Tokyo. — p. 722 ([scan leaf 791](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n791/mode/1up))

**Barristers' Society, Japan** Founded June, 1896; issues a monthly periodical; members numbered 1,700 at the end of Feb., 1916. Dues, ordinary membership 20 sen per month. Directors Y. Ishiyama, S. Ikeda, K. Harada, T. Hanai, etc. Add. Nishi-Hibiya, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 722 ([scan leaf 791](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n791/mode/1up))

**Botanical Society, Japan** Founded 1882; publishes a monthly, Botanical Magazine. Dues ¥3.60 per annum. Members number 400. Pres. Dr. J. Matsumura; Chief Dir. Dr. K. Shibata. Add. Botanical Garden, Tokyo. — p. 722 ([scan leaf 791](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n791/mode/1up))

**Carnegie Endowment for International Peace, Division of Economics and History, Japanese Committee** Est. in July, 1912 in accordance with the International Conference of Economists held in 1911 in Berne. The results of investigation will be published at the head office in New York, U.S.A. President of the National Committee is Baron Y. Sakatani, and Secy. Y. Yamamoto, Office at Senshu Daigaku, Kanda, Tokyo. — p. 722 ([scan leaf 791](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n791/mode/1up))


## Learned and Social Institutions

**Chemical Industry Society, Tokyo** Founded 1878; dues for regular member ¥5 per annum, associate ¥3, these numbering 650 in all. Publishes a monthly Tokyo Kwagakukwaishi. Pres. U. Suzuki; Secy. Y. Shibata, K. Aso. Add. Science College, Imp. University, Tokyo. — p. 723 ([scan leaf 792](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n792/mode/1up))

**Electric Association** Founded 1912; subscription ¥4 to 500 a year. Pres. R. Kimura; Vice-Pres. Dr. I. Nakahara; Chief Secy. B. Kawanishi. Add. Sanchome, Yurakucho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 723 ([scan leaf 792](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n792/mode/1up))

**Emigration Society, Japan** Founded 1914; subscription ¥10 per annum. Members number 500. Pres. Marquis S. Okuma; Vice-Pres. J. Soyeda; Chief Secy. S. Hayakawa. Add. Sanchome, Yurakucho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 723 ([scan leaf 792](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n792/mode/1up))

**Fire Insurance Society, Japan** Created 1907; annual subscription according to the business result of the society. Chairman of the committee is K. Kagami; Chief Secy. K. Takai. Add. Minami-Nabecho Nichome, Kyobashi, Tokyo. — p. 723 ([scan leaf 792](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n792/mode/1up))

**Foreign Trade Association, Japan** Founded 1886; members 466 in all. Representatives, K. Ikeda, K. Otani, O. Suzuki; Secy. T. Ikeda. Add. 7 Sojurocho, Kyobashi, Tokyo. — p. 723 ([scan leaf 792](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n792/mode/1up))

**Geographical Society, Tokyo** Founded 1879; numbers of members 260; dues ¥100 in lump or ¥6 a year. Patron Prince Kan-in; Pres. Marquis N. Nabeshima; Vice-Pres. Visc. Y. Hanabusa, Baron D. Kikuchi; Secys. Dr. F. Omori, K. Inouye, N. Fukuchi. Add. Nishi Konyacho, Kyobashi, Tokyo. — p. 723 ([scan leaf 792](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n792/mode/1up))

**Historico-Geographical Society, Japan** Founded 1889. Dues ¥2 a year. Members number about 800; publishes a monthly organ Rekishi-Chiri. Pres. Marquis M. Hachisuka. Add. Aoyagicho, Koishikawa, Tokyo. — p. 723 ([scan leaf 792](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n792/mode/1up))

**Horticulture Society, Japan** Founded 1889; publishes a monthly magazine. Dues for ordinary members ¥2, special members ¥3, per annum; list of members some 3,000. Pres. Marquis S. Okuma; Vice-Pres. Viscount T. Fujinami. Add. 696 Nakashibuya, Tokyo. — p. 723 ([scan leaf 792](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n792/mode/1up))

**Imperial Aviation Society** Founded 1914; members 7,000 in March, 1916. Dues, regular member ¥100 in lump or annually ¥12 for ten years, common member ¥10 or 3 annually for five years. Pres. Marquis S. Okuma; Vice-Pres. Baron Y. Sakatani. Add. Yurakucho Itchome, Tokyo. — p. 723 ([scan leaf 792](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n792/mode/1up))

**Imperial Railway Association** Founded 1893; publishes an organ Teikoku Tetsudo Kyokwai Kwaiho; members 3,436 in all. Honorary members confined to those who have rendered distinguished services to railway business. Pres. Count M. Terauchi; Vice-Press. Dr. H. Furukawa, Dr. K. Nomura, etc. Chief Secy. R. Unno. Add. Marunouchi, Tokyo. — p. 723 ([scan leaf 792](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n792/mode/1up))

**Industrial Society, Japan** Founded 1896; publishes a monthly journal; subscription, ¥12 per annum. Pres. Viscount Kaneko; Vice-Pres S. Asano. Add. 1 Yamashirocho, Kyobashi, Tokyo. — p. 723 ([scan leaf 792](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n792/mode/1up))

**Jurisprudence Society** Founded 1894; annual dues ¥2; members some 300 at present. Pres. Dr. Baron Chincho Hozumi; Dirs. T. Minobe, G. Suehiro. Add. c/o Hozumi, Haraikata-machi, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 723 ([scan leaf 792](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n792/mode/1up))

**Life Assurance Society** Founded 1903; publishes a quarterly bulletin; subscription levied in proportion to the business result of the Cos. Pres. Taizo Abe. Add. Yurakucho, Kojimachi-ku, Tokyo. — p. 723 ([scan leaf 792](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n792/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Meteorological Society, Japan** —Founded 1882; publishes monthly magazine. Dues, ordinary member ¥2 per annum; members number 337. Pres. Dr. S. Nakamura; Secys. W. Ōishi, T. Okada, T. Yoshida. Add. Meteorological Observatory, Tokyo. — p. 724 ([scan leaf 793](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n793/mode/1up))

**Mining Industrial Society, Japan** —Founded 1885; publishes magazine as its organ. Dues, ordinary member ¥6 annually and ¥3 for admission; supporting member is asked to contribute ¥200 or upward in lump; members 1,200 in all, including 18 honorary members and 57 supporting members. Pres. Dr. W. Watanabe. Add. Kagacho, Kyobashi, Tokyo. — p. 724 ([scan leaf 793](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n793/mode/1up))

**National Aviation Society** —Founded 1915; publishes the Aviation as its organ; members number 2,578 at the end of Mar., 1916. Dues, ¥2 per annum. Pres. Lieut.-Gen. Gaishi Nagaoka; Vice-Pres. T. Kusama. Add. Yobancho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 724 ([scan leaf 793](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n793/mode/1up))

**Oriental Association (formerly called Taiwan Kyokai)** —Founded 1898, for developing resources in Formosa, Korea and Manchuria. Has established school in Tokyo, Seoul, etc. for bringing up students to devote themselves to colonisation work. Subscription ¥3 per annum for ordinary member; issue monthly magazine, Toyo Jiho; members number 500. Pres. Komatsubara, ex-Minister of Education; Vice-Pres. Baron Okura; Dirs. M. Kadono, S. Hayakawa. Add. Marunouchi Tokyo. — p. 724 ([scan leaf 793](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n793/mode/1up))

**Sericultural Society, Japan** —Founded 1892; publishes a Japanese monthly and a French Bulletin as its organ. Reliable information is supplied on application to foreigners abroad. Special member ¥10 in lump or ¥12 in course of 4 years, and ordinary ¥1.50; members number 165,477. Patron Prince Kanin; Pres. Viscount K. Kiyoura. Add. Nishikicho, Kanda, Tokyo. — p. 724 ([scan leaf 793](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n793/mode/1up))

**Society for the Protection of Industrial Property** —Founded 1905; dues ¥5-30 in lump or ¥2 a year. Pres. Viscount K. Kiyoura; Vice-Pres. K. Otani. Add. Yurakucho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 724 ([scan leaf 793](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n793/mode/1up))

**Statistical Society, Tokyo** —Founded 1878; publishes monthly magazine styled Tokei Shushi, and also issues the Imperial Japanese Statistic Annual and a number of statistics. Dues, ¥3 a year and have a membership of 500. Pres. Dr. Baron Yoshiro Sakatani. Add. Yamashita-cho, Kyobashi, Tokyo. — p. 724 ([scan leaf 793](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n793/mode/1up))

**University Graduate's Society** —Founded 1886; admits graduates of Imperial Universities of Tokyo, Kyoto, Kyushu and Tohoku, and also others who have connections with the above institutions. Dues ¥2 per annum, or ¥20 for life; publishes monthly bulletin; comprises 18 committees and 1 chief secretary. Add. Nishikicho, Kanda, Tokyo. — p. 724 ([scan leaf 793](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n793/mode/1up))

**Zoological Society, Tokyo** —Founded 1879; annual subscription ¥5 for class "A" member, ¥3.60 for "B" member; members number about 400; the Zoological Magazine is issued monthly, with "Annotationes Zoologicae Japonenses" as occasional publication. Pres. Prof. S. Watase (c/o Imp. Univ. Tokyo); Secy. Dr. N. Yatsu. — p. 724 ([scan leaf 793](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n793/mode/1up))

### Social Institutions

**America's Friends Society (Beiyu-Kyokai)** —Est. 1906 by Japanese who have stayed in America and by Americans staying in Japan, with the object of promoting friendly relation between the two countries. Num- ber of members about 300; annual dues ¥12. Pres. Viscount Kentaro Kaneko. Add. Gofukucho, Nihombashi, Tokyo. — p. 724 ([scan leaf 793](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n793/mode/1up))


## Learned and Social Institutions

**Belgo-Japanese Association** Founded 1889; members number about 74 comprising only those who have studied in Belgium. Secy., Mr. S. Sakurada, 53 Fujimicho, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 725 ([scan leaf 794](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n794/mode/1up))

**British Society** Formed 1908 by the late Count Hayashi, the late Sir Claude MacDonald, Count Hirosawa, the late Capt. Brinkley, Viscount Inaba, Vice-Admiral Baron Miyabara, Count Kawamura, etc.; dues ¥5 a year for ordinary members or ¥50 in one sum for life. The membership numbers about 250. Add British Embassy, Tokyo. — p. 725 ([scan leaf 794](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n794/mode/1up))

**Franco-Japanese Society** Founded 1886. List of members numbers over 300 including five grades of members; dues of ordinary member ¥3.00 a year. Patrons, Prince Nashimoto and French Ambassador; Chief Dir. K. Furuichi. D. Eng. Add. Hibiya Park, Tokyo. — p. 725 ([scan leaf 794](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n794/mode/1up))

**Indo-Japanese Association** Founded 1902; covers British India, French Indo-China, Dutch islands in the South Seas. Members 570 in all. Pres. Marquis S. Okuma; Vice-Press. Baron N. Kanda, Dr. F. Nanjo; Secy. Y. Soyejima. Add. Hibiya Bldg., Hibiya Park, Tokyo. — p. 725 ([scan leaf 794](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n794/mode/1up))

**Kojunsha** Founded 1879 by graduates of and others instructed in KO University and is a social club. Entrance fee ¥30; annual subscription ¥36. Membership over 1,000. Pres. E. Kamada; Chief Dirs. I. Kadono, H. Ishikawa. Add. Nabecho Nichome, Kyobashi, Tokyo. — p. 725 ([scan leaf 794](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n794/mode/1up))

**Latin American-Japanese Society** Created by Japanese interested in Central and South Americas, or peoples of those republics connected with this country; about 120 in number. Add. Hibiya Park, Tokyo. — p. 725 ([scan leaf 794](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n794/mode/1up))

**The Nippon Club** Est. 1888 by Japanese is a non-political institution. List of members about 500, including country members. Entrance fee ¥30 annual subscription ¥36. The present building was built in 1905. Pres. Count C. Okabe; Hon. Secy. Mr. S. Hayakawa. Add. Yurakucho, Kojimachi, Tokyo; Tel. No. 625 & 5439 Honkyoku. — p. 725 ([scan leaf 794](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n794/mode/1up))

**Russo-Japanese Society** Founded 1906; members number 350; dues, ¥6 a year for ordinary members or ¥200 for life, special members ¥500. Pres. Viscount M. Terauchi; Vice-Pres. Baron S. Goto; Hon. Pres. Russian Ambassador. Add. Uchisaiwaicho, Marunouchi, Tokyo. — p. 725 ([scan leaf 794](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n794/mode/1up))

**The Tokyo Club** Est. 1885 by Japanese and foreigners; entrance fee ¥50; annual subscription ¥35 for city members and ¥10 for country members. List of members about 300. The present building was built in 1908 at the cost of ¥300,000. Pres. H.H. Prince Kan-in; Vice-Pres., British Ambassador Greene, Baron Makino, Mr. Sonoda; Hon. Secy. Mr. A. Medley; Hon. Treas. Mr. M. Kushida. Add. Aoicho, Akasaka, Tokyo. Tel. No. 225—7 & 760 Shimbashi. — p. 725 ([scan leaf 794](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n794/mode/1up))

### Section I. Banks

**Chosen Ginko** (Bank of Chosen), Seoul. (Vid. Chap. Chosen). — p. 726 ([scan leaf 795](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n795/mode/1up))

**Dai Hyaku Ginko** (100th Bank), K.K., Yorozucho, Nihombashi, Tokyo. Est. 77. Pres. K. Ikeda, Mng. S. Asada. — p. 726 ([scan leaf 795](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n795/mode/1up))

**Dai Ichi Ginko** (1st Bank), K.K., Kabutocho, Nihombashi, Tokyo. The first private Bank in Japan, est. July '73. Pres. Baron E. Shibusawa, Gen.-Mng. Y. Sasaki. — p. 726 ([scan leaf 795](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n795/mode/1up))

**Dai Ni Ginko** (2nd Bank), K.K., Sanchome, Honcho, Yokohama. Est. '74. Pres. T. Hara, Mng. K. Yamagata. — p. 726 ([scan leaf 795](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n795/mode/1up))

**Dai San Ginko** (3rd Bank), K.K., Kobunecho, Nihombashi, Tokyo. Est. '78 by the Yasuda family. Pres. Zenshiro Yasuda. — p. 726 ([scan leaf 795](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n795/mode/1up))

**Hokkaido Takushoku Ginko** (Colonization Bank of Hokkaido), K.K., Sapporo, Hokkaido. — p. 726 ([scan leaf 795](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n795/mode/1up))

**Jugo Ginko** (15th Bank), K.K., Kobikicho, Kyobashi, Tokyo. Est. '77 by some Peers and formerly called the Peer's Bank. Pres. I. Matsukata, Vice-Pres. M. Naruse. — p. 726 ([scan leaf 795](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n795/mode/1up))

**Kawasaki Bank** Gs. K., Himono-cho, Nihombashi, Tokyo. Est. July 1893 by the late H. Kawasaki. Pres. H. Kawasaki. — p. 726 ([scan leaf 795](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n795/mode/1up))

**Kitahama Bank** K.K., Kitahama, Osaka. Est. March '97. Pres. T. Takakura. — p. 726 ([scan leaf 795](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n795/mode/1up))

**Konoike Bank** Gm. K., Imabashi, Osaka. Est. May, 1877; is conducted by the Konoike family. Pres. Baron Z. Konoike. — p. 726 ([scan leaf 795](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n795/mode/1up))

**Mitsui Bank** K.K., Surugacho, Nihombashi, Tokyo. Oldest, est. over 200 yrs. ago by Takatoshi Mitsui in the Tokugawa era; organized as Mitsui Bank, K.K. 1909. Pres. Baron Takayasu Mitsui; Mng.-Dirs. S. Hayakawa, S. Ikeda, U. Yoneyama. — p. 726 ([scan leaf 795](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n795/mode/1up))

**Mitsubishi Banking Department** Yayesucho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. Est. 1895 and is conducted by the Mitsubishi Firm. Chief, Dept. M. Kushida. — p. 726 ([scan leaf 795](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n795/mode/1up))

**Mogi Bank** Gm. K., Bentencho, Yokohama. Est. '98, by the Mogi family. Pres. S. Mogi. — p. 726 ([scan leaf 795](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n795/mode/1up))

**Morimura Bank** Gm. K., Nihombashi, Tokyo. Est. '93, by Baron I. Morimura, President. — p. 726 ([scan leaf 795](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n795/mode/1up))

**Murai Bank** Gm. K., Moto-Yokkaichicho, Nihombashi, Tokyo. Founded in Dec. 1906 with capital of ¥2,000 supplied by the Murai tobacco dealers. Pres. K. Murai. — p. 727 ([scan leaf 796](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n796/mode/1up))

**Naniwa Bank** K.K., Awajicho, Osaka. Founded Feb., 1873, formed with the union of the 5th and 32nd national banks. Afterward the bank was amalgamated with four other banks. Pres. M. Matsukata. — p. 727 ([scan leaf 796](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n796/mode/1up))

**Tokai Bank** K.K., Gofukucho, Nihombashi, Tokyo. Est. 1889. Pres. C. Kikuchi. — p. 727 ([scan leaf 796](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n796/mode/1up))

**Yasuda Bank** K.K., Kobunacho, Nihombashi, Tokyo. Est. '80 by the Yasuda family; reorganized K.K. in Aug. '11. Pres. Zennosuke Yasuda. — p. 727 ([scan leaf 796](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n796/mode/1up))

### Section IV. Dry Goods, Knitted Goods, Etc.

**Daimaru Dry Goods Store** Shijo-dori Takakura, Kyoto. Founded 1716, and reorganized as partnership ltd., is a pioneer department store in Kyoto-Osaka district. Pres. S. Shimomura. — p. 780 ([scan leaf 799](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n799/mode/1up))

**Funakoshi & Co** Minami-Kyuhojimachi, Osaka. Manufacturers of and wholesale dealers in handkerchiefs and other cotton goods; recently extended business operations to China and South Seas. Prop. Tatsujiro Funakoshi. — p. 780 ([scan leaf 799](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n799/mode/1up))

**Ishii Hosiery Factory** Temmabashi-suji, Osaka. Manufacturer and wholesale exporter of hosiery, cotton singlets, knitted underwear, etc. Prop. Katsujiro Ishii. — p. 780 ([scan leaf 799](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n799/mode/1up))

**Ito Dry Goods Store, K.K** Sakaecho, Nagoya. Organized as K.K. in 1910; have big branches in Tokyo and Kyoto. Pres. M. Ito; Mng.-Dir. K. Kito. — p. 780 ([scan leaf 799](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n799/mode/1up))

**Kamon & Co** Itchome, Kita-Kyuhojimachi, Higashiku, Osaka. Manufacturers and exporters of knitted goods. — p. 780 ([scan leaf 799](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n799/mode/1up))

**Kawashima Jimbei** Higashi Horikawa Ichijo, Kyoto, is upholsterer and embroider to the Court, and manufacturer of silk fabrics, cut-velvet, kimono, embroidery, being a leading house at Nishijin, Kyoto. Tokyo Branch (Uyedaya), 15 Yariya-cho, Kyobashi. — p. 780 ([scan leaf 799](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n799/mode/1up))

**Mitsukoshi Gofukuten, K.K** Surugacho, Nihombashi, Tokyo. A leading and the oldest dry goods store with a history of three hundred years and also pioneer department store, known as "Harrods in Tokyo," or "Marshal Field in Tokyo." Reorganized as K.K. with capital of ¥2,000 in which the Mitsui family holds larger share. Branches: Osaka, Kyoto, Seoul and Dairen. Awarded gold medals at foreign and domestic exhibitions. Pres. H. Nozaki; Mng.-Dirs. E. Nakamura, T. Asabuki. — p. 780 ([scan leaf 799](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n799/mode/1up))

**Nishimura Dry Goods Store** dealers in and manufacturers of high grade fabrics, embroideries, cut-velvet, kimono, etc., being one of the oldest in Kyoto. Saijo, Karasumaru, Kyoto; Tokyo branch house, Yamashitacho, Kyobashi, Tokyo. — p. 780 ([scan leaf 799](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n799/mode/1up))

**Osaka Knitting and Spinning Co., K.K** Nishinari-gun, Osaka. Est. 1912; cap. ¥[illegible] p.u.; manufacturers of cotton hosiery goods with Mitsui Bussan Kaisha as selling agents; supply 600,000 lbs. of cotton knittings per annum now largely shipped to Europe. Pres. S. Nango; Dirs. C. Kamo, S. Koizumi, M. Kita; Auditor K. Kure. — p. 780 ([scan leaf 799](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n799/mode/1up))

**Shibakawa & Co** Gm. K., Koraibashi, Osaka. Est. 1903, capital ¥500; general importers and exporters of dry goods; have branches in Tokyo, Kobe and London. Chiefly deal in handkerchiefs, woolen goods and general textile fabrics. Prop. Eisuke Shibakawa. — p. 780 ([scan leaf 799](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n799/mode/1up))


## Business Directory

**Shirokiya Dry Goods Store** Nihombashi, Tokyo. A leading dry goods and department store in Tokyo, founded 1662. — p. 731 ([scan leaf 800](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n800/mode/1up))

**K. Sowa & Co** Nichome, Ginza, Tokyo. Sole agents for Kyoto Weaving Co.; have branches at Yokohama, Shanghai and Kyoto. Prop. K. Sowa. — p. 731 ([scan leaf 800](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n800/mode/1up))

**Takashimaya (Iida & Co.), Gm. K** Kyoto and Tokyo, Karasumarudori, Takatsuji, Kyoto. Formerly private firm run by Mr. Shinshichi Iida; reorganized in 1909 as Gm. K., cap. ¥1,000. Branches at Yokohama, Kobe, Fukui, Lyon (Fr.), London (Eng.), Tientsin (China). Prop. S. Iida. — p. 731 ([scan leaf 800](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n800/mode/1up))

### Section V. Arts, Curios, Jewels, and Toys

**Ando Shippo-ten** enamel ware, Ginza, Tokyo. Est. '82 by J. Ando. Average annual output is reported about ¥30,000. Branch, Harbart, U.S.A. Awarded with Gold and Silver medals at Paris, St. Louis, Anglo-Japanese Fairs, etc. — p. 731 ([scan leaf 800](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n800/mode/1up))

**Hattori Watch and Clock Store** Manufacturer of and dealer in watches and clocks, jewels, surveying instruments, etc. Prop. Kintaro Hattori, Ginza, Tokyo. — p. 731 ([scan leaf 800](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n800/mode/1up))

**Ikeda Gm. K** curio merchant, Shimmonzen, Kyoto. Formed Mar. '96, succeeding former Ikeda firm; capital ¥300 with eight partners; branch at Ginza, Tokyo. — p. 731 ([scan leaf 800](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n800/mode/1up))

**Isetsu & Co** 19 Motoiwaicho, Kanda, Tokyo. Est. 1862; manufacturers and exporters of various kinds of toys, 'kakemono,' etc. Prop. Tatsugoro Hirose. — p. 731 ([scan leaf 800](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n800/mode/1up))

**Kitashimizu Katsuzo** Tomino-koji, Kyoto. Toys, especially dolls, largely exported; awarded high prizes at Paris and several domestic fairs. — p. 731 ([scan leaf 800](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n800/mode/1up))

**Kuramochi & Co** (Toyodaya Co.), 13 & 14, Bakurocho, Nihombashi, Tokyo. Est. 1851; wholesale manufacturers and exporters of Japanese toys. — p. 731 ([scan leaf 800](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n800/mode/1up))

**Mikimoto Shinju-ten** jewels and pearls, Ginza, Tokyo. Has piscina at Ago and Gokasho bays near Toba, and cultivates pearls by patented process invented by the proprietor of the firm. Awarded grand prizes at foreign and domestic exhibitions; has gold and silver smith depts. Prop., K. Mikimoto. — p. 731 ([scan leaf 800](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n800/mode/1up))

**Miyamoto Shoko** silver smith and dealer, Yazaemoncho, Kyobashi, Tokyo. Est. 1882; contractor to several Government Depts.; awarded gold and silver medals at foreign and domestic exhibitions. Prop., K. Miyamoto. — p. 731 ([scan leaf 800](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n800/mode/1up))

**Muramatsu Firm** jewellers and makers of platinum vessels used in laboratories, etc.; gold-medal at St. Louis World's Fair. Grand Prize at Paris World's Fair, etc., etc. Temma-cho, Kyobashi; Tokyo. — p. 731 ([scan leaf 800](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n800/mode/1up))

**Samurai Shokai** 20 Honcho, Yokohama. Est. 1900, carved wood furniture-maker and dealer, gold and silver work, also deals in ivory, bronze, gold lacquer, damascene wares, embroideries, etc. Pres. Y. Nomura. — p. 731 ([scan leaf 800](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n800/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Tensho-do** manufacturers of and dealers in silver and bronze art objects, jewels, etc. Gold medals and Grand Prizes at foreign and domestic exhibitions. Ginza, Tokyo. — p. 732 ([scan leaf 801](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n801/mode/1up))

**H. Yamada & Co** Itchome Yokoyamacho, Nihombashi, Tokyo. Est. 1893; is the manufacturer and dealer in all kinds of toys and dolls, and also exports a great deal abroad. Prop. H. Yamada. — p. 732 ([scan leaf 801](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n801/mode/1up))

**Yamanaka Co** partnership unltd., Awadaguchi, Kyoto. Est. 1905, a leading curio establishment in Japan. Branch shop at Boston. — p. 732 ([scan leaf 801](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n801/mode/1up))

### Section VI. Toilets, Chemicals and Drugs

**Azumi Isaburo** apothecary and druggist. Tomitacho, Kitaku, Osaka. Manufacturer of and wholesale dealer in insecticide, est'd 1894. — p. 732 ([scan leaf 801](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n801/mode/1up))

**Fukuhara Shiseido** exporter and importer of all toilet goods, drugs, patent-medicines, etc. Est. 1880. Ginza, Tokyo. — p. 732 ([scan leaf 801](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n801/mode/1up))

**Hoshi Seiyaku Kabushiki Kaisha** Minami-Demmacho, Kyobashi, Tokyo. Cap. ¥250 p.u.; manufactures patent medicines. Pres. H. Hoshi; Dirs. T. Arai, K. Ishimoto. — p. 732 ([scan leaf 801](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n801/mode/1up))

**Iscya Kichijiro** toilet articles, Yokoami, Higashi-Ryogoku, Tokyo. One of the oldest establishments dating from Tokugawa regency; speciality, tooth powders styled "Takarako," "Baikosan" and "Nippon-san." Export large amount to China, Korea, etc. — p. 732 ([scan leaf 801](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n801/mode/1up))

**Kidani Firm** druggist. Ogacho, Kyobashi, Tokyo. An old establishment; speciality, the "Jitsubo-san," medicine for women's disease. Proprietor, Kidani. — p. 732 ([scan leaf 801](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n801/mode/1up))

**Kobayashi, T** Sototemachi, Honjo, Tokyo. Manufacturer of and dealer in tooth powder ("Lion" brand) and toilet soaps; established by his father. His brands are exported to China, India, Australia, South Seas and Russian markets; awarded high medals at foreign and domestic fairs. — p. 732 ([scan leaf 801](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n801/mode/1up))

**Marumiya Shoten** Nihombashi, Tokyo. Manufacturer of and wholesale dealer in toilet-articles, drugs, nutriments, fancy-goods, etc. Keeps a well equipped laboratory. Prop. Zembei Miwa. — p. 732 ([scan leaf 801](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n801/mode/1up))

**Morishita, H** Kitakyutaromachi, Higashiku, Osaka; is the manufacturer of popular patent medicines "Jintan," etc., which are shipped largely to China and South Seas. — p. 732 ([scan leaf 801](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n801/mode/1up))

**Naikoku Seiyaku Kaisha (Japan Drug Co.), K.K** 10. Muromachi Sanchome, Nih., Tokyo. Est. Nov., 1915; cap. ¥1,000 (¥500 p.u.), Government guaranteeing profit of 8%. The Co. produce acetylic salicylic acid, antifebrin, phenacetin etc. Works at Mitake, Minamishinagawa, Tokyo. Pres. Y. Fukuhara; Mng. Dirs. M. Shiobara, Dr. K. Ikeguchi. — p. 732 ([scan leaf 801](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n801/mode/1up))

**Sankyo & Co., K.K** druggists and chemists, Muromachi, Nihombashi, Tokyo. Started at Yokohama in 1899 by Dr. Jokichi Takamine, renowned chemist and originator of "Taka Diastase" and Adrenalin and Mr. Shiohara, now Mng.-Dir. of the Co.; sole agent for Parke, Davis & Co., U.S.A.; removed to Tokyo in '01; incorporated Shiohara's firm with capital ¥500 in '07; K.K. '13 with capital ¥1,212 p.u. Pres. Dr. J. Takamine; Mng.-Dir. Y. Shiobara. — p. 732 ([scan leaf 801](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n801/mode/1up))


## Business Directory

**Toyama Baiyaku Kabushiki Kaisha (Patent Medicine Co.)** Hoshinoimachi, Toyama; the district known from ancient time for patent medicines of all kinds. — p. 733 ([scan leaf 802](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n802/mode/1up))

**Tsumura Juntendo** Tori Sanchome, Tokyo. Manufacturer of "Chujoto," patent medicine for women's disease, and has wide connections abroad. — p. 733 ([scan leaf 802](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n802/mode/1up))

Sonoda, S. Kuno, E. Nakamura, etc. — p. 733 ([scan leaf 802](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n802/mode/1up))

**Osaka Gas Works, K.K** Nakanoshima, Osaka. Est. Oct. 1896. Directors: Pres. C. Kataoka; Dirs. C. P. Cushman, S. Kishi, C. Watanabe. — p. 734 ([scan leaf 803](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n803/mode/1up))

(coal) and 52,000 (hydro), besides supplied 45,000 h.p. by Inawashiro Co. and 100,000 h.p. by Kinugawa Co. Directors: Pres. I. Wakao; Mng.-Dirs. K. Kambe, I. Nakahara, T. Koshiyama. — p. 734 ([scan leaf 803](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n803/mode/1up))

**Tokyo Gas Works, K.K** Nishikicho, Kanda, Tokyo. Est. '85; the only gas Co. in Tokyo City having amalgamated newly opened rivals; total supply 1,624,592,400 c. ft. Directors: Pres. R. Kume; Dirs. S. Ohashi, F. Watanabe, K. Koda, etc. — p. 734 ([scan leaf 803](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n803/mode/1up))

**Ujigawa Electric Co., K.K** Shichijo, Kyoto. Est. 1906; capacity 30,000 k.w.; supply power to Osaka Elec. Light Co. (25,900 k.w.), Kyoto Elec. Light Co. Hayashi. — p. 734 ([scan leaf 803](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n803/mode/1up))

**Tone Electric-Power House, K.K** Maebashi, Gumma-ken. Est. '07; capacity 13,000 k.w., supplying light and running trams (9 m. 16 ch.), also undertake gas (50,000 c. ft.). — p. 734 ([scan leaf 803](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n803/mode/1up))

### Section VIII. Mining, Refining and Metallurgy

**Daito Mining Co., K.K** Yurakucho, Tokyo. Est. 1912; cap. ¥1,700 p.u.; reserves ¥32; div. 16%. Pres. K. Koyeta; Mng.-Dir. S. Sukinoye. — p. 734 ([scan leaf 803](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n803/mode/1up))

**Fujita Co., unltd** Dojima, Osaka. Est. 1902; work Kosaka copper mine, etc.; produce and export electrolytic copper and zinc, ferro alloy; also carries on farming, forest-planting, etc. Cap. ¥3,000 subscribed by the three members of the Fujita family. Pres. Baron H. Fujita. — p. 734 ([scan leaf 803](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n803/mode/1up))

**Furukawa Mining Co., Gm. K** Yayesucho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. Late Ichibei Furukawa's copper works reorganized as such in '05 with capital ¥5,000; own Ashio and six other copper mines besides Innai silver mine; manufacture copper wire, bar and cakes. Pres. Baron T. Furukawa; Mng.-Dir. R. Kondo; Dirs. B. Konda, Inouye, K. Odagawa. — p. 734 ([scan leaf 803](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n803/mode/1up))

**Hoden Petroleum Co., K.K** Nagaoka, Niigata-ken. Est. 1893; one of the leading petroleum Cos.; capital ¥15,250 p.u.; reserves ¥1,665; div. 14%. Pres. K. Hashimoto; Mng.-Dir. T. Watanabe. — p. 734 ([scan leaf 803](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n803/mode/1up))

**Hokkaido Tanko Kisen Kabushiki Kaisha** (Hokkaido Colliery and S.S. Co.), Honkawayacho, Nihombashi, Tokyo. Est. 1889 and were first engaged in coal mining and railway but with the railway nationalization in Oct. '06, expanded the colliery work, shipping service, also undertake the manufacture of cokes and forestry business. In conjunction with Messrs. Armstrong and Vickers, the Co. opened a steel foundry styled the Nippon Seikosho. Also work their own Wanishi Iron Foundry. Capital ¥23,400 p.u.; reserves ¥1,953; debts ¥6,000; div. 2% (premium shares 7.5%). Chairman T. Dan; Mng.-Dirs. T. Uno, T. Isomura. — p. 734 ([scan leaf 803](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n803/mode/1up))

Ito, E. Yamagiwa. — p. 739 ([scan leaf 804](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n804/mode/1up))

**Ishikari Colliery Co., K.K** Uchisaiwaicho I, Koj., Tokyo. Est. 1906; capital ¥3,000 p.u.; reserves ¥342; div. 5%. Pres. S. Asano; Dirs. B. Nakano, G. Mudaguchi, H. Shiota. — p. 739 ([scan leaf 804](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n804/mode/1up))

**Iwaki Colliery Co., K.K** Minato-gashi, Kyobashi, Tokyo. Est. '84; capital ¥1,200 p.u., reserves ¥303; div. 20%. Directors: Chairman S. Asano; Mng.-Dir. K. Kurata. — p. 739 ([scan leaf 804](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n804/mode/1up))

**Kaijima Mining Co., K.K** Nokata, Fukuoka-ken. Est. 1899 by Kaijima family; transformed as K.K. in Dec., '09; cap. ¥2,500 p.u.; own five collieries in Kyushu turning out annually over one million tons of coal. Pres. T. Kaijima; Mng.-Dirs. E. Kaijima, H. Nakane. — p. 739 ([scan leaf 804](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n804/mode/1up))

**Kuhara Mining Co., K.K** Nakanoshima, Osaka. Est. 1912 and reorganized 1915 as Co. ltd. increasing cap. from ¥10,000 to ¥30,000, (20,000 p.u.); mining, chemical-industry and machine-making relating to mining, etc.; possess Hitachi mines and 339 other metallic leases, 92 coal and 21 miscellaneous leases, altogether covering 220,800 acres and annual prod. copper 25,760,000 lbs.; gold 60,000 oz., silver 655,000 oz.; reserves ¥3,264; profit 2nd-half 1915 ¥3,248,350; dividend 28%. Pres. F. Kuhara; Dirs. I. Saito, G. Ayukawa. — p. 739 ([scan leaf 804](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n804/mode/1up))

**Meiji Mining Co., Ltd. K.K** Eita, Kahogun, Fukuoka. Est. 1908; cap. ¥5,000 p.u.; reserve ¥130 div. 7-8%. Directors: Pres. K. Yasukawa, Vice-Pres. K. Matsumoto; Mng. Z. Shiratsuchi. — p. 739 ([scan leaf 804](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n804/mode/1up))

**Mitsui Mining Co., K.K** Surugacho, Nihombashi, Tokyo. Est. 1911; capital 5,000 p.u.; largest coal miners in Japan producing about 4,000,000 tons a year. See Mitsui Firm, under Sec. General. Pres. M. Mitsui; Mng.-Dir. G. Mitsui; Mg.-Dir. N. Yamada. — p. 739 ([scan leaf 804](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n804/mode/1up))

**Nippon Petroleum Co., K.K** Mitsubishi Buildings, Marunouchi, Tokyo. Est. 1838; own Kurokawa wells (see Chap. Mining) etc.; capital ¥12,500 p.u.; reserves ¥1,957; div. 20%. Pres. H. Naito; Dirs. T. Yamaguchi, S. Honma, H. Kusumi, O. Matsukata. — p. 739 ([scan leaf 804](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n804/mode/1up))

**Osaka Zinc Co., K.K** Dojima, Osaka; refineries and makers of zinc wares, granulated zinc, zinc for coating and galvanizing, etc. Est. '12; cap. ¥810 p.u., dividend over 180% in 1st half '16. Mng.-Dir. M. Shiomi. — p. 739 ([scan leaf 804](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n804/mode/1up))

**Sumitomo Firm** Osaka, one of the oldest millionaire houses in Japan, combining banking, mining, refining, electric wire dep'ts, etc., owns Besshi Copper Mine, etc., and runs copper works, electric wire and cable works, both at Osaka, Fertilizer Factory at Niihama, Iyo; manufactures steel ingots, steel forgings, etc. for railways, shipbuilding yards, etc. Pres. Baron Sumitomo; Mng.-Dir. K. Nakada. — p. 739 ([scan leaf 804](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n804/mode/1up))

### Section IX. Brewery

**Dai Nippon Brewery Co., K.K** Meguro, Tokyo. Formed March, 1906 by amalgamating Ebisu, Sapporo and Asahi breweries. Brands; "Ebisu," "Sapporo," "Asahi," and "Munchenner" beers. Pres. K. Makoshi; Mng.-Dir. C. Uemura. — p. 739 ([scan leaf 804](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n804/mode/1up))

Yonei; Dirs. R. Kondo, S. Uryu, T. Tanaka, S. Ida. — p. 736 ([scan leaf 805](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n805/mode/1up))

**Meidi-Ya, K.K., Yokohama** Sole agent for the above. Is also a leading grocer and provision establishment. (Vid. Sec. XXIV.) — p. 736 ([scan leaf 805](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n805/mode/1up))

**Nishinomiya Sake Brewery Co., K.K., Nishinomiya, Hyogo** Est. Apr. 1889; annual products 24,000 koku; is the largest sake brewery. — p. 736 ([scan leaf 805](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n805/mode/1up))

**G. Tanaka & Co., Choshi, Chibaken** Est. 1616. one of the oldest brewery of Japanese soy. "Higeta Shoyu;" have been honored with several medals at exhibitions home and abroad. Prop. G. Tanaka. — p. 736 ([scan leaf 805](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n805/mode/1up))

**Teikoku Beer Brewery Co., K.K., Dairi, Fukuoka-ken** Est. May, 1912. Have capacity to produce 65,000 koku a year. Pres. R. Oka; Mng.-Dir. I. Sumida. — p. 736 ([scan leaf 805](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n805/mode/1up))

**Teikoku Mineral Water Co., K.K., Hirano, near Kobe; and Sakamoto, Nihombashi, Tokyo** Est. Jan. 1907; capital ¥480 p.u. Awarded gold medal at St. Louis Exposition in 1904. Chairman of Directors K. Hamaguchi; Mng.-Dir. S. Nakaya; Dirs. J. Yamana, T. Kuwawara. — p. 736 ([scan leaf 805](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n805/mode/1up))

**Tokai Pulp Mnf. Co., K.K** Yariyacho, Kyobashiku, Tokyo. Est. 1907; div. 8%. Pres. K. Okura; Dirs. Y. Haraguchi, H. Okura. — p. 738 ([scan leaf 807](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n807/mode/1up))

**Tokyo Paper-Board Co., K.K** Minami-Senju, Tokyo. Est. '88; capital ¥1,000 p.u.; reserve ¥387; div. 9%. Products in 2nd half, 1915 printing paper 5,636,000 lbs. and paper-board 3,600 tons. Mng.-Dir. S. Asano; Dirs. R. Yamanaka, T. Miyama, R. Suzuki. — p. 738 ([scan leaf 807](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n807/mode/1up))

**Toyo Paper-Mill Co., K.K** Toyosaki-machi, Osaka-fu. Est. 1906; manufacture rice paper for cigarette. Pres. S. Inouye; Mng.-Dir. T. Nakamura. — p. 738 ([scan leaf 807](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n807/mode/1up))

**Yokkaichi Paper-Mill Co., K.K** Yokkaichi, Miye-ken. Est '87. Product of paper in 2nd half, 1915, 8 million kin. Directors: N. Shigemori, H. Okawa. H. Itakura, I. Kumazawa. — p. 738 ([scan leaf 807](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n807/mode/1up))

### Glass and Ceramics

**Asahi Glass Mnf. Co., K.K** Amagasaki, Hyogo prefecture. Est. 1909; cap. ¥500 p.u.; manufacturers of plate glass; run by the Iwasaki family; branch-shop established at Kawasaki, near Tokyo. Mng.-Dir. S. S5. — p. 738 ([scan leaf 807](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n807/mode/1up))

**Onoda Cement Mnf. Co., K.K** Onoda Asa-gun, Yamaguchi prefecture. Est. 1881; cap. ¥1,600; res. ¥977; loans ¥496; div. 10%. Mng.-Dir. E. Fukuhara. Dirs. S. Kasai, S. Iida, T. Fukuhara. — p. 738 ([scan leaf 807](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n807/mode/1up))

**Osaka Ceramic Co., K.K** Dojima, Kitaku, Osaka. Est. 1882; cap. ¥1,150 p.u.; res. ¥240; loans ¥190; div. 10%; annual produce sundry varieties of bricks including ordinary bricks at the factories at Sakai, Kishiwada, Kaizuka, etc. Pres. K. Isono; Dir. K. Hirooka. — p. 738 ([scan leaf 807](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n807/mode/1up))

**Sakura Portland Cement Co., K.K** Funadaikumachi, Kitaku, Osaka. Est. 1907; cap. ¥504 p.u.; div. 5%. Pres. I. Hiraga; Mng.-Dir. M. Sakamoto. — p. 738 ([scan leaf 807](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n807/mode/1up))

**Shimada Glass Works** Shin-Yodogawa, Osaka. Est. 1903; cap. ¥300; glass wares, as lamp shields, cups, dishes, and flower vases, etc.; largely exported to China, India and South Seas. Prop. M. Shimada. — p. 738 ([scan leaf 807](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n807/mode/1up))

**Shinagawa White Brick Mnf. Co., K.K** Kita-Shinagawa, Tokyo-fu. Est. 1875 and organized as K.K. in 1903; cap. ¥1,250 p.u.; res. ¥197; div. 16%; yearly output estimated at 30,000,000 fire-proof, and 15,000,000 decorative; have a factory at Osaka and 3 at Fukuoka prefecture. Mng.-Dir. M. Yamanouchi. — p. 738 ([scan leaf 807](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n807/mode/1up))

**Shofu Porcelain Co., Cs. K.K** Honcho-dori, Kyoto. Est. about 1861 and organized as such in 1906; manufacturers and wholesale exporters of Japanese crockery wares; have a large factory in Nagoya to produce export wares, and sale office at Kyobashiku, Tokyo; largely export to China, India, South Seas and Australia. Prop. Kajo Shofu. — p. 738 ([scan leaf 807](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n807/mode/1up))

Tazaki; Dirs. N. Hasegawa, J. Mogi, S. Asada. — p. 789 ([scan leaf 808](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n808/mode/1up))

Chairman, C. Sekine. — p. 712 ([scan leaf 811](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n811/mode/1up))

Directors: Chairman O. Watanabe; Mng.-Dirs. S. Yamazaki, S. Kikushima. — p. 712 ([scan leaf 811](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n811/mode/1up))

### Section XVII. Printing and Publishing Houses

**Hakubun-Kwan** , publishing house, Honcho, Nihombashi, Tokyo, founded 1887 by the late Sahei Ohashi, father of the present proprietor, Shintaro Ohashi. Has contributed not a little to the progress of learning and intellectual activity by minimizing the cost of publication and supplying the publications at lowest possible price. He issues 15 kinds of magazine and 6,000 varieties of books, over 30 varieties of serial works, 20 of them still in course of publication. — p. 712 ([scan leaf 811](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n811/mode/1up))

**Kaisei-Kwan** , Kobinata-Suidocho, Koishikawa Tokyo. A leading publisher of text-books for schools: publishes Westernized Japanese song books. Prop. T. Nishino. — p. 712 ([scan leaf 811](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n811/mode/1up))

**Kyobun Kwan** (Methodist Publishing House), Ginza, Tokyo. Leading book-sellers and stationers in Tokyo, also undertake printing. Agents, Ginn & Co., Boston. E. J. Igrehart (Gen. Mng.) — p. 712 ([scan leaf 811](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n811/mode/1up))

**Maruzen Kabushiki Kaisha** , Publishers, book-sellers and stationers, Tori-Sanchome, Nihombashi, Tokyo. Est. 1880. capital 350 p.u.; connections world wide. Director K. Oyaidzu. Branches: Osaka, Kyoto, Sendai, and Fukuoka. — p. 712 ([scan leaf 811](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n811/mode/1up))

**Shimbi Shoin, K.K** , publishers of fine art work, Sakana-cho, Kyobashi, Tokyo. Reorganized in 1906 as K.K., succeeding Mr. Tajima's private establishment. Awarded gold medals at home and foreign exhibitions. S. Tajima (Mng.-Dir.) See Chap. on Arts and Crafts. — p. 712 ([scan leaf 811](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n811/mode/1up))

**Tokyo Printing Co., K.K** , Printer, Kabutocho, Nihombashi, Tokyo. One of the leading printing shops in Tokyo. Reorganized as K.K., Mar. 1896; capital ¥237 p.u.; reserves ¥144; div. 12%; contractors to several Government Departments. Awarded gold medals at home exhibitions. Mng.-Dir. S. Hoshino. — p. 712 ([scan leaf 811](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n811/mode/1up))

**Tokyo Tsukiji Kappan Seizojo, K.K** , Tsukiji, Tokyo. Pioneer printing and type foundery, est. 1873; capital ¥300; awarded gold medals at foreign and home exhibitions for art printing and type-founding; contractors to Government. President S. Nomura. — p. 712 ([scan leaf 811](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n811/mode/1up))

### Fishery and Marine Products

**Daito Fisheries Co** K.K., Kochi, Kochi-ken. Est. July 1907; capital ¥200 p.u.; div. 10%. Catches are chiefly whales. Pres. Z. Inouye. — p. 743 ([scan leaf 812](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n812/mode/1up))

**Fusan Fisheries Co** K.K., Fusan, Korea. Est. May, 1889, organized into present Co. in May, 1907. Capital ¥210 p.u.; div. 10%. Chiefly engaged in fish market, also marine production and ice making. **Nippon Fisheries Co** K.K., Homminatocho, Kyobashi, Tokyo. Est. Nov. 1906; capital ¥68 p.u.; div. 7.35%; catches are chiefly whales and herrings. Pres. D. Aoki. — p. 743 ([scan leaf 812](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n812/mode/1up))

**Teikoku Fisheries Co** K.K., Shimoyamatedori VI., Kobe. Est. Jan. '07; capital ¥500 p.u.; div. 5%. Chiefly engaged in salmon, yellow-tail and trout fishery and trawling. Chairman K. ### Shipping

**Chosen Yusen Kaisha** K.K., Seoul, Korea. Est. '12; cap. ¥1,500 p.u.; reserves ¥58; debts ¥93; div. 7%. Own 31 vessels and 10,325 tons gross. Pres. K. Harada; Mng.-Dir. H. Yoshida. — p. 743 ([scan leaf 812](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n812/mode/1up))

**Japan-China Steamship Co** = Nisshin S.S. Co. — p. 743 ([scan leaf 812](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n812/mode/1up))

**Nippon Yusen Kaisha** K.K., Yurakucho, Koj., Tokyo. Pres. R. Kondo; Vice-Pres. T. Suda; Mng.-Dirs. T. Hori, T. Hayashi; Dirs. K. Harada, T. Tanii, R. Negishi, R. Toyokawa. — p. 743 ([scan leaf 812](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n812/mode/1up))

**Toyo Kisen Kaisha** K.K., Yurakucho, Koj., Tokyo. Pres. S. Asano; Dirs. H. Okawa, K. Okura, M. Shiraishi, Y. Ito, S. Nakajima, R. Asano. — p. 743 ([scan leaf 812](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n812/mode/1up))

### Shipbuilding

**Hakodate Dockyards Co** K.K., Hakodate, Hokkaido. Est. Nov., 1896; cap. ¥570 p.u.; reserves ¥89; debts ¥640; div. 8%. Pres. T. Kawata; Mng.-Dir. K. Kondo. — p. 743 ([scan leaf 812](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n812/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Ishikawajima Dockyards, K.K** Ishikawajima, Tokyo. Shipbuilding and Engineering works; one of the oldest private establishments; reorganized in Jan. 1887 as K.K. with expanded business; capital ¥1,020 pu. Pres. K. Watanabe; Mng. T. Uchida. Vid. Chap. Industry. — p. 744 ([scan leaf 813](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n813/mode/1up))

**Kawasaki Dockyards Co., K.K** Kobo. The forerunner of the Tokyo Tsukiji Dockyard, founded in 1878 by Mr. Shozo Kawasaki; reorganized in '96 as K.K.; cap. 12,500 p.u.; reserves ¥3,209; loans ¥13,474; div. 12%. Pres. Kojiro Matsukata; Vice-Pres. Yoshitaro Kawasaki. — p. 744 ([scan leaf 813](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n813/mode/1up))

**Mitsubishi Dockyards** established by the Mitsubishi Firm; see the Mitsubishi Firm under Sec. General. — p. 744 ([scan leaf 813](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n813/mode/1up))

**Osaka Iron Works, K.K** Kawaguchi, Osaka. Est. March, 1914 as K.K. Pres. J. Yamaoka; Mng.-Dirs. R. Yamaguchi, R. Kimura. — p. 744 ([scan leaf 813](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n813/mode/1up))

**Uraga Dockyards Co., K.K** Yurakucho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. Pres. T. Machida; Mng.-Dirs. H. Oki, T. Yamaguchi, etc. — p. 744 ([scan leaf 813](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n813/mode/1up))

**Yokohama Dockyards Co., K.K** Irifunecho, Yokohama. Est. June 1891; cap. ¥2,475 p.u.; reserves ¥912; div. 9%. Chairman of Dirs. R. Hara; Mng.-Dirs. S. Yamada, J. Kondo. — p. 744 ([scan leaf 813](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n813/mode/1up))

### Section XXI. Hotels

**Fujiya Hotel, K.K** Miyanoshita, Hakone. Formed '93 by the Yamaguchi family; is reputed as one of the best hotels in Japan, and is provided with hot spring bath. — p. 744 ([scan leaf 813](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n813/mode/1up))

**Imperial Hotel, K.K** Uchi-Yamashita-cho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. Est. in 1907; cap. ¥1,200; Directors, K. Okura (Pres.), A. Hayashi (Mng.) — p. 744 ([scan leaf 813](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n813/mode/1up))

### Section XXII. Miscellaneous

**Akita Timber Co., K.K** Noshiro, Akita. Est. March, '07, amalgamating with the Noshiro Hanzai, Akita Seizai and Noshiro Zaimoku Cos.; capital ¥1,200 p.u. Directors; N. Isaka (Pres), D. Takemura, S. Kikuchi (Mng.-Dir.) — p. 744 ([scan leaf 813](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n813/mode/1up))

**Fukuda & Co** Nishiiru, Muromachi, Kyoto. Est. about 400 years ago; gold foil store exporting its goods to Europe, America and China; awarded highest honours at foreign and domestic exhibitions. Prop. Jusuke Fukuda. — p. 744 ([scan leaf 813](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n813/mode/1up))

**Hada Cotton Belt Mnf. Co** Sotocmachi, Honjoku, Tokyo. Manufacturers of and dealers in cotton belts; obtained a patent for "Hada belt machine" 1899; supply to the Mitsui, Mitsubishi, Furukawa, Kuhara and others. — p. 744 ([scan leaf 813](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n813/mode/1up))

**Malay Rubber Plantation Co., K.K** Uchisaiwaicho, Tokyo. Est. '12 with cap. ¥125 p.u. is undertaking rubber planting at Malay Island, leasing land 2,890 acres. Directors: Chairman Y. Isobe; Mng.-Dirs. S. Matsumiya, C. Asada, S. Tajiri. — p. 744 ([scan leaf 813](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n813/mode/1up))

**Nagoya Timber Co., K.K** Atsuta, Nagoya, Aichi prefecture. Est. 1907; cap. ¥400 p.u.; div. 7%. Pres. K. Hasegawa; Mng.-Dir. T. Suzuki; Dirs. K. Harada, K. Takeuchi. — p. 744 ([scan leaf 813](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n813/mode/1up))

**Nippon Cork Co., K.K** Yanagiwaramachi, Honjoku, Tokyo. Est. 1814; home demand is almost monopolized by the Co.; also largely export to Shanghai, etc. — p. 744 ([scan leaf 813](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n813/mode/1up))


## Business Directory

**Nippon Denkyu Co** (electric bulb), K.K., Sugamomachi, Tokyo. Est. 1915; cap. ¥250 p.u. Chairman S. Ohashi; Mng.-Dirs. T. Hatano, T. Yoshimura; Dirs. K. Makoshi, K. Shin, K. Arao. — p. 745 ([scan leaf 814](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n814/mode/1up))

**Nippon Crucible Co** K.K., Shimo-Shibuya, Tokyo-fu. Est. 1883; cap. ¥250 p.u.; div. 12%; in 1907 the Co. amalgamated with Imperial Crucible Co., and Osaka Crucible Co.; latest yearly sales reached 3,500,000 pieces; largely export to China, India and South Seas. Dirs. M. Saegusa, S. Iwasaki; Mng. K. Taguchi. — p. 745 ([scan leaf 814](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n814/mode/1up))

**Nippon Rubber Co** K.K., Tamahimecho, Asakusaku, Tokyo. Est. 1900; cap. ¥156 p.u.; div. 10%. Chairman S. Nakamura; Dirs. E. Otsuka, N. Okuma, S. Yoshida. — p. 745 ([scan leaf 814](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n814/mode/1up))

**Nishikawa & Co** Shijodori, Kyoto city. A leading bamboo wares establishment with a branch store in Tokyo. Bamboo screens for parlor first designed by the store, are much vogue in America. Prop. Genjiro Nishikawa. — p. 745 ([scan leaf 814](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n814/mode/1up))

**Osaka Electric Apparatus Mnf. Co** K.K., Nishinari-gori, Osaka-fu. Est. 1915. Dirs. H. Suzuki, S. Kadoma, S. Imanishi. — p. 745 ([scan leaf 814](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n814/mode/1up))

**Oriental Exploitation Co** K.K., Yurakucho, Koj., Tokyo. Est. July 1909 by Baron Shibusawa, Baron R. Kondo, T. Masuda, S. Hayakawa, Hara and other leading businessmen of Tokyo with the object of making investment for railway, mining and engineering enterprises in China; capital ¥250 p.u. Dir. K. Furuichi. — p. 745 ([scan leaf 814](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n814/mode/1up))

**Osaka Electric Bulb Co** K.K., Nishinari-gun, Osaka. Est. 1907; cap. ¥650 p.u.; res. ¥67; div. 10%; special div. 5%. Pres. S. Watanabe; Dirs. K. Otsuka, K. Kimura. — p. 745 ([scan leaf 814](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n814/mode/1up))

**Otaru Timber Co** K.K., Otaru, Hokkaido; leading timber suppliers in Japan. Own three steamships and undertake shipping; contractors to the Imperial Gov't Rlys. and South Manchuria Rly. Co.; capital ¥1,410 p.u. Directors, K. Okura (Pres.), T. Doi (Mng.); Branches, Tokyo, Kobe and Dairen. — p. 745 ([scan leaf 814](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n814/mode/1up))

**Sakura-Gumi** Gs. K., Yaesucho, Kojimachiku, Tokyo. Boot and Shoe makers, with close connections with the Nippon Leather & Co. Partners, J. Watanabe, J. Sato. — p. 745 ([scan leaf 814](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n814/mode/1up))

**Takeuchi & Co** Gs. K., Bakurocho, Nihombashi, Tokyo. Est. about 50 years ago; organized as Gs. K., 1911, cap. ¥300; one of the oldest makers of safes in Japan, capable to turn out 600 cases per month. — p. 745 ([scan leaf 814](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n814/mode/1up))

**To-a Kogyo (Industrial) Co** K.K., Yurakucho, Tokyo. Est. 1899; cap. ¥250 p.u.; div. 10%. The Co. supply funds for undertakings in China. Directors: Prs. K. Furuichi; Dirs. S. Iwashita, J. Kadono, K. Shiraiwa. — p. 745 ([scan leaf 814](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n814/mode/1up))

**To-a Tobacco Co** K.K., Minami Nabecho, Kyobashi, Tokyo. Est. '06; cap. ¥1,700 p.u.; annual div. 12%; is carrying on the business in Chosen and Manchuria. Products for 2nd half-year 1914, in Chosen 643,201,400 pieces of cigarette and 4,499,000 momme of cut tobacco, in Manchuria 226,550,000 pieces of cigarette and 1,980,000 momme of cut tobacco. Directors: Pres. K. Sasa; Mng.-Dir. T. Fujita; Dirs. R. Ezoye; M. Iwaya, etc. — p. 745 ([scan leaf 814](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n814/mode/1up))

**Tokunaga Shoten** Yokoyamacho, Nihombashi, Tokyo. Manufacturers of and dealers in hair brushes; have a factory at Koishikawa, Shibuya and Tamagawa; annual output is estimated at ¥100. Prop. Y. Tokunaga. — p. 745 ([scan leaf 814](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n814/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Tokyo Cordage Mnf. Co., K.K** Kyobashiku, Tokyo. Est. 1837 ; cap. ¥1,875 p.u.; res. 1,040; div. 15%. Pres. K. Yamada; Mng.-Dirs. S. Asano, K. Okura, S. Fukayama. — p. 746 ([scan leaf 815](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n815/mode/1up))

**Tokyo Electric Co., K.K** Kawasaki, Kanagawa-ken. Est. Apl. 18 6; cap. ¥2 [illegible]00 p.u.; manufacture electric lamps etc. Pres. I. Fujioka. — p. 746 ([scan leaf 815](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n815/mode/1up))

**Toyo Rubber Co., K.K** Azumamachi, Tokyo-fu. Est. 1901 ; cap. ¥225 p.u.; res. ¥24; div. 8%. Mng.-Dir. N. Nagatomi; Dirs. H. Okawa, M. Onishi, K. Kimura, E. Tanaka. — p. 746 ([scan leaf 815](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n815/mode/1up))

**Yokohama Nursery Co., K.K** Nakamura-cho Yokohama. Est. '90; exporters of nursery stocks, seed and plants; capital ¥500. Branches: London New York, etc. Directors. U. Suzuki (Mng.-Dir.), S. Iida (Mng.) — p. 746 ([scan leaf 815](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n815/mode/1up))

### Exporters and Importers

**D. Nagase & Co** Osaka, general exporters and importers; line of exports.—general Japanese produce, drugs, oils, chemicals, manufactured goods etc.; line of imports.—chemicals, dyes, drugs etc.; branches at Kobe, Kyoto and Tokyo, and London office 65 Fenchurch St., E.C. Pres., D. Nagase, Dojima Hama-dori, Osaka. — p. 746 ([scan leaf 815](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n815/mode/1up))

**E. H. Hunter & Co** Hirano-cho, Kobe. Formed 1868 by E. H. Hunter; first engaged in rice export business but have begun many years since to import machineries and also engaged in antimony refining business; have special connection with Osaka Iron Works. Prop. Ryutaro Hunter. — p. 746 ([scan leaf 815](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n815/mode/1up))

**Gosho Goshi Kaisha** Nakanoshima, Osaka. Est. 1905 as partnership firm with capital ¥1,000; manufacturers and exporters of underwear, etc., annual export amounting to ¥4,000, also manufacture and export yarns to China and India; branches at Kobe and Tsingtau. Acting partner, Y. Kitagawa. — p. 746 ([scan leaf 815](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n815/mode/1up))

**Kawamata Kempu Seiren Kabushiki Kaisha** Masagocho, Yokohama. Leading silk exporters, est. '99 with capital ¥100 p.u. Brand: Kawamata Habutaye, Deer brand. Mng.-Dir. K. Komatsu. — p. 746 ([scan leaf 815](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n815/mode/1up))

**Kodera & Co** Newchwang, Manchuria. Oil factors and shipping agents. Branches: London, Dairen, Kobe, Tokyo, Vladivostock, Mukden, Harbin, etc. Prop. Sokichi Kodera, Nakayamatedori, Kobe. — p. 746 ([scan leaf 815](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n815/mode/1up))

**Meidi-Ya, K.K** Importers and dealers in groceries, provisions and agents of Kirin Beer, Nunobiki Tansan Mineral Water, Jas. Buchanan's Whisky, J. Brown's Scotch Whisky, etc. Head Office; Honcho. Yokohama. Est. 1885 by late Mr. H. Isono, reorganized as partnership unlimited company 1903; changed into limited liability company May '11. Warranted purveyors to the Imperial Household and the Imperial Navy. Capital ¥500. Branches: Tokyo, Osaka, Kobe, Moji and Seoul. Directors: G. Yonei (Pres.), C. Isono (Vice-Pres.), M. Miyaji (Mng. Dir.), K. Sano (Dir.). — p. 746 ([scan leaf 815](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n815/mode/1up))

**Masuda & Co., Gm. K** Honcho, Yokohama. Importers and exporters; est. Feb. '12; Cap. ¥500. Principal exports.—Timber, sulphur, superphosphates, spelter, metals and minerals, Portland cement, fish-oil, sugar, flour, etc.; principal imports,—sugar, grains, sulphate of ammonia, iron, steel, hides and skins, etc. Branches: Tokyo, Kobe, Osaka, Sydney, Shanghai, Dairen, Hankow, Tientsin, Tsingtau, Tainan, Takao, Nawa, Shimonoseki, Otaru, Nagoya, Newchwang, and London. Have flour mills, of flour, sugar refinery, etc., also possess Matsuo sulphur mine. Dirs. Fusajiro Nakamura and Masutaro Masuda. — p. 746 ([scan leaf 815](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n815/mode/1up))


## Business Directory

**Mitsui Bussan Kaisha** K.K., General importers and exporters, Surugacho, Nihombashi, Tokyo. Established in the latter part of 10th century as a dry goods store, and the Mitsui Bussan were organized in 1876 to undertake general trading but more particularly foreign trade. Reorganized in Oct. '09 as Joint Stock Co. with other enterprises, all practically owned by the 11 branches of the Mitsui family; capital ¥20,000 p.u. Directors: Pres. G. Mitsui; Mng.-Dirs. S. Watanabe, K. Fukui and M. Fujise. Branches: London, New York, &c.; connection world wide. — p. 747 ([scan leaf 816](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n816/mode/1up))

**Nippon Seicha Kaisha** (Japan Tea Firing Co.), K.K., general merchants and tea exporters, Miyagawa-cho, Yokohama. Founded Feb. 1894 with the object of exporting Japanese tea to other countries chiefly tea produced in Shizuoka; capital ¥100 p.u. Directors: K. Otani (Pres.), Konosuke Otani (Vice-Pres.), K. Kameda (Act. Mg.) — p. 747 ([scan leaf 816](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n816/mode/1up))

**Nippon Tea Exports Co., K.K** Aioicho, Kobe. Est. Feb. 1898; capital ¥250 p.u.; chiefly deal in tea produced at Kyoto and vicinity. Branches: Shizuoka, Fushimi, Chicago and New York. Directors: K. Ito (Pres.), H. Komada (Mng. Dir.), T. Yabuta (Mng.) — p. 747 ([scan leaf 816](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n816/mode/1up))

**Ogura & Co** 16 Kitanakadori Yokohama (export dep't). Dealers in Manila hemp, China hemp, jute, fertilizers, straw and chip braid, Formosa hats, flax canns, Manila rope, grain and seeds, etc. Head office at Tokyo; branches at Osaka, Kobe, Manila, Hankow; factories at Tokyo, Yokohama and Choshi; sale agents in Europe, Iida & Co. at 122 Wood Street, London, E.C. — p. 747 ([scan leaf 816](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n816/mode/1up))

**Okuragumi** see under Section General. — p. 747 ([scan leaf 816](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n816/mode/1up))

**Yamanaka & Co** No. 2, Andojimachi, Minamiku, Osaka, general importers and exporters of copper, lead, tin, spelter, antimony, and other metals. — p. 747 ([scan leaf 816](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n816/mode/1up))

**Yonei & Co** General importers and exporters, Ginza, Tokyo. Est. in 1887 by late K. Isono under the name of Isono & Co.; contractors to the several Government Departments; agents for many foreign Cos. Branches: Osaka, Kobe, Seoul, &c. Prop. G. Yonei. — p. 747 ([scan leaf 816](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n816/mode/1up))

**Yu-asa Firm** Gochome, Yamamoto-dori, Kobe. General exporters and importers. Prop. Takenosuke Yu-asa. — p. 747 ([scan leaf 816](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n816/mode/1up))

**Yu-asa Firm** Takakura Higashi-iru, Gojo, Kyoto. Importers and exporters; dealers in metal wares, miscellaneous goods, etc. Prop. Shichizaemon Yu-asa. — p. 747 ([scan leaf 816](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n816/mode/1up))

### Section XXIV. General

**Mitsubishi & Co** Gs. K., Mining, shipbuilding, ironworks, banking, &c. Yayesucho, Koj., Tokyo. The Company was known as the Mitsubishi Mail Steamship Co. until 1885 then amalgamated with the Kyodo Unyu Kaisha, forming present Nippon Yusen Kaisha, to which was handed over shipping business. In 1894, was reorganized as partnership limited company, capital ¥15,000. Directors: Pres. K. Iwasaki; Mngs. K. Nambu, S. Sho. Dept. Chiefs: S. Sho (Internal & General), S. Harada (Mining & Iron Works), K. Kimura (Mining), T. Eguchi (Business), H. Maruta (Shipbuilding), Z. Kirishima (Real Estate), M. Kushida (Banking.) — p. 747 ([scan leaf 816](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n816/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Mitsui & Co** Gm. K., Surugacho, Nihombashi, Tokyo. Est. 1893 with cap. 50,000. Controlling centre of all the undertakings run by the Mitsui. Pres. Baron Hachiroemon Mitsui, Mng.-Dir. Baron Hachirojiro Mitsui. — p. 743 ([scan leaf 817](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n817/mode/1up))

**Nippon Shoji Kaisha (Japan Trading Co., Ltd.)** K.K., Kabutocho, Nihombashi-ku, Tokyo. Est. 1916, cap. ¥500 (¼ p.u.); general exporters and importers; licensed sellers of gunpowder, dynamite, etc. Pres. H. Kawasaki; Mng.-Dir. M. Ogawa. — p. 743 ([scan leaf 817](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n817/mode/1up))

**Okuragumi** K.K., General exporters and importers, Ginza, Tokyo. The firm organized as a partnership with Baron Kihachiro Okura, well-known merchant in the country, president, in 1873. Reorganized as a joint stock Co. in Dec. '11; capital yen 4,000 p.u. The firm are the first Japanese firm engaged in foreign business; divided into manufacturing, spinning, mining, engineering, electrical, shipping, forestry, architecture, and insurance departments. Directors: Pres. Baron K. Okura; Vice-Pres. C. Kadono; Dirs. Kumema Okura, Kishichiro Okura, K. Takashima, H. Okura and U. Yamada. — p. 743 ([scan leaf 817](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n817/mode/1up))

**Sumitomo Ginko** Bankers, Osaka; first established in about 1871, and reorganized in 1895 with capital of yen 1,000. Again reorganized, as a joint stock Co. in '12, cap. ¥7,500 p.u. Began foreign draft business in 1903. Connection with Europe and America. In the return of 2nd half 1915, the reserve fund shows yen 1,100. Branches in Tokyo, Yokohama and 15 others. Proprietors, Baron K. Sumitomo; Mng.-Dir. K. Yukawa. — p. 743 ([scan leaf 817](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n817/mode/1up))

**Suzuki & Co** unltd., Sakaecho, Kobe. Est. 1887, capital ¥500. General merchants and manufacturers; Industrial Dep't.,— camphor, fish-oil, electrolytic cathode, copper, spelter, etc. Import Dep't—sugar, grains, machineries, chemicals, etc. Export Dep't,— sugar, grains, metals, provisions, etc. Branches and Agencies at London, New York, Melbourne, Vladivostock, Shanghai, etc., etc. Manager, B. Nishikawa; Rep. of the Co., Mrs. Suzuki. — p. 743 ([scan leaf 817](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n817/mode/1up))

**Takata Shokai** Gs. K., Eirakucho, Marunouchi, Tokyo. General importers and exporters. Est. 1880, succeeding Mr. Bair's business, a German merchant; reorganized 1908, cap. yen 1,000. Contractors to Government and their connections are world wide. Branches: London, New York, Osaka and Shanghai. Dirs. Shinzo Takata, (Pres.) Nobujiro Takata and Kamakichi Takata. — p. 743 ([scan leaf 817](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1917/page/n817/mode/1up))
